# Decisions log

<!-- Committed. Newest at the top. One entry per decision. A settled decision is reopened only by the owner. -->

## 2026-10-14 — M1d-2: bootstrap-secrets restore and the owner-only key-version override
- **Decision:** (1) **`bootstrap-secrets restore --env-file <env>`** (`infra/host/skiw_host/bootstrap_secrets.py`) reads the decrypted `skiw-escrow-1` bundle on stdin (the owner pipes `age --decrypt` over SSH; the private identity never reaches the host) and checks it all in memory before writing anything: the format, every inventory app key present and no other entry (a DB password is refused), each version a `v<n>:<base64>` line, not repeated, equal to its SHA-256 (constant-time compare) and at least 32 bytes (`app/config/env.py` `MIN_KEY_BYTES`). Any refusal, a non-empty store or an existing `.restored` exits 2 with nothing written and no directory created; messages name the key and the entry number, never a value (UNI-09). (2) **Order and commit point (same as `init`, M1d-1 / PR 38 R1):** a fresh escrow bundle of exactly what will be installed is written first (so a version minted by the override is escrowed before it exists on the host) and its directory entry made durable; then every version is installed verbatim, one file per key, oldest first; then `/etc/skiw/secrets/.restored` (root, 0400, content: the UTC time); then the store directory is synced. **The marker is the last file before the commit point**: a failure writing any key file or the marker removes everything the run wrote (exit 1, retryable). After the commit point the override lines and the summary line form the final log step; if any of them fails the keys, the bundle and the marker are kept and the run exits 3 with a value-free message that repeats the override names and versions so the owner can record them by hand. (3) **`init` and `restore` both refuse when `.restored` exists**, with a message naming it (checked before the generic non-empty-store check). (4) **`--allow-new-key-version NAME`** (repeatable; duplicates collapse; restore only; app-key names only, else exit 2 before anything is read or written): adds one new newest version from `os.urandom(32)`: `v1` when the bundle lacks the key (the only way a bundle missing a key is accepted), otherwise the newest version number plus one; older versions are kept so data under them stays readable. Each override is one line, `restore minted <name> v<n> (--allow-new-key-version)`, on stderr, in the journal and in the deploy log; restore mints nothing else. (5) `main()` takes an injectable `stdin: BinaryIO | None` again (test seam; default `sys.stdin.buffer`). (6) **Evidence (2026-10-14, macOS):** `make host-test` 63 passed, 9 deselected (49 before; 14 new in `test_bootstrap_secrets.py`: verbatim restore then `init` and `restore` refuse, seven bad-bundle cases each refused for its own reason with nothing written, non-empty store, override minting and recording, override refusals, value secrecy across init + restore + override, rollback when the marker write fails, exit 3 on a journal failure after restore, escrow-before-keys and marker-before-commit order for restore). Each check was shown to fail in a scratch copy: the SHA-256 check removed, a missing key accepted, a non-inventory entry accepted, the length check removed, the marker ignored, the marker not written, the override minting a second key, the override allowed for `init`, the override line skipped, the override line carrying the key value, the rollback removed. Not repeated in this PR: the real-age, root-container restore run (done on the unsplit build, recorded in M1d-1).
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1d:** none beyond the M1d-1 split. The plan says restore "checks the manifest" and "mints nothing unless" the override; this adds that restore also writes a fresh escrow bundle and that the override never replaces a version, only appends a newest one.
- **Still OPEN:** the narrow noninteractive elevation that the streamed restore needs (PR 39 review R1): the operator account has password-protected `sudo` (spec H2), but the restore pipeline has no terminal and its stdin is the plaintext bundle, so the runbook command is conditional (`ssh -T ... sudo -n ...`, with a `sudo -n -l bootstrap-secrets` preflight before decrypting) until M1f provisions a `sudoers` rule for that one command (not global passwordless `sudo`, not root login, no password in the stream) and M1k proves it, including that a missing rule refuses with no store change. An override makes data encrypted or signed under the lost version permanently unreadable (accepted by design; the owner decides each use and records it); on hosts (M1k): real `age` with the owner's recipients, the off-host copy of the bundle, and a restore on a fresh VPS; G5, G10, G11 as in M1d-1.
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §3 M1d; spec §8.4 H4, H6; UNI-03, UNI-09, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** installing the bundle's versions without re-escrowing (an overridden key would exist on the host with no escrow); writing the marker after the commit point or as part of the log step (a marker failure would then leave a restored host with no record that it was restored, outside the rollback); writing the marker before the keys (a crash between the two would leave a marker on a partial store); an override that replaces the lost version in place (would silently change which key reads existing data); reading the bundle from a file on the host (the plaintext would touch the host's disk).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw_host/bootstrap_secrets.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_bootstrap_secrets.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M1d; each use of the override is the owner's decision at recovery time

## 2026-10-14 — M1d-1: bootstrap-secrets init, secret inventory and the escrow bundle
- **Decision:** (1) **Inventory** (`infra/host/skiw_host/secrets.py`): the H4 names, each with its kind, the Compose services that read it and the owning uid. App keys (minted, escrowed): `django_secret_key`, `tenant_token_key`, `approval_mac_key` (web, worker, migrate), `lookup_decoy_key` (migrate), `tenant_source_key`, `login_blob_key`, `audit_mac_key`, `field_kek`, `blind_index_key`, `crm_jar_kek` (web, worker), all uid 10001. DB passwords (set by `bootstrap-db`, M1e; never escrowed): `skiw_app_password` (10001), `skiw_migrator_password` (10001), `skiw_backup_password` (10003), `postgres_password` (999), `skiw_readonly_password` (root; no service mounts it, socket only). Owner-supplied: `ghcr_read_token` (root; read by `skiw-deploy` as root, M1g). The Compose config test now takes its secret-per-service map from this inventory and gains an `owners` check (each mounted secret's owner is the service's uid, with a planted case); a docker-free test checks that the `compose.yml` secret files are exactly the inventory names that have readers. (2) **`bootstrap-secrets init`** (`infra/host/bootstrap-secrets`, `skiw_host/bootstrap_secrets.py`, `skiw_host/escrow.py`; Python stdlib): it refuses when the store holds any file; validates everything in memory; then **write the escrow bundle first** and install the key files after it, removing everything the run wrote on any failure, so no key is installed without its escrow and a failed run leaves an empty store to retry. Files are written as a `0600` `O_EXCL` temp file in the same directory, fsynced, set to `0400`, chowned to the inventory uid (group root), hard-linked into place (never replaces a file), then the directory is fsynced. Store and escrow directories root `0700`. `restore` (with the `.restored` marker and the owner-only `--allow-new-key-version` override) is M1d-2: the owner split the plan's M1d in two on 2026-10-14 because the whole was about 650 lines. After the escrow bundle is written its directory entry (and every ancestor the run just created) is made durable with an fsync before the first key file is installed, and the secret store directory is synced after the last (PR 38 review R1). The commit point is the last key file: a failure before it rolls everything back (exit 1, nothing left); a failure of the final journal or deploy-log write after it keeps the keys and the bundle and exits 3 with a value-free message, and the runbook says not to run `init` again. Exit 0 done, 2 refused, 1 failed, 3 installed but the log step failed. (3) **Bundle format** (plaintext before age): JSON `{"format": "skiw-escrow-1", "created": <UTC>, "keys": {<name>: [{"line": "v<n>:<base64>", "sha256": <hex of the line>}]}}`; app keys only. (4) **Escrow recipients (G5):** `SKIW_ESCROW_RECIPIENTS` in `infra/host/env/<environment>.env` (empty in all three files until the owner gives the public recipients; the script then refuses). Only `age1…` values are accepted; a value containing `AGE-SECRET-KEY-` or anything else is refused without being printed. (5) **age pin (UNI-14):** v1.3.2; `age-v1.3.2-linux-amd64.tar.gz` SHA-256 `cbe24006683f8eb669266162894b9a522a1af52f2665fbc63a4bb032ed26ac10`, computed from the download on 2026-10-14 and equal to the asset digest GitHub's release API reports; the `.proof` file is **not verified**. The script runs `/usr/local/bin/age` and refuses unless `age --version` prints `v1.3.2`, and checks the output starts with the age header. Installing it on hosts is M1f (`apply`), in the backup image M1g. (6) **Test seams:** `--root` and `--age` (hidden options; production defaults `/` and `/usr/local/bin/age`), an injected `chown` and journal in `main()`; tests use a temp root, a fake `age` and values generated per run. (7) **Evidence (2026-10-14, macOS; the count is for the unsplit build with `restore`, the init-only PR has 49 passed after the PR 38 review R1 fix):** `make host-test` 53 passed (20 new in `test_bootstrap_secrets.py`, 2 new in the Compose config test). Each check was shown to fail in a scratch copy: a key line printed to stderr or put in the journal line, a DB password added to the bundle, the SHA-256 check removed, a missing key accepted, the private-identity message echoing the value, any recipient accepted, the marker ignored, mode 0440, the rollback removed, the override minting a second key, the backup uid changed. Outside the suite, in a throwaway container as root (Alpine, the real age v1.3.2 amd64 binary under emulation, identity generated inside the container and discarded): `init` installed 10 files `400 10001:0`, directories `700 0:0`, bundle starting with the age header; a second `init` refused; on the unsplit build, `age --decrypt | bootstrap-secrets restore` on a second root installed byte-identical files and `.restored` (the restore code ships in M1d-2); no key value appeared in stdout or stderr.
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1d:** the plan's M1d is split in two PRs (init here, restore in M1d-2); the name `skiw_readonly_password` follows the `compose.yml` pattern (no file name was fixed); the ghcr token is placed by a runbook command, not by the script; the deploy log path `/var/log/skiw/deploy.log` is a builder value [A] that M1g must use or change here.
- **Still OPEN:** G5 (owner's public escrow recipients, staging and production); G11 (the staging-check secret name is not in the inventory until the mechanism is chosen); G10 (`ghcr_read_token` type and owner); whether `skiw_readonly` needs a password at all if `pg_hba` allows it only over `local` with peer auth (M1e); the age `.proof` verification; on hosts (M1k): real recipients, off-host copy of the bundle, a restore on a fresh VPS, `check-hardening` of owners and modes (M1f).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §3 M1d, §2, §6; spec §8.4 H4, H6, T16; UNI-01, UNI-07, UNI-08, UNI-09, UNI-14, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** installing the keys before the bundle (a failure of `age` would leave unescrowed keys); `rename` into place (can replace an existing file); passing recipients on the command line (they belong to host config); a YAML parser for the compose check (new dependency; the rendered-config test already covers readers and uids).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/bootstrap-secrets`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{secrets,escrow,bootstrap_secrets}.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_bootstrap_secrets.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` (new), `docs/runbooks/README.md`.
- **Versions:** age v1.3.2 (tool pin, above); no new Python or npm dependency.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M1d; G5, G10, G11 left to the owner

## 2026-10-14 — M1c: Caddy edge, where each security header is applied, live edge tests
- **Decision:** (1) **Caddy config:** `infra/host/caddy/Caddyfile` (hosts) and `infra/host/caddy/ci.Caddyfile` (CI only, `tls internal`) are two entry files with the same global options (`admin off`) that import the shared rules in `infra/host/caddy/edge.caddy`; `compose.yml` mounts only `Caddyfile`, `edge.caddy` and `maintenance.html`, read-only. The hostname is `{$SKIW_HOSTNAME}` (empty on hosts until G9, so Compose refuses to render). An explicit `http://{$SKIW_HOSTNAME}` block sets HSTS and answers `308` to the fixed target `https://{$SKIW_HOSTNAME}{uri}`; it never proxies. The HTTPS site sets HSTS, `request_body max_size 25MB` (decimal, 25 000 000 bytes; FILE-02 outer cap), `reverse_proxy web:8000` with transport `read_timeout`/`write_timeout` 60 s, and `handle_errors 502 503 504` serving `maintenance.html` (static, no script, no external resource, `lang="en-MY"`, navy on white) with status 503, `Retry-After: 30` [A], `Cache-Control: no-store` and HSTS. No `trusted_proxies`: Caddy drops client-sent `X-Forwarded-For/-Proto/-Host` and sets its own. A `make host-test` test fails if the host files contain `tls internal`, `local_certs`, `skip_install_trust` or `trusted_proxies`, if `admin off` is missing, or if `compose.yml` names `ci.Caddyfile`. (2) **Where each header is applied (UNI-17):** HSTS `max-age=31536000` — Caddy, on every response including the HTTP→HTTPS redirect, the maintenance page and proxied app responses (M1c); Django sets the same value from M2 (spec T6), and Caddy sets it deferred (`header >…`), so a response carries exactly one HSTS header. HTTP→HTTPS redirect and TLS — Caddy. Nonce-based CSP and `frame-ancestors 'none'` — Django, from M2 (not set by this PR). `X-Forwarded-Proto` — set by Caddy; Django trusts it (`SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER`) only when `DJANGO_BEHIND_CADDY=1`, set in `compose.yml` for the app services; unset locally and in unit tests, and `env.flag` treats any other value as unset (not trusted). (3) **Live edge tests** (`infra/host/tests/test_edge_live.py`, marker `host_live`, `make host-test-live`, CI `host` job, timeout 10 → 20 min): build the app image and pass its local image ID (`sha256:<64 hex>`, the content digest of the image config) as `SKIW_IMAGE`, because the registry digest that `compose.yml` requires on hosts exists only after a push (M1h); start caddy, web and db from `compose.yml` with `env/ci.env` under a random project name, plus `infra/host/tests/edge/compose.edge.yml` (test only: `ci.Caddyfile`; ports on `127.0.0.1` chosen by Docker, so no root, no privileged port and no clash on the runner or a laptop, while `compose.yml` still publishes 80/443 and its config test still checks that; `local` logging, as Docker Desktop has no journald; the role bootstrap `docker/postgres/10-roles.sh` until M1e; the probe settings mounted into `web`; the stub) and a generated override that points every secret at random values written per run to a pytest temp dir. Nothing secret is committed. The probe view (`is_secure()` and the `X-Forwarded-For` Django received) lives in `infra/host/tests/edge/edge_probe/`, is mounted only by the test and is never in the app image. The 25 s case uses a stdlib stub run from the app image under the network alias `web` while `web` is stopped, so the request goes through the production `reverse_proxy web:8000` and its timeouts. `down -v --remove-orphans` runs even when start-up fails. (4) **Caddy volumes (M1b-2 open item):** the image's `/data/caddy` and `/config/caddy` are mode 1777 and Docker copies them into a new named volume, so uid 10002 wrote its internal CA and certificate in the live run. (5) **G13 observation:** Caddy as uid 10002 bound container ports 80/443 in the live run; `compose.yml` keeps the H1 wording (`cap_add: NET_BIND_SERVICE`) unchanged. (6) **Evidence (2026-10-14, Docker 29.6.1, Compose v5.3.0, macOS):** `make host-test-live` 7 passed in about 60 s. Each check was shown to fail in a scratch copy: HSTS removed (redirect, app page and maintenance tests fail), `trusted_proxies` added (forwarded-header test fails), `max_size 100MB` (413 test fails), `read_timeout 10s` (25 s test fails: 504 turned into the maintenance page), `handle_errors 404` only (502 instead of 503), `DJANGO_BEHIND_CADDY=0` (`is_secure()` false), `tls internal` in the host `Caddyfile` (`make host-test` fails).
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1c:** the probe is mounted by the test rather than kept in a settings module in the app tree; the stub runs as a container under the alias `web` rather than as a host process; `compose.yml` gains the Caddy file mounts and `DJANGO_BEHIND_CADDY` (the compose tests stay green); the CI `host` job builds the app image a second time (the `image` job also builds it). A plain-HTTP request whose `Host` is not `SKIW_HOSTNAME` gets Caddy's automatic 308 to the same host without HSTS (seen in the prototype run; it never reaches `web`).
- **Still OPEN:** G9 (hostnames, DNS, ACME issuer); on hosts (M1k): `/readyz` 200 through Caddy with a publicly valid certificate and HSTS; G13; `Retry-After` 30 s is a builder value until the deploy outage is measured (M1k); Caddy advertises HTTP/3 (`alt-svc: h3`) but Compose publishes TCP only (clients fall back to HTTP/2) — owner or M1f decides whether to publish UDP 443 or turn HTTP/3 off; HSTS `includeSubDomains`/`preload` are not set (spec says one year only).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §3 M1c, §6; spec §8.4 H1, H2, D26; UNI-07, UNI-13, UNI-14, UNI-16, UNI-17, FILE-02.
- **Alternatives rejected:** an `import` hook or env-driven `tls` issuer in the host `Caddyfile` (the host config would carry a test entry point); a CI route to a host-process stub (would test another `reverse_proxy` block, not the production one); publishing 80/443 in CI (needs free privileged ports; the rules tested do not depend on the port); letting uvicorn/gunicorn trust proxy headers (`forwarded_allow_ips`); a probe view in the app's settings or URLconf.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/caddy/`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/tests/test_edge_live.py`, `infra/host/tests/edge/`, `app/config/settings.py`, `app/tests/test_settings_fail_closed.py`, `pyproject.toml` (marker `host_live`), `Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/runbooks/ci.md`.
- **Versions:** no new dependency; Caddy image unchanged (`caddy:2.11.7-alpine@sha256:d8542f48…`, M1b-2).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M1c; G9 and G13 left to the owner

## 2026-10-13 — M1b-2: host Compose stack and Compose config tests
- **Decision:** (1) **Compose layout:** one project `infra/host/compose.yml` (name `skiw`), separate from the local `docker-compose.yml` (`make dev` and `make test-live` unchanged). Services per spec §8.4 H1: `caddy`, `web`, `worker` (`python app/manage.py runworker`, `stop_grace_period` from host config), `migrate` (profile `deploy`, started only by `skiw-deploy`), `db`, `clamav`, `backup` (profile `ops`). Networks `edge`, `app` (internal), `ops` (internal), `egress`, each with a fixed subnet from `infra/host/env/<environment>.env` (builder values: `172.31.10–13.0/24` on hosts, `172.31.20–23.0/24` in CI; not yet checked against iCore's own networks). `db` and `backup` are on internal networks only; only `caddy` publishes ports (80, 443). Users: app image `10001` (`skiw`), `caddy` `10002`, `backup` `10003`, `db` `999` (`postgres`) from the start, which works with `cap_drop: ALL`, `read_only` and a `/var/run/postgresql` tmpfs (tried locally on Docker 29.6.1). Memory limits per H1 in `production.env`; lower `staging.env` limits (about 3.3 GB on the 4 GB NODE1) are a builder assumption [A]. (2) **Hardening exceptions** (named in `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py` with their reasons; a test fails when one is no longer needed; each exception is bounded: Caddy may add only `NET_BIND_SERVICE`, ClamAV only the five listed capabilities, and `migrate` and `backup` must keep `restart: "no"`, with planted tests that reject a wider capability set or `restart: always`): `caddy` `cap_add: NET_BIND_SERVICE` (H1 wording; G13 is still open); `clamav` runs its root entrypoint with a writable root FS and `CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, FOWNER, SETGID, SETUID` (the smallest set it started with under amd64 emulation, network off; M1i decides non-root); `migrate` and `backup` have no restart policy (one-off). (3) **Digest pinning (UNI-14):** each tag resolved on 2026-10-13 with `docker buildx imagetools inspect <image:tag>` (multi-arch index digest): `caddy:2.11.7-alpine@sha256:d8542f48…`, `clamav/clamav:1.5.4@sha256:638d9020…` (amd64 only), `postgres:17.11@sha256:d74eeac9…` kept equal to `docker-compose.yml` (the tag now resolves to a newer index `2d2b8998…`; Dependabot or a reviewed bump moves both). The app image is `${SKIW_IMAGE}` (required); `ci.env` holds a test-only digest of 64 zeros. (4) **Secrets (H4, UNI-07/08):** Compose file secrets from `/etc/skiw/secrets/<name>`, mounted only into the services the test's inventory names: app keys and `django_secret_key`, `skiw_app_password` in `web`/`worker`; `django_secret_key`, `skiw_migrator_password`, `tenant_token_key`, `approval_mac_key`, `lookup_decoy_key` in `migrate`; `postgres_password` in `db`; `skiw_backup_password` only in `backup` (TEN-02). File names are fixed here for M1d. No secret value is in `environment:`. The app side of these files (reading `*_FILE` values) is M1b-1 (PR 32). (5) **Fail closed:** `SKIW_HOSTNAME` (G9) and `SKIW_WORKER_HARD_EXIT_S`/`SKIW_WORKER_STOP_GRACE_S` (set by the M4 plan, D5) are empty in `staging.env` and `production.env`, so Compose refuses to render either file until they are set; the test proves both files render and pass every check once those values are supplied. (6) `make host-test` runs the Compose tests (`docker compose config --format json`, no daemon); they skip locally without the CLI and fail when `CI=true`.
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1b:** `backup` runs `pg_dump --version` from the pinned `postgres` image until M1g builds the pre-migration dump image. Caddy keeps container ports 80/443 and `NET_BIND_SERVICE` (G13 undecided). Observed for G13: Docker sets `net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start=0` in container network namespaces, so a non-root Caddy bound port 80 with `cap_drop: ALL` and `no-new-privileges` locally; with the kernel default (1024) the bind fails even with `NET_BIND_SERVICE`, as the plan says.
- **Still OPEN:** G9 hostnames; G13; M4 values for the worker grace; whether the named volume `caddy-data` (root-owned from the image) is writable by uid 10002 (M1c); clamav as non-root (M1i); staging memory limits (load check).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §3 M1b; spec §8.4 H1, H2, H4, H6, H13, D5; UNI-04, UNI-07, UNI-08, UNI-14, TEN-02, FILE-03, EVT-06.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `docker compose config -q` only (cannot check the rules); secrets in `environment:` (H4).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/env/`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `Makefile`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`.
- **Versions:** images as in (3); no new Python or npm dependency.
- **Exception to a rule?** no (the hardening exceptions in (2) are H1 `[A]` items, each named in the test with its reason)
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M1b; G13 and G9 left to the owner

## 2026-10-13 — M1b-1: secret files and /readyz
- **Decision:** (1) **Secret files (H4, UNI-07/08):** The app reads `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY_FILE` (`v<n>:<base64>` lines, each at least 32 decoded bytes; the newest base64 text is `SECRET_KEY`, older ones `SECRET_KEY_FALLBACKS`, newest first) and `DATABASE_PASSWORD_FILE` (exactly one line); a malformed file, both forms of a value, or a password in both the URL and the file stops startup, with errors that never echo file content (UNI-02/03/09). Env values keep working for local and CI. `DJANGO_REQUIRE_DB_TLS` stays unset on hosts: spec v1.14 H6 dropped TLS on host-internal networks, which closes the 2026-10-06 "staging TLS connection check" item. (2) **`/readyz`** (spec §8.3): 200 `ok` only when `SELECT current_user` is `skiw_app` and `jobs.startup.pending_migrations()` (the startup gate's check, renamed from `_pending`, unchanged) is empty; otherwise 503 `not ready`.
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1b:** `/readyz` lives in `app/jobs/views.py` (tests in `app/jobs/tests/test_readyz.py`), not `app/health/views.py`, because import-linter keeps `health` and `jobs` independent and `jobs` is on the TEN-10 list of modules that may query the database.
- **Still OPEN:** the Compose stack that mounts these files is M1b-2 (the plan's M1b is split in two PRs of ≈ 310 and ≈ 500 lines; owner chose the split on 2026-10-13).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §3 M1b; spec §8.4 H4, H6, §8.3; UNI-01, UNI-02, UNI-03, UNI-07, UNI-09, TEN-01, TEN-02.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a new shared package for the migration check (would widen the TEN-10 list); letting `health` import `jobs` (breaks the independence contract); secrets in `environment:` (H4).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/config/{env,settings,urls}.py`, `app/jobs/{startup,views}.py`, `app/jobs/tests/`, `app/tests/test_secret_files.py`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`.
- **Versions:** no new Python or npm dependency.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M1b; split chosen by the owner

## 2026-10-13 — M1a: PR split, CDK removal, host tooling scaffold and secret-rule test
- **Decision:** (1) **PR split (gate G1, owner, 2026-10-13: yes):** M1 is built as 11 PRs of ≤ 400 LOC each, M1a–M1k, as in `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` §1 (spec §9 says 1–4 PRs per milestone and ≈ 400 LOC per PR; M1 cannot meet both). (2) **CDK removal (UNI-05):** the CDK skeleton (`infra/app.py`, `infra/cdk.json`, `infra/skiw_infra/`, `infra/tests/`), the `infra` dependency group (`aws-cdk-lib`), the `--no-group infra` Dockerfile flag, `make infra-test` and the CI `infra` job are removed; spec v1.14 replaced the AWS design with the iCore VPS (§8.4). `infra/host/tests/test_layout.py` fails if any deleted file, any file under `infra/` outside `infra/host/`, or any file under `scripts/` other than `deploy-prod` appears. (3) **Host tooling:** Python 3.12, standard library only, package `infra/host/skiw_host/` (plan §1: one language with the app, mypy strict and ruff already in CI, no new dependency, Ubuntu 24.04 ships 3.12). Two import-linter contracts: `skiw_host` imports no app package and none of `django`, `psycopg`, `procrastinate`; app packages do not import `skiw_host` (UNI-04). (4) **Test command:** `make host-test` runs `pytest infra/host/tests` (unit only in M1a) in a new CI `host` job on every PR (UNI-13); `make host-test-live` is a stub that prints that no live host tests exist yet; `make typecheck` covers `app infra/host`; Semgrep registry path `app infra/host web/src`. (5) **gitleaks key names (UNI-07):** `lookup_decoy_key`, `field_kek`, `blind_index_key`, `crm_jar_kek` (`agents/PROJECT.md` §7) added to the `skiw-secret-key-assignment` regex and keywords. `make secrets-test` writes random values generated at test time into a temp directory and asserts one finding per name and none for bare names; it runs in the CI `security` job after the gitleaks install, skips locally without gitleaks and fails (does not skip) when `CI=true`. It is excluded from `make test` by the `secrets` marker. No fake secret is committed. (6) **Dependabot:** `docker-compose` ecosystem for `/` and `/infra/host`, weekly, `chore(deps)` (closes the 2026-10-10 known gap). Whether it bumps `@sha256` digests is checked on the first Dependabot run after M1b adds `infra/host/compose.yml`.
- **Still OPEN:** gates G2 to G17 of the plan §0 are not answered by this entry; no VPS is bought and no host gate has run. **Owner answer (2026-10-13, option 1):** the owner keeps iCore as the chosen host and asks that Hostinger (Kuala Lumpur VPS, KVM plans, listed by the vendor at US$8.99 a month for 8 GB on a 2-year term, US$14.99 on renewal) be compared in the vendor gate (Q38, G-VENDOR, M1k): both vendors get the same written questions. Hostinger's contract entity, DPA, residency terms and OS list were not found on its plan page and are unchecked. Hostinger Web App hosting (Node.js only) cannot run SKIW. No spec change.
- **Why:** Plan M1a; spec v1.18 §8.4, §9 M1; UNI-04, UNI-05, UNI-07, UNI-13.
- **Alternatives rejected:** 4 PRs of ≈ 800–1 000 LOC (G1 option b); shell or a second language for host scripts; a committed gitleaks fixture with a path allow-list (PR 9 review R1).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` (removes `aws-cdk-lib` and its transitive packages), `Makefile`, `Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.gitleaks.toml`, `.env.example`, `docs/runbooks/`, `docs/plans/2026-10-05-m0-scaffold.md` (historical note). `agents/PROJECT.md` §7 `pending M1` is updated in a separate docs PR after merge.
- **Versions:** no new dependency; removed `aws-cdk-lib==2.272.0`.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (G1, 2026-10-13); builder (deep tier: layout, test design)

## 2026-10-12 — Hosting: iCore Malaysian VPS
- **Decision:** (1) **Host (owner, 2026-10-12, "go with icore"):** SKIW runs on one Malaysian VPS from iCore Technology Sdn. Bhd. — hosting decision paper `docs/specs/2026-10-11-hosting-options.md` v1.6, option B4: plan NODE2, 8 GB / 4 cores, RM 59.90/month per the provider's page; servers in CJ1 Data Center, Cyberjaya and AIMS Data Center, Kuala Lumpur (paper F41; page re-read 2026-10-12, which also states "99.9% Uptime Guaranteed" and a separate "server backup solution"). Postgres 17 runs on the same VPS. (2) **Owner answers (question dialog, 2026-10-12):** (a) the server operator is Charlie, the owner; (b) one web instance is acceptable — a short outage on deploy or crash is bearable — and HA or a database standby is not required in v1; (c) the AI chat and Google Drive routes are decided later: gates G-AI and G-DRIVE stay locked at launch, no AWS account is opened now, and a separate reviewed change picks each route before its switch can open; (d) the owner asked whether the Cloudflare R2 free tier can replace Azure for the off-site backup copy. (3) **R2 facts (verified 2026-10-12):** free tier 10 GB-month, 1 M Class A and 10 M Class B operations, free egress, US$0.015/GB-month beyond (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/pricing/); location hints (wnam, enam, weur, eeur, apac, oc) are best effort, not a guarantee, and jurisdictions are EU, US and FedRAMP only — no Malaysia guarantee (https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/reference/data-location/). So an R2 copy is a cross-border transfer and does not meet spec D19 "backups in Malaysia" without the PDPA process (adviser-named section 129 condition and evidence, transfer notice, record). (4) **Spec:** v1.14 replaces the AWS hosting design with the iCore VPS design (new §8.4, H1–H13) and keeps the AWS design as the documented upgrade path (D24, Appendix C). (5) **Production approval (Q42, owner's words, 2026-10-12):** "I accept the owner-held deploy key. I'm staying on GitHub Free. Keep the production key outside GitHub; I run production deploys only for the exact build with green CI and successful staging checks." So the production deploy key is **not** stored in GitHub (not an environment secret, not a repository secret); only the owner runs production deploys, and only for the exact digest with a green CI run on `main` and passed staging checks; a manual rollback goes only to a digest previously accepted in production (spec v1.15 §8.4 H3). Staging still deploys automatically with its own forced-command key, stored as a GitHub repository secret — acceptable for staging only, a stated residual risk (spec T25). The repository stays on GitHub Free.
- **Still OPEN (not decided by this entry):** the backup cadence and freshness limit (spec Q34), key custody for the offline dump and escrow keys and the sealed second copy (Q35), the off-site copy location (Q36), R2 yes or no after the PDPA adviser (Q37), the iCore vendor gate results (G-VENDOR, Q38), the AI route (Q39) and the Drive route (Q40), and the maintenance window and DB password rotation interval (Q41). **The vendor gate has not been run and has not passed.** No real client data goes on any host until the spec §8.4 H12 gates (G-VENDOR, G-S1, G-DRILL, G-LOAD, G-BACKUP) pass.
- **Why:** The owner found AWS Step 1 (≈ US$245, ≈ RM 1 000/month) too expensive (2026-10-11). B4 keeps primary data, backups, files and logs in Malaysia for about RM 100–140/month with staging and an off-site copy (spec §8.2), and the owner accepts a single server, manual recovery in hours and operating it himself.
- **Alternatives rejected:** AWS `ap-southeast-5` as specified to v1.13, and trimmed AWS A2 (cost; kept as the upgrade path); DO, Render or App Platform in Singapore (every store cross-border); Azure Malaysia West VM (owner chose iCore); an unprotected single VPS with no off-host copy or drill; Cloudflare R2 as the only off-site copy (no Malaysia guarantee).
- **Scope / affects:** spec v1.14: §1, D3, D4, D5 (supervisor wording only), D9, D12, D13, D14, D15, D16, D17, D18, D19, D23, D24, §4, §4.1, §4.2, Flows 5.2, 5.6, 5.7, 5.8 (note), §5.10, §6, T1, T2, T8, T9, T13–T16, T18, T19, T23–T25, §8.1–8.4, §9 (M0, M1, M2, M4, M5, M6, M11, M16, "Blocked until"), §10 (Q34–Q42), Appendices A–C. After approval: `agents/PROJECT.md` reconcile (§1 identity, §3 `infra/`, §7 `make dev` MinIO, §8 pack parameters, §9 residency line and locked switches, §10 SA-key line, §11 production approval), the M1 PR removes the `infra/` CDK skeleton (UNI-05) and rewrites the deploy, rollback and restore runbooks (UNI-16).
- **Versions:** spec 1.13 (`e3141585`) → 1.14 (`03c5d6b5`) → 1.15 (review Round 17 fixes: rule mapping H14, atomic clean-file publish, ordered recovery set, accepted-digest rollback; Q42 recorded) → 1.16 (review Round 18 fixes: owner-signed restore transitions for audit-anchor epochs, consistent DB/files backup cut with soft-delete hold) → 1.17 (review Round 19 fixes: `file_tombstone` tenant-table evidence, bounded backup coordinator in the ops access mapping) → 1.18 (review Round 20 fix: `file_tombstone` trigger permits the FK `document_id` → NULL transition); decision paper v1.6
- **Exception to a rule?** no. The closed role set gains `skiw_backup` (BYPASSRLS, read-only), used only by the `backup` ops service, never by the running app (TEN-02 holds). The production manual approval is the owner-held key outside GitHub (Q42 decided above; `agents/PROJECT.md` §11 wording follows in the reconcile). Design determination: the backup schedule runs in a dedicated ops worker, not the app worker, so the BYPASSRLS credential stays out of every app container (TEN-02); it remains a worker-owned schedule with no HTTP trigger (EVT-06; spec §8.4 H6, H14).
- **Decided by:** owner (Charlie as operator; one web instance; AI/Drive route later; Q42 owner-held production key outside GitHub, GitHub Free); builder drafting (deep tier); review Round 17 findings fixed in spec 1.15

## 2026-10-10 — M4 worker enqueue: runworker on DjangoConnector, polling delay, periodic-parent exception
- **Decision:** (1) **Worker connector (owner, 2026-10-10, option A, then Q33b yes):** `manage.py runworker` is the only worker entrypoint and keeps the `DjangoConnector` (no swap to `PsycopgConnector`), so a sync job handler enqueues in its own `with_agency()` transaction. Procrastinate calls this configuration "not suitable for workers"; the accepted mitigation is the mandatory M4 worker tests and a CI gate on every Procrastinate or asgiref upgrade (UNI-13). (2) **Polling delay (Q33a yes):** up to the default 5 s of extra delay for a healthy idle worker with free capacity; not a start guarantee under backlog or long tasks. (3) **Periodic parents (Q2/Q32 yes):** for the single agency in v1, the unsigned periodic system-parent exception (allow-listed name, exact timestamp args, cron-aligned, inside a replay window) is accepted; signed or provenance-checked parents are required before a second agency is added. (4) Stalled jobs are never re-queued automatically; they are reported, and an operator releases one after confirmed termination (M5).
- **Why:** PR 8 review R2 P2: Procrastinate 3.10.0 `procrastinate worker` swaps the app connector, which breaks same-transaction handler-side enqueue and per-agency fan-out. Spec v1.9 to v1.13 (PR 19, review Rounds 12 to 15).
- **Alternatives rejected:** handler-side `job_outbox` plus relay (second enqueue path, singleton relay, cross-agency reads); automatic stalled-job recovery (heartbeat age is not proof of process death; the library does not fence finishes).
- **Scope / affects:** spec D5, 4.1 (`job_fanout`), 4.2, T1, T17, M4, M5, Q2, Q32, Q33. The M4 implementation PR must also update the plan M4 hand-over list, the 2026-10-09 entry below, `app/jobs/management/commands/runworker.py` and `agents/PROJECT.md` section 9.
- **Versions:** spec 1.13; Procrastinate 3.10.0; asgiref 3.12.1
- **Exception to a rule?** no (UNI-13 gate specified for M4)
- **Decided by:** owner (answers recorded in the review session 2026-10-10); builder (design drafting by the deep tier)

## 2026-10-10 — M0d: GitHub Free plan limits, CI security gates and the image web build
- **Decision:** (1) **Plan (owner, 2026-10-10, option A, "follow bob"):** the repository stays private on GitHub Free; revisit GitHub Pro before production. Checked with the GitHub API on 2026-10-10: branch protection and rulesets return 403 "Upgrade to GitHub Pro"; secret-scanning push protection, CodeQL and dependency-review are not available for a private repo on this plan. **Not relied on:** all four. **Enabled by the builder on the owner's instruction:** Dependabot alerts and Dependabot security updates (both confirmed by the API). This answers ASK-3 and ASK-4: both are unavailable, not open. (2) **Merge discipline replaces branch protection:** GitHub cannot block a merge, so the kit gate is the control: the builder confirms green CI on the exact PR head before `approved N` writes the receipt (builder working rule from this date; the kit's `START.md` does not state this check, owner to decide on a kit change), and the owner merges only after `approved N`. Nothing technical stops a push to `main`; the owner pushes baselines and merges. (3) **Secret scanning:** gitleaks v8.30.1 runs in CI over the full history (binary, SHA-256 verified; the gitleaks-action is not used, it needs a licence for organisations). CI detects only after a push, so a leaked secret must be rotated. The only pre-push control is the local hook `.githooks/pre-push` (`make hooks`): it scans the commits being pushed, fails closed if gitleaks is missing, and has one visible escape (`SKIW_SKIP_SECRET_SCAN=1`, logged; CI still scans). A local hook is bypassable. Config `.gitleaks.toml`: defaults plus one rule, `skiw-secret-key-assignment` (a `PROJECT.md` §7 key name assigned a literal of 16+ characters); there is no path exemption (a first version exempted `docs/specs/**`; review R1 showed that hid value assignments there). Fixtures prove bare names pass and the same value assignment fails in `docs/specs/`, `docs/plans/` and `app/`. (4) **Other gates (UNI-13; PR 9 review R1 corrected the gitleaks exemption and the Trivy policy below):** `pip-audit==2.10.1 --strict`, `pnpm audit --audit-level high`, Semgrep 1.179.0 registry packs `p/python p/django p/typescript p/react` anonymously with `--metrics=off` (works without login), Trivy v0.75.0 via `aquasecurity/trivy-action` v0.36.0 (`ed142fd0…`) failing on any HIGH/CRITICAL finding, fixed or not (the approved gate; `ignore-unfixed` was tried and withdrawn after review). To keep the gate honest the runtime base moved from Debian slim (44 HIGH/CRITICAL findings, none fixable upstream) to `python:3.12.15-alpine@sha256:1b668429…` (0 findings); Dependabot proposes new digests; accepting any finding needs an owner-approved, reviewed policy with per-finding assessment, CDK synth test (empty stack, region `ap-southeast-5` from context, no account literal). No new Python or npm dependency. (5) **Image:** a digest-pinned `node:24.21.0-slim` web stage builds `web/dist` (pnpm 11.6.0 from `packageManager`); `python -m shell.verify_dist` fails the image build if the manifest is missing, invalid JSON, lacks `index.html`, or names a missing file. The runtime image no longer ships `uv`/`uvx` (mounted for `uv sync` only; they carried HIGH GHSA-4w2j-m93h-cj5j in quinn-proto) and the runtime base is now Alpine (see (4); the interim move of the Debian digest to `05cda977…` is superseded). (6) **Cache constant:** `ASSET_CACHE_SECONDS = 31_536_000` in `app/shell/views.py` (closes the PR 2 FE-51 condition).
- **Why:** Plan M0d (`docs/plans/2026-10-05-m0-scaffold.md`), UNI-07, UNI-13, spec T16/T17. T16 (push protection) and T17 (CodeQL, dependency-review) stay open risks; the controls above reduce, not remove, them.
- **Alternatives rejected:** GitHub Pro now (cost; revisit before production); making the repo public (business content); `gitleaks-action` (licence); a GitHub-hosted-only secret check with no local hook.
- **Scope / affects:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.githooks/`, `.gitleaks.toml`, `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `Makefile`, `app/shell/`, `infra/`, `extension/`, `docs/runbooks/`, `agents/PROJECT.md` §7, `pyproject.toml`.
- **Known gaps:** Dependabot has no `docker-compose` entry (the pinned `postgres` digest in `docker-compose.yml` is not auto-updated; a 4-line follow-up). The CI steps (gitleaks download and checksum, trivy-action, infra job) were verified by CI itself, not before the push.
- **Exception to a rule?** no (UNI-13 is partly process-enforced because branch protection is unavailable; recorded here)
- **Decided by:** owner (plan option A); builder (tool choices)

## 2026-10-09 — M0c: Procrastinate queue, queue grants and the migration-linter gate
- **Decision:** (1) **Packages (UNI-06 ladder):** `procrastinate[django]==3.10.0` (adds attrs 26.1.0, croniter 6.2.4, python-dateutil 2.9.0.post0, six 1.17.0; MIT). Needed because spec D5 chooses a Postgres queue with same-transaction enqueue; Django 5.2 ships no task worker, no existing dependency does this, and it avoids Redis/Celery. Dev only: `django-migration-linter==6.0.0` (adds appdirs 1.4.4, toml 0.10.2; Apache-2.0), required by spec D16. Semgrep 1.179.0 runs through `uvx` with `--metrics=off` (UNI-11); it is not a project dependency. Licences and upstream maintenance were not re-checked online in this session. (2) **Enqueue (spec D5):** `jobs.enqueue_in_tx` is the M0c subset: synchronous `.defer()` through `DjangoConnector` on the Django alias inside `atomic()`; ids as canonical strings; refusals (running event loop, not in `atomic()` or connector not `DjangoConnector`, `agency_id`, task not from `jobs.registry`, ids not exactly the declared uuids) happen before any SQL. Probe task only; its stored task name is the explicit `jobs.noop` (`registry.job` takes the name, so it never depends on the module path). The commit, rollback and invisible-before-commit tests pass as `skiw_app`, so the `job_outbox` fallback is not used. (3) **Queue grants:** exactly spec §4.1, by migration `jobs.0001_procrastinate_queue_grants` run as `skiw_migrator`; owner mapping 2026-10-07: the four Procrastinate tables are operational queue state, evidence tables stay append-only (TEN-03). The grant audit and the defer, fetch, finish, retry test run as `skiw_app`. Worker `delete_jobs` is `never` (`PROCRASTINATE_WORKER_DEFAULTS`). The built-in `remove_old_jobs` task cannot act as `skiw_app` (its DELETE raises `InsufficientPrivilege`; tested). (4) **Migration-linter gate (D16, UNI-15):** `lintmigrations --warnings-as-errors --exclude-apps procrastinate --no-cache` runs in `make migrate` (plan §3 now says so; it needs a database, so it cannot run in `make lint`), hence in CI through `make test-live`, using a dev-only settings module `config/settings_migration_lint.py` (the linter must be an installed app; it must not be in runtime settings). Limitation: the linter reads `RunSQL` text for destructive patterns and reversibility; it does not understand GRANT/REVOKE, so `jobs.0001` passes without an ignore and privileges are proven by the grant audit and lifecycle tests. Negative test: a scratch migration with `DROP COLUMN` and one with irreversible `RunSQL` each failed the gate (evidence in the PR body; the scratch files were deleted). (5) **Startup gate (spec D16, PROJECT §9 "startup fails closed on unapplied migrations"; PR 8 review R1 P1):** `jobs.startup.require_migrated()` checks the migration plan as `skiw_app` (read-only) and raises `ImproperlyConfigured` naming unapplied migrations. It runs when `config.asgi` is imported (web) and in the `runworker` command (worker: gate, then `procrastinate worker`; the Compose worker uses it). It runs in a short-lived thread because the ASGI server imports the app inside a running event loop, where Django refuses sync database calls. `jobs.0001` also grants `skiw_app` SELECT on `django_migrations` (spec §4.1 framework-table row). Tests start the real entrypoints as `skiw_app` with a migration row removed and restored through the owner role. (6) **Test harness:** tests use the already-migrated database (no test-database creation, no flush, no `transaction=True`) because `skiw_app` is `NOCREATEDB` and has no TRUNCATE; the probe table is created through the migrator URL with SELECT and INSERT for `skiw_app`; `procrastinate_events` is read as the migrator since `skiw_app` has no SELECT on it by design.
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-05-m0-scaffold.md` M0c; spec v1.8 D5, §4.1, §4.2; reviewer conditions on PR 7.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `job_outbox` relay (the guarantee held). Celery or Redis. Granting EXECUTE on trigger functions (not needed; proven by the lifecycle test).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/jobs/`, `app/config/{settings,settings_migration_lint}.py`, `semgrep/`, `.semgrepignore`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `Makefile`, `docker-compose.yml` (worker service), `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`; rules UNI-04/06/15/16, TEN-01/02/03/10, EVT-01/05/06.
- **Unresolved spec requirement for M4:** `manage.py procrastinate worker` swaps the global app connector to a `PsycopgConnector` for the whole worker run, so enqueueing from inside a task handler (the M4 periodic fan-out) cannot be same-transaction as written. `enqueue_in_tx` currently fails closed there. M4 needs a reviewed spec solution (spec change plus review) before task-handler enqueueing or the periodic fan-out is built; recorded in the plan's M4 hand-over list.
- **Known limits:** the Semgrep ban also flags Django's `QuerySet.defer("field")` once ORM code exists (add a narrow ignore then). After `make test-live`, leftover test jobs on a shared volume make a dev worker log "Task was not found" for the two periodic probe jobs (harmless).
- **Exception to a rule?** no (the TEN-03 mapping is the owner decision of 2026-10-07)
- **Decided by:** builder (packages, harness, linter settings); owner (TEN-03 mapping, earlier)

## 2026-10-08 — Agent kit upgraded 1.7.1 → 1.7.3
- **Decision:** The kit files `agents/README.md`, `agents/START.md` and `agents/roles/reviewer.md` are replaced with kit 1.7.3 (owner, `go` on 2026-10-08). `agents/PROJECT.md` "Kit version used" is now 1.7.3; nothing else in that file changed. 1.7.2 adds Part B defaults (setup applies listed defaults and reports them). 1.7.3 adds the reviewer snapshot fallback for a read-only `.git`: if `git worktree add` fails, the reviewer works from an exact-commit snapshot. Checked by sha256: README `5d4b6f2a…`, START `93ec136e…`, reviewer `29febdaf…` (the hashes the owner gave). The other 17 kit files are byte-identical to the committed 1.7.1 copies (git shows them unmodified); no manifest file was available to compare.

- **Alternatives rejected:** Rebasing PR 4 to carry the upgrade (the PR was already approved; a new head would void its receipt).

- **Exception to a rule?** no

## 2026-10-07 — Brand colours and the reviewer environment
- **Decision:** (1) **Brand colours (owner, 2026-10-07, option A):** navy `#001C30` and gold `#FFB300`, read from https://www.skiwealth.com/ (home page CSS), on the Fernly-style layout. Navy is the primary (sidebar, brand text); gold is the active item and the skip link, always with navy text on it. Gold is never text on white (about 1.8:1) and never a focus ring on white: the focus ring is navy, and gold inside the navy sidebar. The earlier Doc2 lime is dropped. The site font (Poppins) is not adopted; the system font stack stays until the owner decides. Spec D2 wording is in PR 3 (spec v1.8). Until that is fixed the reviewer uses an isolated local clone, and CI (`make test-live`, `make e2e`) is the independent runtime proof.

## 2026-10-06 — M0b web shell: toolchain, serving, UI direction and confirmed M0 answers
- **Decision:** (1) **Owner answers confirmed 2026-10-06:** Node 24 (LTS; `.node-version` and `engines` pin 24.21.0, `engineStrict`, `make check-node`; local switching with `fnm`, CI via `actions/setup-node` reading `.node-version`); UI language English, `<html lang="en-MY">`; ASK-1 option (a) (Procrastinate Django integration, synchronous `.defer()` on the Django connection, spec D5 revised to v1.8 and sent to spec review before M0c merges; M0c tests must prove commit and rollback include the job in the same transaction); ASK-6 option (b) (app and worker run as `skiw_app` from M0; M0c adds the queue grants; no TEN-02 exception). (2) **Web stack pins:** React 19.3.0, Vite 8.3.2, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.1, TypeScript 6.0.3 (not 7.x: typescript-eslint 8.71.0 peers `<6.1`), eslint 10.12.0 + typescript-eslint 8.71.0 (strictTypeChecked) + eslint-plugin-react-hooks 7.1.1, vitest 5.0.3 + jsdom 30.1.2 + @testing-library/react 16.3.3, @playwright/test 1.63.0 + @axe-core/playwright 4.13.0 (axe in the browser test is required by A11Y-06), @types/node 24.19.1 (Playwright config reads `process.env`), pnpm 11.6.0. (3) **Serving:** Django serves the Vite build same-origin (spec D2): `/` renders a template from `web/dist/.vite/manifest.json`; `/assets/<path>` uses Django's `serve` rooted at `web/dist/assets` with `Cache-Control: max-age=31536000, immutable` (hashed names; traversal is rejected with 400/404). No WhiteNoise: it is a new dependency and its middleware must be async-capable (D26); edge caching comes with M1. The production image does not yet contain `web/dist`; M0d adds the web build stage (Compose mounts `web/dist` for now). (4) **UI direction (owner, 2026-10-06):** follow the look and interaction of the Fernly dashboard demo (https://demo.codeandchill.store/fernly/#dashboard): soft grey-green page background, white large-radius cards, left sidebar with a pill-shaped active item, top bar, forest-green primary, calm spacing. That demo is a paid third-party template: it is a visual reference only; no code, CSS, icons or images are copied, and the Fernly name and logo are not used. Tokens are our own (`web/src/styles.css`). The earlier Doc2 visual language (lime brand, Fraunces) and spec D2's "tokens from the SK iWealth design system" are superseded for visuals by this direction; Doc2's information architecture is not yet adopted (M0b shell has one "Today" link). **Open for the owner:** does the SK iWealth brand colour override the Fernly green, and which Doc2 sections are in scope? Spec D2 wording needs a revision once answered.
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-05-m0-scaffold.md` M0b; owner confirmations and the UI direction given on 2026-10-06.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `pnpm` `useNodeVersion`/`executionEnv` (did not switch the runtime in pnpm 11.6.0). Node 26 (Current, not LTS until 2026-10-28). WhiteNoise. Copying the Fernly template (licence).
- **Scope / affects:** `web/`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `package.json`, `.node-version`, `app/shell/`, `app/config/{settings,urls}.py`, `Makefile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (web and end-to-end jobs pulled forward from M0d), `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`; rules FE-01/05/06/07/13/14/32/40/50/51/60/70, A11Y-01/02/06, UNI-06/14.
- **Versions:** as listed; actions `pnpm/action-setup` v6.1.0 (`ea17c68d…`), `actions/setup-node` v7.0.0 (`82076278…`).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (answers, UI direction); builder (pins, serving approach)

## 2026-10-06 — Minimal CI pulled forward into PR 1 (live role checks)
- **Decision:** PR 1 carries a minimal GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): install, lint, typecheck, unit tests, `make test-live` (disposable Postgres via Compose, `make migrate` as `skiw_migrator`, then tests that the real Django app connection reports `current_user = skiw_app`, that `skiw_app` is not superuser/BYPASSRLS and cannot create objects, and that `skiw_migrator` owns the database and schema and can), and a Compose `/healthz` smoke test. Pytest marker `live` is excluded from `make test` by default. M0d extends this workflow instead of replacing it.
- **Why:** PR 1 review Rounds 2–4 stayed Blocked only because the reviewer's sandbox cannot run Docker or Postgres, so TEN-02 (hard) had no independent runtime proof. The owner chose option B (CI at the exact head) on 2026-10-06. Also records the owner decision (option A) to give the reviewer a checkout-capable environment; this CI is the independent evidence either way.
- **Alternatives rejected:** Accepting the builder's local runs as the proof (not independent). Waiting for M0d (blocks M0a).
- **Scope / affects:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `Makefile` (`test-live`), `pyproject.toml` (marker), `app/tests/test_live_db_roles.py`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`; rules UNI-13, UNI-14, TEN-01, TEN-02.
- **Versions:** `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1`), `astral-sh/setup-uv` v10.2.0 (`c18668ad3cf93ea998bef934396af7bb5c839dc7`), uv 0.11.24; GitHub-hosted runner pinned to the explicit label `ubuntu-24.04` (changed from `ubuntu-latest` after PR 1 review Round 5, UNI-14); bump it deliberately in a reviewed change when moving to a newer Ubuntu.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (option B)

## 2026-10-06 — Runtime vs migration database URLs and TLS options (PR 1 review R1)
- **Decision:** `DATABASE_URL` is always the runtime URL (`skiw_app`); migrations use a separate `MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL` (`skiw_migrator`) that `make migrate` injects as `DATABASE_URL` for that one process only. The URL parser keeps the libpq TLS options `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `sslcrl` in Django `OPTIONS`, rejects any other option, repeated options and invalid `sslmode` values, and with `DJANGO_REQUIRE_DB_TLS=1` accepts only `sslmode=verify-full` or `verify-ca`. Non-local deployments must set that flag.
- **Why:** PR 1 review Round 1 findings P1-1 (TEN-01/02: a host-run app must not run as the schema owner) and P1-2 (spec D4: TLS options were silently dropped).
- **Alternatives rejected:** Keeping one `DATABASE_URL` and documenting the role (still lets a host-run app use the migrator). Passing the whole query string to libpq (would allow arbitrary connection options).
- **Scope / affects:** `.env.example`, `Makefile`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `app/config/env.py`, `app/config/settings.py`, `app/tests/test_database_url.py`; rules TEN-01, TEN-02, UNI-03, spec D4. Still open: a staging connection check that proves the live connection is TLS-protected, due before the M1 deploy.
- **Versions:** none
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder, following reviewer findings

Dev tools: mypy 1.19.1 + django-stubs/-ext 5.2.9 (mypy stays below 1.20 because django-stubs 5.2.9 requires it; mypy 2.x not used), ruff 0.16.10, import-linter 2.15, pytest 9.1.1, pytest-django 4.14.0; `aws-cdk-lib` 2.272.0 in the `infra` group (used from M0d). uv 0.11.24.
