## 2026-10-15 — M1e split in two PRs; one role file for local, CI and hosts
- **Decision:** (1) M1e is two PRs: M1e-1 (`bootstrap-db`, the closed role set, local and CI roles) and M1e-2 (per-role `pg_hba.conf`). (2) `infra/host/db/roles.sql` is the one source of roles for hosts, local dev and CI; it holds no password. Passwords come from `/etc/skiw/secrets` (hosts) or `.env` (local). (3) `bootstrap-db` sends SCRAM-SHA-256 verifiers, never plaintext, on stdin, and signs in with a temporary 0600 libpq password file, not `PGPASSWORD` (H4). (4) `roles.sql` refuses, atomically, any membership of the six roles except `skiw_backup` in `pg_read_all_data` (TEN-02); it never revokes on its own.
- **Why:** The full change was 700 added lines. One role file stops local and host roles from drifting apart.
- **Alternatives rejected:** One 700-line PR. Separate role lists for local and hosts.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/`, `docker/postgres/10-roles.sh`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example` (`POSTGRES_USER=postgres`). Local databases need `make clean` once.
- **Versions:** none
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split); builder (role file, verifiers)

## 2026-10-15 — M1e-2: per-role pg_hba.conf rendered from the host's subnets
- **Decision:** `infra/host/db/pg_hba.conf` is a template; `bootstrap-db prepare` fills the database name and the `app` and `ops` subnets from `env/<environment>.env` and writes `/etc/skiw/db/pg_hba.conf` (root, mode 0444, no secret). Compose mounts it read-only through a `configs` entry and starts `db` with `-c hba_file=`. `skiw_app` and `skiw_migrator` connect only from `app`, `skiw_backup` only from `ops`, `skiw_readonly` and `postgres` only over the socket; all else is rejected (`scram-sha-256`).
- **Why:** The role limits (TEN-02, TEN-10) must hold at the database, not only in Compose. The same subnets feed Compose and the file, so they cannot drift apart.
- **Alternatives rejected:** A fixed file with literal subnets (drifts from env files). `trust` for the socket (the review of PR 40 wants real authentication).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/db/`, `bootstrap-db prepare` (now needs `--env-file`), live tests with probe containers on each network. Local and CI dev databases keep the image's default `pg_hba.conf`.
- **Versions:** none
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (within the approved plan)

## 2026-10-15 — M1f-1a: host files installed by `apply`; host values for G7 and G15; the restore sudoers rule
- **Decision:** (1) **M1f is three PRs** (owner, 2026-10-15): M1f-1a host files (this), M1f-1b users, the Docker apt repository and pins, units and reloads, M1f-2 `check-hardening`. (2) **G4 assumed (a), Ubuntu 24.04 LTS** (recommended, not yet answered); a Debian answer changes the apt suite and the ssh unit in M1f-1b. (3) **G7 and G15 are host values, not code:** `SKIW_SSH_PORT`, `SKIW_SSH_POLICY` (`allowlist` or `fail2ban`), `SKIW_SSH_ALLOW` (CIDRs, set exactly when the policy is `allowlist`, so an empty list never means fail2ban silently) and `SKIW_AUTO_REBOOT` are empty in `staging.env` and `production.env`, and `apply` refuses (exit 2, nothing installed) until the owner fills them; the rates (SSH 10/minute, web 30/second per source) and the journal cap (production 4G, staging 2G) are builder values [A]. (4) **`apply`** (stdlib) checks every value, renders the templates, checks the nftables, sshd and sudoers candidates with `nft -c`, `sshd -t` and `visudo -c` in a scratch directory, then installs only the files that differ, root-owned, with named mode constants (0644, sudoers 0440, executables 0755), including `skiw_host`, `db/` and the templates under `/usr/local/lib/skiw`, where `bootstrap-db` finds `db/` (tested). (5) **nftables** replaces only `table inet skiw` (never `flush ruleset`, which would drop Docker's rules); the web limit is in the forward chain too, because Caddy's 80/443 arrive through Docker's DNAT. (6) **sshd drop-in `00-skiw.conf`** (plan: `50-skiw.conf`): sshd keeps the first value it reads, and cloud images ship `50-cloud-init.conf` with `PasswordAuthentication yes`. (7) **Sudoers** `/etc/sudoers.d/skiw-ops`: `skiw-ops ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: /usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-secrets restore --env-file /etc/skiw/host.env`, the full argument list pinned with no wildcard, so the test-only `--root`/`--age`, the owner-only `--allow-new-key-version`, another env file or `init` do not match; all else stays password `sudo` (H2). The runbook preflight greps `sudo -n -l` for that exact line. (8) **Docker pin chosen for M1f-1b:** Engine `5:29.9.1-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble` (owner, over the plan's 29.9.0: newest patch in the repository on 2026-10-15), Compose plugin `5.6.0-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`, repository key fingerprint `9DC8 5822 9FC7 DD38 854A E2D8 8D81 803C 0EBF CD88`. (9) age v1.3.2 is installed by a runbook step for now.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1f; spec §8.4 H1, H2, H4, H7; UNI-07, UNI-14, UNI-16. Values the owner has not given stay empty so a host cannot be built on a guess.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `flush ruleset` in nftables.conf; an empty `SKIW_SSH_ALLOW` alone meaning fail2ban; a wildcard or a bare command in the sudoers rule; global passwordless `sudo`.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/{apply,nftables.conf,sshd,docker,apt,fail2ban,journald,sudoers.d}`, `infra/host/skiw_host/apply.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_apply.py`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/README.md`.
- **Versions:** none installed by this PR (Docker pin above is applied in M1f-1b).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split, `SKIW_SSH_POLICY`, drop-in name, Docker 29.9.1, age by runbook); builder (G4 assumption, [A] values, file layout); G4, G7, G15 left to the owner

## 2026-10-17 — M1f-1b: users, Docker packages at their pins, units and reloads in `apply`; bootstrap-db log line
- **Decision:** (1) **Docker apt key:** `apply` refuses (exit 2, nothing changed) unless `/etc/apt/keyrings/docker.asc` (placed by the operator) is root-owned, not a link, not writable by group or others, and `gpg --show-keys` finds exactly one primary key, fingerprint `9DC8 5822 9FC7 DD38 854A E2D8 8D81 803C 0EBF CD88` (Docker Release (CE deb)); a second key in the file would also be trusted by apt. (2) **Pins** (one source, `infra/host/apt/skiw-docker.pref`, priority 1001; read from download.docker.com 2026-10-15): `docker-ce`, `docker-ce-cli` `5:29.9.1-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`, `docker-compose-plugin` `5.6.0-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`; repository `infra/host/apt/docker.sources` (noble, stable, `Signed-By` the key). Only packages not installed (`dpkg-query` status `ii`) or at another version are installed; `containerd.io` comes as a dependency, unpinned. (3) **Users:** `skiw-deploy` and `skiw-ops` (operator, added to `sudo`); either is removed from `docker` if found (root-equivalent, H2). (4) **Units:** `nftables`, `unattended-upgrades`, `docker`, `ssh.service` (and `fail2ban` under that policy) enabled; `ssh.socket` disabled and stopped so sshd binds the configured `Port` itself (Ubuntu 24.04 socket activation). (5) **Reloads only for changed files:** `nft -f /etc/nftables.conf` (never the unit: its reload and stop flush Docker's rules), restart `ssh.service`, `docker`, `systemd-journald`, `try-restart fail2ban`. They are written to `/var/lib/skiw/apply.pending` (root, 0644) before any file is installed and the file is removed only when all have run, so a run that fails (exit 1) still owes them next time; a pending file that is not root-owned or is a link refuses. (6) **Stale jail:** under `allowlist` an installed `/etc/fail2ban/jail.d/skiw.local` is removed, printed, and fail2ban restarted if it runs; the package and Ubuntu's default sshd jail stay (not removed by `apply`). fail2ban is decided from `SKIW_SSH_POLICY` only. (7) **bootstrap-db** writes one value-free line per `prepare`/`apply` to the journal (`bootstrap-db`) and the deploy log, reusing `bootstrap_secrets.record`/`to_journal` (now with a program name); a log failure after the work exits 3.
- **Deploy log:** `bootstrap_secrets.record` (shared with `bootstrap-db`) writes the deploy log with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and refuses (exit 3, nothing written through) a log directory that is a link, not owned by the running user or writable by group or others, and a log file that is not a regular file of that user or is writable by others. Found while reviewing the `bootstrap-db` log line; same class as the PR 42 findings.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1f; spec §8.4 H1, H2, H7; UNI-14 (pins), UNI-16. M1f-1a left these out by the owner's split.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `systemctl reload nftables`; restarting every service on every run; reloads without a pending record (a failed run would never redo them); keeping `ssh.socket` (it keeps port 22); trusting any key in the keyring file.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw_host/{apply,bootstrap_db,bootstrap_secrets}.py`, `infra/host/apt/`, `infra/host/fail2ban/jail.local` (comment), tests, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`.
- **Versions:** Docker Engine 29.9.1, Compose plugin 5.6.0 (apt strings above).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split, pins); builder (pending file, ssh.socket switch, stale jail removal); G4, G7, G15 still open

## 2026-10-19 — M1f-2a: `check-hardening`, read-only and fail-closed; host checks first
- **Decision:** (1) **M1f-2 is two PRs** (owner, 2026-10-19): M1f-2a the framework and the host checks (this), M1f-2b the secret-store, container-environment, role-set and `pg_hba.conf` checks, each a new entry in the check registry. (2) **`infra/host/check-hardening`** (`skiw_host/hardening.py`, stdlib), installed by `apply` in `/usr/local/sbin`, is read-only and prints one `PASS name` or `FAIL name: what` line per check; exit 0 all passed, 1 a check failed, 2 refused (not root; the env file invalid, a link, not root-owned or writable by others). (3) **Fail closed:** a missing tool, a non-zero exit, undecodable or unparsable output fails that check; unparsable output is reported by exception type only, so no text that might hold a value is echoed. (4) **Reads as root** open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, judge type, owner, group and mode on the open file (`fstat`), refuse a link or a writable-by-others directory on the way (`apply.untrusted`), and fail if the size changed during the read. (5) **Checks:** `sshd` (every `sshd -T` value of the keys in `00-skiw.conf`; a second `port` fails), `nftables` (the live `table inet skiw` against the rendered file loaded into a throwaway network namespace with `unshare --net` and listed by the same nft, compared as JSON without handles, metainfo or dynamic set elements; checked against real nft 1.0.9 on Ubuntu 24.04, whose output is the test fixture), `unattended-upgrades` (`systemctl is-enabled`, `apt-config dump`: periodic runs, only the security origin, the G15 reboot value, `APT::Periodic::Enable` not `0`), `docker-daemon` (`docker info` live-restore and log driver, `daemon.json` no-new-privileges and no TCP host), `listening-sockets` (`ss -H -tlnp`: on non-loopback addresses only 80, 443 and the SSH port), `docker-group`, `stale-files` (reported, never removed: the fail2ban jail under `allowlist`, other `skiw*` sudoers files, an sshd drop-in sorting before `00-skiw.conf` that sets a checked key, an owed `apply.pending`).
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1f; spec §8.4 H2 (host hardening check), H7 (monthly run from the health timer, M1j); UNI-09.
- **Alternatives rejected:** comparing `nft list ruleset` text with the file (nft rewrites it); defaulting a check to PASS when its tool is absent; deleting stale files from the check.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/check-hardening`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{hardening,apply}.py`, `infra/host/tests/{test_check_hardening,test_apply}.py`, `infra/host/tests/fixtures/nft-ci.json`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/README.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split); builder (checks, canonical nft form, output format)

## 2026-10-20 — M1f-2b: secret, container, role-set and pg_hba checks in `check-hardening`
- **Decision:** four entries added to the check registry, on the read-only, fail-closed framework of M1f-2a (unchanged). (1) **`secret-store`:** `/etc/skiw/secrets` is a root:root 0700 directory reached through no link and no group- or other-writable directory, never listed through a link; it holds exactly the inventory names (`skiw_host/secrets.py`) plus the optional `.restored` marker; each file, opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and judged with `fstat`, is regular, 0400, owned by its inventory uid and group root. (2) **`container-env`:** every secret value (a whole line, and the part after `v<n>:` of a key line) of at least 16 characters (owner, 2026-10-19) is searched as a substring in the environment of every running container (`docker ps -q --no-trunc`, `docker inspect`); a shorter credential is compared as a whole value and also fails the check as unsupported, and an empty secret file fails (PR 46 review R1: no credential may pass unchecked). Each id from `docker ps` must appear exactly once in `docker inspect` (matched by `Id`), with a `Config` object, an `Env` that is null or a list of strings, and a string `Name`; anything else fails. Output names the secret file, container and variable (only when they are plain names), never a value (tested with generated values on every run). (3) **`role-set`:** one query through `docker compose exec db` with bootstrap-db's password-file wrapper reads the WHOLE role inventory (no name filter: oid, login, superuser, bypassrls, createdb, createrole, replication, connection limit), every role setting (`pg_db_role_setting`, per database too) and every membership. Allowed: the six roles with exactly the attributes, limits, settings and memberships parsed from `infra/host/db/roles.sql`; `postgres` as the bootstrap superuser (oid 10, the only superuser); PostgreSQL's predefined roles (`pg_*` with an oid below 16384, made by initdb; none may log in, be superuser or bypass RLS) Memberships, with `pg_auth_members`' `admin_option`, `inherit_option` and `set_option`: exactly `roles.sql`'s grants (options from its `WITH` clause, else the PostgreSQL 16+ defaults: no admin, set, inherit as the member role's) plus the PostgreSQL 17.11 initdb baseline, listed explicitly in `BASELINE_MEMBERS` from a native run (`pg_monitor` in `pg_read_all_settings`, `pg_read_all_stats`, `pg_stat_scan_tables`, all admin false, inherit true, set true). There is no blanket exception for grants among predefined roles: any other membership, a missing one, a pair granted twice, or changed options fails, so `skiw_backup` can neither lose its read access (`INHERIT FALSE`, `SET FALSE`) nor gain delegation (`ADMIN OPTION`) unnoticed; the grantor is not compared (PR 46 review R2). Any other role or setting fails (PR 46 review R1). Diagnostics are built only from names in that allowed list and `roles.sql`'s setting names: other roles are counted, malformed rows counted, and no setting value or database row is printed. The fixture is the query's real output on the pinned Postgres 17.11 after `roles.sql`. It needs `--compose-file`, which must be under the root, root-owned and not a link; without it the check fails (path fixed in M1g). (4) **`pg-hba`:** `/etc/skiw/db/pg_hba.conf` (root, 0444) equals `render_hba` of the env text read once at start (`render_hba` takes that text; closes the PR 41 open item).
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1f; spec §8.4 H4 (owners and modes, no secret in container environments), H6 (closed role set, per-role pg_hba); UNI-07, UNI-08, UNI-09; TEN-02.
- **Alternatives rejected:** printing a matched value's position or length; trusting `docker ps` alone (a container may stop before `inspect`); a fixed role table in the code (would drift from `roles.sql`).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw_host/{hardening,bootstrap_db}.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_check_hardening.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/README.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split, 16-character minimum, compose path as a required option until M1g); builder (check rules)

## 2026-10-21 — Vite 8.3.2 to 8.3.4 for GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm
- **Decision:** `web` pins `vite` `8.3.4` (was `8.3.2`, recorded in the 2026-10-06 entry above, which stays as history). The `security` job (`pnpm audit --audit-level high`) began to fail on 2026-10-21 for `vite` `>=8.3.0 <=8.3.2` through `launch-editor` (`fs.existsSync()` check bypass on Windows, severity high; fixed from `vite` 8.3.3). The maintainer advisory is dated 2026-10-06 and GitHub's advisory database records 2026-10-20; the failure was first seen in CI on 2026-10-21. Only `web/package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` change.
- **Why:** UNI-14 (exact pins, patched versions); the CI rule that any high finding fails stays unchanged and was not suppressed. `8.3.4` is the newest release that passes pnpm's release-age gate; the latest, `8.3.5`, would need a `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` entry, which would weaken that gate.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `8.3.5` with a release-age exception; an audit ignore for the advisory; waiting for Dependabot PR 50 (ten packages in one change, and `main` stays red until it merges).
- **Scope / affects:** `web/package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`; CI `security` job; PR 47 and every open branch.
- **Versions:** `vite` 8.3.2 to 8.3.4. Checked: `make audit-web` reports no known vulnerabilities; lint, typecheck, pytest 383, vitest 3 and `pnpm build` pass; all four CI jobs pass.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (open the bump as its own PR); builder (version choice)

## 2026-10-21 — M1g-1: skiw-deploy core (staging), forced command, sudo rule, installed compose path
- **Decision:** (1) **Split (owner):** M1g is two PRs. M1g-1: `infra/host/skiw-deploy-ssh` (forced command; reads only `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND`, accepts exactly `deploy <digest> [<run-id>]` or `rollback <digest>`; the key role is the `--key production|staging` argument of the `authorized_keys` line, never the caller's), `infra/host/skiw-deploy` + `skiw_host/deploy.py` (H3 steps 3–7 and 11 for staging), `infra/host/sudoers.d/skiw-deploy`. (2) **sudo rule:** one command, its arguments pinned by a sudo regular expression (sudo 1.9.10+; Ubuntu 24.04 ships 1.9.15), so `--root` and any other shape do not match; `apply` installs it 0440 after `visudo -c`. (3) **Host config:** `SKIW_ENVIRONMENT` (must equal the key role) and `SKIW_IMAGE_REPOSITORY` (`ghcr.io/charliekhc/skiw-platform`; the image is always `<repository>@<digest>`, so no tag or foreign repository can be named; the GHCR login user is the repository owner). (4) **Paths:** `apply` installs the stack as `/srv/skiw/compose.yml` with `/srv/skiw/caddy/`; `SKIW_IMAGE` lives in `/var/lib/skiw/deploy.env` (root, 0644), written only by `skiw-deploy`; `check-hardening` `role-set` uses both by default (`--compose-file` stays as an override). (5) **Pull:** `docker login --password-stdin` into a temporary 0700 `--config` directory under `/run`, removed after the pull, so the token is on stdin only and never stays on disk; `docker image inspect` must show the requested digest. (6) **Pre-migration dump hook:** when `migrate --plan` is not empty, `docker compose --profile ops run --rm -T backup predeploy` must print exactly `predeploy <time>.dump.age <sha256>`; anything else stops the deploy before `migrate`. The dump image is M1g-2, so until then a deploy with a pending migration stops (fail closed). (7) **Health (step 7):** `/readyz` 200 through Caddy on the host (`curl --resolve <hostname>:443:127.0.0.1`) within `SKIW_DEPLOY_HEALTH_TIMEOUT_S`, then a `procrastinate_workers` row newer than the switch with a heartbeat within 2 × 10 s (procrastinate 3.10's default interval); for a repeat of the running digest Compose keeps the worker, so its fresh heartbeat is the proof instead (PR 47 R1). Every probe is bounded by the time left (subprocess timeout that kills the client, curl `--max-time`, `statement_timeout`/`lock_timeout` in the database) and an answer after the deadline is a failure. (7a) **Recovery boundary (PR 47 R1):** the state replacement, `up -d` (300 s bound) and the health checks are one boundary; any failure there, including `up` exiting 1 after changing services, restores the state file's exact previous content and runs `up -d` with the previous digest (300 s bound); a failed first deploy stops `web` and `worker` and removes the state file, so a failed image never becomes a switch-back target. A `LOG_ALERT` journal line names the failed step and the outcome, with a separate `RECOVERY FAILED` text when the recovery itself fails (delivered off-host by M1j); the exit is 1 and the lock is released; migrations are never reversed. (8) Exit codes 0/1/2/3 as the other host scripts; refusals happen only before the pull; one value-free line per run to the journal and the deploy log.
- **Deferred to M1g-2:** production deploys (refused with exit 2 until the G11 staging check and the accepted-digest record exist), `--rollback` (refused, exit 2), the contract-migration refusal and `migration_labels` management command, the predeploy dump image (`infra/host/backup/predeploy/`), the G12 staging checks and state line, the key fingerprint in the log line (the forced command knows the role only), `restore.md` "Restore the pre-migration dump".
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1g; spec §8.4 H3; T25; UNI-09, UNI-14, UNI-15, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a sudo rule with `*` wildcards (matches any extra argument); the token in `~root/.docker/config.json` (stays on disk); `docker compose up` for web and worker only (the first deploy must also start Caddy); passing `SKIW_IMAGE` only through the state file (the new image must be used by `migrate` before the switch, so the image is given in the environment, which Compose prefers to the env file).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/{skiw-deploy,skiw-deploy-ssh,sudoers.d/skiw-deploy,env/*.env}`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{deploy,apply,hardening}.py`, `infra/host/tests/{test_deploy,test_apply,test_check_hardening}.py`, `docs/runbooks/{deploy,rollback,host-rebuild,README}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split); builder (paths, hook interface, health probe, sudo regex)

## 2026-10-21 — Repo moved to the organization skiw-platform; image path; worker probe in the TEN-10 mapping
- **Decision:** (1) The repo is now `https://github.com/skiw-platform/skiw-platform` (private), an organization on the GitHub free plan; the owner's account `charliekhc` is an organization owner. The 2026-10-05 entry above stays as history. `agents/PROJECT.md` §4 Remote is updated. The free plan does not enforce branch protection or required checks on private repositories, so "never push to `main`" and "the owner merges" remain process rules. (2) The deployed image repository is `ghcr.io/skiw-platform/skiw-platform` (`SKIW_IMAGE_REPOSITORY` in `infra/host/env/*.env`, `SKIW_IMAGE` placeholder in `ci.env`, and the tests that name it). The M1g-1 entry above, written before the move, names the old owner in its examples. (3) The `skiw-deploy` worker-registration probe is added to the TEN-10 ops-access mapping in `agents/PROJECT.md` §9: socket access as `postgres` through `psql_script`, two fixed read-only statements on `procrastinate_workers`, bounded by `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`; no tenant table, no caller SQL. **Why `postgres` and not `skiw_readonly`:** `roles.sql` gives `skiw_readonly` no table grants yet ("its grants arrive with its first use"), so it cannot read `procrastinate_workers`; the probe needs only that one table, but widening a break-glass role for a deploy check would couple two purposes, and `postgres` is already the socket-only identity of `bootstrap-db` inside the `db` container. The probe adds no data write and no new network path. If `skiw_readonly` later gets a narrow grant on `procrastinate_workers`, the probe can move to it.
- **Why:** PR 47 review round 3 deferred two P2s to this PR (image path after the org move; probe not in the closed TEN-10 mapping), to be done before M1h publishes the first image. The old path would make the first push and every `SKIW_IMAGE_REPOSITORY` check point at an account that does not own the repo.
- **Alternatives rejected:** keeping `ghcr.io/charliekhc/...` (the old account does not own the repo, so the first push by `GITHUB_TOKEN` would not match it); rewriting the 2026-10-05 and M1g-1 entries (history is kept, this entry supersedes them).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/env/{ci,production,staging}.env`, `infra/host/tests/{test_check_hardening,test_compose_config,test_deploy}.py`, `agents/PROJECT.md` §4 and §9, plan M1h checklist line 178, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (move to the organization; run this follow-up); builder (wording of the TEN-10 mapping)

## 2026-10-22 — M1g-2a: pre-migration dump image and `migration_labels`
- **Decision:** (1) **Dump image** `infra/host/backup/predeploy/`: the Dockerfile builds FROM the db's `postgres:17.11` digest, copies the Python runtime from `python:3.12.15-slim-trixie@sha256:cb33f7f7…` (the postgres image has none; same Debian release), and fetches age v1.3.2 with `ADD --checksum` against the SHA-256 recorded on 2026-10-14 (M1d-1). `predeploy.py` (stdlib) runs `pg_dump -Fc` as `skiw_backup` without the data of `guard.tenant_mac_key`, `guard.approval_mac_key` and `guard.lookup_decoy_key`; every byte goes to `age -r` for the owner's dump recipients (G5), and the same bytes go to a validator, `pg_restore -f /dev/null`, for as long as it reads them. **What is validated:** the stream starts with the custom-format magic `PGDMP`; pg_dump, age and the validator each exit 0; the validator reads the header, the table of contents and, in archive order, every data block it writes into a script (decompressing each), and the script goes to /dev/null. Nothing is loaded into a database: a dump that passes is readable, not proven restorable (the restore drill is M6). `pg_restore --list` was rejected: it reads only the table of contents and may exit 0 before the end of the stream (PR 54 R1; a corrupted data block passes `--list` and fails `-f /dev/null`). pg_restore may stop reading early on success or failure; its pipe is then dropped and the copy to age continues, and its exit code alone is the verdict. **Bounds:** Python holds one 64 KiB copy chunk and hashes the ciphertext in 1 MiB reads; pg_restore holds one data block; the only disk use is the encrypted output. **Durability:** after age exits, the ciphertext is hashed, made 0400 and fsynced (age closes without a sync); it is hard-linked to its final name, the `.sha256` is written with `O_EXCL` and fsynced, the temporary name is removed and the directory is fsynced; only then is the one line skiw-deploy expects printed. Any failure, a sync included, withholds that line, removes what was written and exits 1. The password only reaches a 0600 libpq file in tmpfs. (2) **`SKIW_BACKUP_IMAGE` rule:** Compose `backup` runs `${SKIW_BACKUP_IMAGE:?}`, the predeploy image **by digest**, published by CI in M1h; Compose `build:` stays forbidden (UNI-14), so a host never builds it. `SKIW_DUMP_RECIPIENTS` (public `age1…` values) reaches `backup` only. `apply` makes `/srv/skiw/backups/predeploy` 0700 for uid 10003 in the `mkdir` call and refuses a link there. (3) **`migration_labels`** (`app/config/management/commands/`; `config` added to `INSTALLED_APPS` so Django finds it): prints JSON head, contract list and every migration from the files only (no database); a contract migration sets `skiw_contract = True`; a test fails for an own migration with `RemoveField`, `DeleteModel`, `RenameField`, `RenameModel` or `AlterField` that lacks the flag. The guard reads the operations that run against the database: top-level ones and, recursively, `SeparateDatabaseAndState.database_operations`; its `state_operations` run no SQL and are not inspected; an operation class Django does not define counts as a contract operation (fail closed). SQL inside `RunSQL`/`RunPython` is not parsed: a drop written that way is caught by review only. The list is empty today.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1g; spec §8.4 H3 step 4, H6; G5; UNI-09, UNI-14, UNI-15, TEN-02.
- **Alternatives rejected:** building the image on the host (unpinned build inputs at deploy time); a Debian `python3` package (an apt version not pinned by digest).
- **Verified natively:** the image built; against a throwaway Postgres 17.11 it wrote a dump whose checksum matched, which age-decrypted to an archive `pg_restore --list` reads, holding the public rows and no data of `guard.tenant_mac_key` (its definition only); a bad recipient and a wrong database left no file. `migration_labels` ran in the built app image without a database. After PR 54 R1, with the pinned images on an internal network: 100,000 rows gave an 8.26 MB archive (beyond any pipe buffer); the image printed the one line, the `.sha256` checked, a throwaway age identity decrypted it, the row count and an MD5 over every row matched the database, no guard key data was in it; the restore runbook's commands (stdin, socket libpq file, `--role=skiw_migrator`, one transaction) restored a damaged database to the same rows, owner and grants. The predeploy.py of the reviewed head failed on the same database (a reader stopped early).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/backup/predeploy/`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, `infra/host/skiw_host/apply.py`, `app/config/{settings.py,management/}`, tests, `docs/runbooks/{deploy,restore,README}.md`.
- **Versions:** `python:3.12.15-slim-trixie` (index `sha256:cb33f7f771ecec2ef1225cf39b159591a040391bd73e4c01f00fdfc4c47611d2`, resolved 2026-10-22), predeploy image only; age v1.3.2 (pin unchanged).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (split, scope); builder (image layout, output line, dump directory)

## 2026-10-22 — M1g-2b: production deploy, staging check (G11 (b)), accepted-digest record, rollback
- **Decision:** (1) **G11 option (b):** production asks staging over SSH with a dedicated key whose `authorized_keys` line on staging is `restrict,command="/usr/local/sbin/skiw-staging-status"`; that program ignores the command, reads `/var/lib/skiw/staging-status` and prints only its last line plus `running=yes|no` (the digest in `/var/lib/skiw/deploy.env`). The call pins staging's ed25519 host key from host config (`SKIW_STAGING_SSH`, `SKIW_STAGING_HOST_KEY`; `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, `BatchMode`, a temporary known_hosts, 30 s bound). Production refuses unless ssh exits 0 and prints exactly one line naming that exact digest, `head=match`, `checks=passed` and `running=yes` (the known_hosts line uses the fixed `HostKeyAlias` `skiw-staging` with `HostKeyAlgorithms=ssh-ed25519`, so any SSH port matches). **New secret name:** `staging_status_key` (owner-supplied, root 0400), held on production only; the inventory now records the environments that hold each secret and `check-hardening` checks per environment (the key is searched as its joined body). (2) **G12:** staging writes its status line only after `/readyz` 200 through Caddy (certificate checked) and the worker check; `checks=passed` only when the image label `skiw.migration-head` equals the head `migration_labels` prints in the image (`head=unlabelled|differs` otherwise). (3) **Accepted-digest record** `/var/lib/skiw/accepted-digests`: created root 0644 (synced with its directory) and made append-only with `chattr +a` before the pull, verified with `lsattr -d`, whose output must be exactly `<flags> <path>` with `a` among the flags (a `chattr` that exits 0 and sets nothing is caught); a host where that fails, or a record with a malformed, cut (no final newline) or non-ASCII line, is refused. After a healthy production deploy one line is appended (digest, commit from `org.opencontainers.image.revision`, run id, staging check time, deploy time, `/readyz` result, migration head, contracts, the owner key's fingerprint from `SKIW_DEPLOY_KEY_FINGERPRINT`). The append checks the open file (regular, one link, root, 0644) and syncs the file and its directory; a failed append or sync leaves the deploy running, alerts and exits 3 with the line in the log (checked values only) for the hand append in deploy.md. (4) **Applied contracts:** the image's contract migrations are added to `/var/lib/skiw/applied-contracts` **before** `migrate`, not after it: `migrate` can apply a contract migration and then fail on a later one, and a list written only on success would then let a rollback cross it (the plan's "whose `migrate` succeeded" wording is replaced by "was started"). A contract listed that was never applied only refuses more rollbacks. (5) **Rollback** (owner key only): digest in the record, the pulled image's `org.opencontainers.image.revision` equal to the record's commit for it, no staging check, no `migrate`, refused when an applied contract migration is not among the target's migrations (refusals after the pull change only the local image cache); then the M1g-1 switch, health and recovery boundary. (6) **Staging status freshness:** before the switch staging removes its old status line, so a failed deploy (also of the running digest) leaves none and production refuses until a later deploy passes; a failed status write is a recovery step (`failed its staging status line`, switched back). (7) **Durability:** every file `skiw-deploy` writes (state file, applied contracts, status line, record) is written with `O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` (`fchmod` 0644 on the open file), fsynced, renamed into place and the directory fsynced before the next step or the success line; a failed sync never reports success. (8) **Bounds:** every tool call goes through the timed runner (`docker pull` 900 s; login, inspect, `migrate --plan`, `migration_labels`, lsattr, chattr 120 s; staging status 30 s; switch and recovery 300 s; health by the time left), except `migrate` itself: killing its `compose run` client leaves the container migrating after the lock is released, so it stays unbounded (stated limit). (9) **Pre-migration dump bound (PR 54 R2 P3):** `pg_dump --lock-wait-timeout=30000` (ms; real PostgreSQL 17.11 probe: with an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock held it exits 1 after 2 s at `--lock-wait-timeout=2000`, writing nothing); skiw-deploy bounds the whole dump run by the new host value `SKIW_DEPLOY_DUMP_TIMEOUT_S` (1800) and runs it as `compose run --name skiw-predeploy`, so on a timeout `docker rm --force skiw-predeploy` stops it (a killed `compose run` client leaves its container running; probed with Compose v5.3.0, which also refuses a second run under the same name). (10) **Contract operations (PR 54 R2 P2):** `AlterModelTable` (a new `db_table` renames the table an older release reads) joins `RemoveField`, `DeleteModel`, `RenameField`, `RenameModel` and `AlterField` in `CONTRACT_OPERATIONS`, direct or inside `SeparateDatabaseAndState.database_operations`. (11) The applied-contract refusal covers every deploy, not only `rollback`: a `deploy` of an older digest that does not ship an applied contract migration is refused before the dump, and the status key's body lines are also searched one by one in container environments. (12) H3 step 11 (deferred from M1g-1): on production the journal and deploy-log line of every `deploy` and `rollback` (refusals after the host config too) carries `fingerprint <SKIW_DEPLOY_KEY_FINGERPRINT>`, the one owner key production's `authorized_keys` admits; on staging the line carries the role only (`key staging`): its one deploy key is the CI secret `STAGING_DEPLOY_KEY` (T25), so the role names it.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1g; spec §8.4 H3 steps 3, 4, 7, 9–11; G6, G11, G12; UNI-07, UNI-09, UNI-15, T25; PR 54 R2 (P2 `AlterModelTable`, P3 lock wait).
- **Alternatives rejected:** G11 (a) an authenticated `/version` endpoint (a new app endpoint and a bearer token on both hosts); taking the head from image labels only (labels come from CI; the image's own files are the truth, the label is compared).
- **Depends on M1h:** the image labels `org.opencontainers.image.revision` and `skiw.migration-head`; until then production deploys are refused and staging never reports `checks=passed`.
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw_host/{deploy,apply,hardening,secrets}.py`, `infra/host/skiw-staging-status`, `infra/host/backup/predeploy/predeploy.py`, `app/config/management/commands/migration_labels.py`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, tests, `docs/runbooks/{deploy,rollback,host-rebuild,README}.md`, plan §3 M1g.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (G11 (b) default, split); builder (status line and record formats, label names)

## 2026-10-23 — M1h: CI publish by digest, the gated staging deploy job and `scripts/deploy-prod`
- **Decision:** (1) **Names:** the app image is `ghcr.io/skiw-platform/skiw-platform:<commit>`; the pre-migration dump image is `ghcr.io/skiw-platform/skiw-predeploy:<commit>` (the name `ci.env` and the tests already use). Hosts take both by digest only. Workflow artifacts `image-digest` and `predeploy-digest`, each one file `digest.txt` holding exactly `sha256:<64 hex>` (no newline), 30 days, `if-no-files-found: error`, no `overwrite`. (2) **`publish`** (push to `main` only, `needs` all four gate jobs, the only job with `packages: write`, login with the job's own `github.token` through `--password-stdin` and a temporary `DOCKER_CONFIG`): builds the app image once, runs `migration_labels` in it with no network, read-only, all capabilities dropped; `skiw_host.labels` turns that answer into `org.opencontainers.image.revision`, `skiw.migration-head` and `skiw.contract-migrations` (comma-joined; empty when none). It checks the answer with the same parser `skiw-deploy` uses: `deploy.parse_migration_labels` is split out of `migration_labels()` so both share one rule. On any doubt it prints nothing and exits 1. The labels are added by a `FROM <built image>` build (the image config changes, the layers stay the same; checked locally), Trivy (v0.75.0, HIGH/CRITICAL) scans that tag, and before the push the step checks that the image IDs equal the scanned IDs. The digest comes from the image's `RepoDigests`: exactly one `sha256:` entry of that repository, or the step fails. (3) **Dump image gate:** the predeploy image is built, Trivy-gated and pushed in the same job only when the repository variable `SKIW_PUBLISH_PREDEPLOY` is `enabled`; it is absent today because the image fails the gate (below). (4) **`deploy-staging`** (`needs: publish`, `contents: read` only) runs only when the repository variable `SKIW_STAGING_DEPLOY` is `enabled`. It writes `STAGING_DEPLOY_KEY` to a 0600 file in a temporary directory and runs `ssh skiw-deploy@<SKIW_STAGING_SSH> deploy <digest> <run id>`. The host key is pinned from `SKIW_STAGING_HOST_KEY` in `staging.env` (`StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, `BatchMode`, a temporary known_hosts, `HostKeyAlias skiw-staging`, ed25519 only, `-F /dev/null`). The command is bounded at 3600 s, in concurrency group `deploy-staging`, never cancelled. It reuses the two keys M1g added for G11, because every env file must have the same key set; staging's own `skiw-deploy` ignores them. A test fails if `staging.env` and `production.env` both set them and they differ. (5) **Workflow concurrency:** a newer push cancels an older pull-request run only; runs on `main` are never cancelled, so a deploy is never cut mid-run. (6) **`scripts/deploy-prod`** (Python 3.12 stdlib, owner's machine, owner's own `gh` login; exit 0 deployed, 2 refused, 1 failed). The production address, host key and key path come from `~/.config/skiw/deploy-prod.env`, never from the repository; it must be the user's own file, not group- or other-writable, and the key file the user's, with no group or other bits. Checks, all before any SSH: `gh run view` exactly `success`/`push`/`main`/`ci` with a 40-hex `headSha`; `gh api …/compare/<headSha>...main` answering `ahead` or `identical` with `behind_by` 0 and `base_commit.sha` equal to the commit. (The build brief said "identical or behind"; that is the wrong way round: for `compare/A...B`, `behind` means A has commits B lacks, so A is not on B; checked against the live API. The plan says only "on `main`".) The run must list exactly one unexpired `image-digest` artifact for that run and commit, and `gh run download` into a fresh temporary directory must give only `digest.txt`, a regular file of at most 128 bytes, exactly equal to the argument. `gh` takes only the first artifact of a name and skips expired ones (checked on gh 2.94), hence the separate list check. Each `gh` call is bounded (60 s, download 120 s); `GH_REPO`/`GH_HOST` from the environment are dropped. Output that `gh` gives is never printed. Then `ssh` with the same pinning as staging (host alias `skiw-production`; no `BatchMode`, so ssh asks for the key's passphrase), bounded at 3600 s. (7) **Gates cover `scripts/`:** ruff (`extend-include`), mypy (`make typecheck`) and Semgrep (`scripts` added to the CI path).
- **Pre-migration dump image fails the Trivy gate** (2026-10-23, Trivy v0.75.0, the M1g-2a Dockerfile built locally): Debian 13.7 base 66 (65 HIGH, 1 CRITICAL, mostly without a fixed version), `gosu` (from the postgres image) 26, `age` 4 (built with an older Go; fixed in Go 1.27.3). A scratch probe outside the repository (`postgres:17.11-alpine` by digest, `gosu` removed, the Python runtime from `python:3.12.15-alpine`, `age` v1.3.2 built from source with `golang:1.27-alpine`) scanned clean, 0 HIGH/CRITICAL. That is a path for the owner, not a change here: it moves the db's pg_dump to another base and builds age from source in place of the checksummed release download.
- **Deviations from plan §3 M1h:** the dump image publish (M1g-2a "published by CI in M1h") is gated by a variable and off; the wrapper takes the production address and keys from a local config file; `test_workflows.py` also checks the staging address/host-key agreement and the parser's own refusals.
- **Still OPEN (owner):** fix the dump image, then set `SKIW_PUBLISH_PREDEPLOY` and `SKIW_BACKUP_IMAGE` (Compose refuses every command while it is empty, so no deploy runs before it); G7 for staging: GitHub-hosted runners have no stable addresses, so staging must run `SKIW_SSH_POLICY=fail2ban` to take CI deploys (deploy.md); G6/T25 the staging key and `STAGING_DEPLOY_KEY`; G10 the hosts' read token must read both packages (a new GHCR package is private and is linked to the repository by its `org.opencontainers.image.source` label); staging's address and host key in `staging.env`; then `SKIW_STAGING_DEPLOY`. **Unproven until a push to `main`:** the `publish` job itself (GHCR package creation with `github.token`, the `RepoDigests` value on the runner's image store, the artifact upload). **Until M1k:** a real staging deploy from CI and a production deploy through the wrapper.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1h, §2 (upload-artifact); spec §8.4 H3 steps 1–3 and 8, T25, Q42; PROJECT §9, §11; UNI-06, UNI-07, UNI-09, UNI-13, UNI-14, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `docker/build-push-action` and `docker/login-action` (two more third-party actions; the CLI does the same); building twice (once to label, once to push: the pushed image would not be the scanned one); taking the digest from `docker push` output (free text); a GitHub environment with protection rules for staging (the variable is enough for one key and costs no plan feature); `ssh-keyscan` or `accept-new` (trust on first use); keeping the dump image in the gate unconditionally (`main` would fail every push until it is fixed).
- **Scope / affects:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/deploy-prod`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{labels,deploy}.py`, `infra/host/env/staging.env` (comment), `infra/host/tests/test_{workflows,deploy_prod,labels}.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `docs/runbooks/{deploy,ci,README}.md`.
- **Versions:** `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.2 at `cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9` (the commit the `v7.0.2` tag points to, checked 2026-10-23; UNI-06: GitHub's own action, the only supported way to hand a file from a run to `gh run download`, no runtime dependency in the app; Dependabot's `github-actions` group updates it). No new Python or npm dependency.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder, within plan M1h (names, variable gates, label format, config file); owner (2026-10-23): one PR at about 1,350 lines with the dump image publish off, and the dump image fix as the next separate PR; owner open items above

## 2026-10-23 — Dump image passes the Trivy gate; deploy log written before stderr
- **Decision:** (1) **Dump image** `infra/host/backup/predeploy/Dockerfile`, three stages, every base by digest and all on Alpine 3.24: the final stage is `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24` (`pg_dump`/`pg_restore` exactly 17.11, the db server's version; `gosu` removed); the Python 3.12.15 interpreter, `libpython` and standard library are copied from `python:3.12.15-alpine3.24` with `pip` and `ensurepip` left out; `age` v1.3.2 is built from source in `golang:1.27.3-alpine3.24`. The build fails unless `python3 -I -c "import hashlib, ssl, subprocess, tempfile"` works and `pg_dump --version`, `pg_restore --version` and `age --version` print exactly `… 17.11` and `v1.3.2`. User 10003, entrypoint and the `predeploy.py` contract are unchanged; its fixed `PATH` loses the Debian-only `/usr/lib/postgresql/17/bin` (the tools are in `/usr/local/bin`). (2) **age from source:** `ADD --checksum=sha256:b07c28c6…` of `https://github.com/FiloSottile/age/archive/refs/tags/v1.3.2.tar.gz`, then `go build -trimpath -ldflags "-X main.Version=v1.3.2" ./cmd/age` with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` and the default `GOPROXY`/`GOSUMDB`: every module is checked against the archive's `go.sum`, and a missing entry fails the build. (3) **CI:** the `image` gate job builds the dump image and runs the same Trivy step as for the app image (v0.75.0, `HIGH,CRITICAL`, exit code 1, no ignore) on every PR and push; `SKIW_PUBLISH_PREDEPLOY` and the `publish` job are unchanged. (4) **PR 57 P2:** `record()` writes the journal and the deploy log first, then the stderr copy (in a `finally`, so the line still shows when the log write fails). A stderr write that fails (`BrokenPipeError`/`OSError`, or a closed stream) is dropped, and `sys.stderr` is replaced by an in-memory stream: otherwise the unwritten bytes stay buffered and the interpreter's exit flush fails again, which turns every exit code into 120 (probed with Python 3.12). **A lost stderr means only that the caller did not see the line:** the journal and the deploy log are the record, and the exit code stays the one the run earned (0 done, 1 failed, 2 refused, 3 done but the log or record failed). Every stderr message of `skiw-deploy`, `bootstrap-secrets` and `bootstrap-db` goes through the same `say()`. (5) **PR 57 P3 correction to the M1h entry (5):** "runs on `main` are never cancelled / a newer push waits" is wrong. GitHub keeps one pending run per concurrency group: a running publish or deploy is never cancelled, but a waiting run is replaced by a newer push, so that commit gets no image; `scripts/deploy-prod` refuses its run, which did not succeed. `ci.yml`, `ci.md` and `deploy.md` now say so. (6) **PR 57 P3:** a test proves `skiw_host.labels` refuses well-formed JSON over 1 MiB and accepts exactly 1 MiB.
- **Why:** the M1h dump image failed the gate (Debian 13.7 base 66 HIGH/CRITICAL, `gosu` 26, `age` 4 from an older Go), so `SKIW_PUBLISH_PREDEPLOY`, `SKIW_BACKUP_IMAGE` and every deploy were blocked; PR 57 R1 (P2 lost log line on a dropped SSH session, deferred to this PR; two P3s); spec §8.4 H3 steps 4 and 11, H6; UNI-09, UNI-13, UNI-14, UNI-16.
- **Base choice, measured 2026-10-23 (arm64 build; Trivy v0.75.0, `HIGH,CRITICAL`):** (a) postgres alpine base + copied Python: 0 findings; 321 MB on disk; all 45 OS packages in the apk database, so Trivy sees every shared library `pg_dump` uses; the copied Python needs only musl, `libssl3` and `libcrypto3`, the same apk versions (3.5.9-r0) in both pinned bases. (b) python alpine base + copied `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`, `libpq` and their libraries: 0 findings; 62 MB; but five copied libraries (`libldap`, `liblber`, `libsasl2`, `liblz4`, `libzstd`) belong to no apk package, so Trivy never scans them, and the copy list must be redone whenever the postgres build links something new. (a) is chosen: a scan that sees every library outweighs 260 MB on disk (pulled once per new digest; the base layers are shared between builds). In both, the PostgreSQL and CPython builds are from source and invisible to Trivy, as in the app image and the M1g-2a image.
- **Supply-chain trade (age):** before, the image held the release binary checked against a SHA-256 recorded on 2026-10-14; that binary was built with an older Go and carries its 4 findings. Now the source archive is checked by SHA-256 and built with the pinned Go, so a Go fix arrives with a Go digest bump, not a new age release. What is given up: the binary is no longer the one its author signed off on; the build also trusts the Go image and the module proxy, bounded by the archive's `go.sum` (and GOSUMDB for anything not in it, which `-mod=readonly` turns into a failure). The binary's build info names its module `(devel)` (no VCS data in an archive), so Trivy checks its dependencies and the Go standard library but cannot match an advisory against age itself; age advisories are followed by its pinned version here. Verified: the archive was downloaded twice (same SHA-256); `git get-tar-commit-id` gives `b74dce4cdbe35b5e5f66c06d9612b72f89028758`, the commit `refs/tags/v1.3.2` names (`git ls-remote`), and its tree equals a clone of that tag file for file; `go version -m` on the built binary: go1.27.3, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath=true`, dependencies at the `go.sum` hashes.
- **Verified natively (2026-10-23):** the image built for linux/arm64 and linux/amd64; Trivy 0 HIGH/CRITICAL on both (OS packages and the age binary). In throwaway containers on an internal Docker network (no host ports), Postgres `postgres:17.11@sha256:d74eeac9…` with 100,000 rows under FORCE RLS and a `guard.tenant_mac_key` row; the image, read-only, all capabilities dropped, uid 10003, printed the one line; the `.sha256` checked (`sha256sum -c` and equal to the printed sum); files 0400; a throwaway age identity decrypted a `PGDMP` archive of 3.1 MB without the guard key data; `pg_restore --single-transaction` into a fresh database gave the same count and MD5 over every row (`100000 06bd03378534f32db04561cf266dc608` both sides) and 0 guard key rows. A pg_dump that writes a non-PGDMP stream, a real archive with damaged data blocks (`pg_restore failed`) and a wrong database each exited 1 and left nothing. Both architectures gave the same results.
- **Alternatives rejected:** Debian slim bases (the findings above, mostly without a fixed version); `apk add python3` or `postgresql17-client` (apk versions are not pinned by digest, and Alpine's client need not be 17.11); keeping the release binary of age (its Go findings stay until upstream rebuilds); an ignore file or `--ignore-unfixed` (needs an owner-approved policy, ci.md); scanning the dump image only in `publish` (a finding would reach `main` before it fails).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/backup/predeploy/{Dockerfile,predeploy.py}`, `infra/host/compose.yml` (comment), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{bootstrap_secrets,bootstrap_db,deploy}.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_{predeploy,deploy,bootstrap_secrets,labels,workflows}.py`, `docs/runbooks/{ci,deploy}.md`. `restore.md` is unchanged: it uses the db container's `pg_restore` and no path inside this image.
- **Versions** (each resolved 2026-10-23 from Docker Hub's tag API and confirmed with `docker buildx imagetools inspect` against the registry; index digests, multi-arch): `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24` `sha256:eee6c6e1b0894fa4fe0075eb054af9fd1cf39a35321c2d4110626cfe506411fb`; `python:3.12.15-alpine3.24` `sha256:b658b05ce9c4eb97ec6fc19424818a1f31993659c2b505a532f470e83807815d`; `golang:1.27.3-alpine3.24` `sha256:b8c9b82affbbb82a020bbcf998dad2ba9516944ac7e883524642a481a9505e9c` (build stage only); age v1.3.2 source archive `sha256:b07c28c6c4bdafa272073a310b75bc22c49da8904585a89c30e5ca4233e63843` (commit `b74dce4c…`). Retired from this image: `python:3.12.15-slim-trixie`, the Debian `postgres:17.11` digest (still the db server's) and the age release-binary checksum (still used for hosts, host-rebuild.md). No new action, Python or npm dependency.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (fix the dump image as its own PR after M1h, with the PR 57 P2 and both P3s); builder (base choice, age build, the lost-stderr rule)

## 2026-10-23 — M1i: file store harness
- **Decision:** (1) **Module:** `app/documents/storage.py` is the files volume (spec §8.4 H5): root `settings.FILES_ROOT` from `SKIW_FILES_ROOT` (empty or relative: every call refuses); layout `tmp/<uuid>.part`, `tmp/<document>.<attempt>.part`, `quarantine/<agency_id>/<document>`, `clean/<agency_id>/<document>`. Every directory component is opened `O_NOFOLLOW`, new files `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`, mode 0600, directories 0700 (a new directory's parent is fsynced). An agency or document id becomes a path component only as `str(uuid.UUID(str(id)))`; anything else (for example `../x`, which mypy would stop but a caller could slip past) is a `StorageError` before any file is touched. Each file is fsynced before its rename and the target directory after it, before success is returned. (2) **No database in storage (TEN-10):** `documents.storage` issues no SQL. Row access is a `FileRows` protocol the caller passes (`lock` = `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, `load`, `status`, `set_status`, `set_clean`), bound to the caller's open transaction: from M4/M16 the scan job's or request's `with_agency()` transaction (H5 already places the publish there), in M1 a test-only probe table (G14). So storage needs no TEN-10 allow-list entry; the advisory-lock order (lock, then re-read) is storage's and is tested. (3) **Upload** `write_quarantine(agency_id, stream, max_bytes)`: FILE-01 magic allow-list on the first bytes at offset 0 — PDF `%PDF-`, PNG `89 50 4E 47 0D 0A 1A 0A`, JPEG `FF D8 FF` (stricter than PDF readers, which accept a header anywhere in the first 1 KiB); FILE-02 limit checked before each chunk is written, so an endless client is cut at `max_bytes` + one chunk; refusals leave nothing on disk; returns id, mime, size, SHA-256. The caller inserts the `pending` row and enqueues the scan in one transaction (EVT-01, M16). (4) **Publish** `publish_clean(rows, document_id, scanner, max_bytes)`, H5 steps 1–6 under the lock: clean-and-verifies → no change; clean-but-not → `integrity_failed`; terminal statuses are returned unchanged; source verified against `source_size`/`source_sha256` (`failed` on mismatch or a missing file); `clamd` INSTREAM (`infected` on FOUND; `failed` on a scan-limit hit, see (11)); the output is copied to `tmp/<document>.<attempt>.part` while the bytes read are hashed again and must still be the verified source, then re-validated (magic, size), fsynced, and `os.replace`d onto `clean/<agency_id>/<document>` (the object uuid is the document id, so a retry atomically replaces what a crashed attempt left; a committed clean artifact is never replaced because step 1 runs first); the row gets `clean_size`, `clean_sha256`, `scanner_db_version`, `object_key`, `clean`. Verdicts are return values, so the caller commits the status it set; a `ScanError` or `StorageError` raises and the row stays as committed (retry with backoff, EVT-05). After the commit the caller calls `discard_quarantine`. (5) **Verified open** `open_verified(rows, row)`: only a `clean` row (any other status is refused unmarked) whose `object_key` is exactly `clean/<its agency>/<its id>` (another agency's or document's key, or a key that is not two canonical uuids, is a failed verification); size and SHA-256 are checked over the whole file before the handle is returned; a mismatch, a malformed key, a missing file or a symlink marks the row `integrity_failed`, logs an alert line with the document id only, and then raises `FileIntegrityError`. The mark is written through the caller's `FileRows` and is only durable if the caller commits it (see "Not built here"). (6) **Sweeper helpers:** `sweep_tmp` removes `tmp/*.part`, `sweep_quarantine(rows, agency_id)` removes one agency's quarantine files whose row is absent or `clean`, both only when older than `FILES_SWEEP_AGE_S`; pending, infected and failed sources stay (their retention is M16). (7) **Failpoints:** five named crash points (`upload_write`, `after_quarantine_rename`, `output_write`, `after_publish_rename`, `before_quarantine_delete`) raise `SimulatedCrash`, a `BaseException`, so no `except Exception` cleanup runs, as in a real crash. They fire only when `settings.FILES_FAILPOINTS is True`; no settings module defines it, no environment variable is read for it, and a test fails if any non-test module other than `storage.py` names it. (8) **clamd client** `app/documents/clamd.py` (stdlib socket): `zINSTREAM\0`, 4-byte network-order lengths, chunks ≤ 64 KiB, zero-length terminator; one deadline bounds connect, send and reply; only `stream: OK` is clean, `stream: Heuristics.Limits.Exceeded.<limit> FOUND` is an incomplete verdict (never clean), any other `stream: <name> FOUND` is infected; a size-limit error, any other reply, a missing NUL, a closed socket or a timeout is a `ScanError`. `VERSION` gives `scanner_db_version`. (9) **Boundary tooling (UNI-04):** import-linter cannot see setting use and cannot name `django.db` (it refuses subpackages of external packages, checked), so: contracts "Only documents may import documents.storage" and "documents.storage and documents.clamd touch no database" (psycopg, procrastinate), `documents` added to the existing app contracts; Semgrep `files-volume-only-storage` rejects `settings.FILES_ROOT`, `getattr(…, "FILES_ROOT")`, `"SKIW_FILES_ROOT"` and `/srv/files` literals outside `storage.py`, `config/settings.py` and the harness tests; `documents-storage-no-db` rejects any `django.db` import in `storage.py`/`clamd.py`. (10) **Where the tests run:** unit tests in `make test`; the probe-table harness (`files_probe`, created over `MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`, `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC`, `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` to `skiw_app` only, dropped at session end; no RLS, like `jobs_probe`) in `make test-live` (CI `checks`); EICAR against a real `clamd` in `make host-test-live` (CI `host`): the clamav image pinned in `infra/host/compose.yml` with that file's capability set, `no-new-privileges`, 1536 MiB, `CLAMAV_NO_FRESHCLAMD=true`, a loopback port, removed afterwards. The workflow is unchanged. (11) **clamd scan limits (review PR 59):** by default clamd stops scanning a file at `MaxFileSize`, `MaxScanSize`, `MaxRecursion` or `MaxFiles` and still answers `stream: OK` (probed on the pinned 1.5.4 image: a 2 MiB stream over `MaxFileSize 1M`, a 25-level nested zip at the default `MaxRecursion`, a zip over `MaxFiles 3`: all `stream: OK`). The `clamav` service sets `CLAMD_CONF_AlertExceedsMax: "yes"`; the image's root entrypoint `/init` writes each `CLAMD_CONF_<Name>` into `/etc/clamav/clamd.conf` (read in the image and checked in a running container), after which the same inputs answer `Heuristics.Limits.Exceeded.MaxFileSize`, `.MaxRecursion`, `.MaxScanSize`, `.MaxFiles`. `documents.clamd` returns such a reply as `Verdict(incomplete=True)` (`clean` is false) and `publish_clean` sets the row `failed`, not `infected`: the file is not known to be malicious, only not fully scanned, and the result is deterministic, so a retry would hit the same limit; `failed` is terminal, never served, and keeps its quarantine source (the sweeper keeps `failed`). The planned mounted `clamd.conf` for non-root clamav must keep `AlertExceedsMax yes` (`test_clamav_reports_a_scan_cut_short_by_a_limit` asserts the Compose value; the live test reads the `CLAMD_CONF_*` values from `compose.yml`, so it runs with the production setting, and lowers only `MaxFileSize` to 1M). A legitimate file that hits a limit is refused as `failed`; the user re-uploads a smaller file or the owner raises the limit.
- **Assumptions [A] resolved:** sweep age 1 h (spec H5 [A]) as `FILES_SWEEP_AGE_S = 3600` in settings; magic list as in (3); the size limit is not a storage constant: the caller passes `upload.max_mb` from the per-agency setting (UNI-02; M16), the harness passes its own; ClamAV in CI: no staging fallback is needed — the pinned image ships its signatures (`main`, `daily`, `bytecode` CVDs, daily 28150) so clamd starts with freshclam off and no network download; clamd answered PING about 11 s after start locally (amd64 under emulation on arm64); CI start time is not yet measured. Probed against that clamd 1.5.4: `stream: OK`, `stream: Eicar-Test-Signature FOUND` (`Eicar-Signature` for the string with a trailing newline), `INSTREAM size limit exceeded. ERROR`, `UNKNOWN COMMAND`, no reply to a cut-short stream; EICAR is found only at offset 0, so an EICAR file never passes the magic check and the publish test places it in quarantine directly. The EICAR string is joined from two halves at test time; no repository file holds it whole.
- **Not built here:** the durable `integrity_failed` mark on download: `open_verified` marks the row through the caller's `FileRows` and then raises, so an M16 endpoint that calls it inside `transaction.atomic()`/`with_agency()` and lets `FileIntegrityError` propagate rolls the mark back (only the log line stays). The M16 caller must catch it and commit the mark before answering (the harness's `download()` does). Image re-encoding and EXIF stripping, decode and page-count checks (`upload.max_pages`) — they need a decoder dependency (UNI-06) and land with M16; the PDF/image output is a verified copy. The real `document` table, RLS, `with_agency()`, the scan job, retention of infected sources, the alert channel (an error log line only) and `SKIW_FILES_ROOT`/clamd address in `compose.yml` (no caller before M16).
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1i and G14; spec v1.18 §8.4 H5, H1, §4.1 `document`, §4.2 TEN-10; FILE-01, FILE-02, FILE-04 (part), EVT-05, UNI-02, UNI-04, UNI-06, UNI-13.
- **Alternatives rejected:** SQL inside `documents.storage` (would need a TEN-10 entry the spec does not give it); a `config/settings_test.py` with failpoints on (the image ships `app/`, so `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` could select it in production); a fresh clean uuid per attempt (crashed attempts would leave unreferenced clean files to sweep); a third-party clamd client (UNI-06; the protocol is a few lines); clamd in the local `docker-compose.yml` under `make test-live` (every local run would pay the image and memory cost); an EICAR fixture file.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/documents/`, `app/config/settings.py`, `semgrep/files-volume-only-storage.{yml,py}`, `pyproject.toml` (import-linter), `Makefile` (`host-test-live` path), `docs/runbooks/{local-dev,ci}.md`, `infra/host/compose.yml` (`CLAMD_CONF_AlertExceedsMax`), `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`.
- **Versions:** no new dependency. `clamav/clamav:1.5.4@sha256:638d9020…` reused from `compose.yml` (amd64 only; the test pulls and runs it with `--platform linux/amd64`). clamd protocol from docs.clamav.net "ClamD protocol" (read 2026-10-23) and the probe above.
- **Still OPEN:** clamav as non-root (M1b): the image has `/init-unprivileged`, but it does not apply `CLAMD_CONF_*` (needed for `ConcurrentDatabaseReload no` and `AlertExceedsMax yes`), so non-root needs a mounted `clamd.conf` that keeps both; not changed here. The backup service (uid 10003, M6) cannot read 0600 files owned by the app uid; M6 decides group or uid.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (2026-10-23: G14 yes, the harness runs on the test-only `files_probe` table; one PR at about 1,490 lines); builder (deep tier), the rest within plan M1i

## 2026-10-23 — M1j: health checks and alert sender
- **Decision:** M1j is split; this is M1j-1 (checks, alert payload, journal, units). (1) **`skiw-health`** (`infra/host/skiw-health`, `skiw_host/health.py`, Python 3.12 stdlib, root) runs eleven checks every 5 minutes: `disk`, `memory`, `swap`, `load`, `containers` (with restarts as events), `readyz`, `tls-certificate`, `dump`, `security-updates`, `reboot`, `deploy-alert`; `--hardening` runs `check-hardening` instead (monthly); `--test` sends one `alert-test` message. Every check reads through an injectable wrapper (tool runner with a 30 s bound per call, clock, `statvfs`, CPU count, the TLS request) and is bad when its input cannot be read (fail closed); a value is built from numbers, service and check names only. The only HTTP request is its own `GET /readyz` to Caddy on 127.0.0.1:443 with SNI and certificate verification for `SKIW_HOSTNAME` (one connection gives the status and the certificate's `notAfter`), the whole request bounded by one 10 s deadline (SIGALRM) on top of the socket timeout. `deploy-alert` counts skiw-deploy's `LOG_ALERT` lines (M1g) through `journalctl --identifier=skiw-deploy --priority=alert --output=json --output-fields=PRIORITY` after a stored cursor (the first run: the last hour) and sends the count, never a message. (2) **Thresholds** (UNI-02, named constants in `health.py`): disk ≥ 80 % of space or inodes (plan); `MemAvailable` < 15 % (the G-LOAD headroom); swap ≥ 50 % used; 15-minute load > 1.0 per CPU; certificate < 14 days left (Caddy renews at 30); a dump temporary file older than 1 h; security updates pending 48 h. The dump age is shown and not judged (`DUMP_AGE_H = None`) until M6 sets the Q34 cadence: predeploy dumps follow migrations only. (3) **Bounded flapping:** per check a grace before the first alert (0 for disk, certificate, dump, reboot, deploy-alert; 240 s = two runs for memory, swap, load, containers, readyz; 48 h for security updates), a reminder every 6 h while bad, `recovered: <value>` after 240 s fine (the monthly check: at once); a check flipping between runs sends nothing new. A check is announced only once its message was delivered, so an undelivered alert is due again next run; events not delivered wait in the state file (at most 20). After the first failed delivery in a run the rest wait for the next run, so one run waits on a failing channel once. (4) **State** `/var/lib/skiw/health.state` and `hardening.state`: the path checked (no link, no group/other-writable directory), then opened with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and the open file checked: regular, root:root, 0644, one link, at most 64 KiB, every field checked; otherwise started fresh and reported as `health-state`. Written by `deploy.write_state` (temporary file `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`, fsync, chown root, rename, directory fsync). No lock: two runs at once can only duplicate a message. (5) **Alert** (`skiw_host/alert.py`): exactly `check`, `host` (`<environment>:<node name>`), `value`; check and host must be plain names; the value has email-shaped and IC-shaped (12 digits, dashes optional) text replaced, control characters removed, 200 characters at most (UNI-09). Every alert goes to the journal first (`LOG_ALERT`, ident `skiw-health`). `deliver` takes a transport (one 30 s deadline per message, a failure named by its exception type only) but **no transport is built here**: `SKIW_ALERT_CHANNEL`, `_SERVER`, `_FROM`, `_TO` are in the host env files, empty, and any value refuses (exit 2); with no channel every due message is undelivered. (6) **Exit codes:** 0 all fine and delivered; 1 a check bad, its messages delivered; 2 refused, nothing checked; 3 a due message undelivered (no channel included, so every host exits 3 while a check is due until the channel exists) or the state not written; 3 wins over 1. (7) **Units** (`infra/host/systemd/`): `skiw-health.service` oneshot, root with `CapabilityBoundingSet=CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` (the 0700 dump directory of uid 10003), `ProtectSystem=strict` with `ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/skiw`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `PrivateTmp`, `PrivateDevices`, kernel/cgroup/clock/hostname protection, `RestrictNamespaces`, `MemoryDenyWriteExecute`, `AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6`, `TimeoutStartSec=280`; `skiw-health.timer` `*:0/5`, `Persistent=true`, `RandomizedDelaySec=30s`. `skiw-hardening.service` oneshot, root with all capabilities (`check-hardening` needs `unshare --net`, `nft`, `sshd -T`, `docker compose exec`), `ProtectSystem=full`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `PrivateTmp`, `TimeoutStartSec=900`; `skiw-hardening.timer` `monthly`, `Persistent=true`, `RandomizedDelaySec=1h`. The plan names only `skiw-hardening.timer`; its service is added because a timer starts the service of its name. (8) **`apply`** installs `skiw-health` (0755) and the four units (0644), runs `systemctl daemon-reload` when a unit file changed (an owed reload like the others) and then `systemctl enable --now` both timers. **`check-hardening`** gains `health-timers` (both timers enabled and active); its other checks are unchanged. (9) **M1j-2, not built here:** the two G8 transports (mail by `smtplib.SMTP_SSL`, implicit TLS on 465; Telegram Bot API `sendMessage` over HTTPS; both verifying the system CAs, no proxy, no redirect), the channel settings that select them, the credential `/etc/skiw/secrets/alert_credential` (root 0400) and its H4 inventory entry, and the runbook steps to turn a channel on.
- **Why:** Plan §3 M1j; spec v1.18 §8.4 H7, H2, D17, D19, Q20, G15; UNI-02, UNI-09, UNI-11, UNI-19. Split by the owner for review size: the checks and their sender-side guarantees stand alone, and nothing can leave the host until G8 anyway.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `curl` for `/readyz` (a second request and a parsed tool output for the certificate); `openssl s_client` for the certificate (free-text output); per-socket timeouts alone (a server sending a byte at a time never trips them); reading `MESSAGE` of skiw-deploy's alert lines (log text off the host, UNI-09); a lock between runs (a stuck run would silence every later one); thresholds in the host env file (one more key per threshold in three files; nothing differs per host today); `alert_credential` in the inventory now (nothing reads it before M1j-2, and check-hardening already fails a host holding a file outside the inventory).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw-health`, `infra/host/skiw_host/{health,alert,apply,hardening}.py`, `infra/host/systemd/skiw-{health,hardening}.{service,timer}`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, `infra/host/tests/{conftest,test_health,test_alert,test_units,test_apply,test_check_hardening,test_compose_config}.py`, `docs/runbooks/{health-alerts,host-rebuild,README}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (stdlib only).
- **Still OPEN (owner):** G8, the Q20 channel (mail via the agency provider, or a Telegram bot), and its UNI-11 record as an external service outside Malaysia (D17, D19); M1j-2 (the transports) follows. The M6 dump-age threshold (Q34). **Unproven until M1k:** on a real host, the unit sandboxing with the real docker CLI, `apt-get` and `journalctl`, the `apt-get --simulate` line format and the journal cursor across rotation; delivery off the host after M1j-2 and G8.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder, within plan M1j (thresholds not set by the spec or plan, flapping rule, the extra service unit); the split by the owner; owner open items above

- **Decision:** The lockfile moves two transitive dev packages to patched versions: `zod` 4.6.5 to 4.6.7 (GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5, fixed from 4.6.6; path `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`) and `undici` 8.11.2 to 8.12.3 (denial of service on excessive content-encodings, fixed from 8.12.2; path `jsdom`). `pnpm update -r zod undici` also moved `@babel/runtime` 7.29.7 to 7.29.10 because `zod`'s dependants share it. Only `pnpm-lock.yaml` changes; no range in `web/package.json` changes. The `security` job on `main` (run 40068411894, merge of PR 60) failed on these two findings; PR 60 changed no web file.
- **Why:** UNI-14 (patched versions); the CI rule that any high finding fails stays unchanged and was not suppressed. Both versions passed pnpm's release-age gate.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `pnpm.overrides` entry (a permanent pin for a one-time bump); an audit ignore; waiting for Dependabot (`main` stays red until it merges).

- **Versions:** `zod` 4.6.7, `undici` 8.12.3, `@babel/runtime` 7.29.10. Checked: `pnpm audit --audit-level high` reports no known vulnerabilities; lint, typecheck, pytest 862, vitest 3 and `pnpm build` pass; Semgrep 0.
- **Exception to a rule?** no

## 2026-10-24 — Q20 answered: Telegram alert channel and UptimeRobot uptime ping (UNI-11, D19 record)
- **Decision:** (1) **Alert channel:** a Telegram bot owned by the owner, sending to the owner's own chat (G8 option b). The host sends one HTTPS `sendMessage` request per alert from `skiw-health`; the text is the existing alert text (check name, host, value) after the UNI-09 scrubber. The bot token is an owner-supplied host secret, `/etc/skiw/secrets/alert_credential` (root, 0400, one per environment, not escrowed). (2) **External uptime ping:** UptimeRobot, free plan, one HTTPS monitor per environment on `https://<SKIW_HOSTNAME>/healthz` (public; the view returns the text `ok` and nothing else). (3) **Weekly heartbeat:** every Monday, first run at or after 00:00 UTC, check `heartbeat`, value `N checks, M bad`. The owner rule: no heartbeat by Monday 10:00 Kuala Lumpur time → check the host. (4) **Spec:** the amendment `docs/specs/2026-10-24-alert-channel-and-uptime-ping.md` (v1.2; review Round 1 Revision required at 1.0 `c39fe297`, Round 2 Approved at 1.1 `ea6ee2fb`) and spec v1.19 (text changes only). (5) **UNI-11 record, services outside Malaysia:** *Telegram* receives the alert text only. What the value can hold is the table "Alert values" in the amendment §7 (mount paths and percentages, service names with a status or restart count, `status <code>`, days to certificate expiry, dump file name and age, counts, check names, step or exception type names). It also learns that the owner's alerts exist and when they are sent, and the owner's chat id. *UptimeRobot* receives the public host name, the answer (up or down) and its timing, and the owner's own contact details. Neither receives a data subject's data, a secret, a log line or tool output. (6) **PDPA (D19):** one rule, the owner's decision of 2026-10-24 (option a): the owner's own confirmation in this entry is enough, and no adviser sign-off is needed before M1j-2 merges, because only the owner's own contact data and system data cross and no data subject's data. This holds only while that stays true; if a data subject's data ever enters an alert or a monitor, D19's full process (adviser-named s129 condition, evidence, notice, record) applies first. **Owner confirmation:** given in the reviewer session on 2026-10-24 and confirmed again by the owner merging this PR. (7) **UptimeRobot plan facts (checked by the reviewer on 2026-10-24):** the terms (uptimerobot.com/terms §3, changelog 26 May 2026) allow any use, including commercial; the help article "Who Should Use UptimeRobot's Free Plan?" (modified 28 Sep 2026) gives 50 monitors and 5-minute checks and names internal tools; the pricing page (modified 10 Aug 2026) still says "hobby and non-profit projects". The pricing page does not show which alert contacts the free plan includes. The owner confirms email or app push works on the free account before M1k. A short "down" during a deploy is expected (Caddy serves a 503 maintenance page).
- **Why:** PR 60 (M1j-1) can only write alerts to the journal until a channel is adopted; UNI-11 and PROJECT §11 require a reviewed spec change and a decisions entry before a new external service. The heartbeat answers the PR 60 review risk that nothing watches `skiw-health` itself.
- **Alternatives rejected:** email through the agency's provider (owner chose Telegram); a dead-man service for the heartbeat (a third UNI-11 service); Telegram only with no uptime ping (leaves a dead host to the H6 off-site check); a daily heartbeat (noise).
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/specs/2026-10-05-skiw-platform-stack-design.md` (v1.19: H4, H7, D17, D19 (5), cost line, UNI-11, Q20), the new amendment spec, this log. No code. M1j-2 (Telegram transport, `alert_credential`, heartbeat, the two PR 60 deferred P2 items, runbook) follows after `review spec` of v1.19 and `setup`. Still open: Telegram Bot API facts (to be checked and cited with a date before M1j-2 is built), the bot and chat (owner creates them on each host, M1k), the UptimeRobot monitors and contacts (owner, M1k).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Telegram, uptime ping yes, UptimeRobot, weekly heartbeat, PDPA rule a); builder (heartbeat day and time, wording)

## 2026-10-25 — M1j-2: Telegram transport and weekly heartbeat
- **Decision:** (1) **Transport** (`skiw_host/alert.py`, stdlib only): one HTTPS `POST` per message to the constant `https://api.telegram.org/bot<token>/sendMessage` with a JSON body of exactly `chat_id`, `text` (the three-field alert text, after the UNI-09 scrubber) and `link_preview_options: {"is_disabled": true}`; no `parse_mode` (plain text). The opener has `ProxyHandler({})` (no environment proxy), a redirect handler that follows nothing (a 3xx is a failure) and an `HTTPSHandler` with the default verifying context. `SEND_S` is 10 s (was 30): the socket timeout and one SIGALRM deadline for the whole message. Delivered only on status 200 with a JSON object whose `ok` is `true`, read up to 64 KiB; anything else, 429 included, is undelivered (exit 3, owed as built). A failure is named by its type, an HTTP status or the type of a connection failure's cause (`HTTPError 401`, `URLError gaierror`, `Undelivered`), never its text: the URL holds the token. (2) **Settings:** `SKIW_ALERT_CHANNEL` is `telegram` or empty; any other value, or an empty channel with `SKIW_ALERT_TO` set, refuses (exit 2). `SKIW_ALERT_TO` is a chat id: an optional `-` and up to 16 digits, no leading zero. `SKIW_ALERT_SERVER` and `SKIW_ALERT_FROM` are removed from the env files. (3) **Token:** `/etc/skiw/secrets/alert_credential`, read when a message is sent: the path checked (no link, no writable directory), opened `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, the open file regular, root:root, 0400, one link, at most 128 bytes, holding one token `[0-9]{5,16}:[A-Za-z0-9_-]{30,64}` (one trailing newline allowed); anything else is `UnsafeCredential` and the token is never put in a URL; a missing file is `FileNotFoundError`. Both are undelivered (exit 3), not refusals, so the checks still run and the journal names the cause. (4) **Inventory and decision 13:** `alert_credential` is an owner-supplied H4 entry (root, not escrowed, both environments); `secrets.held(environment, channel)` includes it if and only if the channel is `telegram`, so `check-hardening` `secret-store` fails on a missing token with the channel on and on a present token with it off, and searches container environments for it when held. (5) **Weekly heartbeat:** check `heartbeat`, value `N checks, M bad`, due on every run whose week (`week()`: ISO week of the time shifted back by `HEARTBEAT_WEEKDAY` = Monday and `HEARTBEAT_HOUR` = 0 UTC) differs from the state's `heartbeat` (the week of the last delivered one). It is not in the owed list: an undelivered one is simply due again, and a new week replaces an old week's, never queued twice. Same sender, scrubber and journal-first rule.
