Due only while a channel is configured (owner decision 2026-10-25): with `SKIW_ALERT_CHANNEL` empty there is no heartbeat, no journal line for it, and a healthy host exits 0 (real alerts with no channel stay journal-only with exit 3); with `telegram` and the token file missing or unsafe the heartbeat stays due and undelivered (exit 3). (6) **PR 60 P2, monthly retry:** `--hardening` only runs `check-hardening` and writes `{bad, value, at}` to `hardening.state`; a new 5-minute check `check-hardening` reads it (grace 0, recovery 240 s), so its alert, reminder, recovery and retry are those of any check, every 5 minutes. A result older than `HARDENING_AGE_S` (33 days: the monthly timer is at most 31 days plus its random hour apart) is bad (the timer stopped); none yet is fine. One writer per state file, so no lost update. (7) **PR 60 P2, stale lists:** `security-updates` is also bad when `/var/lib/apt/periodic/update-success-stamp` is older than `LISTS_S` = 48 h (`apt-daily.timer` runs twice a day with up to 12 h random delay and updates at most once a day, so a working host is at most about 36 h old); a missing stamp is bad (fail closed). With the check's 48 h grace a stale host alerts after about 96 h. (8) **Unit:** `skiw-health.service` unchanged; a test pins that it can read the token and reach the Bot API (root, `ProtectSystem=strict`, nothing hides `/etc/skiw/secrets`, no network restriction, `AF_INET`/`AF_INET6`, no nftables output hook). `skiw-hardening.service` header updated: it no longer sends. (9) **`/healthz`:** a test pins the body `ok` and `Content-Type: text/plain` (UptimeRobot keyword monitor). (10) **Runbook** health-alerts.md: turning the channel on (BotFather, writing the token with shell built-ins only, finding the chat id on the host without the token in a command line, env values, `apply`, `check-hardening`, `skiw-health --test`), the failure names, rotation with BotFather `/token`, the heartbeat owner rule (Monday 10:00 Kuala Lumpur), UptimeRobot setup and the deploy note.
- **Why:** Amendment 2026-10-24 (v1.2) decisions 2-5, 10-13 and §7 tests; PR 60 review R1 P2 items; UNI-02, UNI-03, UNI-07, UNI-08, UNI-09, UNI-11. Telegram facts checked 2026-10-25: `sendMessage` `chat_id` and `text` (1-4096 characters), `link_preview_options` optional (https://core.telegram.org/bots/api#sendmessage); about one message per second per chat, then 429 (https://core.telegram.org/bots/faq) [A]; BotFather `/token` issues a new token and revokes the old (https://core.telegram.org/bots/features#botfather); the token is the bot id, a colon and a secret (https://core.telegram.org/bots/api); the lengths in the token pattern are an assumption [A].
- **Alternatives rejected:** a Telegram library or `requests` (UNI-06; stdlib suffices); a URL template formatted at the call (Semgrep/ruff flag a non-literal URL; the f-string keeps the scheme and host literal); reading the token at start-up and refusing on an unsafe file (exit 2 would skip every check and look like a config error); the heartbeat in the owed list (it would queue a stale week behind the new one); two writers on one state file for the monthly retry (`--hardening` and the 5-minute run could lose each other's update); `Restart=on-failure` on `skiw-hardening.service` (reruns the whole 10-minute check to resend one message); apt's `/var/lib/apt/lists` mtime for staleness (touched by failed updates too); the starting patch's SMTP transport (G8 chose Telegram).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/skiw_host/{alert,health,hardening,secrets}.py`, `infra/host/env/{ci,staging,production}.env`, `infra/host/systemd/skiw-{health,hardening}.service` (comments only), `infra/host/tests/{conftest,test_alert,test_health,test_check_hardening,test_units}.py`, `app/tests/test_healthz.py`, `docs/runbooks/{health-alerts,host-rebuild,README}.md`, this log. New alert value shapes (amendment §7 table): `security-updates` `package lists not updated for N h`; `check-hardening` `the last monthly run is N days old`; numbers only.
- **Versions:** none new (stdlib only).
- **Still OPEN:** M1k real-host proof (`skiw-health --test` to the owner's chat from both hosts; the UptimeRobot monitors up and down; the apt stamp present on Ubuntu 24.04 with `update-notifier-common` [A]; the sandbox with the real tools). The owner confirms email or app push on the UptimeRobot free account before M1k.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder, within amendment 2026-10-24 (token pattern, `HARDENING_AGE_S`, `LISTS_S`, the single-writer monthly design, the heartbeat kept out of the owed list); owner 2026-10-25 (no heartbeat without a channel; one PR); earlier owner decisions are in the 2026-10-24 entry

## 2026-10-25 — Hostinger replaces iCore as the VPS vendor (UNI-11, D19 record)
- **Decision:** (1) **Vendor (owner's words, 2026-10-25):** "hostinger VPS"; then "1" to the question whether Hostinger replaces iCore; then "a, either kvm1 or 2, yes": Hostinger is the vendor for **both** hosts (production and staging), each on a KVM 1 or KVM 2 plan, on Ubuntu 24.04 LTS (plan G4 = (a); Debian 12 dropped). iCore Technology Sdn. Bhd. (the 2026-10-12 choice, entry of that date) is withdrawn. (2) **Spec:** the amendment `docs/specs/2026-10-25-hostinger-vps-vendor.md` v1.2 (hash `2ef0bb3aaf6509025c324058f4a3b0dee8e950d6`; review Round 1 of 1.0 `2c1e7134` and Round 2 of 1.1 `b6c1d426` fixed; Round 3 Approved) and the main spec v1.19 → **v1.20** (text changes only, amendment §3 decision 10: D3, D19, D24, T24, §8.2, §8.3, §8.4 H1, H2, H4 LUKS line, H6, H8, H12 G-VENDOR (still eleven items), UNI-11, the control table, Q1, Q19, Q36, Q38, new Q43 and Q44). No design change. (3) **Data centre:** a Malaysian location, picked by the owner at purchase. Hostinger lists "Asia: India, Indonesia, and Malaysia" under VPS (KVM); the page names the country only [V: https://www.hostinger.com/support/1583267-where-are-your-servers-located, page modified 2026-09-15, read by the spec reviewer 2026-10-25]. Kuala Lumpur as the city comes from a Hostinger launch post and is not verified [A: https://www.hostinger.com/blog/?p=8035; its date was not read]. The country, city and site of each VPS and of any backup or snapshot copy are confirmed in writing under G-VENDOR item 1 before any real data. (4) **Plans [V: https://www.hostinger.com/vps-hosting and https://www.hostinger.com/vps/servers/malaysia, read by the spec reviewer 2026-10-25; list prices may change]:** KVM 1 = 1 vCPU, 4 GB RAM, 50 GB NVMe, US$6.49 a month promo, renews at US$11.99; KVM 2 = 2 vCPU, 8 GB RAM, 100 GB NVMe, US$8.79 a month promo, renews at US$14.99; both on a 24-month term, "all plans are paid upfront" (about US$211 for KVM 2 and US$156 for KVM 1 before tax). The owner's 2026-10-13 record (M1a entry) said US$8.99 for 8 GB; the page now shows US$8.79. Whether the CPU cores are dedicated is not stated [A, G-VENDOR item 8]. CPU is half the iCore sizing (production 2 vCPU against 4 cores; staging 1 vCPU against 2). RAM rule (spec H1): the `SKIW_MEM_*` caps without `migrate` must be at most the plan's RAM minus 1 GiB; production 6656 MiB against 7168 MiB on KVM 2 passes; staging 3328 MiB against 3072 MiB on KVM 1 fails by 256 MiB, so KVM 1 for staging needs the staging caps lowered by at least 256 MiB first (a PR of its own), or staging takes KVM 2. The CPU test is G-LOAD on the production plan; the fallback is a larger Hostinger plan (KVM 4; size and price not recorded). (5) **Provider backups:** Hostinger sells "free weekly backups" on every plan; the pages do not say whether they are on by default, where they sit or how long they are kept [V: same pages, 2026-10-25]. At purchase the owner turns automatic backups and snapshots off for each VPS if Hostinger allows it and records the setting in the G-VENDOR entry; if they cannot be turned off and Hostinger does not confirm in writing a Malaysian location, a retention and deletion on termination (item 3), the answer is blocking. (6) **G-VENDOR** keeps eleven items, reworded for Hostinger (spec v1.20 H12): item 1 asks for the country, city and site; item 3 whether automatic backups are on by default and can be turned off; item 6 the contracting entity, its governing law and its processor terms; item 7 whether staff or systems outside Malaysia can reach guest disks, the console or the control panel ("yes" is blocking until the owner records a D19 basis); item 10 whether Ubuntu 24.04 LTS is offered as a clean image. Both hosts in one Hostinger account only if item 11 accepts it. (7) **Off-site copy (Q36):** option (a) is now a second small Hostinger VPS (KVM 1; price open); it shares the vendor, and possibly the account, of the host (T24). (8) **UNI-11 record, Hostinger holds two kinds of data:** (a) **all project data on the VPS disks** (database, files, backups on the host, logs and the secret store), in Malaysia, gated by G-VENDOR (no real data until all five H12 gates pass); (b) **the owner's own account data at Hostinger**: name, contact and payment details; the control panel is run by a foreign group [A: entity not verified, G-VENDOR item 6]. (9) **PDPA (D19):** the owner's PDPA rule (a) of 2026-10-24 (entry of that date) applies to (b): the owner's own confirmation in this entry is enough, because only the owner's own contact and payment data are involved and no data subject's data. (a) stays in Malaysia under D19; any access from outside Malaysia found under item 7 is blocking until a D19 basis is recorded. **Owner confirmation:** the owner confirms, by merging this PR, that (b) holds only the owner's own data (rule (a) of 2026-10-24).
- **Still OPEN:** Q43 plan size (proposal: KVM 2 for production, KVM 1 for staging with lower caps); Q44 contract term; G-VENDOR has **not been run and has not passed**; no VPS is bought; no real data is on any host; Q34 to Q37 and Q41 stay open (Q38 now means the Hostinger gate). `agents/PROJECT.md` is reconciled by `setup` after `review spec` of v1.20.
- **Why:** Owner decision. Hostinger is a new service that holds all data, so UNI-11 and D19 need a reviewed spec change and this entry before any host is bought. The host design (Compose, Caddy, nftables, SSH rules, secrets, backups, health checks) does not depend on the vendor.
- **Alternatives rejected:** iCore Technology Sdn. Bhd. (the 2026-10-12 choice; withdrawn by the owner); Hostinger for production only, with staging on another vendor (the owner chose both hosts).
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/specs/2026-10-05-skiw-platform-stack-design.md` (v1.20), `docs/specs/2026-10-25-hostinger-vps-vendor.md` (new), `docs/specs/2026-10-11-hosting-options.md` (one-line note at the top), `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` (G2, G3, G4, G8 note, UNI-11 row, vendor lines), `docs/runbooks/{deploy,rollback,restore,incident-pdpa-breach,local-dev}.md` (vendor name), this log. No code. Later: `agents/PROJECT.md` (setup); `infra/host/env/staging.env` caps only if the owner picks KVM 1 for staging (own PR with the Compose config tests).
- **Versions:** spec 1.19 (`75ac19f6`) → 1.20; amendment 1.2 (`2ef0bb3a`).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Hostinger for both hosts; KVM 1 or KVM 2; Ubuntu 24.04); builder (wording, within the approved amendment)

## 2026-10-26 — Q43 answered: production KVM 2, staging KVM 1 (monthly); staging caps lowered
- **Decision:** (1) **Plans (owner's words, 2026-10-26):** "just stick with kvm1 monthly for staging and kvm2 for prod": production on Hostinger **KVM 2** (2 vCPU, 8 GB), staging on Hostinger **KVM 1** (1 vCPU, 4 GB) on a **monthly** term. The 1-month price was not read [A: unverified; the amendment's prices are for 24 months upfront]. **Q43 is answered.** **Q44** (term) is answered for staging (monthly) and **stays open for production**. (2) **Staging caps** (amendment 2026-10-25 §2 conditional exception, decision 3), `infra/host/env/staging.env`: `SKIW_MEM_WEB` 640m → **512m**, `SKIW_MEM_DB` 768m → **640m**; the sum without `migrate` goes from 3328 MiB to **3072 MiB** (3712 with `migrate`), exactly the KVM 1 bound of 4096 MiB minus 1 GiB for the host. `SKIW_MEM_CLAMAV` stays **1280m**; `SKIW_MEM_MIGRATE` stays 640m. Proven only by a load check [A] (M1k). A new test (`test_compose_config.py`) parses the `SKIW_MEM_*` values strictly (`m` or `g` only) and asserts the RAM rule for production (8192 MiB plan) and staging (4096 MiB plan), and ClamAV at least 1280 MiB on staging; it fails on the old staging values. Production stays at 6656 MiB against 7168 MiB. (3) **A monthly staging may lapse.** While it is lapsed the owner turns the repository variable `SKIW_STAGING_DEPLOY` off; staging must exist and pass before each production deploy (D15), so production does not deploy while staging is lapsed. The rebuild checklist is in `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` ("Staging on a monthly term"). (4) **Spec wording:** the main spec v1.20 still says Q43 and Q44 are open; that text is carried to the next spec change. This entry is not a spec change.
- **Why:** Owner decision. The amendment's RAM rule failed for staging on KVM 1 by 256 MiB, so the caps drop first, in this PR with the Compose config tests (amendment §2). KVM 1 keeps staging the cheapest separate host (H8).
- **Alternatives rejected (owner):** staging on a local Mac VM with an open SSH port (H8 says a separate VPS; the arm64/amd64 image digest risk; it exposes the owner's network); BitVPS (no Malaysian location, a thin company, per-day billing); free tiers (RAM, ARM, a new vendor); one shared server for both (H8; 10 GB of caps do not fit KVM 2; the staging key would reach production).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/env/staging.env` (two caps, two comments), `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `docs/plans/2026-10-12-m1-vps-infrastructure.md` (G3 row), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` (new section), this log.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Still OPEN:** Q44 for production (the term); G-VENDOR has **not been run**; no VPS is bought; no real data is on any host; the real memory peak is measured in M1k.
- **Exception to a rule?** no (the amendment §2 conditional exception, not an exception to a rule)
- **Decided by:** owner (plans, staging term, rejected options); builder (which caps to lower, within amendment decision 3)

## 2026-10-27 — M2 plan: owner answers Q1–Q4 and three plan additions
- **Decision:** (1) **Owner's words, 2026-10-27:** "1a 2a 3a 4a". Q1 (a): HSTS `max-age=31536000` only, equal on Caddy and Django; T6's "+ preload" is fixed at the next spec change. Q2 (a): interim session lifetime from host config (`SKIW_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_H=12`, `SKIW_SESSION_IDLE_MIN=30`), moved into `setting` in M5. Q3 (a): the CI Compose proof counts for "session survives a redeploy and a restart"; a staging repeat goes on the M1k evidence list. Q4 (a): HttpOnly `__Host-csrftoken` cookie, token in the `no-store` shell. (2) **Owner additions:** the CSRF gate is a behaviour test on cookie-auth mutating routes, with a reviewed list for token-auth routes, and django-ninja's CSRF handling is read before M2d; M3 returns the new CSRF token in the login response because login rotates it; `expire_date` is bound into the M2c HMAC (plan Risk 7).
- **Why:** Owner decision. A scan for `csrf_exempt` misses a route that skips CSRF another way; a behaviour test does not. Binding `expire_date` closes a row edit that extends a session.
- **Alternatives rejected (owner):** HSTS with preload (hard to undo, hostnames not fixed); no lifetime policy in M2; waiting for staging before M2 is done; `CSRF_USE_SESSIONS` (writes a row for every anonymous page load).
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/plans/2026-10-27-m2-headers-sessions-csrf.md`, this log. No code.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Still OPEN:** the spec text for the MAC input and for HSTS preload (next spec change); django-ninja not yet read.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q1–Q4, additions); builder (wording, MAC layout)

## 2026-10-30 — M2a: security headers in Django; where each header is applied (UNI-17 update)
- **Decision:** (1) **CSP:** own middleware `app/shell/csp.py` (`ContentSecurityPolicyMiddleware`, a `MiddlewareMixin`, so sync and async capable like Django's own). Every request gets a fresh nonce (`secrets.token_urlsafe(16)`, 128 bits, kept in `request.META` under a private key; `shell.csp.nonce(request)` reads it), and every Django response gets exactly one `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'nonce-<n>' 'strict-dynamic'; object-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (spec T6; assigned, so a view cannot add a second one). (2) **`MIDDLEWARE`** (`app/config/settings.py`): `SecurityMiddleware`, `XFrameOptionsMiddleware`, `shell.csp.ContentSecurityPolicyMiddleware`; CSP is innermost, so 404 and 405 responses made inside the stack carry it too. `SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS = 31_536_000`, no includeSubDomains or preload (owner Q1 (a), 2026-10-27); `SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT = False` (Caddy redirects; `/readyz` probes use plain HTTP); `X_FRAME_OPTIONS = "DENY"` (the Django default, set explicitly). `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` are Django 5.2.17 defaults [V `conf/global_settings.py:662-668`], applied by `SecurityMiddleware`. HSTS is set by Django only on a secure request [V `middleware/security.py`]. (3) **Shell:** `nonce="{{ nonce }}"` on the one module `<script>`; the shell response is `Cache-Control: no-store` so a cached page never reuses a nonce. The `data:,` icon (blocked by `default-src 'self'`) is replaced by a same-origin `web/src/icon.svg` (navy square, gold bar), which Vite bundles with a content hash (`assets/icon-<hash>.svg`, listed under `assets` of the `index.html` manifest entry). The shell view reads that URL from the manifest and answers 503 if the build has no hashed icon, so every `/assets/` file stays hashed (spec D2, FE-34; PR 71 review R1). `web/src/icon-hash.test.tsx` builds twice with different icon content and requires different hashed URLs. `web/index.html` (Vite dev) links the same file. The `#root` markup is unchanged, so hydration stays byte-equal. (4) **Where each header is applied (updates the M1c record, UNI-17):** CSP with `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, `Cache-Control: no-store` on the shell — Django, on responses from `web`. HSTS `max-age=31536000` — Caddy and Django, the same value; Caddy sets it deferred, so a proxied response carries one HSTS header. HTTP→HTTPS redirect and TLS — Caddy. Responses Caddy answers itself (the 308 redirect and the maintenance page) carry HSTS only and no CSP, `X-Frame-Options` or `nosniff`; accepted because the redirect has no body and the maintenance page is static with no script and no external resource (M1c). (5) **`check --deploy`** (`app/tests/test_deploy_checks.py`; the `compose.yml` `x-app-env` variables, but `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY` as a 67-character env value instead of the host's `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY_FILE`): with Django 5.2.17 exactly `security.W003` (no CSRF middleware until M2d, which removes it), `security.W005` and `security.W021` (Q1 (a)) and `security.W008` (Caddy redirects) fire, each with its reason in the test; set equality, so a new or a fixed ID fails the test. With the host file form, `security.W009` fires as well (see Still OPEN). A further case shows `DJANGO_DEBUG=1` is caught (`security.W018`). (6) **Async:** `app/tests/test_middleware_async.py` checks `async_capable is True` for every `MIDDLEWARE` entry (spec D26 (1)); a header test also runs through `AsyncClient`. (7) **Evidence (2026-10-30, macOS, no Docker daemon):** `make lint typecheck test` green (pytest 980 passed, 1 xfailed; vitest 3 passed) and `make host-test` green (814 passed). Browser check (an approximation of `make e2e`, which needs Docker): `make web-build`, the app under uvicorn with `django.core.asgi.get_asgi_application()` (no database, so not `config.asgi`), `playwright test` 36 passed, desktop and mobile. Each check was shown to fail in a scratch copy: nonce removed from the template (the CSP e2e test and the console test fail: script blocked), the `data:,` icon put back (both e2e tests fail: image blocked), the CSP header value replaced by a wrong one (8 unit tests fail), `SECURE_HSTS_PRELOAD = True` (the deploy-check and HSTS tests fail). Removing the icon link entirely left the e2e console clean in headless Chromium; the same-origin icon was kept so the result does not depend on how a browser reports a `/favicon.ico` 404.
- **Deviations from plan M2a:** `web/index.html` also gets the icon change (dev server only); the `dist` test fixture moved to `app/shell/tests/conftest.py` for reuse. Not run here: `make e2e`, `make host-test-live` (the edge test now also asserts one CSP header through Caddy) and `make test-live` (no Docker daemon); CI runs them.
- **security.W009 (owner chose (c), 2026-10-30):** a host `django_secret_key` version is 32 random bytes, 44 base64 characters (M1d-1); Django's W009 counts characters (50), so `check --deploy` with the host file form fires W009. Accepted with this reason: strength is measured in bytes by `config.env` (`MIN_KEY_BYTES = 32`), which refuses a shorter key at start-up. `test_host_key_file_fires_w009_only_as_a_character_count` pins the accepted warning and `test_host_key_file_below_32_bytes_is_refused` pins the byte check. T6's "+ preload" wording is fixed at the next spec change (Q1).
- **Why:** Spec T6, §9 M2 ("Header assertion tests (UNI-17/18) pass"), D26 (1); plan `docs/plans/2026-10-27-m2-headers-sessions-csrf.md` M2a; UNI-06, UNI-17.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `django-csp` (a new dependency for about 30 lines; UNI-06 ladder: Django 5.2 has no CSP middleware [V: `django/middleware/` holds `cache, clickjacking, common, csrf, gzip, http, locale, security` only], and an own middleware is enough); `img-src data:` (changes the T6 policy); `never_cache` on the shell (adds `max-age=0, no-cache, must-revalidate, private` and `Expires`; `no-store` alone is what the plan names); `SECURE_SSL_REDIRECT = True` (Caddy owns the redirect; it would break the plain-HTTP `/readyz` probes).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/shell/csp.py` (new), `app/config/settings.py`, `app/shell/views.py`, `app/shell/templates/shell/shell.html`, `app/shell/verify_dist.py`, `web/src/icon.svg` (new), `web/src/icon-hash.test.tsx` (new), `web/index.html`, `app/shell/tests/{conftest,test_shell,test_security_headers,test_verify_dist}.py`, `app/tests/{test_middleware_async,test_deploy_checks}.py`, `web/e2e/shell.spec.ts`, `infra/host/tests/test_edge_live.py`; UNI-06, UNI-17, FE-61, FE-70.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17).
- **Exception to a rule?** no (W003, W005, W008, W021 are reasoned deploy-check allowances, not rule exceptions)
- **Decided by:** owner (Q1 (a)); builder (deep tier), within plan M2a; W009 left to the owner

## 2026-10-30 — M2b: hashed database session store with a fixed absolute deadline
- **Decision:** (1) **Own app and model:** `accounts` (in `INSTALLED_APPS`; `django.contrib.sessions` is not installed) holds `accounts.session_model.Session(AbstractBaseSession)` on table `django_session` with a 64-character `session_key` primary key; Django's own model allows 40, too short for a SHA-256 hex digest. `accounts.models` re-exports it. Migration `accounts.0001_session` creates the table and then grants `skiw_app` `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on it (DELETE because D6 revocation is a row delete; no TRUNCATE, no column grants; the reverse revokes them). `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py` lists `django_session` as the one DELETE grant outside the queue. (2) **Stored key:** the row key is `sha256(cookie).hexdigest()` of the 32-character cookie value (`accounts.session.stored_key`); the raw value never reaches SQL. `auth_stepup.session_key_hash` and `outbox_approval.session_key_hash` reuse this exact format later. A cookie that is not exactly 32 characters from Django's `VALID_KEY_CHARS` is refused before any query. (3) **Lifetime:** `SessionStore` keeps one absolute deadline per session, set when the first row is written (now + `SKIW_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_H` hours) and read back from the row on load. `save()` and `cycle_key()` keep it; `start_new_lifetime()` (to be called just before `login()` in M3) makes the next write take a fresh one; `flush()` drops it. `set_expiry`/`aset_expiry` raise `NotImplementedError` and `get_expiry_*` take no arguments, so nothing can slide or move it. A row past its deadline loads as absent and the middleware deletes the cookie. `clear()` on a session not yet read raises (Django's `clear()` skips the load, and the next save would give the old row a fresh deadline); `flush()` bypasses that guard. (4) **Cookie:** `__Host-skiw_session`, `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, `SESSION_SAVE_EVERY_REQUEST = False`; `MIDDLEWARE` gains `SessionMiddleware` after `SecurityMiddleware`. (5) **Settings:** `SKIW_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_H` (1 to 72) and `SKIW_SESSION_IDLE_MIN` (1 to 1440) via `config.env.bounded_int`: ASCII digits only, a bad or missing value raises `ImproperlyConfigured` naming the variable, never the value. Values 12 and 30 (§8.1) in `.env.example`, `TOOL_ENV`, local Compose `app`/`worker`, the CI label step, and `infra/host/env/{ci,staging,production}.env`; host Compose passes them to `web`, `worker` (via `x-app-env`) and `migrate` (listed separately, so `migrate` keeps its `skiw_migrator` URL and password file). The idle value is validated now; M3 enforces it, and M5 moves both values into `setting`. (6) **Boundaries:** import-linter contract "Only accounts may import the session store and its model (TEN-10)" (sources `config, health, shell, jobs, documents, skiw_host`; `accounts.models` stays importable), `accounts` added to the existing contracts; Semgrep rule `sessions-store-only` refuses `Session.objects` and SQL naming `django_session` outside the store, its model, its migration and its tests.
- **A1 (Django 5.2.17 source read, not memory):** `contrib/sessions/backends/db.py` and `backends/base.py` (every method; pinned by `app/accounts/tests/test_session_store_surface.py`, which fails when Django adds or removes one), `contrib/sessions/base_session.py`, `models.py`, `migrations/0001_initial.py`, `middleware.py` (`get_expiry_age()` before `save()`; cookie delete on an empty session), `contrib/auth/__init__.py` (`login`, `alogin`, `logout`: lazy load, then `cycle_key()` or `flush()`), `http/response.py` (`delete_cookie` adds `Secure` for `__Host-`), `db/backends/postgresql/operations.py` (`last_executed_query`), `test/utils.py` (`CaptureQueriesContext`), `test/client.py` (an explicit `HTTP_COOKIE` overrides the jar), and the `django-stubs` session stubs. **A2:** a custom `SESSION_ENGINE` with its own `AbstractBaseSession` model does not need `django.contrib.sessions` installed.
- **Deviations from plan M2b:** (a) the plan says override every database method; `save`, `asave`, `create`, `acreate`, `clear_expired`, `aclear_expired` are inherited, because they reach the database only through the overridden `exists`, `create_model_instance`, `get_model_class` (each reason in `DB_STORE` of the surface test). (b) `acreate_model_instance` delegates to the sync one (no I/O). (c) The `clear()` guard is an addition. (d) `ImproperlyConfigured` as the plan names, although `config.env` has its own error classes.
- **ASK (M3):** import-linter forbidden contracts also refuse indirect imports, so `shell` importing `accounts.models` breaks the TEN-10 contract through `models → session_model → session` (and the layers and independence contracts already refuse app-to-`accounts` imports). M3 must decide how user code reaches `accounts` (for example `allow_indirect_imports` on this contract, or moving the re-export).
- **Evidence (2026-10-30, macOS, Docker):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept; Semgrep 0 findings, `--test` 4/4; the 16 new files not yet tracked by git scanned by path, 0 findings), `make typecheck` (111 files), `make test` (1055 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (817 passed), `make migrate` (lintmigrations OK; `migrate accounts zero` and re-apply OK), `make test-live` (94 passed), `make host-test-live` (30 passed), `make e2e` (36 passed).
- **Why:** Spec D6, §8.1, TEN-10, UNI-16, UNI-18; owner Q2 (a) (2026-10-27); plan `docs/plans/2026-10-27-m2-headers-sessions-csrf.md` M2b.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `django.contrib.sessions` with its model (40-character key cannot hold the hash; it would store the raw cookie); a signed-cookie engine (no server-side revocation, D6); a sliding expiry (Q2 (a): absolute).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/` (new), `app/config/{env,settings}.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `semgrep/sessions-store-only.{yml,py}` (new), `.env.example`, `Makefile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, `app/tests/{test_env_wiring,test_session_settings}.py` (new), existing tests given the two variables, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `docs/runbooks/{local-dev,host-rebuild}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q2 (a)); builder (deep tier), within plan M2b; the M3 ASK left to the owner

## 2026-10-31 — M2c: session rows MAC-bound under `tenant_source_key`
- **Decision:** (1) **Format:** `session_data = v<n>:<mac>:<Django-signed blob>`; `mac` = unpadded base64url HMAC-SHA-256 under key version `n` over `skiw-session-v1` ‖ field(stored key) ‖ field(`expire_date` as UTC ISO-8601 to the microsecond) ‖ field(blob), each field an 8-byte big-endian length then UTF-8 bytes. The label `skiw-session-v1` and the layout are builder choices (plan A7). Parsing is one ASCII-only regex (`v[1-9][0-9]{0,5}`, a 43-character MAC, then the blob, which itself contains colons); the MAC is compared as bytes with `hmac.compare_digest`. (2) **Versions:** the highest listed version signs every save; every listed version verifies (spec §8.4 H4). (3) **Store (`accounts.session`):** `create_model_instance` seals with the stored key and the deadline it writes, so every `save()`/`cycle_key()` recomputes the MAC; M2b keeps the deadline fixed, so the MAC stays valid across saves (test). `load`/`aload` read the row (a past `expire_date` is still absent, M2b), verify the MAC against the row's own `session_key` and `expire_date`, and only then take the deadline and decode. On a bad MAC, an unlisted version or malformed data they return `{}` and clear `_session_key`, as Django's not-found path does, so the next write mints a new key and the rejected row is never updated in place; the middleware deletes the cookie; the rejected row's deadline is never taken. `_renew_lifetime` is untouched. One warning on `django.security.SuspiciousSession` per rejection, with only the reason class (malformed/unlisted, or MAC mismatch) and no key, cookie, MAC or session content (UNI-09). (4) **Key reader (`config.env`):** `parse_key_lines` is the one line parser; `key_versions` (Django `SECRET_KEY`, base64 text) and the new `versioned_keys(name) -> list[(version, bytes)]` both use it. `versioned_keys("TENANT_SOURCE_KEY")` reads `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY_FILE` on hosts or `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY` (same lines) locally and in CI; both set, neither set, a short (< 32 bytes) key, a repeated version or bad base64 fail closed, and no error carries key text. (5) **Lazy read:** settings define only the loader `SKIW_TENANT_SOURCE_KEYS = partial(env.versioned_keys, "TENANT_SOURCE_KEY")`, never called at import, because `migrate` imports settings without the `tenant_source_key` mount (H4). `accounts.source_key.require_key()` calls it once (cached) on first use; `config.asgi` calls it before `require_migrated()`, so `web` refuses to serve without a valid key. (6) **Wiring:** `infra/host/compose.yml` `x-app-env` gains `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY_FILE: /run/secrets/tenant_source_key` (so `web` and `worker`; `migrate` has its own environment and no key); local Compose `app` and `worker` take `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY` from `.env`. (7) **Rotation:** adding a higher version and keeping the old one until its sessions expire (≤ `SKIW_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_H` hours) keeps users signed in; removing it or replacing the file logs everyone out (runbooks `host-rebuild.md`, `local-dev.md`; UNI-16).
- **Deviations from plan M2c:** (a) `require_key()` and the MAC live in a new `accounts.source_key`, not `accounts.session`: the M2b import-linter contract "Only accounts may import the session store and its model (TEN-10)" has `config` as a source and counts indirect imports, so `config.asgi` cannot import `accounts.session`; `accounts.source_key` touches no database and imports neither the store nor the model, so the contract stays as it is. (b) `accounts` may not import `config` ("Apps do not reach into config internals"), so the loader reaches `accounts` through `django.conf.settings` (point 5). (c) **No key value is committed anywhere, `.env.example` included:** the `skiw-secret-key-assignment` gitleaks rule matches any `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY=<16+ key characters>` line (checked with gitleaks v8.30.1: a generated line is flagged), and its comment rules out path exemptions (PR 9 review R1). `make .env` instead appends a random `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY=v1:<openssl rand -base64 32>` line; `.env.example` documents it in a comment. CI gets its key the same way (`make test-live` makes `.env` before `make e2e`). (d) `Makefile` `TOOL_ENV` and the CI label step are unchanged: they only import settings, which never read the key; the unit tests that need a key set their own. (e) Only `web` runs the start-up check; the `worker` never runs `config.asgi` and serves no sessions, so it reads the key lazily if it ever needs it (plan: "asgi.py only").
- **Not covered here:** the TEN-11 variant "a row whose agency/user was edited" needs the user and agency keys of M3/M4; M2c proves the mechanism with an arbitrary data key (plan M2c Tests).
- **Why:** Spec §4.1 `django_session`, §9 M2 ("a row with edited data, or data copied from another row, is rejected (logged out)"), TEN-11, §8.4 H4, UNI-07, UNI-09, UNI-16; owner addition 2026-10-27 (`expire_date` in the MAC, plan Risk 7); plan `docs/plans/2026-10-27-m2-headers-sessions-csrf.md` M2c.
- **Alternatives rejected:** an `ignore_imports` hole in the TEN-10 contract for `config.asgi` (weakens the contract `app/documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py` checks); reading the key at settings import (breaks `migrate`); a gitleaks allowlist for an `.env.example` placeholder (changes secret-scan config; generation needs none); deleting the rejected row on load (a write on a read; it expires anyway).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/{session,source_key}.py`, `app/config/{env,settings,asgi}.py`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example`, `Makefile`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_session_hmac_live.py`, `app/tests/test_startup_key.py` new; `test_secret_files.py`, `test_env_wiring.py`, `test_session_store_surface.py`, accounts `conftest.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`), `docs/runbooks/{host-rebuild,local-dev}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M2c; deviation (c) left to the owner to confirm

## 2026-10-31 — M2d: CSRF on every mutating route, `route()` registration and the redeploy proof
- **Decision:** (1) **Middleware and cookie (owner Q4 (a)):** `CsrfViewMiddleware` right after `SessionMiddleware`; `CSRF_COOKIE_NAME = "__Host-csrftoken"`, Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, `CSRF_COOKIE_AGE = None` (browser-session cookie, builder value, plan A8), `CSRF_USE_SESSIONS = False`, no `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` (same origin, D2). The token is rendered into the `no-store` shell as `<meta name="csrf-token">`; the SPA sends it as `X-CSRFToken` from M3. `check --deploy` no longer allows `security.W003`. (2) **Failure view `config.csrf.failure`:** 403, header `X-Skiw-Csrf: rejected`, and a plain-text code taken from the prefix of Django's reason (`origin`, `referer`, `no-cookie`, `cookie-format`, `token-missing`, `token-invalid`, `other`). The reason itself is never sent: `REASON_BAD_ORIGIN` and `REASON_BAD_REFERER` embed the client's header (UNI-09, and the registry rule `reflected-data-httpresponse`). (3) **`config.routes.route(path, view, *, name, methods, auth)`** is the only way a plain view enters a URLconf (Semgrep `urls-route-only`): it wraps the view in `require_http_methods(methods)` and records `skiw_methods` and `skiw_auth` (`"cookie"`, used for every route so far, public ones included, because a browser sends its cookies to them; or `"token"`). It refuses an empty or repeated method list, TRACE or any unknown method (Django's CSRF treats TRACE as safe, the gate does not) and any other auth. The views lost their own `@require_GET`, so dispatch and the gate read one list. (4) **Gate (`app/tests/test_csrf_gate.py`):** walks `config.urls` and `tests.csrf_urls` (config.urls plus one test-only cookie-auth POST route) and fails on a callable without `skiw_methods`, a pattern without a name, a missing fixture, a fixture that does not resolve the path parameters or reaches another pattern, a repeated (name, method), a token-auth mutating pair not in `TOKEN_AUTH_ROUTES`, and a stale `TOKEN_AUTH_ROUTES` entry. Cases run with `Client(enforce_csrf_checks=True)` over HTTPS: for each cookie-auth mutating pair, a valid token with `Origin: https://testserver` gets the handler's outcome (2xx, 400 or 422, no marker), and missing, wrong and cross-site-`Origin` requests get the CSRF marker with the expected code; for each route, every unsafe verb it does not accept gets 405 with a valid token. `TOKEN_AUTH_ROUTES` starts empty; an entry needs a reason and a reviewer sign-off. (5) **Semgrep:** `no-csrf-exempt` (new; it did not exist before M2d) rejects in `app/` `csrf_exempt`, setting `csrf_exempt`, `csrf_processing_done`, `_dont_enforce_csrf_checks` or `_ninja_csrf_exempt`, and a `csrf=False` call argument (django-ninja's switch on cookie auth classes); `urls-route-only` rejects `django.urls.path` / `re_path` outside `app/config/routes.py` and the app test packages. (6) **Redeploy proof (owner Q3 (a)):** `infra/host/tests/test_session_live.py` (`host_live`, its own stack from `test_edge_live.py`'s fixture) sets a session through Caddy, restarts `web` (same value, same row count), builds a second image with an extra label (new image ID), runs `migrate` with it and recreates `web` (container and image ID changed, same value), then replaces `tenant_source_key` with a single new version and force-recreates `web` (the same cookie gets an empty session and a cookie delete). The probes in `edge_probe/urls.py` return the value they made in a response header and answer `get` with a status only (204 same, 409 different, 404 none), so no request or session data reaches a response body.
- **Step 0 — django-ninja read (source and docs, not memory):** django-ninja is not in `uv.lock` and not installed, so the version the lockfile would take today was read: **1.7.3** (latest on PyPI 2026-10-31), the wheel `django_ninja-1.7.3-py3-none-any.whl` (sha256 `5e3c2dcead83a2512ea772dbc435ca51632cf46bf3be1523bac41e5bb394891c`) unpacked outside the repo, and `docs/docs/reference/csrf.md` at tag `v1.7.3`. Findings: (a) **every ninja view is `csrf_exempt` at the Django middleware level** (`operation.py` `PathView.get_view`, both the sync and the async wrapper set `csrf_exempt = True`), so `CsrfViewMiddleware` never checks a ninja route; (b) there is no `csrf` option on `NinjaAPI` or `Router` in 1.7.3 (no `csrf` in `main.py` or `router.py`); the docs: "CSRF protection turned OFF for all operations, but will automatically enable csrf for Cookie based authentication"; (c) CSRF is enforced only by `APIKeyCookie` and its subclasses (`SessionAuth` / `django_auth`, `SessionAuthSuperUser`, `SessionAuthIsStaff`), through `ninja.utils.check_csrf`, which runs a fresh `CsrfViewMiddleware.process_request` / `process_view`, called from `APIKeyCookie._get_key` (`security/apikey.py:48-54`) before `authenticate` runs; `APIKeyCookie(csrf=False)` switches it off, and so does Django's `@csrf_exempt` on the operation function (`operation.csrf_exempt` sets `request._ninja_csrf_exempt`); (d) `HttpBearer`, `HttpBasicAuth`, `APIKeyHeader`, `APIKeyQuery` and `auth=None` never check CSRF, and with several auth classes they run in order and the first truthy result wins, while an exception (the CSRF `HttpError` included) ends the run (`operation.py:324-339`, async twin from line 568), so a cookie route that lists a bearer class first is only as strong as the bearer check; (e) on a CSRF failure `check_csrf` calls our failure view but ninja discards that response and raises `HttpError(403, "CSRF check Failed")`, so a ninja refusal never carries `X-Skiw-Csrf: rejected`; (f) ninja's `TestClient` sets `_dont_enforce_csrf_checks`, so the gate must keep Django's `Client(enforce_csrf_checks=True)`.
- **Consequence for the gate (ninja half):** there is nothing to discover yet. A ninja view is a closure with no `skiw_methods`, so the first ninja route fails the completeness check (fail closed). The PR that adds django-ninja must first extend the gate: discover operations from the routers (`methods`, path, `auth_callbacks`), treat a mutating operation as cookie-auth unless every auth class is a token class in `TOKEN_AUTH_ROUTES`, refuse `auth=None` on a mutating operation, and recognise a ninja CSRF refusal (for example a project cookie auth class that raises with the marker). **ASK (owner, for that PR):** how a ninja CSRF refusal is marked.
- **Deviations from plan M2d:** (a) the plan's `GET /probe/session/get` "returns" the value; here the test sends the expected value in `X-Probe-Value` and the probe answers by status only, because session data in a response body trips the CI registry rule `reflected-data-httpresponse` (PR 73). (b) The fixtures carry no authenticated session: there is no login before M3, and with `CSRF_USE_SESSIONS = False` the session plays no part in the check; M3 adds a logged-in fixture. (c) App test packages keep `path()` in their test-only URLconfs (`app/accounts/tests/urls.py`): `accounts` may not import `config` (import-linter), and `config.urls` never includes them; the gate's own test URLconf is in `app/tests` and uses `route()`. (d) The 20 MB POST in `test_body_cap_is_25_mb` now expects the CSRF 403 with the marker instead of 200: it still proves the body passed Caddy's cap and reached Django, because Django's ASGI handler reads the whole body before it builds the request (`core/handlers/asgi.py:174`, `read_body` 256-275). (g) **Size:** about 597 lines of production, test and Semgrep code (production about 85, the gate test 209, `test_session_live.py` 120, probe and edge changes about 50, Semgrep rules and fixtures 94) and 26 of docs, against the plan's estimate of about 380; the four-PR limit leaves no further split, so this is left to the owner. (e) The shared `dist` test fixture moved from `app/shell/tests/conftest.py` to `app/conftest.py`. (f) `no-csrf-exempt` is new here, not kept from earlier.
- **Planted-regression proof (2026-10-31, `make test` scope, edits reverted after):** (1) `healthz` given `methods=("GET", "PATCH")`, fixtures untouched: `(healthz, PATCH)` joined the inventory and its 4 cases (valid, missing, wrong, cross-site) ran and passed, 35 passed in total (32 before); its PATCH 405 case dropped out. (2) The same plus `csrf_exempt(healthz)`: the 3 CSRF-marker cases failed (status 200, not 403), valid passed. A new cookie-auth `items/<int:pk>` PATCH route with `csrf_exempt`: without a fixture the completeness check failed (`item: no fixture`); with a fixture its 3 marker cases failed; without `csrf_exempt`, all 4 passed (39 passed). (3) A `path("bare", …)` callable without `skiw_methods`: completeness failed (`bare: not mounted by config.routes.route()`); this case is also a permanent test.
- **Evidence (2026-10-31, macOS):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept; Semgrep 6 rules 0 findings, `--test` 6/6), `make typecheck` (120 files), `make test` (1118 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (818 passed), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 226 rules, 135 files, 0 findings). `infra/host/tests/test_session_live.py` alone ran once through `pytest -m host_live` on Docker Desktop: 3 passed. **Not run:** the full `make host-test-live` (so the changed body-cap assertion in `test_edge_live.py` is unproven), `make test-live`, `make e2e`.
- **Why:** Spec T6 ("Django CSRF on all mutating routes (no `CSRF_ENFORCE` switch)", "cookies `__Host-`, Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict"), A.19, §9 M2 ("session survives a redeploy and a restart of the one `web` container"), D2, UNI-05, UNI-17, UNI-18; owner Q3 (a), Q4 (a) and the 2026-10-27 additions; plan `docs/plans/2026-10-27-m2-headers-sessions-csrf.md` M2d.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `csrf_exempt` scan alone (misses a route that skips CSRF another way, owner 2026-10-27); `CSRF_USE_SESSIONS = True` (owner Q4); Django's default failure page (inline style the CSP blocks, plan Risk 8); the reason text in the response (echoes client headers).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/config/{settings,urls}.py`, `app/config/{routes,csrf}.py` (new), `app/health/views.py`, `app/jobs/views.py`, `app/shell/views.py`, `app/shell/templates/shell/shell.html`, `app/conftest.py` (moved), `app/tests/{csrf_fixtures,csrf_urls,test_csrf_gate}.py` (new), `app/tests/test_deploy_checks.py`, `semgrep/{no-csrf-exempt,urls-route-only}.{yml,py}` (new), `infra/host/tests/edge/edge_probe/urls.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_edge_live.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_session_live.py` (new), `docs/runbooks/{local-dev,deploy}.md`; UNI-05, UNI-09, UNI-17, UNI-18, UNI-19, AUTH-13.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17). django-ninja 1.7.3 read only, not added.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q3 (a), Q4 (a)); builder (deep tier), within plan M2d; the ninja-marker ASK left to the owner

## 2026-10-31 — M3 plan owner answers; M3a: auth groundwork (security log, key-version bound, worker key check, the config.urls -> accounts.views edge, e2e on localhost)
- **Decision:** (1) **Owner answers to the M3 plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §8), 2026-10-31, in the builder chat: all (a) to Q1–Q10.** In the plan's own words: Q1 (a) "an M4-core plan (agency root, `guard`, ownership step, `with_agency`, policies, schema check, TEN-08 harness) runs after M3c and before M3d; the rest of M4 after M3; the spec's milestone text is fixed at the next spec change"; Q2 (a) "nine PRs M3a–M3i ≤ ≈ 400 each (plus M4-core)"; Q3 (a) "plain `route()` views with own boundary validation for M3's ≈ 10 auth endpoints and hand-written TS types; ninja comes in its own PR that first extends the CSRF gate"; Q4 (a) "M3 writes `security_event` rows (`login.new_device`, `account.locked`) and a WARNING line; the mail route and an app alert path come with a spec change before M17 cutover"; Q5 (a) "operator command `accounts_invite` on the host prints a single-use enrolment link valid 30 min [A]; staff invites by the owner come with M4-rest "Manage staff""; Q6 (a) "required host-config variables with bounds (M2b pattern, list in §3 M3b), the step-up age moved into `setting` in M5, the rest deployment-wide"; Q7 (a) "do not install it: own `accounts.auth.login()` on Django's session, CSRF and signing"; Q8 (a) "the exact app hostname per environment (`localhost` locally and in CI)"; Q9 (a) "defer both to M4-rest; M4-rest's done-when must name and run a test for AUTH-03 (every action checked against the §6 matrix) and a test that an owner/admin account without a passkey cannot sign in or reach data"; Q10 (a) "accept this one named, limited edge". (2) **Owner PR 74 answers** (recorded in `.agent/reviews/PR-74.md` and the PR 74 log): **Q1 = A** — `make .env` generates the local `TENANT_SOURCE_KEY`, `.env.example` holds no key, no gitleaks allowlist, M2c deviation (c) confirmed; **Q2 = A** — the M3 plan adds a server-side revocation marker with a test (built in M3d). (3) **Correction to the 2026-10-31 M2d entry, "Evidence" ("Not run: the full `make host-test-live`, `make test-live`, `make e2e`"), dated 2026-10-31:** CI run 41172009362 (`pull_request` on `feat/m2d-csrf-and-redeploy-proof`, head `436280b6`, conclusion success) ran host-test-live 33 passed, test-live 124 passed, e2e 36 passed; the builder's local re-run reported the same counts. (4) **`django.security` to stderr (carry-over (a)):** `LOGGING` in `app/config/settings.py`: handler `stderr` (`logging.StreamHandler`, `ext://sys.stderr`, WARNING, format `%(levelname)s %(name)s %(message)s`), logger `django.security` at WARNING with `propagate: False`, `disable_existing_loggers: False` (Django's default `django` handlers and library loggers are kept). Django's default sends `django.*` to the console only with DEBUG on, so the M2c `SuspiciousSession` warning reached nothing on hosts. Tests that read these records through `caplog` attach its handler to `django.security` (fixture `security_log`, accounts tests), because the logger no longer propagates to the root. What else now reaches stderr (Django 5.2.17 source read): `django.security.csrf` WARNING `Forbidden (<reason>): <path>` per CSRF refusal (`middleware/csrf.py` `_reject`; the reason embeds the client's `Origin` or `Referer` for those two failures), and `django.security.<SuspiciousOperation class>` ERROR with the traceback (`core/handlers/exception.py`; `DisallowedHost` names the client's `Host`); `log_response` escapes control characters in its arguments (`utils/log.py`). These carry paths and client-sent header values, no token, cookie or key; only the session-row line is tested here. (5) **Key-version bound (carry-over (c)):** `KEY_LINE` in `app/config/env.py` and `infra/host/skiw_host/bootstrap_secrets.py` is `v([1-9][0-9]{0,5})`, as `accounts.source_key.SEALED` reads versions back; `hardening.py` (detection only) stays unbounded. (6) **Worker key check (carry-over (j)):** `runworker` calls `settings.SKIW_TENANT_SOURCE_KEYS()` before `require_migrated()`, so `jobs` still imports no `accounts`. This reverses M2c deviation (e) ("Only `web` runs the start-up check"): M3 adds `login_blob_key` and M4 needs `tenant_source_key` in jobs. (7) **Import contract (M2b ASK, owner Q10 (a)):** the TEN-10 contract "Only accounts may import the session store and its model" gains `accounts.models` in `forbidden_modules` and the one edge `ignore_imports = ["config.urls -> accounts.views"]`, with `unmatched_ignore_imports_alerting = "warn"`: import-linter 2.15 fails an ignore that matches no import (checked: exit 1 with or without an `accounts/views.py`), and no view is mounted until the first login route; that PR sets the option back to the default (error). `warn` hides nothing: an unmatched ignore removes no edge from the graph, and a mistyped or widened ignore fails the contract fixture tests, which lint with the same `pyproject.toml` text. No `accounts/views.py` is created here. Semgrep `semgrep/config-accounts-imports.yml` (three rules, `app/config/` only): any `accounts` import in `config` other than `accounts.views` and `accounts.source_key`, and any import by string (`importlib.import_module`, `import_string`, `__import__`, `django.urls.include`) of `accounts…`, is refused; `accounts.views` only in `config/urls.py`; `accounts.source_key` only in `config/asgi.py` (M2c deviation (a)). Contract fixture tests (`app/documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py`, stub `accounts/views.py` that imports `accounts.session`): `config.urls -> accounts.views` kept; `config.urls` importing `accounts.session` or `accounts.models` (also beside `accounts.views`) broken; `accounts.views` from `config.other`, `shell` or `jobs` broken. (8) **E2E on `http://localhost:8000` (carry-over (k)):** the Playwright base URL moves from `http://127.0.0.1:8000` (WebAuthn refuses an IP RP ID). Finding (Playwright 1.63.0 with its bundled Chromium, macOS, 2026-10-31; not yet run on the CI Linux runner): Chromium (both projects) keeps the `__Host-csrftoken` cookie the shell sets on plain `http://localhost:8000` (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/) and sends it back: after a reload the value is unchanged, while a cookie-cleared reload gets a new one (the test fails then). It also keeps it on `http://127.0.0.1:8000` (control run). So the Caddy `tls internal` fallback is not needed. **Dated note, 2026-10-31 (M3b-1):** CI run 41206976831 (`pull_request` on `fix/m3a-auth-groundwork`, head `5b3c6a99`, conclusion success, job `checks` on `ubuntu-24.04`) ran `make e2e` against `http://localhost:8000` (the config default; ci.yml sets no `E2E_BASE_URL`), both Playwright projects, 38 passed, so the `__Host-csrftoken` cookie test has run on the CI Linux runner; the "not yet run on the CI Linux runner" above is corrected [V `gh run view 41206976831` and its log]. (9) **Renewed-row test (carry-over (d)):** after `start_new_lifetime()` + `cycle_key()` + save, a fresh `SessionStore(new key)` reads the data and the fresh deadline, with no `django.security` record, sync and async.
- **Deviations from plan M3a:** (a) the Semgrep rule also allows `config/asgi.py -> accounts.source_key` (existing since M2c); the plan's wording ("only in `config/urls.py`, only from `accounts.views`") would fail on `main`'s own code. (b) `unmatched_ignore_imports_alerting = "warn"` until the first view is mounted (point 7). (c) The stderr test runs `python -c` with `django.setup()` instead of `manage.py shell -c`: the same logging setup without the shell's start-up output. (d) **Size:** about 420 lines of code (production about 37, tests about 247, Semgrep rules and fixture 138) plus docs, against the plan's estimate of about 230 and the 400 target.
- **Evidence (2026-10-31, macOS, Docker Desktop):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" for the unmatched ignore; Semgrep 9 rules 0 findings, `--test` 9/9), `make typecheck` (122 files), `make test` (1142 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (819 passed), `make test-live` (126 passed), `make host-test-live` (33 passed), `make e2e` (38 passed), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 226 rules, 137 files, 0 findings). Each new check was shown to fail first: before the fix the stderr test saw an empty stderr from a subprocess that exited 0, the old code accepted a seven-digit version and `runworker` had no key check, so those tests failed (8 failed, 45 passed); after the fix, reverting each change alone failed its tests (env `KEY_LINE`: 3; bootstrap `KEY_LINE`: 1; the `runworker` call: 4; the `django.security` logger: 1); dropping the ignore, widening it to `config.** -> accounts.views` or to `config.urls -> accounts.**` each failed a contract fixture test. The Semgrep path scoping was checked with planted files under `app/config/` (removed after).
- **Not covered here:** `bootstrap-secrets restore --allow-new-key-version` on a key whose newest version is `v999999` would mint `v1000000`, which the app refuses; not handled (unreachable in practice).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §3 M3a, §4, §7 Risk 5; spec TEN-10, UNI-04, UNI-09, UNI-16, §8.4 H4; owner answers above.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a lazy dotted-path view resolver in `route()` (hides the edge from the linter; plan §3 M3a); `allow_indirect_imports` on the contract or moving the re-export (owner Q10 (a)); creating `accounts/views.py` and importing it from `config.urls` now (an unused import; it does not change the unmatched-ignore error by itself); Caddy `tls internal` for e2e (not needed, point 8).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/config/{settings,env}.py`, `app/jobs/management/commands/runworker.py`, `infra/host/skiw_host/bootstrap_secrets.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `semgrep/config-accounts-imports.{yml,py}` (new), `web/playwright.config.ts`, `web/e2e/shell.spec.ts`, tests (`app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_worker_key_check.py` new; `app/tests/test_startup_key.py`, `app/documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py`, `app/accounts/tests/{conftest,test_session_hmac_live,test_session_lifetime_live}.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_bootstrap_secrets.py`), `docs/runbooks/{local-dev,host-rebuild}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17, import-linter 2.15, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (M3 plan Q1–Q10 (a); PR 74 Q1 = A, Q2 = A); builder, within plan M3a

## 2026-10-31 — M3b-1: interim auth config, `login_blob_key` reader and start-up checks, `django.request` to stderr
- **Decision:** (1) **Split:** plan M3b came to about 815 added lines of code, tests and config (420 in M3b-1, 395 in the crypto half, not counting `uv.lock`), past the plan's ≈ 450 split rule (§1), so it is two PRs: M3b-1 (this: config and key wiring, no new dependency) and M3b-2 (`cryptography`, `argon2-cffi`, `accounts.auth.blob`, `accounts.auth.passwords`; its own entry records the dependency ladder and hashes). (2) **Interim auth values (owner Q6 (a), UNI-02):** twelve required `config.env.bounded_int` settings, read at settings import, ASCII digits only, a missing or out-of-range value raises `ImproperlyConfigured` naming the variable and its bounds, never the value. Values (T5, Flow 5.1, T18, §8.1; the last two builder values [A]) and bounds (builder values [A]): `SKIW_ARGON2_MEMORY_KIB` 65536 (19456–131072: the floor is OWASP's minimum of 19 MiB; the cap keeps two parallel verifies inside the staging `web` limit of 512 MiB), `SKIW_ARGON2_TIME_COST` 3 (2–10), `SKIW_ARGON2_PARALLELISM` 1 (1–8; every combination meets libargon2's memory ≥ 8 × lanes), `SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_ACCOUNT` 5 (3–20), `SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_IP` 20 (5–200), `SKIW_AUTH_WINDOW_MIN` 15 (1–1440), `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_AFTER` 10 (3–50), `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` 30 (1–1440), `SKIW_STEPUP_MAX_AGE_MIN` 5 (1–15), `SKIW_TOTP_SKEW_STEPS` 1 (0–2), `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN` 5 (1–15), `SKIW_ENROL_LINK_MIN` 30 (5–1440). Wired into every context M2b used: `.env.example`, `Makefile` `TOOL_ENV`, local Compose `app`/`worker`, the CI label step (`env:` and `-e`), `infra/host/env/{ci,staging,production}.env`, host Compose `x-app-env` (`web`, `worker`) and `migrate`. **No reader yet for any of them:** the three Argon2 values are read from M3b-2, the other nine from M3c–M3h (throttle and lock M3c, pending login and enrolment link M3e, TOTP skew M3f, step-up age M3h); they are validated now so every host carries them before the first reader merges, as M2b did with `SKIW_SESSION_IDLE_MIN`. This is not dead code under UNI-05. The step-up age moves into `setting` in M5; the pre-tenant values stay deployment-wide. (3) **`login_blob_key` reader:** `SKIW_LOGIN_BLOB_KEYS = partial(env.versioned_keys, "LOGIN_BLOB_KEY", max_version=0xFFFF)`, lazy like M2c (`migrate` has no mount). **Addition to the plan:** `versioned_keys`/`parse_key_lines` take `max_version` (default 999999, unchanged for other keys), because the plan's blob layout stores the version in two bytes; a higher version is refused naming the line, never the key. `config.asgi` and `runworker` call it after the tenant key and before `require_migrated()` (through settings: `config.asgi` imports only `accounts.source_key` under Semgrep `config-accounts-imports`, `jobs` imports no `accounts`). Hosts: `LOGIN_BLOB_KEY_FILE: /run/secrets/login_blob_key` in `x-app-env` (the secret was already minted by `bootstrap-secrets` and mounted into `web` and `worker`, `infra/host/skiw_host/secrets.py`); locally `LOGIN_BLOB_KEY` from `.env`, which `make .env` appends as a random `v1:` line; `.env.example` has a comment only (owner PR 74 Q1 = A pattern). (4) **`django.request` to stderr (PR 77 review residual risk):** logger `django.request` at ERROR on the existing `stderr` handler, `propagate: False` like `django.security`. Django's default sent its 5xx records to `mail_admins` (no ADMINS) and the debug-only console, so a 500 on a host left no log line. What now reaches stderr (Django 5.2.17 source read: `core/handlers/exception.py` `response_for_exception`, `core/handlers/base.py` `get_response`, `utils/log.py` `log_response`): for an uncaught exception, `ERROR django.request <reason phrase>: <request.path>` (control characters escaped; `request.path` has no query string) followed by the traceback (file paths, source lines and the exception's own message, no local variables); a view that returns a 5xx gets the same line without a traceback. The `request` object is only in the record's `extra`, which the `%(levelname)s %(name)s %(message)s` format does not print, so no headers, cookies or body. 4xx records (`Not Found`, `Method Not Allowed`, `Bad request`, `Forbidden (Permission denied)`) are WARNING and dropped. Code that puts request data into an exception message would still log it; that is a review point for later views. **Side effect:** with DEBUG on, local runs no longer show Django's 404/405 warnings in the console (the record no longer reaches the `django` logger's debug console handler). (5) **Tests share one helper:** `app/tests/settings_env.py` (`REQUIRED`, `AUTH_BOUNDS`) replaces the hand-written session values in the nine subprocess tests.
- **Mutation proof (2026-10-31, each edit reverted after):** killed: the `max_version` check (2 tests failed), the asgi call (4), the `runworker` call (4), the memory floor lowered to 1024 or the TOTP cap raised to 5 in settings (15 each), the `django.request` logger removed or set to WARNING (1 each), `LOGIN_BLOB_KEY_FILE` dropped from `x-app-env` or added to `migrate` (1 each), an auth value dropped from `migrate` (1), the Makefile key line removed (1), the key dropped from the local `worker` (1), a `-e` missing from the CI label step (2), a staging.env value changed (1). **Survived:** `django.request` with `propagate: True`: with DEBUG off the parent `django` handlers (debug-only console, `mail_admins` with no ADMINS) print nothing, so the stderr test cannot tell; it only matters with DEBUG on (a duplicate line).
- **Evidence (2026-10-31, macOS, Docker Desktop):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before; Semgrep 9 rules 0 findings, `--test` 9/9), `make typecheck` (124 files), `make test` (1372 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (832 passed), `make audit-python` (no known vulnerabilities), `make test-live` (126 passed), `make host-test-live` (33 passed; it builds the app image), `make e2e` (38 passed; the `app` container starts with the new `.env` values and key), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 0 findings).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §1 (split rule), §3 M3b, §4, §5 (UNI-02, UNI-03, UNI-07/08, UNI-16); owner Q6 (a); spec T5, Flow 5.1, T18, §8.1, §8.4 H4; PR 77 review residual risk (`django.request`).
- **Alternatives rejected:** reading the auth values lazily (the plan and M2b read bounded values at settings import, so a bad host config fails every service at once); a separate `max_version` check in the blob module only (`web` and `worker` would start with a key they cannot use); `django.request` at WARNING (every 404 and 405 would be logged, with client-chosen paths).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/config/{env,settings,asgi}.py`, `app/jobs/management/commands/runworker.py`, `.env.example`, `Makefile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/env/*.env`, tests (`app/tests/{settings_env,test_auth_settings}.py` new; `test_security_log_stderr.py`, `test_startup_key.py`, `test_env_wiring.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_worker_key_check.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, and the env helper in `test_registry`, `test_database_url`, `test_deploy_checks`, `test_secret_files`, `test_session_settings`, `test_settings_fail_closed`), `docs/runbooks/{local-dev,host-rebuild}.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q6 (a), Q7 (a)); coordinator (split, per plan §1); builder, within plan M3b

## 2026-11-01 — M3b-2: `cryptography` and `argon2-cffi`, login and credential blobs, Argon2id hashing; auth bounds no weaker than the spec (closes PR 78 P2)
- **Decision:** (1) **Dependency ladder (UNI-06; PyPI JSON API and GitHub API, re-checked 2026-11-01).** `cryptography==50.0.2` (first upload 2026-09-30, Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause): needed for AES-256-GCM and HKDF, which the stdlib lacks (no AEAD). Repo `pyca/cryptography` is not archived; its last commit is from 2026-10-31. Its musllinux_1_2 abi3 wheels cover x86_64 and aarch64; the image is `python:3.12.15-alpine`. Wheel `cp311-abi3-musllinux_1_2_x86_64` is 5,030,152 bytes. `argon2-cffi==25.1.0` (2025-06-03, MIT; pure-Python wheel, 14,657 bytes): needed because spec T5 names Argon2id and the stdlib has only scrypt. Django's `Argon2PasswordHasher` needs `django.contrib.auth` (owner Q7 (a): not installed) and has other parameters. Repo `hynek/argon2-cffi` is not archived, its last commit is from 2026-10-01 (last push 2026-11-01), and it has 3 open issues and PRs together; maintenance is judged fine (plan A6). Transitive: `argon2-cffi-bindings` 26.1.0 (2026-08-20, MIT; musl abi3 wheel 26,785 bytes), `cffi` 2.1.1 (2026-08-03, MIT-0; cp312 musl wheel 223,597 bytes) and `pycparser` 3.12 (2026-10-22, BSD-3-Clause; pure Python, 62,600 bytes). All five are the latest release, none is yanked, PyPI lists no vulnerability, and the GitHub Advisory Database lists none for `cryptography` 50.0.2 or `argon2-cffi` 25.1.0. The two direct pins are in `[project].dependencies`; `uv lock` adds the transitive ones. `uv.lock` sha256, sdist then the musllinux x86_64 (or `py3-none-any`) wheel: `cryptography` `7b46165bb56eb4704e2eaaf86f3c940d19154535d9b0ca7d6d590b04060e00d5` / `85d0d9a31b9098e98534226d5686b47264b95e62ce459dc2e62fdfc809f9fe93`; `argon2-cffi` `694ae5cc8a42f4c4e2bf2ca0e64e51e23a040c6a517a85074683d3959e1346c1` / `fdc8b074db390fccb6eb4a3604ae7231f219aa669a2652e0f20e16ba513d5741`; `argon2-cffi-bindings` `63505c71542a44b68b1e38060450fb006404170da375feb31af153e7f9c6205d` / `ffff613aaa9ce6236766e2fc6dc560bb5abde7a2e2416e3db1f9ae395a2b4dd4`; `cffi` `dd31f52ea1086513bb9df30f8fcee9b8918323ae067a3d5b78bc826a000712be` / `210019b6c7cf07f081b4c54635c8cf744377001350e29cc0f81c4377b4797735`; `pycparser` `f03387877eab82ce01bbdfd0ba7252ee55a90b1c708d5129e2ce915475cf1db6` / `a076f3f489b39b43ed13563a1dafe42447445bec2e294154ec5c57fcdda4c85e` (each hash matches PyPI). (2) **`accounts.auth.blob` (spec §4.1 `login_blob_ct`, `lookup_blob_ct`):** blob = 2-byte big-endian key version ‖ 12-byte random nonce ‖ AES-256-GCM ciphertext ‖ 16-byte tag. Each version's AES key is HKDF-SHA-256 of that version's decoded key bytes (info `skiw-blob-key-v1`), so a key longer than 32 bytes still gives one AES-256 key. AAD = version bytes ‖ `skiw-login-v1` ‖ normalised email (NFKC, then strip, then lower-case) for a login blob, or version bytes ‖ `skiw-credential-v1` ‖ credential id for a credential blob. The plaintext is padded to one size per purpose. Login: user id 16 ‖ agency id 16 ‖ 2-byte length ‖ Argon2 encoded hash ‖ zeros, to 192 bytes, so L = 222. Credential: user id ‖ agency id, 32 bytes, so L = 62 (plan [A3]). An Argon2 parameter change therefore cannot tell old blobs from new ones. A hash too long for the size raises `ValueError`; it is never cut. The newest listed version seals; every listed version opens. A failed open (wrong length, unlisted version or bad tag) returns `None` and logs nothing, because in M3e every unknown email reaches it with a decoy. Keys come from `settings.SKIW_LOGIN_BLOB_KEYS()` (M3b-1), read once per process (`require_key`, cached). (3) **`accounts.auth.passwords` (spec T5, AUTH-04):** `argon2.PasswordHasher` with `Type.ID`, a 16-byte salt and a 32-byte hash, both fixed because L depends on them. Memory, time and lanes are read from settings on each call (one cached hasher per parameter set). `verify` returns False on a mismatch or a malformed hash; `needs_rehash` follows the current parameters. `dummy_verify` runs one verify against a dummy hash of a random password, made once per parameter set. (4) **Bounds correction (closes PR 78 review P2):** the weak end of each bound is now the spec or owner value, so a weaker host config fails settings import and needs an owner decision. `SKIW_ARGON2_MEMORY_KIB` is 65536–131072 (T5: 64 MiB). The cap of 131072 KiB (128 MiB) is unchanged: two parallel verifies stay inside the staging `web` limit of 512 MiB. `SKIW_ARGON2_TIME_COST` is 3–10 (T5: t=3). `SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_ACCOUNT` is 3–5 (Flow 5.1: 5). `SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_IP` is 5–20 (Flow 5.1: 20). `SKIW_AUTH_WINDOW_MIN` is 15–1440 (Flow 5.1: 15). `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_AFTER` is 3–10 (Flow 5.1: 10). `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` is 30–1440 (Flow 5.1: 30). `SKIW_STEPUP_MAX_AGE_MIN` is 1–5 (T5, §8.1: ≤ 5 min). `SKIW_TOTP_SKEW_STEPS` is 0–1 (Flow 5.1: ±1). `SKIW_ENROL_LINK_MIN` is 5–30 (owner Q5 (a): 30). `SKIW_ARGON2_PARALLELISM` stays 1–8 and `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN` stays 1–15, because neither has a spec value. The strict ends are builder values [A]. The lower bound of 3 for failures per account and for lock-after is kept, not 1: it is about usability, not security, since below 3 one typo plus one retry would throttle or lock a user. The configured values and every host env file are unchanged (they already equal the spec values). The M3b-1 entry above records the old bounds as they were on 2026-10-31; this point supersedes them.
- **Duty for M3e (not built here):** the dummy hash is made lazily on the first `dummy_verify`. So the first unknown-account login after a start or restart would pay one extra Argon2 hash, a timing difference from a known account. M3e must warm the dummy hash (one `dummy_verify` at start-up, before serving) and test that it does. A code comment in `passwords.dummy_verify` says so.
- **Deviations from plan M3b:** (a) **Public API:** the plan names `blob.seal(key_version, plaintext, aad)` / `blob.open(blob, aad)`. The module exposes `seal_login`, `open_login`, `seal_credential` and `open_credential` instead, which build the padded plaintext and the purpose AAD themselves. The general `_seal`/`_open` are private and always seal with the newest version. A caller can neither choose an old key version nor build an unpadded or mislabelled blob. (b) The plan says the dummy hash is "made once at start-up"; it is made on first use, and warming it is M3e's duty (above). (c) **Size:** 446 added lines of code, tests and config, not counting `uv.lock` (production 181, tests 263, config 2), against a split target of about 395 for this half. The bounds correction and its test are the difference.
- **Argon2 known answer:** phc-winner-argon2 `src/test.c` at commit `f57e61e19229e23c4445b85494dbf7c07de721cb`, Argon2id v=19, t=2, m=256 KiB, p=1, password `password`, salt `somesalt` → `$argon2id$v=19$m=256,t=2,p=1$c29tZXNhbHQ$nf65EOgLrQMR/uIPnA4rEsF5h7TKyQwu9U1bMCHGi/4`. `argon2.low_level.hash_secret` reproduces it, both on the host and inside the built image, and `passwords.verify` accepts it. The vector uses parameters below the bounds on purpose; `verify` reads the parameters from the hash.
- **Risk 3 (Argon2 memory), at the T5 values (64 MiB, t=3, p=1). Neither run was on the production host or on x86_64.** On macOS arm64 (host Python, 2026-10-31), one verify took 83 ms and 4 parallel verifies 99 ms; process peak RSS was 84 MiB after one hash and 276 MiB after 4 parallel. In the built image, linux/arm64 musl under Docker Desktop (18 vCPU VM, 2026-11-01), one verify took 64 ms and 4 parallel 74 ms; peak RSS was 77 MiB after one hash and 269 MiB after 4 parallel. That is about 64 MiB per concurrent verify, to compare with `SKIW_MEM_WEB=512m` on staging (`SKIW_WEB_WORKERS=2`). The `web` service's base memory under load was not measured. The per-IP and per-account limits (M3c) bound how many verifies an attacker can force.
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-01, each edit reverted after; `app/tests` plus the two new test files; the second run used `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` with `__pycache__` removed and gave the same results).** *Bounds, all killed except one:* memory floor 19456 in `settings.py` only (16 failed); floor 19456 in both `settings.py` and `AUTH_BOUNDS` (2: the spec-value test and the real-hash floor test); failures-per-account cap 20 in both (1, the spec-value test only); skew cap 2 in both (1); window floor 1 in both (1); step-up cap 15 in both (1); enrolment cap 1440 in `settings.py` only (16); the `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` line removed (19); lock-after floor 4 in `settings.py` only (16). **Survived:** pending-login cap 60 in both files. That setting has no spec value, so nothing outside `AUTH_BOUNDS` pins it, which is the case PR 78 P2 leaves to review. *Crypto (2026-10-31, on the combined tree), all killed:* AAD without the email; no padding; no size guard; no length check on open; no email normalisation; sealing with the oldest version; Argon2 type I; salt 8; parameters ignored; dummy not cached; dummy with fixed parameters; `verify`'s exception narrowed.
- **Evidence (2026-11-01, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before; Semgrep 9 rules, 0 findings; `--test` 9/9), `make typecheck` (129 files), `make test` (1403 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (832 passed), `make audit-python` (pip-audit 2.10.1: no known vulnerabilities), `make test-live` (126 passed), `make host-test-live` (33 passed; it builds the app image with the new wheels), `make e2e` (38 passed; the `app` container starts with the new dependencies), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 226 rules, 144 files, 0 findings). Built image: linux/arm64, where it imports all five packages at the pinned versions, round-trips AES-GCM and reproduces the known answer. The CI `image` job builds on `ubuntu-24.04` (x86_64), the platform of the x86_64 wheel hashes above, and runs Trivy there.
- **Not proven here:** Trivy on the new image (CI only, not installed locally; the CI `image` job runs it); the image build and Risk 3 figures on x86_64 Linux and on the Hostinger VPS (all local runs were arm64); that HKDF is used rather than the raw key, and that the version bytes are in the AAD (neither is visible to a test, since another version means another key); the M3e dummy-hash warm-up (a duty, tested in M3e); uniform timing between known and unknown accounts (M3e).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §2, §3 M3b, §7 Risk 3, [A3], [A6]; spec T5, Flow 5.1, §4.1, §8.1, §8.4 H4; UNI-02, UNI-06, UNI-13; owner Q5 (a), Q6 (a), Q7 (a); PR 78 review P2.
- **Alternatives rejected:** Django's `Argon2PasswordHasher` (needs `django.contrib.auth`, Q7 (a), and has other parameters); the raw key bytes as the AES key (a key longer than 32 bytes would be refused or cut); a public `seal(key_version, …)` (deviation (a)); room in the bounds below T5 for RAM (that is a T5 change: owner decision and an exception here, PR 78 P2); a lower bound of 1 failure (above).
- **Scope / affects:** `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `app/accounts/auth/{__init__,blob,passwords}.py` (new), `app/config/settings.py` (bounds), tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_{blob,passwords}.py` new; `app/tests/settings_env.py`, `app/tests/test_auth_settings.py`).
- **Versions:** `cryptography` 50.0.2, `argon2-cffi` 25.1.0, `argon2-cffi-bindings` 26.1.0, `cffi` 2.1.1, `pycparser` 3.12 (new); Django 5.2.17.
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q5 (a), Q6 (a), Q7 (a)); builder (deep tier), within plan M3b and per PR 78 review P2 (bounds)

## 2026-11-01 — M3c-1: `auth_throttle` and `security_event` tables, grants and audit, Semgrep `auth-tables-accounts-only`; PR 79 P3 blob tests
- **Decision:** (1) **Split:** plan M3c (estimate ≈ 330) came to about 700 added lines of code, tests and config with the throttle, the event writer and their tests, past the plan's ≈ 450 split rule (§1). So it is two stacked PRs: M3c-1 (this: the tables, grants, audit and Semgrep rule) and M3c-2 (`accounts.auth.throttle`, `client_ip`, `accounts.auth.events.record_event`, `SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY`, their tests and mutations; its own entry). (2) **Tables as Django models, not raw SQL:** `app/accounts/auth/tables.py` defines `AuthThrottle` (`auth_throttle`: `key_hmac bytea`, `window_start timestamptz`, `count int`, primary key `(key_hmac, window_start)` through Django 5.2's `CompositePrimaryKey`) and `SecurityEvent` (`security_event`: `id uuid` primary key, `at timestamptz`, `kind varchar(64)`, `email_hmac bytea null`, `ip_hmac bytea null`, `details_json jsonb`), as in §4.1 and plan M3c; `accounts.models` re-exports them. Migration `accounts/0002` is the two generated `CreateModel` operations plus one `RunSQL` for the grants. A first draft used `RunSQL` for the DDL and failed `lintmigrations`: django-migration-linter splits the `sqlmigrate` output into lines, so a multi-line `CREATE TABLE` was flagged "NOT NULL constraint on columns", and the `DROP TABLE` in `reverse_sql` was flagged "DROPPING table". `CreateModel` passes the linter, has a working reverse, and `makemigrations --check` now catches drift between the models and the schema. **Addition to the plan [A]:** CHECK constraints `length(x) = 32` on `auth_throttle.key_hmac`, `security_event.email_hmac` and `security_event.ip_hmac` (named `<table>_<column>_32`), and `auth_throttle.count >= 0`. Every key and HMAC is a 32-byte HMAC-SHA-256 value, so the database itself refuses a raw email or IP in those columns. NULL still passes the two nullable columns, for events with no email or IP. (3) **Grants (TEN-01/02, TEN-03, §4.2):** `skiw_migrator` owns both tables. `skiw_app` gets `auth_throttle` SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and `security_event` SELECT, INSERT only (append-only evidence). The `RunSQL` reverse revokes the same grants. The audit in `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py` is extended: every user table must be in a closed list (queue tables, the two auth tables, `django_session`, `django_migrations`, plus the test-only `jobs_probe`/`files_probe`); the `skiw_app` ACL entries on the auth tables must be exactly the plan list (`aclexplode`); the effective-privilege test covers both tables; the TEN-03 append-only test now requires `security_event` to exist instead of skipping it. New `app/accounts/tests/test_auth_tables_live.py` (live, as `skiw_app`): a 20-byte or a 33-byte value is refused by each CHECK, and `skiw_app` gets InsufficientPrivilege on UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE of `security_event` and on DELETE and TRUNCATE of `auth_throttle`. (4) **Semgrep `auth-tables-accounts-only` (UNI-04, TEN-10, plan M3c):** outside `app/accounts/auth/`, `app/accounts/migrations/` and `app/accounts/tests/`, it flags `AuthThrottle`/`SecurityEvent` construction and `.objects`. Each is matched bare and with the `accounts.models.` and `accounts.auth.tables.` paths, because Semgrep resolves an aliased import to its full module path. It also flags any string literal that has a write or DDL verb (`insert`, `update`, `delete`, `truncate`, `copy`, `merge`, `alter`, `drop`, `grant`, `revoke`, `lock`) before `auth_throttle`, `security_event` or `auth_stepup` (from M3h). The `--test` fixture has 7 `ruleid` and 2 `ok` lines. (5) **PR 79 review P3 (Bob):** two tests in `test_auth_blob.py`. One seals with a key listed as both v2 and v1, rewrites the header to v1 and expects `open` to fail, which proves the version bytes are in the AAD. The other decrypts with `AESGCM(raw key)` and the exact AAD and expects `InvalidTag`, which proves the AES key is derived (HKDF) and is not the raw key.
