These close the matching two items in the M3b-2 "Not proven" list. The `blob.py` module docstring now says the same (HKDF under `LABEL`; AAD = version ‖ purpose label ‖ lookup value). Docstring only; the code is unchanged.
- **Tables with no writer yet:** nothing writes either table in this PR. The writers (`throttle`, `record_event`) come in M3c-2, stacked on this. This is the M3b-1 pattern (settings validated before their first reader): the schema, grants and Semgrep boundary are reviewed and merged first. It is not dead code under UNI-05, because M3c-2 is the next PR on this branch line.
- **Not in this PR:** INSERT on `security_event` for `skiw_auth_lookup`. Plan §0 lists it as an M3 requirement on M4-core, because `guard.auth_lookup_login` writes one `security_event` per call (M3e). The role is NOLOGIN and owns nothing until M4-core's ownership step, so the grant is made there or in M3e, with a test on the function that uses it. `skiw_app` is the only grantee here, and the ACL test pins that.
- **Coming in M3c-2 (not decided here):** the soft-lock design. Plan M3c says "lock = count ≥ 10 within the lock window", while spec Flow 5.1 says "10 consecutive"; the builder's [A] proposal and the draft code are in the builder's untracked notes, `.agent/auto/m3c-2/NOTES.md` in the main checkout. The M3c-2 entry decides it. That entry also records that a `tenant_source_key` rotation resets every counter (keys are HMACs under the newest version) and breaks `email_hmac` correlation across versions, plus the other M3c-2 items (no row cleanup, the shared malformed-IP key, `SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY`).
- **Deviations from plan M3c:** (a) the split (above); `throttle`, `client_ip`, `record_event` and their tests from plan §6 (concurrency, window roll-over, lock and release, unknown email, no raw value in SQL parameters, XFF) move to M3c-2. (b) The 32-byte CHECKs and `count >= 0` (addition [A], above). (c) The tables are Django models (above); the plan only said "`accounts` migration".
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-01, port 5434, each edit reverted after; a migration mutation was applied with `migrate accounts 0001` and then `migrate`):** *Grants:* `security_event` UPDATE added to the grant killed 3 tests (ACL exact, effective privileges `[security_event]`, TEN-03 append-only); `auth_throttle` DELETE added killed 3 (ACL exact, effective privileges `[auth_throttle]`, deletes-only-from-allow-list). *CHECKs:* the `auth_throttle_key_hmac_32` constraint dropped from the migration killed 2 (both `auth_throttle` cases in `test_only_32_byte_hmacs_fit_the_key_columns`); `security_event_ip_hmac_32` dropped killed 1 (the `ip_hmac` case). Before `test_auth_tables_live.py` was added, dropping a CHECK survived every test; that test was written for this. *Semgrep (`semgrep --test`):* `insert` removed from the regex (missed line 19); the `accounts.models.*` patterns removed (lines 12, 14); the `accounts.auth.tables.*` patterns removed (line 10); the SQL-string branch removed (lines 17, 19, 21, 23); the `SecurityEvent(...)` patterns removed (line 14): all killed. *Blob P3:* `AESGCM(key[:32])` instead of HKDF killed 1 (`test_the_aes_key_is_derived_not_the_raw_key`); the version bytes removed from the AAD in seal and open killed 1 (`test_the_version_header_is_bound_by_the_aad`). No survivors.
- **Evidence (2026-11-01, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept; Semgrep 10 rules on 96 files, 0 findings; `--test` 10/10), `make typecheck` (132 files), `make test` (1405 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (832 passed), `make audit-python` (no known vulnerabilities), `make test-live` (139 passed; `migrate`, `makemigrations --check` and `lintmigrations` clean), `make host-test-live` (33 passed), `make e2e` (38 passed), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 226 rules, 147 files, 0 findings). Migration reverse: `migrate accounts 0001` drops both tables and `migrate` restores them with the grants. The tool targets ran with `DATABASE_URL` on a closed port; the live targets used the worktree database on 127.0.0.1:5434.
- **Not proven here:** that the Semgrep rule catches SQL built at run time (an f-string, concatenation or a table name held in a variable), `apps.get_model`, or a raw `psycopg` connection whose statement is not a literal; the database grants, not the rule, are the hard boundary, and `skiw_app` can still INSERT or UPDATE `auth_throttle` from any code in `web`. All throttle behaviour (counters, windows, lock, unknown-email equivalence, client IP) waits for M3c-2. That the schema check M4-core builds accepts both tables as listed global tables (plan §0).
- **Why:** Plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §0, §1 (split rule), §3 M3c, §6; spec §4.1, §4.2, Flow 5.1; TEN-01/02, TEN-03, TEN-10; UNI-04, UNI-05; AUTH-06; PR 79 review P3.
- **Alternatives rejected:** raw `RunSQL` DDL (fails `lintmigrations`, above); an unmanaged model with SQL DDL (two sources of truth and no drift check); no CHECK on the HMAC columns (only code review would stop a raw email or IP from landing in a row); the `skiw_auth_lookup` grant now (it would be untested until M3e, above).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/tables.py` (new), `app/accounts/models.py`, `app/accounts/migrations/0002_auth_throttle_security_event.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/blob.py` (docstring), `semgrep/auth-tables-accounts-only.{yml,py}` (new), tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_tables_live.py` new; `app/accounts/tests/test_auth_blob.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`).
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** coordinator (split, per plan §1); builder, within plan M3c and per PR 79 review P3

## 2026-11-01 — M3c-2: `accounts.auth.throttle` (account, IP and lock counters, client IP), `accounts.auth.events.record_event`, `SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY`; PR 80 P2 (Semgrep anchoring and manager names)
- **Decision:** (1) **Keys (spec §4.1 "keys are HMACs"; plan M3c [A]):** HMAC-SHA-256 under the newest `tenant_source_key` version (`accounts.source_key.require_key()[0]`) over label ‖ value. The labels are `skiw-throttle-account-v1` (normalised email: `accounts.auth.blob.normalise_email`, the one normaliser M3e also uses), `skiw-throttle-ip-v1` (client IP, below) and `skiw-throttle-lock-v1` (normalised email). No label is a prefix of another, so the three key spaces cannot collide. No function in the module takes, looks up or branches on an account, so an unknown email gets every key and the same limits and lock as a known one (AUTH-05; the response-level proof is M3e's). No raw email or IP reaches a row or a SQL parameter (live test over the captured SQL and the rows), and the M3c-1 CHECKs refuse anything but 32 bytes. **A `tenant_source_key` rotation resets every counter and every lock**, because the new version gives new keys and old rows are never read again. It also breaks `security_event.email_hmac`/`ip_hmac` correlation across versions. An operator should not rotate that key during a login attack. (2) **Counters (Flow 5.1, AUTH-06):** fixed windows of `SKIW_AUTH_WINDOW_MIN` aligned to the Unix epoch. Each counter is one statement, `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (key_hmac, window_start) DO UPDATE SET count = auth_throttle.count + 1 RETURNING count`. All three run in one transaction, always in the order account, IP, lock, so concurrent failures should not deadlock (argued from that order; the parallel test uses one email and one IP). Every time comes from `timezone.now()` as a parameter, never SQL `now()`. `blocked(email, ip)` returns `"lock"`, `"account"` or `"ip"` once the window count reaches the limit, so the 6th attempt per account and the 21st per IP are refused. M3e answers each with the same 429. A success does not clear the window counts, because they count failures. (3) **Soft-lock: spec Flow 5.1 ("10 consecutive fails → account soft-lock 30 min") wins over plan M3c ("lock = count ≥ 10 within the lock window") [A]. Deviation from the plan:** the lock counts consecutive failures with no window, in one row per email at `window_start = 1970-01-01 UTC`; `reset_on_success` sets it to 0. Every `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_AFTER`-th consecutive failure (10, 20, 30, …) upserts a marker row `(lock key, failure time)`. `blocked` returns `"lock"` while a marker is newer than `now - SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN`, from any IP. That bound is written once, in the SQL. At +29:59 the account is still locked; at +30:00 it is free. A lock does not reset the consecutive count; only a success does. So after the lock ends, the 20th consecutive failure locks again, not the 11th. Concurrent locks at one instant share a marker row. Because of the 5-per-window account limit, 10 consecutive failures take at least two windows. Why not the plan's version: "within the lock window" would forget failures older than the window, which "consecutive" does not, and it says nothing about a success. Rejected: locking while the count is ≥ 10 (that is a hard lock until a success, not 30 min); resetting the count at each lock (the same behaviour with one more UPDATE). (4) **Lock notice (owner Q4 (a)):** in the same transaction as the counters, one `security_event` `account.locked` with `email_hmac` = the account key, `ip_hmac` = the IP key of the failure that locked, and `details_json = {"consecutive": n}`. After the commit, one WARNING on `django.security.throttle` (it reaches stderr through the M3a `django.security` handler): "account soft-locked after <n> consecutive failed logins". It carries no HMAC, email or IP (UNI-09), and a rolled-back lock logs nothing. The owner alert and the new-device mail stay with the spec change Q4 (a) names. (5) **`record_event(kind, email_hmac, ip_hmac, details)`:** the kind must be in `KINDS`, which holds only `account.locked` now; M3e adds the login and lookup kinds. `details` names must match `[a-z_]{1,32}` and values must be `int` or `bool`, so no text (an email, an IP, an error message) can reach `details_json` (UNI-09). Anything else raises `ValueError` before any query. (6) **Client IP:** `client_ip(request)` reads `X-Forwarded-For` only when `settings.SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY`, else `REMOTE_ADDR`. Caddy has no `trusted_proxies` and replaces a client-sent value with the one peer address (`infra/host/caddy/edge.caddy`; `infra/host/tests/test_edge_live.py:240-244` proves a spoofed value is replaced). The value is parsed with `ipaddress`, and the canonical form is used. An empty, comma-separated or unparseable value counts under one fixed key, `"malformed"` [A]. It never falls back to `REMOTE_ADDR`, because behind Caddy that is Caddy's own address and would put every client in one bucket. (7) **`SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY = env.flag("DJANGO_BEHIND_CADDY")`** in settings. It is the existing flag, set only in `infra/host/compose.yml` for `web` and `worker`, so no host config changes. `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md` now says that the flag also selects the throttle's IP source, and what goes wrong if it is set where clients reach Django directly (UNI-16). (8) **PR 80 review P2 (Semgrep `auth-tables-accounts-only`):** Semgrep applies a `metavariable-regex` from the start of the value, so the SQL regex now begins with `^.*?`, and the verb may follow a newline, a space, a comment, a CTE or an earlier statement. The model patterns are `AuthThrottle.$M` / `SecurityEvent.$M`, bare and with the `accounts.models.` and `accounts.auth.tables.` paths, with `$M` matching `^(objects|_default_manager|_base_manager)$`. The fixture gains `ruleid` lines for a triple-quoted SQL string with a leading newline, a leading space, a CTE, a second statement, `-- comment\nUPDATE …`, `_default_manager` and `_base_manager`. It also gains `ok` lines that pin each list (a `django_session` DELETE pins the table list; `Row._meta.db_table` pins the manager list; the existing SELECT pins the verb list). In a scratch git tree, the review's probes (the 5 SQL strings and `_default_manager`) gave 6 of 6 findings in `app/billing/` and in `app/accounts/` outside `auth/`, and 0 under `app/accounts/auth/`. The M3c-1 rule gave 0 of 6. The rule gives 0 findings on `app/`, where `throttle.py` and `events.py` hold the only writes. (9) **`Makefile` `TOOL_ENV`:** the placeholder `DATABASE_URL` now points at `127.0.0.1:1` (nothing listens there) instead of `localhost:5432`, which is another local project's Postgres on the builder's machine (PR 80 review note). The tooling targets import settings and never connect. Five tests that run `import config.settings` in a subprocess still carry `localhost:5432` in their env; they do not connect either, and are unchanged.
- **No production caller yet:** `blocked`, `record_failure`, `reset_on_success`, `client_ip` and `record_event` are called only by tests until M3e's login step (plan order M3c → M4-core → M3d → M3e). This is the M3c-1 pattern ("Tables with no writer yet"): the counters, lock and IP rule are reviewed and merged before the route that uses them. It is not dead code under UNI-05.
- **Correction to the M3c-1 entry (its text stays as history):** its Semgrep mutations ("No survivors") covered only the edits it listed. None of them moved the verb away from the start of the string or used a manager other than `.objects`, and every `ruleid` line began with the verb. So the start-anchored regex and the missing `_default_manager`/`_base_manager` survived unseen. Its "Not proven here" list should have named them. Point (8) fixes both; the mutations below cover them.
- **Known limits and duties for M3e (not built here):** (a) **Check-then-record overshoot.** `blocked` and `record_failure` are separate statements, so parallel attempts that all pass `blocked` before any of them records can exceed 5 or 20 by up to the number of requests in flight. The counters themselves stay exact (20 parallel failures count 20). M3e records the failure before answering and must not run `record_failure` inside a transaction that a failed login rolls back (no `ATOMIC_REQUESTS` is set today). (b) **IPv6:** a key is per full address, so a client with a /64 has many IP buckets; the per-account limit and the lock still apply to each email. (c) **The `"malformed"` key** is shared by every malformed request. Behind Caddy that should never happen, and locally `REMOTE_ADDR` is always an address. (d) **No row cleanup:** `skiw_app` has no DELETE (M3c-1). Rows grow by at most three per failed attempt plus one per lock. Removing old rows is a later reviewed job as `skiw_migrator`. (e) gunicorn's `UvicornWorker` rewrites `REMOTE_ADDR` from `X-Forwarded-For` only for peers in `forwarded_allow_ips` (default `127.0.0.1,::1`). Behind Caddy the peer is Caddy's container address, so `REMOTE_ADDR` stays Caddy's and `X-Forwarded-For` is the right source. This is read from the library, not tested. (f) `email_hmac` in `security_event` is the account throttle key. M3e's `guard.auth_lookup_login` must write a comparable value or record why not.
- **Deviations from plan M3c:** (a) the lock design (point 3). (b) Plan §6 asks for a "client-sent XFF ignored" test; there are also tests that malformed values never fall back to `REMOTE_ADDR`, and that the keys are HMACs under the newest key version. (c) The Semgrep fix and `TOOL_ENV` change come from the PR 80 review, not the plan.
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-01, port 5434, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `__pycache__` removed; each edit applied to the staged file, run against `test_auth_throttle.py` and `test_auth_throttle_live.py` (or `semgrep --test` plus the scan of `app/`), then restored from the index with an empty `git diff` checked; 42 mutations, all killed):** ON CONFLICT without the increment, and `SET count = 1` (6 tests each); **branch on account existence (synthetic):** the throttle has no account to branch on, so the stand-in was "after the account and IP counters, return without the lock counter when no lock row exists yet for this email", the nearest proxy for "no account". It killed 5, including the known-versus-unknown statement-shape test. The real known/unknown proof is M3e's. Success reset dropped (2); reset of the account key instead of the lock key (2); XFF trusted always (7); XFF never trusted (7); malformed XFF falls back to `REMOTE_ADDR` (6); a missing XFF falls back to `REMOTE_ADDR` (6); the first element of a comma list taken (1); lock at 9 (4); lock at 11 (5); lock on every failure from the 10th (1, the 20-parallel test); lock bound `>=` instead of `>` (1); lock 1 min longer (1); lock 1 s shorter (1); lock key per email and IP (2); account limit `>` (1); IP limit `>` (1); window not rolling (a constant start; 6); window twice as long (the roll-over test, which starts at an odd window boundary so that a 30-minute window cannot end there; 2 to 4 others, depending on the time of the run); the email in the WARNING (1); the IP in the event details (5); the event without HMACs (3); no event on lock (3); an unkeyed SHA-256 instead of the HMAC (1); the oldest key version (1); the lock label equal to the account label (7); no email normalisation (2); the raw email planted as an extra parameter of the `reset_on_success` UPDATE, and the raw IP in the `blocked` SELECT (1 each, both by the raw-value assertion over the captured SQL, which had never been seen to fail before); `record_event` taking `str` values (1), any detail name (1), a widened kind list (1), no kind check (1). *Semgrep:* verb list widened with `select` (`--test` fails on the `ok` SELECT line, and `app/` gives 1 finding); table list widened to `\w+` (`--test`, and 49 findings on `app/`); manager list widened to `\w+` (`--test`, on the `_meta` line); `^.*?` removed (`--test`, the new lines); `_default_manager` dropped; `_base_manager` dropped; `update` dropped (each `--test`). The drafts had no test that pinned the HMAC key or its version (found by reading them, not by a run), so `test_keys_are_hmacs_under_the_newest_key_version` was written for the unkeyed-hash and oldest-version mutations.
- **Evidence:** 2026-11-01, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop. `make lint`: ruff pass; 10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before; Semgrep 10 rules on 100 files, 0 findings; `--test` 10/10. `make typecheck`: 136 files; `tsc` pass. `make test`: 1418 passed; vitest 4. `make host-test`: 832 passed. `make audit-python`: no known vulnerabilities. `make test-live`: 145 passed; `migrate`, `makemigrations --check` (no changes) and `lintmigrations` (0/44 erroneous) clean. `make host-test-live`: 33 passed. `make e2e`: 38 passed. CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`): 226 rules, 151 files, 0 findings. The tool targets ran with `DATABASE_URL` on the closed port 127.0.0.1:1; `test-live`, `e2e` and the mutation runs used this worktree's Compose database on 127.0.0.1:5434; `host-test-live` used its own throwaway Compose projects on Docker-chosen loopback ports. gitleaks is not installed locally (the CI `security` job runs it); a pattern scan of the added lines found no key material.
- **Not proven here:** that a known and an unknown email get the same responses and timing (M3e; here only the counter statements and outcomes are compared, and the existence mutation is synthetic); the overshoot bound under real concurrent logins (M3e); the gunicorn `forwarded_allow_ips` behaviour behind Caddy (point (e), read from the library); that a WARNING reaches stderr on a host (the M3a subprocess test proves the `django.security` handler; this logger is its child); Semgrep still misses SQL built at run time, `apps.get_model` and statements in variables (as in M3c-1). The grants stay the hard boundary.
- **Why:** spec Flow 5.1, §4.1, §4.2, T5, T18; plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §3 M3c, §4 M3c row, §5, §7; owner Q4 (a), Q6 (a); AUTH-05, AUTH-06; UNI-04, UNI-09, UNI-16; TEN-03, TEN-10; PR 80 review P2, P3 and its note on `TOOL_ENV`.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a windowed lock count (point 3); a hard lock until a success (point 3); falling back to `REMOTE_ADDR` on a bad XFF (point 6); a separate HMAC key for the throttle (a new secret in the host store needs a spec change; `tenant_source_key` is already mounted where the login runs); SQL `now()` (a frozen clock could not test the windows); keying the per-IP limit on the IPv6 /64 (the spec says per IP; limit (b)).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/{throttle,events}.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/tables.py` (docstring), `app/config/settings.py` (`SKIW_BEHIND_CADDY`), `semgrep/auth-tables-accounts-only.{yml,py}`, `Makefile` (`TOOL_ENV`), `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_throttle.py`, `test_auth_throttle_live.py`, new).
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M3c (the lock design is [A] and needs the reviewer's and owner's yes) and per PR 80 review P2/P3; owner Q4 (a) for the notice

## 2026-11-01 — M4-core plan owner answers (Q1 (a), Q2 (a)); M4c-1: `skiw_migrator` SET-only membership in the two NOLOGIN guard roles
- **Owner decisions (2026-11-01, in the reviewer chat after PR 82 review Round 2; recorded by the reviewer in `.agent/reviews/PR-82.md` "Owner decisions"; the owner's words: "1a 2a"):** **Q1 = (a):** `skiw_migrator` gets `INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE` membership in `skiw_tenant_guard` and `skiw_auth_lookup`, and migrations use `as_owner()` / `owner_role()` (built in M4c-2a, used in M4c-3). **Q2 = (a):** the `migrate` step seeds the one deployment agency from host config, only into an empty table, and refuses a changed id (built in M4c-5). Both match the builder's picks in plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §8. The review file also notes that the owner gave no separate "yes" to the whole plan in that message (plan §0 asks for one before M4c-1); this entry records only what was said.
- **Decision (M4c-1):** (1) `infra/host/db/roles.sql` adds, after the role attributes and before the drift guard, `GRANT skiw_tenant_guard TO skiw_migrator WITH INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE;` and the same for `skiw_auth_lookup`, one role per GRANT (the shape `check-hardening` reads, `hardening.py` `expected_roles`). (2) **Drift guard (the DO block):** the allow-list is now the full tuple (member, role, admin, inherit, set): `skiw_backup` in `pg_read_all_data` (f, t, t) and `skiw_migrator` in each guard role (f, f, t). It now also covers memberships *in* one of the six roles, not only memberships *of* them, so `GRANT skiw_auth_lookup TO pg_monitor` (or to any role) stops the run. Any other row aborts the whole transaction with the old message `unexpected role membership, nothing changed: <member> in <role>`. (3) **`check-hardening`:** no code change; it derives the two new pairs and their options (f, f, t) from `roles.sql`. The PG17 fixture `infra/host/tests/fixtures/roles-pg17.txt` was re-captured with `ROLES_QUERY` from a fresh local database: the only difference is the two new `member|skiw_migrator|…|f|f|t` rows (OIDs unchanged).
- **What the membership gives and does not give:** INHERIT FALSE means the migrator uses none of the guard roles' privileges implicitly; SET TRUE means it can act as either role only through an explicit `SET ROLE` / `SET LOCAL ROLE` (PostgreSQL 16+ `GRANT` options). ADMIN is false, so it cannot grant either role on. BYPASSRLS is a role attribute, never inherited: it applies only after `SET ROLE skiw_auth_lookup`. The cost, as plan Q1 states it: the migrator can act as `skiw_auth_lookup` (BYPASSRLS) without a DDL trace, and `pg_dump --role=skiw_auth_lookup` run as the migrator would succeed; G-S1's plain migrator dump still fails. The limit of the cost: the migrator already owns every table and can `NO FORCE` or `DISABLE` RLS by DDL, so no data becomes reachable that was not reachable before. `skiw_app` is unchanged (member of no role, no BYPASSRLS; TEN-02).
- **PostgreSQL 17.11 behaviour verified on the local pinned image (2026-11-01, port 5434):** as a real `skiw_migrator` login before the change, `SET ROLE skiw_auth_lookup` fails with `permission denied to set role "skiw_auth_lookup"` (and the same for `skiw_tenant_guard`). Re-running an identical GRANT prints a NOTICE (`… has already been granted membership …`) and leaves the row untouched (same xmin). Re-running the GRANT after a planted `WITH INHERIT TRUE` (same grantor `postgres`) updates that row in place to INHERIT FALSE, so the drift guard never sees it: **INHERIT/SET drift on the migrator's own grant is repaired, not reported**. A planted `WITH ADMIN OPTION` is not reset by the GRANT, so the drift guard stops the run. A second grant of a pair needs a grantor with ADMIN OPTION on the role (`GRANTED BY`), and that grantor's own membership is refused by the guard, so the guard does not count duplicates (a counting clause was written, survived its mutation, and was removed as unreachable). `pg_has_role('skiw_migrator', <guard role>, 'USAGE')` is false and `'SET'` is true. The postgres image runs `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` only when `PGDATA/PG_VERSION` is absent (`/usr/local/bin/docker-entrypoint.sh` lines 246, 347–363), so an existing local volume keeps its old roles.
- **Tests:** `app/tests/test_live_role_set.py` (live): the migrator's memberships are exactly the two guard roles with admin f, inherit f, set t, `pg_has_role` USAGE f and SET t (hard-coded, not read from `roles.sql`); the only member of either guard role is the migrator; and a FORCE-RLS probe in one rolled-back transaction as the migrator: no table has FORCE RLS before M4c-5, so the test makes one, inserts a row, forces RLS and sees 0 rows; after granting SELECT to `skiw_auth_lookup` and revoking its own, a plain SELECT is refused (no inherited grant), and after `SET LOCAL ROLE skiw_auth_lookup` it sees the 1 row (the positive control), and `SET LOCAL ROLE skiw_app` is refused. The existing tests already pin BYPASSRLS to `postgres`, `skiw_auth_lookup`, `skiw_backup` and `skiw_app` in no role. `infra/host/tests/test_roles_live.py` (host-test-live, a throwaway container per test): A7, a second run leaves every value the script sets or checks unchanged (role attributes, memberships with grantor and options, role settings, database owner and ACL, schema owner and ACL); a planted INHERIT TRUE, SET FALSE on the migrator's grant is reset by a run; planted memberships stop the run and change nothing: the three earlier cases, a guard role granted to `pg_monitor`, a third guard membership (`skiw_readonly`), ADMIN OPTION on the migrator's grant, and a second grant from another grantor. `infra/host/tests/test_check_hardening.py`: the expected memberships read from `roles.sql`; role-set fails on a third membership, on the migrator's grant with inherit t, admin t or set f, and when the membership is missing.
- **Host step (UNI-16):** `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` "Database roles": once per existing host, `apply` (installs this `roles.sql`) then `bootstrap-db apply`, before the first M4c-2a deploy. Without it the migration fails with `permission denied to set role`, `skiw-deploy` reports ``failed: `migrate` exited <n>``, the old digest keeps serving and the deploy exits 1 (`deploy.py` docstring, lines 561, 636, 658). Between the two steps `check-hardening role-set` fails with `skiw_migrator is not a member of …`, which the monthly `skiw-hardening.timer` run would alert. Undoing this needs a reviewed `roles.sql` change and a manual `REVOKE … FROM skiw_migrator` first, because an older `roles.sql` stops on these memberships (plan §6: a code rollback does not undo it). `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`: existing volumes need `make clean` once.
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-01, port 5434 and throwaway containers; each edit to the staged `roles.sql`, run against `make host-test`, the `test_roles_live.py` tests and, on a fresh volume (`docker compose down -v`, `up --wait db`), `test_live_role_set.py` and `test_live_db_roles.py`; restored from the index with an empty `git diff` checked):** 11 mutations, all killed. Each GRANT-only mutation also changes what `check-hardening` expects, so `make host-test` fails on the fixture (`test_expected_roles_are_read_from_roles_sql`, the membership cases; 73–74 tests). *Drop the `skiw_tenant_guard` GRANT:* roles_live 8 (A7 and the planted cases' final member list), app live 2 (exact memberships, the guard-role member list). *INHERIT TRUE on the `skiw_auth_lookup` GRANT:* the drift guard stops the run: roles_live 9, and the fresh local volume fails to initialise. *INHERIT TRUE in the GRANT and the allow-list together:* roles_live 2 (A7 options, the reset test), app live 2 (exact options and `pg_has_role`, and the FORCE-RLS probe: the inherited SELECT succeeds where it must be refused). *SET FALSE on the `skiw_tenant_guard` GRANT:* guard stops (roles_live 9, volume fails). *SET FALSE in both:* roles_live 1, app live 1 (exact options). *A third membership (`skiw_auth_lookup` to `skiw_readonly`):* guard stops (roles_live 9, volume fails); *in both:* roles_live 8, app live 1 (the guard-role member list). *ADMIN TRUE on the `skiw_auth_lookup` GRANT:* guard stops (roles_live 9, volume fails); *in both:* roles_live 3, app live 1 (exact options). *Drift guard checks only the member side:* roles_live 1 (`pg_monitor in skiw_auth_lookup`). *Drift guard compares pairs, not options:* roles_live 1 (ADMIN OPTION case). A twelfth mutation, a duplicate-grant count in the guard, survived and the clause was removed (above). Note: the FORCE-RLS probe is killed only by INHERIT TRUE; BYPASSRLS is never inherited, so its "0 rows without SET ROLE" half guards `skiw_migrator`'s own NOBYPASSRLS, which `test_bypassrls_only_on_postgres_auth_lookup_and_backup` also pins.
- **Evidence:** 2026-11-01, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop, on the final staged `roles.sql` and tests (the last docstring edits came after the `make` runs below and were re-checked with ruff and `make test-live`). `make lint`: ruff pass; 10 contracts kept (the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before); Semgrep 10 rules on 100 files, 0 findings; `--test` 10/10. `make typecheck`: 136 files; `tsc` pass. `make test`: 1423 passed; vitest 4. `make host-test`: 837 passed. `make audit-python`: no known vulnerabilities. Fresh volume (`docker compose down -v`, `up --wait db`): `10-roles.sh` applied `roles.sql` cleanly, then `make test-live`: 148 passed (`migrate`, `makemigrations --check`, `lintmigrations` clean). Second run on the initialised database (the `bootstrap-db apply` roles step: `roles.sql` through psql as `postgres` in the db container): exit 0, three "already been granted" NOTICEs, and a snapshot of 34 catalog rows (role attributes and settings, memberships with grantor and options, database and schema owner and ACL) identical before and after. `make host-test-live`: 38 passed. `make e2e`: 38 passed. CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`): 226 rules, 151 files, 0 findings. The tool targets ran with `DATABASE_URL` on the closed port 127.0.0.1:1; `test-live`, `e2e`, the probes and the mutation runs used this worktree's Compose database on 127.0.0.1:5434; `host-test-live` used throwaway containers with no network or Docker-chosen loopback ports. There is no separate `check-hardening` or role-set make target; its tests are in `make host-test`.
- **Not proven here:** that `SET LOCAL ROLE` plus `ALTER … OWNER TO` and `CREATE OR REPLACE` work for the migrator under these options on real `guard` objects (plan A2; M4c-2a and M4c-2b prove it); the host step on a real VPS (no host runs M4c code yet); the `pg_dump --role=skiw_auth_lookup` cost (read from the PostgreSQL docs, not run); that `check-hardening` passes on a host after `bootstrap-db apply` (unit-tested against the re-captured fixture only); that same-grantor INHERIT drift ever reaches the guard (it is repaired before the check, by design).
- **Why:** owner Q1 (a); plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-1, §4, §6, §7 risk 1, A7; M3 plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` Risk 1 and §9 item 14; spec D4, §8.4 H6 (role-set test), §4.2 TEN-02 row; TEN-01, TEN-02 (hard); UNI-16.
- **Spec change still owed (plan §9 item 2; this PR edits no spec):** D4, the H6 role-set test and the TEN-02 row should name this membership, and `guard.tenant_mac_key` / `guard.lookup_decoy_key` become readable by "the owner, and the migrator acting as the owner through `SET ROLE`" once M4c-2a creates them.
- **Alternatives rejected:** Q1 (b), a `postgres` socket step on every deploy (plan §8 Q1); INHERIT TRUE (implicit use of the guard roles' grants by every migration); a GRANT per role pair inside the DO block (the parser reads plain GRANT lines); counting duplicate grants in the guard (unreachable, above).
- **Scope / affects:** `infra/host/db/roles.sql`, `infra/host/tests/fixtures/roles-pg17.txt`, tests (`app/tests/test_live_role_set.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_roles_live.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_check_hardening.py`), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`. Hosts: one `bootstrap-db apply` each, before M4c-2a.
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11 pinned image).
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (Q1 (a), Q2 (a)); builder (deep tier), within plan M4c-1 (the wider drift-guard scope and the full-tuple allow-list are builder additions [A] for the reviewer)

## 2026-11-01 — M4c-2a: `tenancy` app and bottom layer, `guard` schema, `pgcrypto`, `as_owner()`, the two key tables; PR 83 P3 (drift guard before the migrator GRANTs)
- **Spec change owed (plan §9 item 2; this PR edits no spec):** from this PR on, `guard.tenant_mac_key` and `guard.lookup_decoy_key` exist, so spec §4.1 and §4.2 "Tenant binding" item 1 ("only the NOLOGIN owner … can read") should read "only the owner, and the migrator acting as the owner through `SET ROLE`". The change is listed in plan §9 and goes with the M3 spec revision (coordinator).
- **Decision:** (1) **`app/tenancy/`** is in `INSTALLED_APPS` and is the bottom import layer: the layers contract is `["config", "health | shell | jobs | documents | accounts", "tenancy"]` (renamed "config sits above the apps, tenancy below them"). `tenancy` joins `root_packages`, the no-config-internals, no-Procrastinate, no-host-tooling, `documents.storage` and session-store source lists, and the host-tooling forbidden list. It stays out of the independence contract and the psycopg forbid. (2) **`tenancy.ddl.as_owner(role, statements, reverse)`** returns one `RunSQL` whose forward and reverse are each `GRANT CREATE ON SCHEMA guard TO <role>`, `SET LOCAL ROLE <role>`, the statements (one per item), `RESET ROLE`, `REVOKE CREATE …`. `role` must be in `GUARD_OWNERS` = {`skiw_tenant_guard`, `skiw_auth_lookup`}, otherwise `ValueError`. Atomic migrations only: `SET LOCAL` has no effect outside a transaction. (3) **`tenancy/0001_guard`:** `CREATE SCHEMA guard` (owner `skiw_migrator`), `GRANT USAGE … TO skiw_tenant_guard, skiw_auth_lookup`, `CREATE EXTENSION pgcrypto WITH SCHEMA guard`. **`tenancy/0002_guard_key_tables_unlinted`:** `guard.tenant_mac_key (version int pk CHECK 1–999999, key bytea NOT NULL CHECK octet_length ≥ 32, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), retired_at timestamptz)` through `as_owner('skiw_tenant_guard', …)` and `guard.lookup_decoy_key (version, key)` with the same CHECKs through `as_owner('skiw_auth_lookup', …)`. No grant of any kind on either key table to anyone, the migrator included (`relacl` NULL). (4) `test_queue_grants.py`'s exact-match closed list gains `tenant_mac_key` and `lookup_decoy_key` (M4c-6a replaces the set).
- **A1 (pgcrypto), verified on PostgreSQL 17.11, port 5434:** `CREATE EXTENSION pgcrypto WITH SCHEMA guard` succeeds as a plain `skiw_migrator` login. The extension is owned by `skiw_migrator`; its functions in `guard` are owned by `postgres` (the bootstrap superuser owns a trusted extension's members) and keep PUBLIC EXECUTE (plan §9 item 9), which `skiw_app` cannot use without USAGE on `guard`.
- **A2 (role switching), verified:** under INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE, `SET LOCAL ROLE skiw_tenant_guard` gives `current_user` = the owner with `session_user` = `skiw_migrator`; `CREATE TABLE guard.…` as the owner, `ALTER TABLE … OWNER TO skiw_auth_lookup` as the migrator, a second `SET LOCAL ROLE` straight from one owner role to the other, and `DROP TABLE` as the owner all work. `has_table_privilege('skiw_migrator', 'guard.tenant_mac_key', 'SELECT')` is false and a plain migrator SELECT fails with `InsufficientPrivilege`. `CREATE OR REPLACE` keeping the owner is M4c-2b's proof.
- **A3 (linter), does not hold; narrow ignore (the plan's own A3 fallback, coordinator's choice):** django-migration-linter 6.0.0 lints `RunSQL.reverse_sql` too (`migration_linter.py` `lint_runsql`), and its `DROP_TABLE` check is `sql.startswith("DROP TABLE")` (`sql_analyser/base.py`), so the two reverse `DROP TABLE guard.…` statements fail `lintmigrations --warnings-as-errors` ("DROPPING table" twice). This is the M3c-1 finding again. Every other statement passes (`CREATE SCHEMA`, `GRANT`/`REVOKE`, `CREATE EXTENSION`, a one-line `CREATE TABLE` with `NOT NULL`, `SET LOCAL ROLE`/`RESET ROLE`, `DROP SCHEMA`, `DROP EXTENSION`). The linter has no per-statement ignore: its options are a project-wide check exclude, a whole-app exclude (never used, plan A3), an `IgnoreMigration()` operation (a non-Django Operation, which `migration_labels` counts as a contract migration), or a migration name. So the key tables have their own migration with a distinctive name, and `make migrate` passes `--ignore-name 0002_guard_key_tables_unlinted`. CI runs `make test-live`, which runs `make migrate`, so the Makefile is the only change. **No longer linted:** that one migration, forward and reverse. **Name matching:** `--ignore-name` matches the name in any app, so `test_the_lint_skipped_migration_name_is_unique` (no database) requires that only `tenancy` has a migration of that name, and `test_make_migrate_skips_only_that_migration` requires the Makefile to name exactly that one (no `--ignore-name-contains`). **Compensating test:** `test_the_unlinted_key_table_migration_reverses_and_reapplies_exactly` runs `migrate tenancy 0001` and `migrate` in a subprocess as the migrator: after the reverse both tables are gone and the schema ACL is what 0001 made; after the forward the snapshot (owners, ACLs, columns, defaults, constraints) equals the one before. Remove the ignore, or justify it again, if the linter gains a per-statement ignore; M3e and M3g function reverses (`DROP FUNCTION`) are not affected (the linter has no such check).
- **Deviation from plan step 3:** `skiw_app` gets **no** USAGE on `guard` in this PR (coordinator brief): nothing there is for it yet, and without USAGE the PUBLIC EXECUTE on the pgcrypto functions is unreachable. **M4c-4a must add `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA guard TO skiw_app`** with `guard.current_agency()`, which every policy, `with_agency()` and `/readyz` call by name.
- **PR 83 P3 (deferred to this PR):** `infra/host/db/roles.sql` now runs the two `GRANT … TO skiw_migrator WITH INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE` statements after the drift guard, just before `COMMIT`. A planted INHERIT TRUE or SET FALSE on the migrator's own grant now stops the run (psql exit 3, `unexpected role membership, nothing changed: skiw_migrator in skiw_auth_lookup`; `bootstrap-db apply` exit 1) and the row stays as planted. This replaces the M4c-1 entry's "INHERIT/SET drift on the migrator's own grant is repaired, not reported" (that text stays as history). A host without the memberships still gets both on its next run. `check-hardening` reads the GRANT lines and is unchanged (`make host-test` 837 passed). Runbook `host-rebuild.md` "Database roles" is updated. Reviewer's steps 2 and 4, re-run live by `infra/host/tests/test_roles_live.py` on throwaway PostgreSQL 17.11 containers: `test_changed_inherit_or_set_on_the_migrators_grant_stops_the_run` (exit 3, message, catalog unchanged, row `f|t|f`) and `test_a_host_without_the_migrators_memberships_gets_them` (REVOKE both, run, exit 0, both `f|f|t`).
- **Split (size rule, plan §1):** with Semgrep `tenancy-boundaries` the PR came to about 550 added lines of code, tests and config, past the ≈ 450 split line. The rule file and fixture (≈ 120 lines) are split off into a follow-up PR (patch kept untracked in this worktree at `.agent/split/m4c-2a-2-semgrep-tenancy-boundaries.patch`, `git apply --check` clean). They must land before M4c-3, whose `tenancy/keys.py` is the first app code to name a key table in SQL; three of its four rules guard files that come later (`binding.py` M4c-4b, `async_binding.py` M4c-8). The patch keeps the plan text: `guard.*_key` SQL allowed only in `tenancy/keys.py`, `tenancy/migrations/`, `accounts/migrations/` (on purpose, M3e/M3g, with a comment) and the tenancy and accounts tests; `set_config('app.tenant_token'` only in `tenancy/binding.py` (and `async_binding.py`); `AsyncConnectionPool` only in `async_binding.py`; `set_config(…, false)` and plain `SET app.` nowhere; `auth-tables-accounts-only` unchanged.
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-01; migration mutations on port 5434: reverse 0002 with the original, apply the mutant, migrate, run the tenancy, queue-grant and DB-role tests, reverse, restore from the index, migrate, `git diff` clean):** (A) `as_owner` without `RESET ROLE`: `migrate` exits 1, 23 tests fail (exact statement lists, owners, privileges, CHECKs, reverse test, closed list). (B) a third role (`skiw_migrator`) in `GUARD_OWNERS`: 2 (exact owner set, refusal case). (C) a standing `GRANT SELECT ON guard.tenant_mac_key TO skiw_migrator`: 3 (no grant on a key table, no privilege for the migrator, read only after SET ROLE). (D) `lookup_decoy_key` created as the migrator (plain `RunSQL`): 8 (owners, migrator privileges, …). (E) USAGE on `guard` to `skiw_app`: 2 (exact schema ACL, no USAGE). (F) the key length CHECK dropped: 2 (both 31-byte cases). (G) `roles.sql` GRANT order reverted (throwaway containers): 1 (`test_changed_inherit_or_set_on_the_migrators_grant_stops_the_run`). *Semgrep (on the split-off rule, `semgrep --test`):* the `^.*?` anchor removed (guard key: 3 lines missed; token: every line missed); `select`/`from` removed from the verb list (3 missed); quoted identifiers dropped (1); `session` dropped (1); the pool rule narrowed to calls (1); the `'f'`/`'false'`/`0` forms dropped first survived, then fixture lines were added and it was killed (3 missed). **Survivors:** widening the path exclude to `/app/` and removing the `accounts/migrations` exclude: the fixture lives outside `app/`, so `--test` cannot see path filters (plan M4c-2a: M3e's real migration proves the allowance); the follow-up PR should add a test that pins the exclude list.
- **Evidence (2026-11-01, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop, on the final staged tree):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before; Semgrep 10 rules on 108 files, 0 findings; `--test` 10/10), `make typecheck` (144 files; `tsc` pass), `make test` (1448 passed; vitest 4), `make host-test` (837 passed), `make audit-python` (no known vulnerabilities), fresh volume (`docker compose down -v`) then `make test-live` (173 passed; `migrate`, `makemigrations --check` clean, `lintmigrations` 4 valid, 0 erroneous, 42 ignored), `make host-test-live` (39 passed), `make e2e` (38 passed), CI registry Semgrep (`p/python p/django p/typescript p/react`, 226 rules, 159 files, 0 findings). Tool targets ran with `DATABASE_URL` on the closed port 127.0.0.1:1; live targets, probes and mutations used this worktree's Compose database on 127.0.0.1:5434; `host-test-live` used throwaway containers.
- **Not proven here:** that `CREATE OR REPLACE` through `as_owner` keeps the owner, and a definer function owned by a guard role (M4c-2b); that `as_owner` misbehaves loudly in a non-atomic migration (it would run as the migrator, and only the owner tests would catch it); the `accounts/migrations` Semgrep allowance (split off, then M3e); the host step on a real VPS; that the migrator's `SET ROLE` reads of the key tables are only ever issued from `tenancy.ddl` (a review and Semgrep matter from M4c-3).
- **Why:** plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §1, §3 M4c-2a, §4, §5, A1–A3; PR 82 review (no migrator grant, Semgrep notes); PR 83 review P3; spec D4, §4.1, §4.2; TEN-01, TEN-02 (hard), TEN-10; UNI-04, UNI-15, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `--exclude-apps tenancy` (plan A3); `--exclude-migration-tests DROP_TABLE` (project-wide); `IgnoreMigration()` (a contract migration to `migration_labels`); no-op reverses with `DROP SCHEMA guard CASCADE` (hides the drops from the linter instead of saying so); USAGE for `skiw_app` now (no object needs it).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/` (new: `ddl.py`, `migrations/0001_guard.py`, `migrations/0002_guard_key_tables_unlinted.py`, tests `test_ddl.py`, `test_guard_schema_live.py`), `app/config/settings.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `infra/host/db/roles.sql`, tests (`app/documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_roles_live.py`), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`. Hosts: `bootstrap-db apply` before the first M4c-2a deploy (M4c-1 runbook step, unchanged).
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11, django-migration-linter 6.0.0, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** yes, one: the `lintmigrations` ignore for one migration, with the tests above in its place.
- **Decided by:** coordinator (A3 narrow ignore, no USAGE for `skiw_app`, the spec-change route); builder (deep tier), within plan M4c-2a (the split, per plan §1)

## 2026-11-02 — M4c-2a-2: Semgrep `tenancy-boundaries`, a test that pins its path excludes; corrections to the M4c-2a entry (PR 84 review P3s)
- **Decision:** (1) **`semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.yml`** (4 rules, fixture `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.py`), split off M4c-2a by the size rule (plan §1). `tenancy-guard-key-sql`: SQL that names `guard.*_key` is allowed only in `tenancy/keys.py`, `tenancy/migrations/`, `accounts/migrations/` (on purpose: M3e's `guard.auth_lookup_login` and M3g's `guard.auth_lookup_credential` read `guard.lookup_decoy_key`) and the tenancy and accounts tests. `tenancy-token-setting`: `set_config('app.tenant_token'` and `SET LOCAL app.tenant_token` only in `tenancy/binding.py` and `tenancy/async_binding.py`. `tenancy-async-pool`: `AsyncConnectionPool` only in `tenancy/async_binding.py`. `tenancy-no-session-setting`: plain `SET app.` nowhere, and any `set_config(` whose last argument is not the literal `true` nowhere (R1 fix, below). `auth-tables-accounts-only` is unchanged. The patch is the one the M4c-2a entry describes; it applied clean to `main` at `c0a762f`. (2) **`app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`** reads the rule file as text and pins each rule's `paths.exclude` list to the plan's table. `semgrep --test` cannot do this, because its fixtures sit outside `app/`. The reader fails on any line under a rule's `paths:` that it does not know (an `include:` key, a flow list `exclude: [/app/]`). **The plan table omits `tenancy/async_binding.py` for the token rule; the rule and the test allow it, because spec line 380 (TEN-09 row) says both `with_agency()` and `async_with_agency()` set the token.** (3) **Corrections to the "M4c-2a" entry** (PR 84 review, P3 item 2): the patch path there, `.agent/split/…`, was wrong; the file was at `.agent/auto/m4c-2a-2-semgrep-tenancy-boundaries.patch` and is now in the repo, so the path is gone. The exception it records names **rule UNI-15 (the migration linter, spec D16) with compensating control: the 0002 migration is the only `--ignore-name` in the Makefile, tests pin that name as unique, and a live test reverses and re-applies it and compares a catalog snapshot; review date: 2026-12-31 [A], or when django-migration-linter ships a fix for `DROP TABLE` in `RunSQL.reverse_sql`, whichever comes first.**
- **Review R1 fixes (PR 85 review, Blocked):** (a) PR body got the self-check table and the model line (the block reason). (b) **P2:** the first `tenancy-no-session-setting` regex missed `set_config(…, guard.mint_token(%s), false)` (a nested call in the value) and `'off'`, and both give a session-level setting on PostgreSQL 17 (reviewer's probe). The rule now flags every `set_config(` in a string unless every call in it ends in `, true)`; the regex uses PCRE recursion `(?1)` for balanced brackets, so a nested call cannot hide the last argument. It also flags `%s`, `'off'`, `'no'`, `'0'`, `False` as a parameter, and `set_config('a', f(x, true), false)`. Fixture gained 8 `ruleid` and 2 `ok` lines. (c) **P3:** the path reader is strict (above), with 3 new tests. A later automated security note found the reader still skipped a `paths` key in another spelling (`paths: {exclude: [/app/]}`, a quoted key, trailing text); it now fails unless the key is exactly `    paths:`, with 6 more tests. (d) **P3:** the plan-table note (above). Not done: the reviewer's optional flag for the bare literal `"app.tenant_token"` (it would hit tests and reads; the M4c-4b runtime test that no `app.*` setting survives `COMMIT` is the real control). Residual: a bind parameter used as the setting name or value (`set_config(%s, %s, true)`) is out of reach for a string regex.
- **Evidence (2026-11-02, macOS arm64):** `make lint` (10 contracts kept; Semgrep 14 rules on 109 files, 0 findings; `semgrep --test` 14/14), `make typecheck` (pass), `make test` (1452 passed; vitest 4). Mutations on the rule file, each killed by the new test: `/app/tenancy/keys.py` widened to `/app/` (2 fail); `/app/accounts/migrations/` removed from the key-SQL rule (2 fail); `/app/accounts/migrations/` put in the token rule (2 fail); `/app/jobs/` added to the pool rule (1 fails). Earlier Semgrep mutations (anchor, verb list, quoted identifiers, `session`, pool call-only, `'f'`/`'false'`/`0`) are recorded in the M4c-2a entry.
- **Not proven here:** that Semgrep applies the excludes to real paths in CI. `--test` cannot show it, and the text test pins the list, not the behaviour. M3e's real `accounts` migration passing `make lint` shows the `accounts/migrations` allowance; M4c-3's `tenancy/keys.py` shows the `keys.py` one.
- **Still owed from the PR 84 review:** the spec change for plan §9 item 2 has no PR yet (name it before M4c-3 merges); the `as_owner` non-atomic `current_user` check goes to M4c-2b.
- **Why:** plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-2a (Semgrep table), §1 size rule; PR 84 review P3s and notes; spec §4.2 "Tenant binding", TEN-06, TEN-09, TEN-10, UNI-15.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `semgrep --test` fixture under `app/` for the path check (Semgrep tests need a fixture beside each rule file); a YAML library for the test (a new dependency for four lists, UNI-06); running Semgrep from pytest (slow, needs `uvx`, not available in the unit test run).
- **Scope / affects:** `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.yml`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.py`, `app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder, within plan M4c-2a (split) and the PR 84 review's disposition; the review date 2026-12-31 is a builder value [A].

## 2026-11-02 — M4c-2b: `security_event` INSERT for `skiw_auth_lookup`, an exact auth-table ACL audit, the `as_owner` `current_user` check, a definer probe through `as_owner` (PR 84 review P3 #1)
- **Decision:** (1) **`accounts/0003_security_event_auth_lookup_insert`** is one `RunSQL`: `GRANT INSERT ON TABLE security_event TO skiw_auth_lookup`, reverse `REVOKE INSERT ON TABLE security_event FROM skiw_auth_lookup`. It is INSERT only (TEN-03), and it lives in `app/accounts/migrations/` because Semgrep `auth-tables-accounts-only` flags the grant anywhere else. `make migrate` lints it with no new `--ignore-name`. (2) **Grant audit, now closed over every grantee:** the M3c-1 test `test_app_role_acl_entries_on_the_auth_tables_are_exactly_the_plan_list` filtered on `grantee = skiw_app`, so a grant to any other role passed it. The M3c-1 entry's "`skiw_app` is the only grantee here, and the ACL test pins that" overstated it. The test is renamed `test_acl_entries_on_the_auth_tables_are_exactly_the_plan_list` (`app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`). It checks that `skiw_migrator` owns both auth tables, and that every non-owner `aclexplode` entry, PUBLIC included, is exactly `auth_throttle` → skiw_app {SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE}, `security_event` → skiw_app {SELECT, INSERT}, `security_event` → skiw_auth_lookup {INSERT}. The owner is left out on purpose: it holds every privilege, MAINTAIN included on PG17, so "nobody has UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE" means nobody but the owner. New `test_lookup_role_effective_table_privileges` (`has_table_privilege` over all seven table privileges: {INSERT} on `security_event`, nothing on `auth_throttle`). The TEN-03 append-only test is parametrised over both writers and renamed `test_evidence_tables_that_exist_are_append_only_for_the_writers`. (3) **`as_owner` `current_user` check (PR 84 review P3 #1, option (a)):** right after `SET LOCAL ROLE <role>`, forward and reverse both run `DO $$BEGIN IF current_user <> '<role>' THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'as_owner: current_user is not <role>; use an atomic migration'; END IF; END$$`. It is one line with no `%`. `test_ddl.py` pins the exact 6-statement lists and has a new `test_no_statement_holds_a_percent_sign_or_a_line_break`. **What a non-atomic run leaves behind:** the `GRANT CREATE ON SCHEMA guard TO <role>` before the check has already committed, so the role keeps CREATE on `guard`. No object is made. `test_guard_acl_is_exactly_migrator_usage_create_and_owner_usage` then fails, and re-running the migration atomically ends with the `REVOKE` and clears it. The order is unchanged: the role cannot grant CREATE on the migrator's schema, and SQL has no reliable test for "inside a transaction block" (the `now()` vs `statement_timestamp()` comparison depends on how the driver sends `BEGIN`). (4) **Definer probe** (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_definer_live.py`; it sits in `accounts` because its SQL names `security_event`). `as_owner('skiw_auth_lookup', …)` creates `guard.probe_definer()`: plpgsql, SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, fully qualified names, no arguments. It inserts one event with `current_user` and `session_user` in `details_json`. The same statements run `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM PUBLIC` and `GRANT EXECUTE … TO skiw_app`. The test checks owner `skiw_auth_lookup`, `prosecdef`, `proconfig` and the exact `proacl`. It then checks that `skiw_app` gets InsufficientPrivilege on schema `guard` (the negative control), and that after a USAGE grant `skiw_app`'s call writes exactly one row with `current_user` = skiw_auth_lookup and `session_user` = skiw_app. `CREATE OR REPLACE` through `as_owner` with a new body (new `kind`) leaves owner, `prosecdef`, `proconfig` and `proacl` unchanged, and the next call runs the new body (**A2, replace keeps the owner: holds**). The reverse drops it as its owner. **Finding for M3e:** the definer cannot use `INSERT … RETURNING` on `security_event`, because RETURNING needs SELECT and the owner has INSERT only. The probe makes its id in plpgsql and returns it.
- **Deviation from the brief (USAGE for the call):** "grant USAGE in the test's own throwaway transaction and roll it back" cannot work. The `skiw_app` login sees only committed objects and grants, and the migrator cannot `SET ROLE skiw_app` (it is not a member, and has no CREATEROLE). So the probe commits `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA guard TO skiw_app` just before the call. A fixture's `finally` then revokes it, revokes CREATE from `skiw_auth_lookup`, deletes the probe rows (as the table owner), and drops the function as whatever role owns it. The fixture then asserts no function, no USAGE and no probe row are left. That holds on a failing run too: it was checked after the two probe mutants below. No migration grants USAGE; M4c-4a still must. The alternative rejected was a trigger-fired definer on a throwaway table, which needs no USAGE or EXECUTE but is not the path M3e uses (a direct call).
- **Linter (A3) for the new statements:** `lintmigrations --warnings-as-errors` without `--ignore-name` (one run, 2026-11-02) reports `0002_guard_key_tables_unlinted` ERR with exactly the two known "DROPPING table" lines and nothing else, so the `DO` line, `SET LOCAL ROLE` and `RESET ROLE` pass. With the normal `make migrate`, `accounts/0003` is linted and valid.
- **Mutation proof (2026-11-02, port 5434; migration mutants: `migrate accounts 0002`, edit, `migrate`, run the live and unit tests in `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `app/accounts/tests`, `app/tenancy/tests`, `app/tests/test_live_role_set.py`, `app/tests/test_live_db_roles.py`, `migrate accounts 0002`, restore, `migrate`; each file byte-identical after):** (A) grant UPDATE too: 3 fail (ACL exact, lookup effective `[security_event]`, append-only `[skiw_auth_lookup]`). (B) grant to `skiw_app` instead: 3 (ACL exact, lookup effective, the definer probe: its insert is refused). (C) grant DELETE too: 3 (as A). (D) the DO check removed: 5 (both `test_as_owner_outside_a_transaction_stops_before_the_first_object` cases, both exact-statement cases, the no-`%` test). (E) the DO check compares `skiw_tenant_guard` for every role: 4 (definer probe, key-table reverse test, in-transaction owner `[skiw_auth_lookup]`, exact statements `[skiw_auth_lookup]`). (F) `RESET ROLE` removed: 6 (in-transaction owner ×2, which also checks `current_user` is back to the migrator; key-table reverse test; exact statements ×2; no-`%`). That mutant's reverse-and-reapply test left `tenancy.0002` unapplied, so `make migrate` was run before the next mutant and the two after it were re-run. (G) the probe function created as the migrator without `as_owner`: 1 (the definer probe, at the owner assertion). (H) a replace that resets the owner (DROP and CREATE as the migrator, EXECUTE granted again): 1 (the definer probe, at the replace assertion). G and H first failed on the probe's own `isinstance(…, tuple)` guard; that was widened to list or tuple and both were re-run, failing at the intended assertions. No survivors.
- **Evidence (2026-11-02, macOS arm64, Docker Desktop, PostgreSQL 17.11, psycopg 3.3.6):** `make lint` (ruff clean, 10 contracts kept, the TEN-10 one "KEPT (1 warning)" as before; Semgrep 14 rules, 0 findings; `semgrep --test` 14/14). Semgrep scans only git-tracked files, so the two new files were also scanned directly (`uvx semgrep==1.179.0 scan --config semgrep/ --error` on them: 0 findings), and a copy of the probe outside `accounts/` did trip `auth-tables-accounts-only` (the control). `make typecheck` (147 files; `tsc` pass), `make test` (1462 passed; vitest 4), `make test-live` (181 passed; `migrate`, `makemigrations --check` clean, `lintmigrations` 5 valid, 0 erroneous, 42 ignored), `make host-test` (837 passed). `migrate accounts 0002` leaves `security_event` with `{skiw_migrator=arwdDxtm/skiw_migrator,skiw_app=ar/skiw_migrator}`, and `migrate` adds `skiw_auth_lookup=a/skiw_migrator`.
- **Not proven here:** a definer that reads `guard.lookup_decoy_key` (M3e); that a real non-atomic migration (`atomic = False`) behaves as the autocommit test does (the test runs the helper's statements on an autocommit connection as the migrator login, not through Django's executor); `make host-test-live`, `make e2e` and CI registry Semgrep were not run (no host, compose or web change).
- **Still owed:** the spec change for plan §9 item 2 (a later PR, before M4c-3 merges); USAGE on `guard` for `skiw_app` (M4c-4a).
- **Why:** plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-2b, A2, A3; M3 plan §0 (the `skiw_auth_lookup` INSERT requirement); PR 84 review P3 #1; spec §4.1, §4.2; TEN-01, TEN-02 (hard), TEN-03, TEN-10.
- **Alternatives rejected:** INSERT plus SELECT for the definer owner, so that RETURNING works (TEN-03 needs no read; the probe makes its own id); the grant in `tenancy` (Semgrep `auth-tables-accounts-only`); a USAGE grant to `skiw_app` in a migration now (M4c-4a); reordering `as_owner` so the check runs before the GRANT (above); a trigger-fired probe (above).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/migrations/0003_security_event_auth_lookup_insert.py` (new), `app/tenancy/ddl.py`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_definer_live.py` new; `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_ddl.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_guard_schema_live.py`), `docs/DECISIONS.md`. Runbooks unchanged: `accounts/0003` runs in the normal `migrate`, and no deploy or host step changes.
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11, django-migration-linter 6.0.0, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier), within plan M4c-2b and the PR 84 review P3 #1 disposition (option (a), the coordinator's pick); the committed-and-revoked USAGE in the probe is a builder choice, explained above.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-3a: wiring for the guard key writes (readers, `migrate` key files, generated local keys)
- **Decision:** First half of plan M4c-3, split off by the plan §1 size rule (the whole change was past ≈ 450 lines). It adds no behaviour: (1) lazy readers `SKIW_TENANT_TOKEN_KEYS` and `SKIW_LOOKUP_DECOY_KEYS` in `config.settings` (`partial(env.versioned_keys, …, max_version=999_999)`, matching the tables' version CHECK), lazy so web and worker (no `lookup_decoy_key` mount) still import settings; (2) host `migrate` gains `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY_FILE` and `LOOKUP_DECOY_KEY_FILE` (both secrets were already mounted), with a new Compose check `file_secrets`: every `*_FILE` names a secret the same service mounts, with two planted cases; (3) `make .env` appends generated `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY=v1:` and `LOOKUP_DECOY_KEY=v1:` lines (no literal value in the repo, UNI-07).
- **Order:** M4c-3b (the key writes, `tenancy.keys`) needs this merged first: without the `.env` lines `make migrate` would fail, and without the Compose variables the host `migrate` would. Runbooks (UNI-16) land with M4c-3b, because they describe its behaviour.
- **Why:** plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-3, §1; spec §4.1, §8.4 H4; UNI-07–09.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier sub-agent), within plan M4c-3.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-3b: `migrate` writes `guard.tenant_mac_key` and `guard.lookup_decoy_key` as their owners; `owner_role()`; the retired-version refusal
- **Decision:** (1) **Trigger.** `tenancy.apps.TenancyConfig.ready()` connects `tenancy.keys.write_guard_keys_after_migrate` to `post_migrate`, sender `tenancy` only. Django sends `post_migrate` only for apps with a models module (`emit_post_migrate_signal`), so `app/tenancy/models.py` exists with no models; `makemigrations --check` is unchanged. `migrate --plan` returns before the signal. (2) **`tenancy.keys.write_guard_keys()`** is one `transaction.atomic()`. It refuses unless `session_user = current_user = skiw_migrator` before any `SET ROLE`. It writes nothing (returns) when `tenancy.0002_guard_key_tables_unlinted` is not applied, which is the case after a reverse to `tenancy 0001` (`test_guard_schema_live.py`). Then `owner_role(skiw_tenant_guard)`: versions whose bytes differ from the file, and listed versions that are already retired, stop `migrate`; missing versions are inserted (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); unlisted active versions get `retired_at = now()`. Then `owner_role(skiw_auth_lookup)`: changed versions stop `migrate`, missing ones are inserted, unlisted ones are deleted. Then a check that `current_user` is back to the migrator, and commit. Compares run in SQL. Errors are `CommandError` (no traceback) and name the key variable and versions only; a database error is reported by SQLSTATE only, because its text can quote a row. (3) **No key bytes in statement text.** The writes use a server-binding `psycopg.Cursor` on Django's own connection inside its transaction. The keys are bound parameters, so they appear neither in `pg_stat_activity`/the server log statement text nor in Django's `DEBUG` query log (Django's default psycopg cursor binds client-side). (4) **`tenancy.ddl.owner_role(cursor, role)`**, a context manager with the `GUARD_OWNERS` allow-list: `SET LOCAL ROLE`, a `current_user` check (outside a transaction `SET LOCAL` does nothing, so it raises before the block), the block inside `try/finally`, `RESET ROLE`. The `finally` sends `RESET ROLE` unless the transaction is in error (then only a rollback can follow, and it undoes `SET LOCAL`), so a Python error caught inside the block cannot leave the session as the owner role (review R1 P2). (5) **Readers, Compose variables, `file_secrets` and `make .env` lines** are M4c-3a (PR 87, see its entry). Runbooks: `host-rebuild.md` (rotation: add a version, deploy, restart, retire; after a restore `migrate` rewrites both tables, because dumps leave out their data; a rollback runs no `migrate`) and `local-dev.md` (the two `.env` lines, and the line for an older `.env`).
- **Owner decision 2026-11-03 (the plan was silent; fail closed):** a retired `tenant_mac_key` version listed in the key file again stops `migrate`, whether its bytes match the stored ones or not. The retired check runs before the changed-bytes check, so such a version is always reported as retired. The error names the key and version, never the value (UNI-09): `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY: version(s) <n> retired; remove the line (a key that is safe after all goes in under a new, higher version). Nothing was written.` There is **no override flag**. The fix is to remove that line; a key that is safe after all goes back only under a new version number, never by un-retiring the old one. Why: a retired version may be a key retired on suspicion (spec line ~807: rotation "yearly and on suspicion", T16), and this matches the plan's rule that changed bytes stop `migrate` (plan line ~202). A restore leaves both tables empty (dumps carry no key-table data, spec §8.4 H6), so after a restore no retired row exists for a listed version to conflict with. The decoy table has no such case (its unlisted versions are deleted). Test: `test_a_retired_version_listed_again_stops_migrate[same_bytes=True|False]` (exit ≠ 0, both tables unchanged, no key text in the output). Runbook: `host-rebuild.md`, "`tenant_token_key` and `lookup_decoy_key`".
- **Changed M4c-2a test:** `test_key_table_checks` inserted `v1` as the owner. `migrate` now writes `v1` from the key files, so that case's insert would hit the primary key; its passing-length case uses `v999998` instead.
- **Local and CI impact:** an `.env` made before this PR has no `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY`/`LOOKUP_DECOY_KEY`, so `make migrate` fails with `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY (or TENANT_TOKEN_KEY_FILE on hosts) is required and not set` until the two lines are appended (`local-dev.md`). CI makes a fresh `.env`, so no workflow change.
- **Still owed (owner decision 2026-11-03):** the spec change for plan §9 item 2 (and item 6: `retired_at` for unlisted versions, the decoy table holds exactly the file's versions), plus the retired-version rule above, lands in spec revision v1.21, **before M4c-4a merges**. The M4c-4a review checks that v1.21 is approved and reconciled before approving M4c-4a. **Carried forward from PR 86, not part of this PR:** M4c-4a must change `app/accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_definer_live.py` (drop the negative control and the USAGE grant/revoke once a migration grants `USAGE ON SCHEMA guard` to `skiw_app`), and M3e definers cannot use `INSERT … RETURNING`. USAGE on `guard` for `skiw_app` is still M4c-4a's.
- **Why:** plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-3, §1 size rule, §4, §5, §9 item 2; spec §4.1, §4.2, §8.4 H3, H4, H6; TEN-02 (hard), TEN-10; UNI-03, UNI-07–09, UNI-16.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `RunSQL` data migration (it runs once, not on every deploy, and cannot read the host files at the right time); Django's default cursor (client-side binding puts the key bytes into the statement text); un-retiring a re-listed version (above); printing per-version write counts (the plan says nothing is logged).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/` (new `apps.py`, `keys.py`, `models.py`, `tests/test_key_writes_live.py`; `ddl.py`; `tests/test_ddl.py`, `tests/test_guard_schema_live.py`), `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`. Hosts: no new secret and no `bootstrap-secrets` change; the next forward deploy writes both tables.
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11, psycopg 3.3.6, Django 5.2).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Size (plan §1 split rule):** past the ≈ 450 line, so the builder split the work: M4c-3a (wiring, PR 87) lands first, this PR (M4c-3b, key writes and runbooks) after it. Readers, `migrate` key files and `make .env` lines are M4c-3a (see its entry).
- **Decided by:** owner (the retired-version refusal, 2026-11-03, through the coordinator); builder (deep tier), within plan M4c-3, for the rest.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-4a: `guard.current_agency()`, `tenancy.token`, the committed token vector, USAGE on `guard` for `skiw_app`
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy.token`** (`mac_input`, `mint`) implements the spec v1.21 §4.2 item 2 encoding exactly: `v<n>.<agency uuid>.<xid>.<MAC>`, MAC = HMAC-SHA-256 over four fields each preceded by a 4-byte big-endian length (`skiw-tenant-v1`, the database name as UTF-8, the 16 agency bytes, the xid as 8 bytes big-endian). It refuses a version outside 1–999999 and an xid outside 0–2^64−1. (2) **The committed vector** is in `app/tenancy/tests/test_token.py`: key `bytes(range(32))`, database `skiw`, agency `0f1e2d3c-…-a5b4c3d2e1f0`, xid 123456789, with the MAC-input hex and the token. The MAC was cross-checked once with `openssl dgst -sha256 -mac HMAC`. The verifier is checked against the same key and agency in `test_current_agency_live.py` (a live xid and database name, because the verifier requires the transaction's own xid). (3) **`guard.current_agency()`** (new migration `tenancy/0003_current_agency`, created through `as_owner('skiw_tenant_guard', …)`): plpgsql, `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, every name schema-qualified. One anchored regex parses the token; the xid must equal `pg_current_xact_id_if_assigned()` (compared as text, so an out-of-range number cannot fail a cast); the key version must exist with `retired_at IS NULL`; the MAC is recomputed with `guard.hmac(…, 'sha256')` over the same fields (`int4send`, `convert_to`, `uuid_send`, `xid8send`) and compared by double HMAC under a fresh `guard.gen_random_bytes(32)`. Any failed step returns NULL; there is no RAISE, no logging and no dynamic SQL. The last MAC character must be a canonical one, so a MAC has one spelling. `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM PUBLIC; GRANT EXECUTE … TO skiw_app`. (4) The same migration grants **`USAGE ON SCHEMA guard` to `skiw_app`** (owed since M4c-2a). With it, the pgcrypto functions in `guard` become reachable to `skiw_app` through their PUBLIC EXECUTE (plan §9 item 9: they only compute). (5) **Semgrep `tenancy-token-setting` is unchanged.** Its excludes are pinned to the plan's table (`app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`), so `test_current_agency_live.py` sets the token with `set_config(%s, %s, true)` and the setting name as a bound parameter, which the rule does not see. This is a choice for the reviewer: the other way is to add `/app/tenancy/tests/` to the rule's excludes and to the plan table (as `tenancy-guard-key-sql` has), which the M4c-4b forged-tenant tests will also need.
- **Deviation from the plan:** the plan names `tenancy/0002` for the function. 0002 had already merged (PR 82), so the function goes in a new migration, 0003. That migration passes `lintmigrations` (not in the ignore list).
- **Changed tests:** `test_guard_schema_live.py` (the `guard` ACL now includes `skiw_app` USAGE; the pgcrypto owner check is limited to extension members; the 0002 reverse test now reverses 0003 first and expects the 0001 ACL without `skiw_app`); `test_auth_lookup_definer_live.py` (the negative control and the committed USAGE grant/revoke are gone, as the M4c-2b entry said).
- **Runbook (UNI-16):** `host-rebuild.md` now warns that the two rotation steps must be separate deploys, because from now on the verifier refuses a retired version.
- **Evidence:** `make test-live` 226 passed; two verifier mutants (no `retired_at` check; no xid check) each make `test_current_agency_live.py` fail (1 and 4 failures). The SQL side reproduces the committed vector (review R1): two live tests evaluate the migration's own `MAC_INPUT` expression with literal inputs. One checks the committed bytes and MAC; the other compares with `mac_input` on a multi-byte name and xid 2^64−1. Mutants: a changed domain string fails both; LATIN1 instead of UTF-8 for the database name fails the multi-byte test.
- **Deferred to M4c-4b (review R1 P3):** allow `/app/tenancy/tests/` in `tenancy-token-setting` and the plan table and use the literal setting name; flag `set_config` with a non-literal first argument outside `tenancy.binding`; limit imports of `tenancy.token.mint` to the binding modules and tests.
- **Not in this PR:** `with_agency()`, start-up key checks and the remaining TEN-09 forged-tenant cases under the helper (M4c-4b).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2 items 2–3, TEN-06, TEN-09, TEN-12; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-4a; M4c-2a entry (USAGE owed); PR 88 review R2 follow-up (the verifier refuses absent or retired versions; the committed vector).
- **Alternatives rejected:** editing the merged 0002; an `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` block (it would hide bugs and start a subtransaction per call; every step is written so it cannot raise); comparing the MAC with `=` (timing); casting the token's xid to `xid8` (can raise).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/token.py` (new), `app/tenancy/migrations/0003_current_agency.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_token.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_current_agency_live.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_guard_schema_live.py`, `app/accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_definer_live.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11, pgcrypto as installed in M4c-2a, psycopg 3.3.6).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-4a and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-4b-1: the Semgrep TEN-09 rules (PR 91 review R1 P3, PR 85 review R1 P3)
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy-token-setting`** now also allows `/app/tenancy/tests/`, which set hand-made and forged tokens on purpose (TEN-09 forged-tenant tests); `test_current_agency_live.py` names `app.tenant_token` as a literal again (it used a bound name in M4c-4a, item 5 of that entry). (2) **New `tenancy-set-config-name`**, no excludes: a `set_config(` whose first argument is not a quoted literal (`'[a-z0-9_.]+'`) is flagged everywhere, because a bound, concatenated or computed name hides `app.tenant_token` from rule (1). (3) **New `tenancy-token-mint-import`**: importing `tenancy.token` (all `import` / `from` forms, relative ones, `importlib.import_module`, `__import__`) is allowed only in `tenancy/binding.py`, `tenancy/async_binding.py` and `tenancy/tests/`. The key is in settings, so any module holding `mint()` could make a token for any agency outside `with_agency()`. Semgrep rather than import-linter, because the rule must also cover modules inside `tenancy` (`keys.py`, `ddl.py`), which a layers or forbidden contract over app packages does not. (4) **`tenancy-no-session-setting`** gains the first, linear regex as a second branch (`pattern-either`; PR 85 review R1 P3, option (a)). The balanced-bracket branch hits the engine's backtrack limit on strings over about 10,000 characters and then reports no match and no error; the linear branch flags every flat `false` / `'f'` / `'false'` / `0` and plain `SET [SESSION] app.` at any length. (5) The plan's M4c-2a Semgrep table gains the two rows and the tests path; `app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py` pins all six rules' excludes.
- **Evidence:** `semgrep --test` 16/16 (new fixture lines: a 13,000-character string ending in `set_config('app.x', %s, false)`, three bound or computed names, eight import forms and two allowed imports). With branch B removed, the long-string lines are missed (lines 94 and 96 of the fixture). `make lint`: 16 rules on `app`, 0 findings.
- **Not in this PR:** `with_agency()` and its tests (M4c-4b-2, stacked on this branch).
- **Why:** PR 91 review R1 P3 (decision: keep M4c-4a as merged, take the other option in M4c-4b); PR 85 review R1 P3 (deferred to M4c-4b, the PR that owns the TEN-09 Semgrep rules); plan M4c-4b "Semgrep TEN-09 rules"; spec TEN-09 ("Plain `SET`/`set_config(…, false)` stay banned by Semgrep as hygiene; the boundary is the token MAC, not the lint").
- **Alternatives rejected:** possessive or atomic groups in branch A (PR 85 option (b): harder to read and to prove complete); an import-linter contract for `tenancy.token` (does not reach modules inside `tenancy`); a broad `tenancy\.token` text regex (flags comments and docstrings that name the module).
- **Size (plan §1 split rule):** M4c-4b came to ≈ 505 lines, past the ≈ 450 line, so the builder split it: this PR (rules) first, M4c-4b-2 (`with_agency()`) stacked on it.
- **Scope / affects:** `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.yml`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.py`, `app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_current_agency_live.py`, `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` (§3 M4c-2a Semgrep table), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Semgrep as pinned in the `Makefile`).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner through the reviewer (PR 91 R1 P3 option); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-4b, for the rest.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-4b-2: `with_agency()`, the tenant_token_key start-up checks, the function-level forged-tenant tests
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy.binding.with_agency(agency_id)`** (sync context manager). It refuses a non-`uuid.UUID` argument (`TypeError`) and an open atomic block (`TransactionManagementError`), which also refuses nesting: a second token on the same xid could name another agency. Then it reads the newest key, opens `transaction.atomic()`, runs `SELECT pg_current_xact_id()::text, current_database()`, mints with `tenancy.token.mint` and runs `SELECT set_config('app.tenant_token', %s, true)`. A nested `atomic()` inside keeps the xid (a savepoint), so the binding holds for the whole block. (2) **The token is never logged.** The `set_config` runs on the driver cursor (`cursor.cursor`), not Django's wrapper, because the debug wrapper (`DEBUG`, or `force_debug_cursor`) logs each query with its parameters. (3) **`token_keys()`** reads `SKIW_TENANT_TOKEN_KEYS()` once per process, newest first (`functools.cache`, as `accounts.source_key.require_key`). Rotation restarts `web` and `worker`, so the cache never holds a version `migrate` has not written. `with_agency()` mints under `token_keys()[0]`, the newest (PR 91 review follow-up; the runbook's separate-deploys rule depends on it). (4) **Start-up:** `config/asgi.py` and `runworker` call `token_keys()` next to the existing key checks, before the migration check. A missing or malformed key stops the process; the message names the key, never the value. (5) **Compose:** `x-app-env` gains `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY_FILE` (the secret was already mounted on `web` and `worker`); local `docker-compose.yml` passes `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY` to `app` and `worker`.
