(6) **Semgrep `tenancy-token-key-reader`** (PR 92 review R1 P3): `settings.SKIW_TENANT_TOKEN_KEYS` (under any alias) and the string `"SKIW_TENANT_TOKEN_KEYS"` (as in `getattr`, or `keys.py`'s `KeyTable.setting`) are allowed only in `config/settings.py`, `tenancy/keys.py`, `tenancy/binding.py`, `tenancy/async_binding.py` and `tenancy/tests/`. With the key, any module could mint a token for any agency, import rule or not. Comments and docstrings are not flagged. Its exclude list is pinned in `test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`. `tenancy-token-mint-import` also catches `importlib.import_module(".token", …)` and the two-argument `"tenancy.token"` form (the R1 optional item). Mutants: without the attribute pattern 2 fixture lines are missed, without the string pattern 2, without the `.token` pattern 2.
- **Deviation from the plan:** the plan has `asgi.py` and `runworker` call `SKIW_TENANT_TOKEN_KEYS()`. They call `tenancy.binding.token_keys()`, which wraps it and caches the result that `with_agency()` uses, so the start-up check is the same read that minting relies on. "Denied" in the forged-tenant tests means `guard.current_agency()` gives NULL: there is no tenant table yet, so the row-level SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE cases are M4c-7b, as the plan's done-when table says.
- **Tests:** `app/tenancy/tests/test_binding_live.py` (22, as `skiw_app` on Django's connection):
  - the verifier gives the agency; two sequential blocks on one connection each see their own;
  - no token survives the block, nor an exception inside it;
  - mints under the newest key; a control token under an active key does switch, so each forged NULL comes from what that case gets wrong;
  - forged tokens give NULL: random, B-shaped with A's MAC, truncated, extra field, zero MAC, unknown version, retired version;
  - a genuine B token replayed in A's transaction gives NULL; `set_config('app.agency_id', B, true)` does not switch; `COMMIT; BEGIN` without the helper gives NULL;
  - a nested `atomic()` (also one that rolls back) keeps the binding; nesting and an open atomic block are refused; only a `uuid.UUID` is taken;
  - the debug cursor logs the other statements but not the token.
  Start-up: `test_startup_key.py` and `test_worker_key_check.py` (missing, short, seven-digit, no-version: refused before the migration check, no key text). Wiring: `test_env_wiring.py` (local), `test_compose_config.py` (web, worker and migrate read the file; no service takes the value). The `vector_keys` fixture moves to `app/tenancy/tests/conftest.py`.
- **Mutation table** (each run against `test_binding_live.py`): `set_config(…, false)` → 3 fail; no atomic-block refusal → 1; oldest key instead of newest → 1; Django's wrapper for `set_config` → 1; no uuid check → 4.
- **Runbooks (UNI-16):** `host-rebuild.md` (web and worker read the key at start-up and mint under the newest version), `local-dev.md` (`app` and `worker` take `TENANT_TOKEN_KEY`).
- **Not in this PR:** `async_with_agency()` (M4c-8); the row-level forged, leak and EXPLAIN tests (M4c-7b); callers (M3d's wrapper, jobs in M4-rest).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2 "Tenant binding" items 1, 2 and 4; TEN-09; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-4b; PR 91 review follow-up (newest active key); PR 92 review R1 P3 (the key-reader rule).
- **Alternatives rejected:** reading the key file on every call (a file read per transaction, and a key change would apply before `web` restarts); a `connection.execute_wrapper` to hide the token from logs (it still runs inside the debug wrapper's logging); allowing `with_agency()` inside an open atomic block (the outer block may already hold another agency's token on the same xid).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/binding.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/conftest.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_binding_live.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_current_agency_live.py`, `app/config/asgi.py`, `app/jobs/management/commands/runworker.py`, `app/tests/test_startup_key.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_worker_key_check.py`, `app/tests/test_env_wiring.py`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.yml`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.py`, `app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`, `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` (Semgrep table), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django and psycopg as locked).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-4b and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-5-1: the tenant root `agency` (table, RLS, grants) and the private token-key cache (PR 93 review R1 P3)
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy/0004_agency`** creates `agency` (`id uuid` PK, `name varchar(200)`, `timezone` default `Asia/Kuala_Lumpur`, `data_region` default `MY`), owned by `skiw_migrator`. ENABLE + FORCE RLS; a restrictive `agency_tenant` FOR ALL TO `skiw_app` with USING and WITH CHECK `id = (SELECT guard.current_agency())`; a permissive `agency_sel` FOR SELECT with the same predicate (spec §4.2, "the same pattern on `id`, SELECT only"); `GRANT SELECT` only, so `skiw_app` cannot write a row, its own included. The reverse drops all of it (exercised by `test_guard_schema_live`'s migrate-back). (2) **`tenancy.seed.insert_agency()`** is the one write path in this PR and is test-only here: it refuses any role but `skiw_migrator` (session and current) and any connection outside a transaction, then `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'`, `LOCK … ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`, `NO FORCE`, the insert, `FORCE` and a `relforcerowsecurity` check. A failure in between rolls the whole transaction back, so FORCE is never left off. (3) **PR 93 review R1 P3:** `tenancy.binding.token_keys()` becomes private `_token_keys()`; start-up calls `require_token_key()`, which returns nothing, so only `with_agency()` holds the key. Semgrep `tenancy-token-key-reader` also flags `_token_keys` outside the binding modules and tests, and a new `tenancy-token-key-source` flags the strings `"TENANT_TOKEN_KEY"` / `"TENANT_TOKEN_KEY_FILE"` outside settings, `keys.py` and the key tests (reading the env var directly bypasses the setting rule). Both exclude lists are pinned in `test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py` (8 rules). (4) `test_queue_grants.py` lists `agency` as the root table next to the closed global list; M4c-6b's schema check will classify it.
- **Tests:** `app/tenancy/tests/test_agency_live.py` (10): insert is idempotent and the column defaults apply; under FORCE the owner itself sees no rows; a failure after NO FORCE leaves FORCE on; writes refuse a non-migrator role and autocommit; `skiw_app` sees nothing without a token and only the bound agency with one (A, B); INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE as `skiw_app` are denied; the catalog matches the root template exactly (owner, ACL, both policies).
- **Mutation table:** `insert_agency` without FORCE restore → 5 fail, 10 errors; without NO FORCE → 10 errors; Semgrep without the `$S._token_keys` pattern → fixture line 77 missed; without the `"TENANT_TOKEN_KEY"` pattern → line 89 missed.
- **Not in this PR:** the migrate-time seed of the deployment agency, `SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY_ID` / `_NAME`, host and compose wiring, the owner's agency names, runbooks (M4c-5-2, stacked on this branch); the `/readyz` tenant check (M4c-5-3).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1, §4.2 schema check (2); plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-5; PR 93 review R1 P3 (deferred to M4c-5).
- **Alternatives rejected:** a policy naming `skiw_migrator` so the seed can write under FORCE (a standing write path outside the tenant predicate); a BYPASSRLS seed role (a new privileged role for one row); keeping `token_keys()` public with a Semgrep rule only (the return value still carries the key to any caller the rule allows).
- **Size (plan §1 split rule):** M4c-5 with the P3 came to ≈ 700 lines, so the builder split it into three stacked PRs: this one (≈ 350), M4c-5-2 (seed and config), M4c-5-3 (readyz).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/models.py`, `app/tenancy/migrations/0004_agency.py` (new), `app/tenancy/seed.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_agency_live.py` (new), `app/tenancy/binding.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_binding_live.py`, `app/config/asgi.py`, `app/jobs/management/commands/runworker.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.yml`, `semgrep/tenancy-boundaries.py`, `app/tests/test_semgrep_tenancy_paths.py`, `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` (Semgrep table), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner through the reviewer (PR 93 R1 P3, deferred to M4c-5); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-5 and spec v1.21, for the rest.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-5-2: the migrate-time seed of the one deployment agency; `SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY_ID` / `_NAME`; the owner's agency names
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy.seed.seed_after_migrate()`** runs after every `migrate`, from the one `post_migrate` receiver in `TenancyConfig.ready()` (`tenancy.after_migrate`: the guard key writes first, then the seed; `keys.py` no longer connects its own). It runs as `skiw_migrator` in its own transaction: lift FORCE as in M4c-5-1, **then** read every id (under FORCE the owner reads zero rows), and: empty table → insert the configured agency; the configured id present → nothing; anything else → `CommandError` naming the configured id and PROJECT §9, nothing written, `migrate` exits ≠ 0. It never updates or deletes, so a rename waits for the admin helper (M5). It skips when `tenancy/0004` is unapplied (a migrate back past the table). A `psycopg.Error` becomes `agency seed failed (SQLSTATE …); nothing was written`. (2) **Config:** `config.env.deployment_agency()` (lazy, as `settings.SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY`) reads `SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY_ID`, exactly one canonical lowercase uuid (no list, braces, urn, upper case or missing hyphens), and `SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY_NAME`, 1–200 printable characters with no leading or trailing space (`AGENCY_NAME_MAX` equals the model's `max_length`, tested). Errors name the variable and PROJECT §9. (3) **Values (owner, 2026-11-03, asked at `build` M4c-5):** production "SK iWealth Sdn. Bhd.", staging "SK iWealth (staging)"; local "Local agency" and CI "CI agency" per the plan. Each environment has its own generated uuid (local `0d5f8e84-…`, CI `2ab259ac-…`, staging `b9333b4d-…`, production `fb9c1361-…`), non-secret, in `.env.example` (quoted: `make` sources it in a shell) and `infra/host/env/<env>.env`. (4) **Wiring:** host `migrate` takes both; `x-app-env` (web, worker) and local `app` / `worker` take both now for M4c-5-3's `/readyz`. Every reference uses `:?`, so a missing value stops `compose`.
- **Tests:** `test_agency_live.py` gains 10 (seeded by `make migrate` with the defaults; idempotent, also through a second `migrate`; an empty table gets the configured agency; a failure after NO FORCE leaves FORCE on; a second id stops `migrate` and writes nothing; empty, malformed and two-id values stop `migrate`; non-migrator and autocommit refused; the configured agency is visible only to itself). `test_deployment_agency.py` (config refusals). `test_compose_config.py`: each environment has its own canonical uuid and the exact name.
- **Mutation table** (each run against `test_agency_live.py`): read ids before NO FORCE → 2 fail; no refusal → 1; no FORCE restore → 1 fail, 1 error.
- **Local upgrade note:** a local database where `make test-live` ran on M4c-5-1 alone holds the test agencies A and B and no deployment agency, so the seed refuses it (as designed); `make clean` resets it (`local-dev.md`). CI and hosts start from an empty `agency`.
- **Not in this PR:** the `/readyz` tenant check (M4c-5-3).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1 `agency`; plan M4c-5; owner Q2 (a) (`docs/DECISIONS.md` "M4-core plan owner answers"); PROJECT §9 second-agency gate.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a data migration (runs once, cannot refuse a later changed id, and would hold the environment's id in code); upserting the name on every migrate (a host config typo would rename the live agency); two `post_migrate` receivers (Django gives no order between them).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/seed.py`, `app/tenancy/apps.py`, `app/tenancy/keys.py`, `app/tenancy/models.py`, `app/tenancy/migrations/0004_agency.py` (comment only), `app/tenancy/tests/test_agency_live.py`, `app/config/env.py`, `app/config/settings.py`, `app/tests/test_deployment_agency.py` (new), `.env.example`, `infra/host/env/{ci,staging,production}.env`, `infra/host/compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yml`, `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (names, 2026-11-03; Q2 (a), 2026-11-01); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-5, for the rest.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-5-3: `/readyz` checks that the deployment agency binds
- **Decision:** `jobs.startup.database_ready()` gains `tenant_bound()`: inside `with_agency(<SKIW_DEPLOYMENT_AGENCY_ID>)` it runs `SELECT guard.current_agency(), (SELECT count(*) FROM agency WHERE id = %s)` and is ready only for `(agency_id, 1)`. So a `tenant_token_key` that differs from the stored version (the verifier gives NULL) or a missing seed answers 503 and fails the deploy health check (the deploy switches back), instead of every tenant read quietly returning no rows. `ImproperlyConfigured` and `RuntimeError` (a missing or invalid id or key) also give 503; the body stays `not ready`.
- **Tests:** `test_readyz.py` (the parametrized readiness table gains `bound`; ImproperlyConfigured, RuntimeError and OperationalError from the bind give `False`); `test_agency_live.py` (503 for an unseeded configured id; 503 for a different token key). `make host-test-live` starts the real stack, whose health check is `/readyz`. Mutant: `database_ready()` without `tenant_bound()` → 6 fail.
- **Why:** plan M4c-5 ("`/readyz` binds to the deployment agency"); spec §8.3 `/readyz`.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a check at process start only (a key change on a running host would go unnoticed until the next restart; `/readyz` is what the deploy switch reads).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/jobs/startup.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_readyz.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_agency_live.py`, `app/config/settings.py` (comment), `infra/host/compose.yml` (comment), `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py` (comment), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-5.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-6a-1: the `tenant_table()` template
- **Decision:** **`tenancy.template.tenant_table(table, *, commands, composite_fks=())`** returns one `RunSQL` per step, each with its exact reverse: the FK to `agency(id)`; one composite FK `(agency_id, col) → parent(agency_id, id)` per pair; ENABLE + FORCE and the restrictive `<table>_tenant`; one permissive policy per granted command (`_sel` USING, `_ins` WITH CHECK, `_upd` both, `_del` USING; an append-only table passes no UPDATE/DELETE, TEN-03); `GRANT <commands>` to `skiw_app`. `tenant_table_sql()` gives the same steps as typed `(forward, reverse)` tuples, so tests and the live check read the SQL without Django. Names are checked (`[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*`, ≤ 63 including the derived constraint and policy names), never quoted, so a name cannot carry SQL; `agency` as a table or parent and a second FK on one column are refused. Every statement is one line with no `%`.
- **Reverse and ACLs:** the reverse REVOKEs exactly what was granted. The first GRANT turns a NULL (default) ACL into an explicit owner entry, which a REVOKE leaves behind, so the live test compares effective ACLs (`coalesce(relacl, acldefault('r', relowner))`). The same holds for `tenancy/0004`.
- **Tests:** `test_template.py` (15 unit): the RLS statements equal the spec §4.2 code block (read from the spec, whitespace collapsed, `(…same…)` written out); every step and reverse exactly; append-only shape; 11 refusals; the migration linter passes both directions; `tenant_table` wraps each step in a reversible `RunSQL`. `test_template_live.py` (2): on scratch tables, as the migrator, in a rolled-back transaction: the catalog shape (constraints, ACL, deparsed policies), the exact reverse, and a composite FK refusing another agency's parent plus the `agency` FK refusing an unknown agency.
- **Mutation table:** see the PR body (run on this branch).
- **Not in this PR:** `TenantModel` and the closed global list `tenancy.schema.GLOBAL_TABLES` (M4c-6a-2, stacked on this one); the schema check (M4c-6b); isolation registry and EXPLAIN tests (M4c-7); the first real tenant table (M3d).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4 conventions, §4.2 template; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-6a. Split in two because the whole of M4c-6a came to ≈ 550 lines against the ≈ 450 split rule (plan estimate ≈ 200).
- **Alternatives rejected:** quoting names with `psycopg.sql` (migrations take plain strings, and the linter reads them line by line); adding `unique (agency_id, id)` in `tenant_table()` SQL (Django would not know it, so `makemigrations --check` could not catch drift; `TenantModel` carries it in M4c-6a-2).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/template.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_template.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_template_live.py` (new), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-6a and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-6a-2: `TenantModel` and the closed global table list
- **Decision:** (1) **`tenancy.models.TenantModel`** (abstract): `agency_id uuid` and the model constraint `UniqueConstraint(agency_id, id)` named `<app>_<model>_agency_id_id_uq`, so `makemigrations --check` fails if a subclass loses it. A subclass with its own `Meta` must inherit `TenantModel.Meta`. ORM FKs to tenant models use `db_constraint=False` (spec §4); the composite FKs come from `tenant_table()` (M4c-6a-1). (2) **`tenancy.schema.GLOBAL_TABLES`**: the spec §4.1 list, schema-qualified (`public.*`, `guard.*`), with `ROOT_TABLE = "public.agency"`. The waffle entries are django-waffle's three model tables (`waffle_flag`, `waffle_switch`, `waffle_sample`); the PR that installs waffle (M5) checks them against its migrations. (3) **`test_queue_grants.py`** imports the list. Its table test becomes "every existing global table is on the list": every existing table, minus the test-only tables, the root and tables with an `agency_id` column, must be on it; a listed table that does not exist yet is fine. A second test pins the audited grant tables to the list.
- **Tests:** `test_template.py` gains two: `TenantModel` carries the named constraint, a `Meta` that does not inherit drops it, and the autodetector emits a single `RemoveConstraint` for the drift; and the PR 97 R1 P3 boundary pair (a longest derived name of exactly 63 characters is accepted, 64 refused). `test_queue_grants.py`: the two tests above.
- **Mutation table:** see the PR body (run on this branch).
- **Not in this PR:** the schema check and `manage.py check_tenancy_schema` (M4c-6b); isolation registry and EXPLAIN tests (M4c-7); the first real tenant table (M3d).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4 conventions, §4.1 global list; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-6a (second half of the split, see M4c-6a-1).
- **Alternatives rejected:** an exact match of existing tables to the list (the list names tables later milestones create); adding `unique (agency_id, id)` in `tenant_table()` SQL (Django would not know it, so `makemigrations --check` could not catch drift).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/models.py`, `app/tenancy/schema.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_template.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-6a and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-03 — M4c-6b-1: the tenancy schema check `check()`
- **Decision:** `tenancy.check.check(conn, schemas=None, extra_allowed=())` returns sorted `Violation(table, reason)` for every relation of kind r, p, v, m or f outside `pg_*` and `information_schema`. Classification, in this order: (1) on `GLOBAL_TABLES` (or `extra_allowed`, tests only) → global, no shape check; (2) `public.agency` → the root shape of spec §4.2 (id PK, no `agency_id`, ENABLE + FORCE, the restrictive policy on `id` with USING and WITH CHECK, exactly one permissive SELECT on `id`); (3) a view → "unlisted view"; (4) an `agency_id` column → tenant shape (`id`, `agency_id uuid not null`, `unique (agency_id, id)` as a p or u constraint on that column set, ENABLE + FORCE, the restrictive `tenant_table()` policy, permissive policies only of the template shape for role `skiw_app`, the FK `(agency_id) → agency(id)`, and every FK to a tenant table pairing `agency_id → agency_id`); (5) anything else → "not on the global list and has no agency_id column". On every relation, a PUBLIC policy, a `true` predicate and a predicate containing `current_setting` are violations too. List membership comes first, so a listed global with `agency_id` (`bridge_session`, spec §4.1) is global, and an FK to it is an ordinary FK.
- **Predicates** are compared with `pg_get_expr` text (plan A4): `DEPARSED = "(agency_id = ( SELECT guard.current_agency() AS current_agency))"` and the `id` form for the root, the same text the M4c-6a live test reads back from `tenant_table()`. The text depends on `search_path`; `guard` is never on it.
- **Scope of a run:** all relations are loaded and classified, so an FK into another schema is judged by its target; `schemas` limits only what is reported (the fixtures use a scratch schema `chk`).
- **Reasons** name tables, policies and constraints only, never data.
- **Strictness beyond the plan's list, within the spec:** the restrictive policy must be FOR ALL, TO `skiw_app`, with both clauses equal to the template predicate (the spec's "the restrictive policy"); a tenant table with RLS disabled reports "RLS not enabled" separately from "not forced".
- **PR 98 R1 P3:** `test_queue_grants.py` now requires `django_migrations`, `security_event` and `guard.tenant_mac_key` in the checked remainder, so a classification that marks everything tenant fails.
- **Tests:** `test_check_live.py` (18): the real schema passes; compliant parent + child (composite FK) pass; a listed global with `agency_id` passes and an ordinary FK to it passes; 14 fail cases (plan list, plus RLS disabled, PUBLIC policy, no restrictive, restrictive without WITH CHECK, no agency FK, nullable `agency_id`); the real `agency` without FORCE and with a second permissive policy fails.
- **Not in this PR:** `manage.py check_tenancy_schema`, running it last in the migrate step (`make migrate`, host deploy), the `host-rebuild.md` text, `make db-test` and the PROJECT §7 line (M4c-6b-2).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2 "Schema check (M4) — what counts as compliant", TEN-04–07; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-6b. Split because the whole of M4c-6b comes to ≈ 550 lines against the ≈ 450 rule (plan estimate ≈ 280).
- **Alternatives rejected:** reading `pg_policies` (its `roles` shows `{public}` the same way as a role named `public`; `pg_policy.polroles` has 0 for PUBLIC); comparing predicates by normalising text ourselves (the deparse is the stable form, A4); classifying tenant tables before the list (would demand RLS on `bridge_session`).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/check.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_check_live.py` (new), `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-6b and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-04 — M4c-6b-2: `check_tenancy_schema` in the migrate step
- **Decision:** `manage.py check_tenancy_schema` (`app/tenancy/management/commands/`) runs `check()` on the default connection, writes each violation as `<table>: <reason>` to stderr and raises `CommandError("tenancy schema check: <n> violations")`; on none it prints `tenancy schema check: ok`. It runs with no `extra_allowed`, so a leftover test probe table fails it. It is the last line of `make migrate` (so CI's `test-live` fails on a bad schema) and, in `skiw-deploy`, a separate step in the new image right after `migrate`, bounded by `TOOL_S` (120 s, read-only catalog queries). A failure stops the deploy before the state write and the switch; the old digest keeps serving, the migrations stay applied (never reversed), and the tool's output is withheld like every other step.
- **`make db-test`:** db up, `make migrate` (which ends with the check), `pytest -m live app/tenancy`, then the check again on the schema the fixtures leave (every fixture rolls back, so this proves they leave nothing behind). PROJECT §7's `pending M4` line is unchanged: changing it is the owner's call (ASK).
- **PR 99 R1 P3:** `test_check_live.py` gains one case per root rule on the real `agency` (restrictive policy dropped, `agency_id` added, primary key replaced by a unique constraint) and the policy rules on a listed global (`bridge_session` with a `current_setting` policy and a PUBLIC `true` policy).
- **Tests:** `test_check_command_live.py` (2): a clean schema prints the ok line and nothing on stderr; violations are printed one per line and fail the command. `test_deploy.py`: the step order and bounds now include `check_tenancy_schema`; a failed check exits 1 after `migrate`, before any `up -d`, with the old digest still serving, the journal naming the step, and no table name on stderr. `test_check_live.py`: 22.
- **Runbooks:** `host-rebuild.md` "The tenancy schema check (M4c-6b)" (on failure the table stays, fix forward, never reverse on the host); `deploy.md` step 4.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2 "Schema check (M4)"; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-6b (second half of the split, see M4c-6b-1).
- **Alternatives rejected:** running the check inside `migrate` as a post-migrate signal (it would run on every test database setup and on `migrate --plan`); unbounded like `migrate` (the check writes nothing, so killing it leaves nothing running).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/management/` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_check_command_live.py` (new), `app/tenancy/tests/test_check_live.py`, `Makefile`, `infra/host/skiw_host/deploy.py`, `infra/host/tests/test_deploy.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/deploy.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5), within plan M4c-6b and spec v1.21.

## 2026-11-04 — M4c-7a: the TEN-08 isolation harness
- **Decision:** `tenancy/tests/isolation.py` holds `IsolationCase(table, insert(cursor, agency_id, parents) -> id, commands, parents)`; each project app lists its tenant tables as `CASES` in `<app>/tests/isolation_cases.py`, and `registry()` collects them (a table registered twice is an error). `tenancy/tests/test_isolation_live.py` generates, per case, as skiw_app through `with_agency()` on committed rows: **read** (under A, B's row is invisible; under B it is there, the control), **write** (UPDATE and DELETE of B's row affect 0 rows), **cross_insert** (an INSERT naming B, with B's own parents, raises 42501), **cross_update** (A's row moved to B raises 42501), **no_token** (0 rows; INSERT raises 42501) and **append_only** (an ungranted UPDATE or DELETE raises 42501, TEN-03). One **cross-agency FK** test per declared parent: an A row pointing at B's parent raises 23503 (RI checks bypass RLS, so only the composite FK refuses it).
- **Coverage:** `tenancy.check.tenant_tables()` (new, the same classification `check()` uses) must have no table without a case, or the test fails naming them (plan §9 item 4). Every composite FK in `pg_constraint` between tenant tables must equal the cases' declared `parents`, so each one gets its cross-agency test.
- **Proof:** the fixture pair `tenancy_fixture.parent` (all four commands) and `.child` (append-only, composite FK to `parent`), built from `tenant_table()` SQL, passes the schema check and the full set. The meta-test opens a template table with `USING (true)` (restrictive and permissive) and asserts that the generated read check fails with `tenancy_fixture.leaky: under A, 1 of B's rows visible`, as skiw_app through `with_agency()`.
- **Changes from the plan wording (owner answer 2026-11-04, with the reviewer's three conditions):** (1) the leaky table is **committed and dropped** (dropped if it exists before it is made, and in `finally`), not rolled back: skiw_app cannot see the migrator's open transaction, `with_agency()` refuses an open atomic block, and the migrator has no `skiw_app` membership (adding one would change the closed role set). A leftover fails the next `make migrate`, because a `true` policy fails the schema check. (2) The fixture pair is **module-scoped**, not session-scoped, and its schema is dropped before it is made and at the end, so the migrate-back tests (`tenancy` 0004 → 0001 drops `agency`) never meet its FKs. The tests are generated with `pytest.mark.parametrize` from the registry, which is the same pytest mechanism as `pytest_generate_tests`.
- **Agencies:** A and B (`…a7`, `…b7`) are committed with `insert_agency` and kept, as in M4c-5's tests; `make clean` removes them.
- **PROJECT §7 (owner answer 2026-11-04):** the `pending M4` line becomes `make db-test` with a description of what it runs; CI covers it through `make test-live`.
- **PR 100 R1 P3:** `local-dev.md` names the check in the `make migrate` row, adds a `make db-test` row, and says how to clear a leftover `files_probe`, `jobs_probe` or `tenancy_fixture.leaky`.
- **Not in this PR:** row-level forged-tenant, pooled leak and EXPLAIN tests (M4c-7b); real tenant tables and their cases (M3d).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2, TEN-08; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-7a.
- **Alternatives rejected:** one rolled-back transaction with the token set by hand (minting outside `with_agency()` is what TEN-09 forbids, and the key reader is Semgrep-guarded); discovering FKs at collection time (the fixture pair does not exist then), hence the declared-parents cross-check.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/check.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/isolation.py`, `isolation_cases.py`, `test_isolation.py`, `test_isolation_live.py` (new), `agents/PROJECT.md` §7, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M4c-7a; the two wording changes and the §7 line by the owner, 2026-11-04.

## 2026-11-04 — M4c-7b: row-level forged-tenant, leak and EXPLAIN tests
- **Decision:** `tenancy/tests/test_row_level_live.py` runs, for every registered isolation case (today the fixture pair), as skiw_app through `with_agency()` on committed rows:
  - **forged tokens (TEN-09):** M4c-4b's list (random, B-shaped with a bad MAC, truncated, extra field, zero MAC, an absent and a retired key version) plus a genuine B token replayed from B's own transaction, each set inside A's transaction. SELECT sees neither B's row nor A's own, UPDATE and DELETE affect 0 rows, an INSERT naming B raises 42501, and B's row is still there afterwards. `guard.current_agency()` must be NULL under the forgery, and A's own row must vanish, so a forgery that never lands fails the test instead of passing quietly;
  - `app.agency_id` set to B inside A's transaction leaves A seeing only A;
  - `COMMIT; BEGIN` inside `with_agency(A)` hides A's own row and refuses an INSERT;
  - **leak test:** A, then B, on the same backend (`pg_backend_pid()` checked), each sees only its own row; afterwards `current_setting('app.tenant_token', true)` is empty and `guard.current_agency()` is NULL;
  - **EXPLAIN [A5]:** for SELECT and, where granted, UPDATE and DELETE, every `guard.current_agency()` call in the plan sits inside an InitPlan, there is no seq scan, and an index condition reads `agency_id = (InitPlan n).col1`. Seq scans are switched off (`SET LOCAL enable_seqscan = off`), so this shows the `unique (agency_id, id)` index is usable, not that the planner picks it at every table size. Postgres shows one InitPlan for the two identical policy predicates.
- **"UPDATE/DELETE of B rows fail"** (TEN-09 wording) is read as "affect 0 rows, and the row survives": RLS hides the rows, and Postgres does not raise for that. This is the same reading as M4c-7a's write check; only INSERT and WITH CHECK raise 42501.
- **"Pooled connection":** the sync path has no pool; Django's persistent connection is the one connection that is reused. M4c-8 re-runs this set on `AsyncConnectionPool`.
- **M4c-4b's `b-shaped-bad-mac` forgery** swaps that module's own agency id, so this module defines its own (it was a no-op on this module's A, caught by the NULL check while building).
- **PR 101 R1 P3:** the fixture pair moved to `tenancy/tests/conftest.py`. It is still module-scoped (built and dropped by each module that uses it), and a session autouse fixture drops a leftover `tenancy_fixture` schema before the first tenancy test of any run that selects live tests. Without it, a leftover pair (which passes the schema check) failed `test_guard_schema_live`'s reverse migration and a `test_check_live` root rule; with it, the same leftover gave a green run. `local-dev.md` now says the schema check catches `tenancy_fixture.leaky` but not the compliant pair, and that the next live tenancy run drops the pair.
- **Mutants (bytecode off), all killed:** the policy predicate without the `(SELECT …)` wrapper (EXPLAIN, 4 tests); a predicate that fails open on NULL (22); a session-level `set_config(…, false)` token (the leak test, 2); an unindexable `::text` predicate (EXPLAIN, 4); a forgery that is never set, with and without the NULL check (16).
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.2 (the InitPlan note), TEN-09; plan `docs/plans/2026-11-01-m4-core.md` §3 M4c-7b, A5.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a session-scoped fixture pair (the migrate-back tests would meet its FKs, as M4c-7a found); adding the forged set to the harness's `CHECKS` (it needs a second parameter, the forgery, and the harness stays one check per behaviour).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/tenancy/tests/conftest.py`, `test_isolation_live.py`, `test_row_level_live.py` (new), `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M4c-7b.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-1a: `app_user`, and exception messages kept out of logs
- **Split (plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §1 rule, as M4c-6a/6b):** plan M3d (≈ 400 estimated, at the limit) is three PRs. The three tables with their TEN-08 cases, column tests and the PR 78 R1 P2 log fix came to ≈ 660 added lines of code and tests, so they are two stacked PRs: **M3d-1a** (this: the log fix, which must land before the first table that holds personal data, and `app_user`) and **M3d-1b** (`user_factor`, `session_revocation`). **M3d-2** follows: `accounts.auth.login()`, `authorized_action`, idle timeout, the revocation check, the Semgrep `start_new_lifetime` rule, the route registry test, the logged-in CSRF fixture, TEN-11 variant (i) and the renewed-row test (d) through `login()`. Branch names follow the split, not the plan's single `feat/m3d-users-sessions-wrapper`.
- **`app_user` (plan §3 M3d, spec §4.1), a `TenantModel`** in `accounts/user_model.py` (the plan's file name): `email citext` with a **global** `unique (email)` (spec §4.1: login happens before the tenant is known); `status` `invited`/`active`/`disabled` [A, plan] with database default `invited`; `login_blob_ct` null until a password is set, otherwise exactly 222 bytes (`blob.OVERHEAD` 30 + `LOGIN_SIZE` 192); no password-hash column. `skiw_app` gets SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE: no DELETE, an account is disabled, not removed.
- **`citext`** is created in `public` by `accounts.0004` (trusted extension since PG 13, so `skiw_migrator` can create it); the reverse drops it.
- **RLS in a second migration (`accounts.0005`):** Django adds a model's `UniqueConstraint`s at the end of the migration that creates it, and M3d-1b's composite FKs reference `app_user (agency_id, id)`, so every table's `tenant_table()` step follows its `CreateModel` migration (found while building: one migration failed with "no unique constraint matching given keys").
- **`accounts.0004` depends on `tenancy.0004_agency`:** a tenant table must not outlive `agency`. Without the dependency, `test_guard_schema_live`'s migrate-back to `tenancy` 0001 left `app_user` rows whose agency was gone, and the next `make migrate` failed the agency FK. With it, migrating `tenancy` back drops `app_user` first.
- **Two existing tests changed for the agency FK:** `test_agency_live.test_an_empty_table_gets_the_configured_agency` empties `agency` with `TRUNCATE agency CASCADE` (rolled back, as before) instead of `DELETE`, which the FK now refuses. `test_check_live`'s "primary key replaced by a unique constraint" mutation drops `agency_pkey … CASCADE` (tenant FKs depend on it) and asserts only the findings for `public.agency`, because the CASCADE also drops the tenant tables' agency FKs, which the check rightly reports too.
- **Typing:** `TenantModel.Meta.constraints` is annotated `ClassVar[tuple[models.BaseConstraint, ...]]`, so a subclass Meta can add checks after `*TenantModel.Meta.constraints` (mypy otherwise fixes the type at one `UniqueConstraint`). No schema change; `makemigrations --check` is clean.
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/isolation_cases.py` registers `app_user` for the TEN-08 harness and the M4c-7b row-level set. `accounts/tests/test_user_tables_live.py` checks each column rule as `skiw_app` inside one rolled-back `with_agency()` transaction, pinning each refusal to its constraint name. The harness commits its `app_user` rows under A and B and keeps them; `local-dev.md` says so.
- **PR 78 R1 P2 (exception messages in stderr, UNI-09):** `config/logs.py` `NoExceptionText`, now the `plain` formatter in `LOGGING`, prints each traceback with its frames and each exception's class, but not its message, for the whole `__cause__`/`__context__` chain. A database error quotes row values (`Key (email)=(…) already exists`), and from this PR `app_user` holds emails. `test_security_log_stderr.py` now expects a bare `RuntimeError` as the last line. It also adds a view that raises a chained `KeyError` → `ValueError`, with `person@example.test` in both messages: both classes, the cause link and the view's frame reach stderr, and the marker does not. This supersedes the M3b-1 entry's "(the exception's own message …)" in point (4).
- **Mutants (bytecode off), all killed:** dropping `app_user_status_known` and `app_user_login_blob_ct_length`; `email` as `text`; DELETE granted on `app_user`; NO FORCE on `app_user`; `status` default `active`; `app_user` left out of `CASES`; and for the formatter: unwired, keeping the message, printing only the newest exception, and dropping the frames.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1, §4.2, TEN-04–08, UNI-09; plan M3 §3 M3d; PR 78 review R1 P2.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `CHECK` on the email's shape (validation belongs at the boundary, and a check would refuse addresses the boundary accepts); a filter that drops `exc_info` entirely (the frames are what an operator needs to find a 500).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/user_model.py`, `models.py`, `migrations/0004_app_user.py`, `0005_app_user_rls.py` (new), `app/accounts/tests/isolation_cases.py`, `test_user_tables_live.py` (new), `app/tenancy/models.py`, `app/tenancy/tests/test_agency_live.py`, `test_check_live.py`, `app/config/logs.py` (new), `app/config/settings.py`, `app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (`citext` ships with Postgres 17.11).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d, split by the plan's §1 rule.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-1b: `user_factor` and the `session_revocation` marker
- **Decision:** the second half of M3d-1 (split, see the M3d-1a entry), stacked on M3d-1a. Two `TenantModel`s in the plan's files, `accounts/factor_model.py` and `revocation_model.py`, each with a composite FK `(agency_id, user_id)` → `app_user (agency_id, id)` (Django FK with `db_constraint=False`, `PROTECT`, no reverse accessor; the real FK comes from `tenant_table()`):
  - `user_factor` (spec §4.1): `kind` `passkey`/`totp`/`recovery`; `credential_id` unique across agencies (spec §4.1); `sign_count >= 0`, default 0; `lookup_blob_ct` null or exactly 62 bytes (`blob.OVERHEAD` 30 + `CREDENTIAL_SIZE` 32); `public_key` and `secret_ct` free bytea (their layouts come with M3f and M3g). `skiw_app` gets all four commands (factor removal, recovery-code use, plan M3d).
  - `session_revocation(id, agency_id, user_id, session_key_hash null, revoked_at)` (plan carry-over (b), owner PR 74 Q2 = A): `session_key_hash` is null (a user-wide marker) or 64 lower-case hex characters, the shape of `accounts.session.stored_key`; `revoked_at` defaults to Django's `Now()`, which Postgres runs as `STATEMENT_TIMESTAMP()` (the insert's own time, not the transaction's start). Indexes `(agency_id, session_key_hash)` and `(agency_id, user_id, revoked_at)` (plan "Cost"; the user FK's own single-column index is left out, the second index covers it). `skiw_app` gets SELECT and INSERT only (append-only, TEN-03).
- **Migrations:** `accounts.0006` (generated `CreateModel`s) and `accounts.0007` (`tenant_table()` for both, with the composite FKs), split for the same reason as `0004`/`0005`.
- **Grant audit (`app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`):** the DELETE test now allows `DELETE_ALLOW_LIST | TENANT_DELETE_ALLOW_LIST`, the latter `{"user_factor"}` with the plan as its reason; `session_revocation` joins `TEN03_EVIDENCE_TABLES`.
- **Tests:** both tables join `accounts/tests/isolation_cases.py` with `user_id` declared as a parent, so the harness runs a cross-agency FK test for each. `test_user_tables_live.py` gains the column rules of both tables. The harness keeps their rows under A and B; `local-dev.md` says so.
- **PR 103 R1 P3 #2 (deferred here):** the global `uq(email)` and `uq(credential_id)` (spec §4.1; the M3e and M3g lookups resolve them before the tenant is known) were not pinned: a per-agency `UNIQUE (agency_id, email)` passed every single-agency test. `test_the_pre_tenant_lookup_keys_are_unique_across_agencies` asserts each constraint's catalog definition (`pg_get_constraintdef` = `UNIQUE (email)`, `UNIQUE (credential_id)`), so a re-scoped, renamed or missing constraint fails. A catalog check rather than the same value under two agencies, because skiw_app cannot remove a committed `app_user`, and a second row in an open transaction would wait on the first one's unique-index entry.
- **PR 104 R1 P1 (secret scan):** the test's `stored_key`-shaped value was a 64-hex literal named `KEY_HASH`, which gitleaks' `generic-api-key` rule matches; `make secrets` scans the full history, so the value is now derived (`hashlib.sha256(b"session-revocation-test").hexdigest()`) in a branch where no commit ever held the literal. Locally, gitleaks is not installed and `make hooks` was not run on this machine, so the pre-push scan did not catch it; the pinned image (`ghcr.io/gitleaks/gitleaks:v8.30.1`) on a **full** clone (every branch, as CI fetches with `fetch-depth: 0`) is the local check; a single-branch clone misses other refs. **PR 104 closed, branch deleted (owner, 2026-11-04):** the owner chose a new PR (#105) over a force-push or a `.gitleaksignore` allowlist. `gitleaks git` scans every ref, so the kept branch `feat/m3d-1b-factor-revocation` (closed #104, tip 6eb587b, the only ref holding 392c138) still failed #105's `security` job and would have failed every later PR. The owner then approved a **one-off exception** to the builder rule "never delete remote branches": that branch was deleted. GitHub still holds the commits under `refs/pull/104/head` (6eb587b), which CI's checkout does not fetch, and #104's diff stays readable. A full-clone scan afterwards: 482 commits, no leaks. Existing clones keep `origin/feat/m3d-1b-factor-revocation` until `git fetch --prune`. **PR 104 R1 P3 #1:** the model and grant-test comments now name `accounts.0007`.
- **Mutants (bytecode off), all killed:** `app_user_email_key` and `user_factor_credential_id_key` re-scoped to `(agency_id, …)` (the reviewer's PR 103 mutant), and the email key re-scoped under a new name; dropping each of `user_factor_kind_known`, `_sign_count_gte_0`, `_lookup_blob_ct_length`, `_credential_id_key`, `session_revocation_key_hash_shape` and both composite FKs; UPDATE granted on `session_revocation`; DELETE revoked on `user_factor`; `session_revocation` left out of `CASES`; `TENANT_DELETE_ALLOW_LIST` emptied. (The M3d-1a mutants, rerun on this branch, are still killed.)
- **Spec gap (plan §9 item 2, still open):** spec §4.1 does not list `session_revocation`, and D6 still says "Revocation is a row delete". The table follows the owner's PR 74 Q2 = A and the plan; the spec text is fixed at the next spec change, as the plan says.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1, §4.2, TEN-03–08; plan M3 §3 M3d and carry-over (b).
- **Alternatives rejected:** a per-user generation counter on `app_user` (plan M3d "Rejected": SQL in agency A can UPDATE it back); a CHECK tying `credential_id`/`public_key`/`lookup_blob_ct` to `kind = 'passkey'` (the plan does not fix which columns TOTP and recovery rows use until M3f; it can be added then).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/factor_model.py`, `revocation_model.py`, `models.py`, `migrations/0006_factor_and_revocation.py`, `0007_factor_and_revocation_rls.py` (new), `app/accounts/tests/isolation_cases.py`, `test_user_tables_live.py`, `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-2a: `accounts.auth.login()`, Semgrep `start-new-lifetime-login-only`, every logger through the message-free formatter
- **Split:** M3d-2 (see the M3d-1a entry) came to more than the plan §1 limit, so it is two PRs. **M3d-2a** (this): `login()`, its Semgrep rule, carry-overs (d) and (i) through `login()`, and PR 103 R1 P3 #1. **M3d-2b**: `authorized_action`, idle timeout, the revocation check (with the marker written when an authenticated session is rotated), the route registry test, the logged-in CSRF fixture, the forged-`agency_id` tests, and the PR 104 R1 `revoked_at` notes.
- **Decision:**
  - `app/accounts/auth/login.py` `login(request, user, *, factor)` (owner Q7 (a): no `django.contrib.auth`). Django 5.2.17's own `login()` (`contrib/auth/__init__.py`) was read for the steps. In order: a session that already names another `_skiw_user` is flushed (Django does the same, so no other user's data is carried over); `start_new_lifetime()`; `cycle_key()`; `_skiw_user`, `_skiw_agency` (string UUIDs), `_skiw_auth_at`, `_skiw_seen_at` (the same UTC ISO-8601 instant, to the microsecond) and `_skiw_factor` are written; `django.middleware.csrf.rotate_token()`. Unlike Django, a repeat login by the same user also gets a new key (Django skips `cycle_key` then). Data the session held before the login is kept, as in Django; the M3e pending state must remove its own keys. `factor` must be one of `user_factor` kinds (`passkey`, `totp`, `recovery`), otherwise `ValueError`; a request whose session is not the project store raises `TypeError`. `login()` reads no table: the caller (M3e) has verified the password, the second factor and `status`.
  - Semgrep `start-new-lifetime-login-only` (`semgrep/start-new-lifetime-login-only.{yml,py}`): `start_new_lifetime()` / `astart_new_lifetime()`, also through `getattr`, are refused anywhere in `app/` except `accounts/auth/login.py`, the store itself and the accounts tests (the M2b/M3a tests call it on purpose).
  - **PR 103 R1 P3 #1:** `LOGGING` gives the root logger the `stderr` handler at WARNING with the `plain` (`NoExceptionText`) formatter. `django.security` and `django.request` keep `propagate: False`. Every other logger (`procrastinate.worker`, the app's own) now goes through `config.logs` instead of Python's last-resort handler, which printed exception messages. `uvicorn.error` is checked, not changed: under gunicorn the `UvicornWorker` gives it gunicorn's own handlers (`uvicorn_worker/_workers.py:57-60`, formatter `glogging.py:175`, `:220`), but uvicorn logs there only when an exception escapes the ASGI app (`uvicorn/protocols/http/h11_impl.py:414`). Django wraps the handler and every middleware in `convert_exception_to_response` (`core/handlers/base.py:37`, `:95`), which turns a view's exception into a 500 response logged by `django.request`. So `uvicorn.error` does not fire for Django-handled errors.
- **Tests:**
  - `app/accounts/tests/test_login_live.py`:
    - (d): an 11-hour-old session gets a new key and a deadline of now + 12 h, the old row is gone, and a fresh store reads every `_skiw_*` key with no `django.security` record;
    - the CSRF token rotates;
    - the same user's re-login rotates and keeps the data, while another user's login flushes it;
    - an unknown factor is refused;
    - (i), TEN-11, sync and async: a row whose `_skiw_user` or `_skiw_agency` was changed and re-signed with `SECRET_KEY` (the old MAC kept) is rejected and logged out.
  - `app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`: a `procrastinate.worker` ERROR with a chained exception, whose messages both carry `person@example.test`, prints the classes, the cause line and no marker; a plain app logger's WARNING is printed and its INFO is not.
- **Mutants (bytecode off):** 9 killed, 1 equivalent. Killed:
  - in `login()`: no `start_new_lifetime`, no `cycle_key`, no `rotate_token`, no flush for another user, always flush, no factor check, a wrong `_skiw_seen_at`, `_skiw_agency` set to the user id;
  - no root handler.
  - Equivalent: root at INFO. The handler's WARNING level still filters, so the output can't change.
- **Why:** spec D6 ("rotated on login"), D7, TEN-11, UNI-09; plan M3 §3 M3d, §4 M3d row, carry-overs (d) and (i); PR 103 review R1 P3 #1.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `logconfig_dict` for gunicorn that puts `NoExceptionText` on `gunicorn.error`/`uvicorn.error`. It would change the image's start command, and the logger does not carry Django-handled errors. Revisit if a later ASGI app path can raise past Django.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/login.py`, `app/accounts/tests/test_login_live.py` (new), `semgrep/start-new-lifetime-login-only.{yml,py}` (new), `app/config/settings.py`, `app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-2b1: revocation primitives, the marker `login()` writes over an authenticated session, PR 106 R1 P3s
- **Split:** M3d-2b (see the M3d-2a entry) came to ≈ 650 added lines of code and tests with the route registry test and the logged-in CSRF fixture still to write, past the plan §1 limit, so it is three stacked PRs. **M3d-2b1** (this): `accounts.auth.revoke`, the marker in `login()`, the PR 104 R1 `revoked_at` notes and the two PR 106 R1 P3s. **M3d-2b2**: `authorized_action` (AUTH-01 order, idle timeout, the revocation and `status` checks), the forged-`agency_id` tests and TEN-11 variant (i) through it. **M3d-2b3**: the route registry test (AUTH-02) and the logged-in CSRF fixture.
- **Decision:**
  - `app/accounts/auth/revoke.py`: `mark(agency, user, session_key | None)` writes one `session_revocation` row, for one session (`stored_key` of the cookie) or user-wide (`None`); `revoked(user, session_key, auth_at)` is true when a row names this session's key, or a user-wide row of the user has `revoked_at ≥ auth_at`; `revoke_user(agency, user)` (AUTH-07 primitive) is a user-wide marker in its own `with_agency()`. `mark()` and `revoked()` run in the caller's `with_agency()` transaction, so a marker and the row delete commit together.
  - **Clock rule (PR 104 R1 note):** `revoked_at` is the database's statement time (`Now()` = `STATEMENT_TIMESTAMP()`), `_skiw_auth_at` the web process's `timezone.now()`. Both containers run on the one VPS and read its kernel clock, so there is no skew between them in production. A tie counts as revoked (`≥`), so the uncertain case fails closed. A later statement time can only revoke more sessions, never fewer. A session re-issued right after `revoke_user` (M3f/M3h, AUTH-07 "current session re-issued") gets an `_skiw_auth_at` after the marker's statement, so it is not caught; if two clocks ever disagree, the worst case is that the user signs in again.
  - **`login()` over an authenticated session** (plan M3d "a rotation of an authenticated session"): when the session already holds `_skiw_user`, `login()` opens `with_agency(<the old session's _skiw_agency>)`, writes a key marker for the old cookie under the old session's user, then flushes (another user) or rotates (the same user). The old row's delete and the marker commit together; a failed insert raises before any row changes. So `login()` must run outside any atomic block (`with_agency()` refuses nesting); M3e/M3f call it after their own `with_agency()` block has closed. A login over a session without `_skiw_user` (an anonymous or a pending one) writes no marker, as before. The M3e password step's own `cycle_key()` is not covered here: it must retire an authenticated session the same way, which M3e states.
  - **PR 106 R1 P3 #1:** `NoExceptionText.format()` sets `record.exc_text = None` before formatting, so a traceback cached by an earlier handler (the stock `Formatter`, messages included) is never reused. Unlike the reviewer's example, it does not restore the old value afterwards: the message-free text stays cached, so a later stock handler prints that one instead of its own. The reviewer's evidence also showed Django's default `console` handler (DEBUG on) printing the messages itself, so `LOGGING` gives the `django` logger no handlers at level INFO (Django's default level); it propagates to root, and the `stderr` handler prints each record once. This also drops Django's default `mail_admins` handler from `django`, which was never configured (no `ADMINS`). `django.server` (runserver) keeps its own handler.
  - **PR 106 R1 P3 #2:** Semgrep `start-new-lifetime-login-only` matches the attribute (`$SESSION.start_new_lifetime`, `$SESSION.astart_new_lifetime`), so a bound-method alias is flagged as well as a call; the `getattr` forms stay.
- **Tests:**
  - `app/accounts/tests/test_revoke_live.py`: a login over an authenticated session (the same user, or a user of a second agency) deletes the old row and marks its key under the old session's user and agency, so `revoked()` refuses it while the new key passes; a login over a session without a login writes no marker; a marker insert that fails in the database (a key hash that breaks the shape CHECK) leaves the old row, its deadline and its data as they were; a key marker revokes that one session only; a user-wide marker revokes a session signed in a minute before and at `revoked_at` (the tie), not one 1 µs after, and not another user's.
  - `app/accounts/tests/test_user_tables_live.py`: after `pg_sleep` in the same transaction, `revoked_at > now()` and `< statement_timestamp()` of the next statement (statement time pinned).
  - `app/accounts/tests/test_login_live.py`: the re-login test uses committed users (`conftest.committed_user`, kept, TEN-03), because the second login now writes a marker.
  - `app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`: with `DJANGO_DEBUG=1`, a `django.template` ERROR whose `KeyError` carries the marker prints once, as class only; a record whose `exc_text` an earlier stock handler cached is printed without the marker.
  - `semgrep/start-new-lifetime-login-only.py`: two alias cases (`renew = request.session.start_new_lifetime`, the async one).
  - `app/accounts/tests/conftest.py`: `committed_user(status, agency)` and an `agency_b` fixture (the tenancy B agency through `seed.insert_agency`, kept).
- **Mutants (bytecode off):** 14 killed, none survived:
  - in `login()`: no marker; a marker for the new key; under the new user; under the new user's agency; only when another user signs in;
  - in `revoked()`: `>` for `≥`; no user filter on the user-wide row; key markers counted user-wide; no key check; no user-wide check;
  - `revoke_user()` writing a key marker;
  - the formatter reusing a cached traceback; `django` keeping Django's `console` handler;
  - the `revoked_at` default set to `transaction_timestamp()` in the database (restored afterwards).
  - Not a mutant: writing the marker after the rotation instead of before. The rollback restores the deleted row either way, so the failed-insert test cannot tell them apart; before is kept, so nothing is deleted before the insert succeeds.
- **Why:** spec D6 (revocation), AUTH-07, UNI-09; plan M3 §3 M3d carry-over (b); PR 104 review R1 note; PR 106 review R1 P3 #1, #2.
- **Alternatives rejected:** writing the old-key marker in the caller (M3e/M3f) instead of `login()` — two places to forget it; `login()` taking an open transaction — `with_agency()` cannot nest, and the marker must be under the old session's agency, which may differ from the new user's.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/revoke.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/login.py`, `app/accounts/revocation_model.py` (docstring), `app/accounts/tests/{conftest.py,test_login_live.py,test_revoke_live.py (new),test_user_tables_live.py}`, `app/config/{logs.py,settings.py}`, `app/tests/test_security_log_stderr.py`, `semgrep/start-new-lifetime-login-only.{yml,py}`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-2b2: `authorized_action` (AUTH-01 order, idle timeout, the `status` and revocation checks)
- **Split (see the M3d-2b1 entry):** with every item the M3d-2b1 entry gave it, M3d-2b2 came to 524 added lines of code and tests. Two changes bring it to ≈ 450: (1) the registration checks move to **M3d-2b3** with the route registry that needs them — refusing an empty `permission`, refusing an async handler, and a `TypeError` when the session is not the accounts store (until then, `SESSION_ENGINE` is the only thing that makes it the accounts store); (2) the end-to-end login-rotation test is dropped, because M3d-2b1's `test_revoke_live.py` covers the marker, and the idle and user-wide tests here cover a put-back row being refused through the wrapper. `skiw_action` itself stays here, so `permission` is not a dead parameter.
- **Decision:**
  - `app/accounts/auth/wrapper.py` `authorized_action(*, schema, permission)` decorates a sync handler `handler(request, signed, data)`. `Signed` (user id, agency id, factor, `auth_at`, `seen_at`) comes from the session only. `schema` is the action's own validator (owner Q3 (a)): it takes the parsed body and returns the handler's `data`, or raises `InvalidInput`. The view carries `skiw_action = permission`, which `functools.wraps` and `route()`'s `require_http_methods` keep.
  - **Order (AUTH-01), each step a JSON `{"error": <code>}` answer:**
    1. Session: `_skiw_user`, `_skiw_agency`, `_skiw_auth_at` and `_skiw_seen_at` present and parseable, else 401 `signed-out`. M3e's pending keys have other names, so a pending session is signed out here.
    2. Tenant, in one `with_agency(<session's _skiw_agency>)`:
       - idle (`now − _skiw_seen_at ≥ SKIW_SESSION_IDLE_MIN`): a key marker, then a flush, in the same transaction; 401 `idle`;
       - the user is not `active`, or `revoked()`: a flush, 401 `signed-out`. A user who is not `active` gets no marker: status, not the marker, refuses them. So the flow that disables a user (M4-rest "Manage staff") must also call `revoke_user()`, or a put-back row works again once the user is active again.
    3. Role/permission, interim until M4-rest (owner Q9 (a)): `_skiw_factor` must be a `user_factor` kind, else 403 `forbidden`. This doesn't log out. AUTH-03 is n/a until the §6 matrix exists; `permission` names the entry M4-rest will check.
    4. Input (UNI-03): `GET`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS` give the schema `{}`; any other method needs `Content-Type: application/json` and a JSON object (UTF-8). Anything else, or `InvalidInput`, is 400 `invalid` with no detail.
    5. Idempotency: no M3 action needs it.
    6. The handler, inside the same `with_agency()` transaction; an exception rolls it back.
  - `_skiw_seen_at` is refreshed when it is ≥ 1 minute old (plan [A]), because each write re-seals the row. The session middleware saves it after the response. Django's `save()` on an existing key is `force_update` and raises `UpdateError` when the row is gone (`django/contrib/sessions/backends/db.py:114`, 5.2.17), so a refresh cannot bring back a row that a logout in another request deleted.
  - The agency is never read from the request (TEN-11): the body, the query and headers are ignored for the binding and for `Signed`.
  - `accounts` may not import `config` (import-linter), so the wrapper keeps its own `SAFE_METHODS`; M3d-2b3's registry test pins it against `config.routes.SAFE_METHODS`.
- **Tests:**
  - `app/accounts/tests/urls.py` gains a test-only `action` view. It records what it saw in `SEEN` (the `Signed`, the data, and `guard.current_agency()` inside the handler) instead of echoing it; CI's `reflected-data-httpresponse` rule would flag an echo.
  - `app/accounts/tests/test_action_wrapper_live.py` (26 cases, committed users, kept):
    - the handler gets the session's user, agency and factor, and the database binding is the session's agency;
    - no session, or a session without a login: 401;
    - **AUTH-01 order:** a request that fails a step and every later one gets that step's answer, and the handler never runs; only the tenant step logs out;
    - not `active` (invited, disabled): 401 and the cookie deleted;
    - an unknown or missing factor: 403 and still signed in;
    - six bodies refused with 400: an array, a string, truncated JSON, non-UTF-8, a schema refusal, and JSON sent as a form;
    - a safe method gives the schema `{}`;
    - **TEN-11:** a forged agency in the query, the body and three headers is ignored (agency B exists); variant (i) through the wrapper: a row whose `_skiw_agency` was re-signed with `SECRET_KEY` is 401 and logged out;
    - **idle** (frozen clock): 1 µs before the limit passes; at the limit, a marker and a logout; the saved row put back with a fresh idle clock is then refused as revoked;
    - `_skiw_seen_at` is not written at 59 s, and is written at 60 s;
    - **absolute 12 h:** activity every 29 minutes never moves the deadline, and the session ends at it;
    - **user-wide marker:** two older sessions and one at exactly `revoked_at` are refused, also when their rows are put back; one 1 µs later passes. The web clock is set from the marker's own `revoked_at`;
    - a failed idle marker (a key hash that breaks the CHECK) leaves the session alive.
- **Mutants (bytecode off):** 19 killed, none survived:
  - idle `>` for `≥`; no idle marker; no idle flush; idle measured from `_skiw_auth_at`;
  - no status check; `invited` accepted; no revocation check; no flush on refusal;
  - no role check; role checked after input;
  - safe methods parse a body; no content-type check; no object check;
  - refresh `>` for `≥`; never refresh; always refresh;
  - the binding taken from the query; no `skiw_action`; the handler run outside `with_agency()`.
  - Two `_signed()` checks (time zone present, a session key present) survived and were removed: a row that fails the MAC loads empty with no key, and only `login()` writes these keys, with UTC-aware times.
- **Why:** spec D6 (idle 30 min, absolute 12 h), T5; AUTH-01, AUTH-02 (no middleware gate), AUTH-07, TEN-09, TEN-11, UNI-03; plan M3 §3 M3d and §4 M3d row; owner Q3 (a), Q9 (a).
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - a 401 `signed-out` for idle as well: the SPA needs to tell "you were away" apart from a revoked session, and neither code says more than that;
  - logging out on a 403: a missing factor is not an attack on the session, and M4-rest's real permission check must not sign people out.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/wrapper.py` (new), `app/accounts/tests/{urls.py,test_action_wrapper_live.py (new)}`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d.

## 2026-11-04 — M3d-2b3: the route registry (AUTH-02), the wrapper's registration checks and the logged-in CSRF gate
- **Decision:**
  - **Route registry** (`app/tests/test_route_registry.py`, plan M3 Risk 4): it walks `config.urls` (the test pins `ROOT_URLCONF` to it). Every route is either an `authorized_action` handler (its view carries `skiw_action`, which `route()`'s `require_http_methods` keeps through `functools.wraps`) or a name in `PUBLIC_ROUTES` with the reason it needs no login. It fails a route that is neither, a wrapped route that is also listed, a stale entry, and **a route name used by more than one route** (PR 109 R1 P2 #1: Django lets patterns share a name, so an unwrapped route named `healthz` would otherwise pass as the public one). `PUBLIC_ROUTES` starts as `healthz`, `readyz`, `asset` and `shell`; like the gate's `TOKEN_AUTH_ROUTES`, an entry needs the reviewer's sign-off. M3e's login steps will be the first public mutating entries. The test-only URLconfs (`tests.csrf_urls`, `accounts.tests.urls`) are not walked: the app never serves them.
  - **`SAFE_METHODS` pin:** the registry test asserts `accounts.auth.wrapper.SAFE_METHODS == config.routes.SAFE_METHODS` (the copy exists because `accounts` may not import `config`; M3d-2b2).
  - **Registration checks in `authorized_action`** (moved here from M3d-2b2):
    - an empty `permission` raises `ImproperlyConfigured` when the decorator is built;
    - an async handler raises `ImproperlyConfigured`: the sync view would return its coroutine unrun, and it would run after `with_agency()` closed, outside the binding;
    - a request whose session is not `accounts.session.SessionStore` (the MAC-checked store, M2c) raises `TypeError`. Until now `SESSION_ENGINE` was the only guarantee.
  - **CSRF gate with a logged-in session** (M2d deviation (b); plan §3 M3d "CSRF gate: a logged-in fixture"):
    - `tests.csrf_urls` gains `action-probe`, a cookie-auth POST `authorized_action` handler, beside the plain `csrf-probe`. The gate's `Pair` records whether a route is wrapped.
    - In `test_csrf_gate.py` (no database) the browser has no session, so with a valid token a wrapped route must answer the wrapper's 401, and a plain route the handler's own outcome, as before. With no session key the store reads no database (`accounts/session.py` `_get_session_from_db`).
    - `test_csrf_gate_live.py` (live) loads the shell, then signs in through `accounts.auth.login()` server-side (there is no login route before M3e). It gives the client the new session cookie and the rotated `__Host-csrftoken` secret, as the login response will (carry-over (h)). Each cookie-auth mutating route then reaches its handler with the rotated token. It is refused by CSRF with no token, a wrong one, a cross-site `Origin`, and **the token the shell gave before the login** (`token-invalid`), which proves the rotation.
- **Tests:** `test_route_registry.py` (9), `accounts/tests/test_action_wrapper.py` (4, no database), `test_csrf_gate_live.py` (10), and the gate's cases for `action-probe` (7 more in `test_csrf_gate.py`: 1 accept, 3 CSRF refusals, 3 refused verbs).
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, run against the four test files):** 12 killed, none survived:
  - `login()` without `rotate_token()`: killed (the fixture finds no rotated secret);
  - each registration check dropped (3); `SAFE_METHODS` drift; no `skiw_action`;
  - the registry ignoring unwrapped routes, wrapped-and-public routes, or stale entries (3); and, after review R1, the registry ignoring a name used twice (killed by both duplicate-name tests);
  - no `CsrfViewMiddleware`; the wrapper letting a request without a login through.
  - A first run reported every mutant killed because zsh passed the test list as one argument and pytest collected nothing. It was discarded and re-run with a passing baseline first.
- **Why:** AUTH-01, AUTH-02 (no middleware gate), spec T6 (CSRF on all mutating routes), UNI-19 (only intended endpoints unauthenticated); plan M3 §3 M3d, §4 M3d row ("a route without the wrapper fails a registry test", "CSRF gate with the logged-in fixture"), Risk 4; DECISIONS M2d deviation (b), M3d-2b2.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - a registry keyed on the module that defines the view: renaming or moving a module would pass silently, and the check would not prove the wrapper actually ran;
  - walking `tests.csrf_urls` as well: its `csrf-probe` is a deliberately unwrapped test route, and listing test probes as "public" would weaken the reviewed list;
  - a login route for the fixture: that is M3e's work (password step, throttle, uniform errors); `login()` itself is what rotates the token.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/wrapper.py`, `app/accounts/tests/test_action_wrapper.py` (new), `app/tests/{test_route_registry.py (new), test_csrf_gate_live.py (new), test_csrf_gate.py, csrf_urls.py, csrf_fixtures.py}`, `docs/runbooks/local-dev.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3d.

## 2026-11-04 — M3e-1a: login and credential blobs without a cleartext key version (corrects M3b)
- **Split (plan `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` §1 rule):** plan M3e (≈ 400 estimated) holds the lookup function, the password step and enrolment, well past ≈ 450 lines together. It is four stacked PRs: **M3e-1a** (this: the blob change the lookup needs), **M3e-1b** (`guard.auth_lookup_login`, its decoy and its caller), **M3e-2** (`POST /api/auth/login`: input, throttle, lookup, verify, uniform failure, pending state, the rotated CSRF token; the structural and band timing tests) and **M3e-3** (`accounts_invite` and the enrolment link, AUTH-08). M3e-1 was first built as one change of 512 added code and test lines and split before review. Branch names follow the split.
- **Correction to M3b (owner decision 2026-11-04, builder chat, at `build` M3e):** the M3b blob began with the 2-byte key version in clear. The database never holds `login_blob_key`, so `guard.auth_lookup_login` (M3e-1b) cannot give a decoy the same leading bytes: SQL running as `skiw_app` (an injection, say) could call the function and tell a real account (`00 01 …`) from an unknown email (random bytes), which is the enumeration oracle spec §4.1 rules out. The owner chose to **drop the header** (the alternative, copying the version into a guard table, still leaks during a rotation and adds state). The spec fixes no byte layout, so no spec change is needed. Now:
  - blob = 12-byte random nonce ‖ AES-256-GCM ciphertext ‖ 16-byte tag; **login 220 bytes, credential 60** (were 222 and 62). The AAD is still version ‖ purpose ‖ value, with each tried key's own version, so the binding to the key version stays.
  - `blob.open_*` tries **every** listed version every time and keeps the first that opens, so a real blob, a decoy and a blob of the oldest version do the same work.
  - `accounts.0008` replaces the two length checks (`app_user_login_blob_ct_length` 220, `user_factor_lookup_blob_ct_length` 60). No row holds a blob in any environment (nothing seals one before M3e-3's enrolment), so the checks are replaced in place; the migration reverses.
  - **Rotation without a header:** SQL can no longer show which version a row uses. Before a `login_blob_key` version leaves the key file, the re-seal job (spec T16; not built) must open every blob and report the version that opened it, and none may still report the old one. `host-rebuild.md` says so.
- **Tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_blob.py`):** `test_every_listed_version_is_tried_whatever_opens_the_blob` counts the attempts (three versions, three attempts each, for a newest-version blob, a random decoy and an oldest-version blob, and for a credential decoy); `test_a_blob_has_no_fixed_leading_bytes`; the version-header tests become AAD tests (`test_the_version_is_bound_by_the_aad`: the same key under another version fails); `test_the_newest_version_seals_and_every_listed_one_opens` checks the newest key alone opens the new blob. The lengths in `test_auth_passwords.py` and `test_user_tables_live.py` follow.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline first), all killed:** the open stops at the first success; no version in the AAD; the oldest version seals (it survived at first, because the test opened the wrong blob; the test was fixed); a 2-byte header kept.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1 "Pre-tenant lookups" (a decoy "of identical length", "learns nothing it can use"), AUTH-05; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row ("same blob length"); owner decision above.
- **Alternatives rejected:** the version copied into a guard table for the decoy header (leaks while two versions are in use, extra state for `migrate`); two random bytes in place of the header (meaningless bytes; the same effect as dropping it).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/blob.py`, `app/accounts/{user_model,factor_model}.py` (comments; the constants follow `OVERHEAD`), `app/accounts/migrations/0008_blob_lengths_without_header.py` (new), tests (`test_auth_blob.py`, `test_auth_passwords.py`, `test_user_tables_live.py`), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (drop the header, 2026-11-04); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e.

## 2026-11-04 — M3e-1b: `guard.auth_lookup_login` and its decoy (pinned oldest decoy key, column grant, Semgrep rule)
- **Stacked on M3e-1a** (the blob without a cleartext version, which this decoy needs; the split note is there). Plan M3e, second of four PRs.
- **`guard.auth_lookup_login(address public.citext) RETURNS bytea`** (`accounts.0009`, spec §4.1 "Pre-tenant lookups", Flow 5.1 (0), TEN-12): plpgsql, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `VOLATILE STRICT`, owner `skiw_auth_lookup` through `as_owner`, `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, every object schema-qualified, `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM PUBLIC`, `GRANT EXECUTE … TO skiw_app`. It returns exactly one `bytea`: the row's `login_blob_ct`, or for an unknown email **or a user with no password yet** a 220-byte decoy. In order: read the oldest decoy key (none → `RAISE`, the same for known and unknown emails), build the decoy, look up, write the event, return. So both paths do the same SQL work.
  - **Decoy:** HMAC-SHA-256 in counter mode, blocks 1–7 of `hmac('skiw-decoy-login-v1' ‖ int4(i) ‖ lower(address), key)`, cut to 220 bytes. The input is `lower()` of the address, the same equality `citext` uses, so citext-equal emails share one decoy, as they would share a row.
  - **The citext equality is named:** `u.email OPERATOR(public.=) address`. With `search_path = pg_catalog` an unqualified `=` would cast both sides to text and compare case-sensitively (the PR 103 reviewer note). A test stores the address in another case and calls the function directly in a third.
  - **Decoy key version pinned to the oldest (owner point 4):** the function uses the lowest listed `guard.lookup_decoy_key` version, so a rotation that adds a version changes no decoy. Removing the oldest changes every unknown email's decoy at once while real blobs stay the same, so a before/after comparison would separate the two. It must therefore go together with re-sealing every login blob, which also changes every real blob; until the re-seal job exists the oldest line is not removed (`host-rebuild.md`). **Known limit:** a leaked `lookup_decoy_key` lets its holder compute decoys and so tell unknown emails from known ones; this cannot be fixed by rotating until the re-seal job exists.
  - **Events:** every call inserts one `security_event` (`kind = 'login.lookup'`, no HMACs, `details_json = {}`; the owner has INSERT only, so no RETURNING). The row is the same for known and unknown emails: `skiw_app` can read `security_event`, so a detail such as "found" would be an oracle.
  - **Privileges:** `GRANT SELECT (email, login_blob_ct) ON TABLE app_user TO skiw_auth_lookup`, a column grant, so the BYPASSRLS owner reads nothing else of `app_user`. The grant audit's "no column-level grants" test becomes "column-level grants are exactly these two" (`app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`; spec §4.1's "no column-level grants" sentence is about the queue objects).
- **`accounts.auth.lookup.lookup_login(email) -> bytes`** is the only caller. It passes `blob.normalise_email(email)`, the value the blob's AAD binds, and runs outside `with_agency()`; its event commits with it. **Semgrep `auth-lookup-accounts-auth-only`:** SQL text that calls `auth_lookup_login` or `auth_lookup_credential` (a SELECT, FROM, PERFORM or CALL before the name), or a `psycopg.sql.Identifier` that names one, is refused outside `accounts/auth/lookup.py`, the accounts migrations and the accounts tests. Docstrings that only name the function are not matched.
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_login_live.py` (10): the hardening row (owner, `prosecdef`, `proconfig`, exact `proacl`, return and argument types, volatile, strict); the decoy length equals `LOGIN_BLOB_LENGTH`; the owner's column privileges; a known email in any case gives its blob; an unknown email gives a stable decoy that matches a Python reference, is the same for a case variant, differs per email and never opens; a user with no password gets the decoy; decoys have no fixed leading bytes; one identical event per call, known or unknown; a new decoy key version changes nothing, while removing the old one changes the decoys; no decoy key is an error for known and unknown emails alike.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline of 88 first; SQL mutants re-migrate `accounts` 0008 → 0009 each time), all killed:** Python: no normalising in the caller. SQL: the newest decoy key; text equality instead of the citext one, and no `lower()` (both survived at first, because the caller lower-cases; the direct-call cases were added); no event; an event that says whether the email was found; a user with no password gets `NULL`; the key check after the lookup; a 222-byte decoy; EXECUTE left to PUBLIC; table-level SELECT for the owner.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 §4.1 "Pre-tenant lookups", Flow 5.1 (0), (1), TEN-10, TEN-12, AUTH-05; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row (definer-function tests), §7 Risk 2; owner decision 2026-11-04 point 4 (pin the decoy version); PR 103 R1 reviewer note (`public.citext`).
- **Alternatives rejected:** a decoy under the newest decoy key version (a rotation would separate known from unknown); an event with an HMAC of the email under the decoy key (an extra key use for no reader); a table-level SELECT for the owner.
