- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/lookup.py` (new), `app/accounts/migrations/0009_auth_lookup_login.py` (new), `semgrep/auth-lookup-accounts-auth-only.{yml,py}` (new), tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_lookup_login_live.py` new; `app/jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` (the `lookup_decoy_key` paragraph), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (PostgreSQL 17.11, pgcrypto in `guard`, Semgrep 1.179.0).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (the pinned decoy version, 2026-11-04); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e for the rest.

## 2026-11-04 — M3e-2a: `password_step.attempt` (throttle, lookup, verify, uniform failure records) and the PR 111 P3s
- **Split (plan §1 rule):** M3e-2 (the password step and `POST /api/auth/login`) was first built as one change of 515 added code and test lines, before the timing-band test, and split before review: **M3e-2a** (this: the step behind the route, with the structural timing test) and **M3e-2b** (the route: input, pending state, rotated CSRF token, the uniform HTTP answers, the gate entries and the statistical timing band). M3e is now five stacked PRs (1a, 1b, 2a, 2b, 3); the M3e-1a split note named four.
- **`accounts.auth.password_step.attempt(email, password, ip) -> Verified | "limited" | "failed"`** follows plan §3 M3e steps (2)–(7) without the HTTP parts:
  1. `throttle.blocked` for the account, IP and lock keys → `"limited"`, with no lookup, no Argon2 and nothing counted (one query);
  2. `lookup.lookup_login` (the row's blob or a 220-byte decoy, and its `login.lookup` event);
  3. `blob.open_login`; a decoy or another email's blob opens to `None`;
  4. one Argon2 verify: the record's hash, or `dummy_verify` for `None`;
  5. a verified password → `status` read in `with_agency(record.agency_id)` (the only tenant-bound step);
  6. any failure → `record_failure` (the three counters) and one `login.failed` event with the account and IP HMACs and no details → `"failed"`;
  7. success → `reset_on_success` (the lock run only; the windowed counters stay) → `Verified(user_id, agency_id)`.
- **A disabled or invited user with the right password is a full failure** (counters and event, as step 6), not only "the same 401" (plan (7) [A]): counting it keeps the records the same as for a wrong password, and AUTH-06 counts failed logins, which it is. Its extra work (the status read) comes only after a right password, which an attacker guessing at an account does not have.
- **`login.failed` joins `events.KINDS`.** No success event in this PR: the login completes at the second factor (M3f), which can record it.
- **Dummy hash at start-up:** `AccountsConfig.ready()` calls `passwords.warm()`, so each process makes its one dummy Argon2 hash before any request (plan Risk 2: the first unknown-email login would otherwise also pay for it). Each process start, `manage.py` commands included, pays one Argon2 hash (≈ 70 ms at T5 parameters).
- **Kept test users with a blob are unsealed after each test** (`accounts/tests/logins.py` `unseal()`): `accounts.0008` reversed puts back the 222-byte check, and the tenancy reversal test (`test_the_unlinted_key_table_migration_reverses_and_reapplies_exactly`) runs that reverse, which a kept 220-byte blob refuses. That is the reverse failing loudly, as it should; the M3e-1a "the migration reverses" holds while no row holds a blob. M3e-3's enrolment tests do the same.
- **PR 111 R1 P3s (deferred to here):** (1) plan text lines 74, 93, 105 and §6 now say 220/60 bytes, no header, the version in the AAD, every version tried, and the §6 exception for replacing a CHECK in place (`accounts.0008`); (2) Semgrep `auth-lookup-accounts-auth-only` also matches a string literal that is only the function name (`cursor.callproc("guard.auth_lookup_login", …)`, any case, with or without `guard.`), in place of the `Identifier` pattern, which the literal branch covers; the fixture has two `callproc` ruleid lines and an ok line for a longer name.
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/test_password_step_live.py` (9): the right password in another case is verified and resets the run; each of known-wrong, unknown, invited and disabled-right writes one event and one count; after five failures the sixth attempt, even right, is limited with one query, no Argon2, no open and no event (known and unknown); the IP limit at 21; ten consecutive failures across two windows lock past the window and the lock ends after `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN`; **structural timing (plan §4 M3e row (1)):** known-wrong, unknown and invited each run one Argon2 verify with the T5 parameters, no Argon2 hash, one open of a 220-byte blob and the same SQL statement shapes in the same order. `test_auth_passwords.py`: `ready()` makes the dummy hash.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline of 16 first), all killed:** no throttle check; a limited attempt also counted; the unknown path skips Argon2; the unknown path hashes instead of verifying; an extra query on the known path; no status check; the status read outside `with_agency()`; an inactive user returns before the counters; no event for an unknown email; an event that says whether the email was found; no counters; no reset; no warm-up.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 Flow 5.1 (1), §4.1 "Pre-tenant lookups", AUTH-05, AUTH-06; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row, §7 Risk 2; PR 111 R1 P3s.
- **Alternatives rejected:** an inactive user answered as a failure without counting it (the records would differ from a wrong password's); the dummy hash made lazily on the first unknown login (that login would be slower).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/password_step.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/{events,passwords}.py`, `app/accounts/apps.py`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/{logins,test_password_step_live}.py` new; `test_auth_passwords.py`), `semgrep/auth-lookup-accounts-auth-only.{yml,py}`, `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e.

## 2026-11-04 — M3e-2b: `POST /api/auth/login` (uniform answers, pending state, rotated CSRF token, timing band)
- **Stacked on M3e-2a** (`password_step.attempt`; the split note is there). Plan M3e, fourth of five PRs.
- **`accounts.views.login_password`**, mounted as `route("api/auth/login", …, name="auth-login", methods=("POST",), auth="cookie")`, a reviewed `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry (AUTH-02, UNI-19: it runs before any session exists, and leaves only a pending state). It takes a JSON object or a form post (FE-32). Exactly three answers, each `Cache-Control: no-store`:
  - **malformed** (not JSON or not an object, a missing or non-string field, an empty or longer-than-254-character email (RFC 5321's longest path), an email with a NUL, which PostgreSQL text cannot hold, an empty password, any other content type) → the failure answer **with no query at all**: it names no account to count against (plan (1));
  - `"limited"` → `429 {"error": "limited"}`; `"failed"` → `401 {"error": "login-failed"}`. Neither sets a cookie or touches the session;
  - `Verified` → `password_step.start_pending`, then `200 {"next": "second_factor", "csrf": get_token()}` (carry-over (h)).
  - **No password length cap** beyond Django's request body limit (`DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE`, 2.5 MB by default): Argon2 first hashes the password with BLAKE2b, so a long one costs a linear pass, not more Argon2 work, and a cap would be a business value the spec does not set.
- **`start_pending`:** the session is `flush()`ed, so the pending session has a new key, no carried keys and its own lifetime (a `cycle_key()` would keep an earlier session's deadline). It then holds only `_skiw_pending_user`, `_skiw_pending_agency` and `_skiw_pending_until` (now + `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN`, UTC ISO-8601), and `rotate_token()` gives a new CSRF secret. A **signed-in session** at the password step is retired first with a `session_revocation` marker and the row delete in one `with_agency()` transaction, as `login()` does (M3d), so the old row put back by SQL is still refused. The wrapper never treats a pending session as signed in (it has no `_skiw_user`). M3f's second factor reads and checks the pending keys and calls `login()`; **it must mark the pending key when it does** (the PR 107 note).
- **The CSRF gate:** `Fixture.answers` lets a fixture name the status a route answers once past CSRF (the login route answers the gate's `{}` with 401, which the generic "< 300 or 400/422" check would refuse). `assert_past_csrf` checks that status and that the CSRF marker header is absent.
- **import-linter:** `config.urls` now imports `accounts.views`, so `unmatched_ignore_imports_alerting = "warn"` is removed, as the M3a comment planned: an ignore that matches nothing is an error again. The contract tests' stub tree carries that one edge, and a new test proves a stale ignore fails the lint.
- **Tests (`accounts/tests/test_login_password_live.py`, 24):** the right password (in another case) leaves exactly the three pending keys with the expiry, a masked 64-character token unlike the shell's and a session the wrapper does not treat as signed in; a second password step starts a new session with its own deadline and deletes the first; the returned token passes CSRF and the pre-login one gets the 403 `token-invalid` marker; a form post works; known-wrong, unknown, invited and disabled-right get byte-equal status, body and headers (CSP removed), no cookie, and are counted; 11 malformed bodies get the same answer with no query; a 254-character address is not malformed; the sixth attempt's 429 is byte-equal for known and unknown; a signed-in session is retired with a marker and its row deleted; a failure leaves a signed-in session alone. **Timing band (plan §4 M3e row (2)):** live, T5 parameters, a fresh account and IP per request, 5 warm-up calls, 20 interleaved known-wrong/unknown pairs, medians within max(15 ms, 15 %), no retries. Measured here: medians ≈ 79 ms on both paths, within 0.5 ms; a missing dummy verify (≈ 70 ms) fails it. `documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py`: `test_an_ignore_that_matches_nothing_fails_the_lint`.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline of 139 first), all killed:** no NUL check; email cap 255; email cap 253; an empty password allowed; a list body accepted; no form branch; no `Cache-Control`; limited answered as failed; a failure that writes the session; the token taken before the rotation; the unmasked secret returned; `cycle_key()` in place of `flush()` (survived at first; the second-step lifetime test was added); no marker for a signed-in session; a signed-in session's keys carried; no `rotate_token`; a longer pending time; the unknown path without Argon2 (the band); the route registered as token-auth (no CSRF); `"warn"` put back in `pyproject.toml`.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 Flow 5.1 (0)–(1), AUTH-02, AUTH-05, FE-32, UNI-19; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row, carry-over (h), §7 Risk 2.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `cycle_key()` for the pending session (carries the earlier lifetime); a 400 for a malformed body (a second answer that says the input, not the account, was wrong; plan (1) asks for the same failure); a success event here (the login completes at the second factor).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/views.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/password_step.py` (`start_pending`), `app/config/urls.py`, `pyproject.toml`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_login_password_live.py` new; `app/tests/{csrf_fixtures,test_csrf_gate,test_csrf_gate_live,test_route_registry}.py`, `app/documents/tests/test_import_contracts.py`), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e; the `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry needs the reviewer's sign-off (AUTH-02).

## 2026-11-05 — M3e-3a: enrolment links (`accounts_invite`, `enrol.invite` / `enrol.redeem`) and the stored-hash upgrade (PR 112/113 R1 P3 #1)
- **Split (plan §1 rule):** M3e-3 (enrolment, AUTH-08) is two PRs: **M3e-3a** (this: the link, the operator command, the runbook, and the carried P3) and **M3e-3b** (`POST /api/auth/enrol`, the reviewed `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry that calls `redeem` and starts the pending enrolment state). M3e is now six PRs (1a, 1b, 2a, 2b, 3a, 3b).
- **`accounts.auth.enrol`** (plan M3 §3 M3e, owner Q5 (a)):
  - `invite(email, name) -> token`: the email is stored as `blob.normalise_email` gives it (NFKC, trimmed, lower case), the form `lookup_login` passes and the blob's AAD binds, so the user can sign in with what they were invited as; it must pass Django's `validate_email` and be at most 254 characters (the login route's cap, now `blob.EMAIL_MAX`). The name is 1 to 200 characters after trimming, with no NUL. In `with_agency(<deployment agency>)` and under a row lock: no user → a new `invited` one; an `invited` user → a new link over the row as it is now (the stored name stays); `active` or `disabled` → refused ("already enrolled"); an email unique-clash with a user this agency cannot see → refused ("cannot be invited"), naming nothing of that user.
  - The token is `TimestampSigner(salt="skiw-enrol-v1").sign_object({"u": user id, "a": agency id, "f": fingerprint})` under `SECRET_KEY` (and its fallbacks), with the fingerprint the SHA-256 hex of `login_blob_ct`, or empty while it is NULL. `redeem(token, password) -> Verified | None` takes it with `max_age = SKIW_ENROL_LINK_MIN` minutes, then in `with_agency(agency)` locks the row, requires `status = invited` and an equal fingerprint, and writes the new blob. So a link works once (the blob changes), any other unused link for that user dies with it, and two uses at once take turns on the lock, the second seeing the changed blob. Every refusal is the same `None` (expired, tampered, another salt or key, another shape, used, user no longer invited, empty password).
  - **The user stays `invited` after `redeem`** [A]: the password step refuses anyone not `active` (M3e-2a), so a password alone signs nobody in. The first second factor (M3f TOTP, M3g passkey) makes the user `active`, as Flow 5.1 (4) and plan M3e ("requires a second factor before the session completes") ask.
- **`manage.py accounts_invite <email> --name <display name>`** prints only `https://<ALLOWED_HOSTS[0]>/enrol#<token>` on stdout (on hosts `ALLOWED_HOSTS` is `SKIW_HOSTNAME`), and on stderr how long it is valid; a refusal is a `CommandError` (exit 1). The token is in the URL **fragment**, so a browser never sends it in the request line or a `Referer`, and Caddy never logs it; the SPA page (M3i) reads it and posts it. The operator runs it with `compose exec` in the running `web` container, not `compose run` [A — plan said "a one-off `web`-image container"]: a `run` container's stdout goes to its journald logging driver, so the link would be kept in the journal (UNI-09); `exec` output reaches only the operator's terminal. `--name` is required [A]: `app_user.display_name` is NOT NULL and the plan's `accounts_invite <email>` gave no source for it.
- **The stored-hash upgrade (PR 112 R1 P3 #1, carried in PR 113 R1; the reviewer's option (a)):** after a verified password and an `active` status, inside the same `with_agency()` that reads the status, a hash for which `passwords.needs_rehash` is true is re-made with the current Argon2 parameters and re-sealed, by an `UPDATE … WHERE id = … AND login_blob_ct = <the blob this login opened>`, so a password set meanwhile is never overwritten. It happens only after a right password and an active status, so it says nothing to someone guessing. **Residual limit (recorded, runbook "Enrol a user"):** after `SKIW_ARGON2_*` is raised, a user who has not signed in since keeps the old cost, so a wrong password for that account and an unknown email differ in time until they do.
- **Not in this PR:** the route (M3e-3b); security events for invites and enrolments (the plan names none); AUTH-07 is n/a here (an `invited` user has no signed-in session to revoke). **ASK for M3e-3b:** a minimum password length at enrolment. The spec sets none and M3e-2b set no cap; both are the owner's values.
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/test_enrol_live.py` (26): an invite stores the normalised email and trimmed name, its link sets a password that opens and verifies, the user stays invited and cannot sign in; a 254-character address can be invited; works once; a second link dies when the first is used and a new invite gives a working one; expiry at `SKIW_ENROL_LINK_MIN` (5 s before works, 1 s after does not); six tampered forms (payload, signature, another salt, another key, garbage, empty); signed values of another shape; a user no longer invited; an empty password; two uses at once set one password; active and disabled users refused; an email of another agency refused without naming it; six bad emails or names refused (a valid-form 255-character address among them); the command prints one working link; the command exits with the reason. `test_password_step_live.py` (+3): a hash with older parameters is re-made at the next sign-in and not on a wrong password or a disabled user's right one; a current hash is left as it is; a password set during the sign-in is not overwritten.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 AUTH-04, AUTH-05, AUTH-08, Flow 5.1 (4), TEN-09; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row ("enrolment link: works once, expired, tampered, reused"), the runbook line in §6; owner Q5 (a) (30 minutes, operator command); PR 112 R1 and PR 113 R1 P3 #1.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a link with the token in the query string (it would reach server and proxy logs); a random token stored in a new table (a migration and a table for what the signature and the blob fingerprint already give); making the user `active` at `redeem` (a password alone would then sign in, without the second factor D7 requires); option (b) for the P3 (re-enrol after every parameter change).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/enrol.py` (new), `app/accounts/management/commands/accounts_invite.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/password_step.py` (the upgrade), `app/accounts/auth/blob.py` (`EMAIL_MAX`, moved from `app/accounts/views.py`), tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_enrol_live.py` new; `test_password_step_live.py`), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` ("Enrol a user"), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (Django's `TimestampSigner`).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e and owner Q5 (a); the P3 fix is the reviewer's recommended option (a), within the builder's ownership of that finding (option (b) would have needed the owner).

## 2026-11-05 — M3e-3b: `POST /api/auth/enrol` (the second public route, the pending enrolment state) and the password minimum
- **Owner answer (M3e-3a ASK, 2026-11-05, "12, build"):** an enrolment password must have at least **12 characters**, with no composition rules (the reviewer's recommendation; NIST SP 800-63B-4 asks for at least 8 when the password is one of two factors, as here). It is the bounded setting `SKIW_PW_MIN_LENGTH` = 12, bounds 12–64 (UNI-02; named `PW`, not `PASSWORD` [A], because the host Compose guard requires every env name matching `SECRET|PASSWORD|TOKEN|KEY|KEK` to come from a `/run/secrets` file, and exempting a name from that guard would weaken it): the weak end is the owner value, so a weaker host value fails settings import and needs the owner; the strict end is a builder value [A]. Wired into every context the other auth values use (`.env.example`, `Makefile` `TOOL_ENV`, local Compose `app`/`worker`, the CI label step `env:` and `-e`, `infra/host/env/{ci,staging,production}.env`, host Compose `x-app-env` and `migrate`), `app/tests/settings_env.py`, `test_auth_settings.py` (11 fails closed), `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`, and both runbooks. Characters are Python `len`, that is Unicode code points [A]; there is still no upper cap beyond the request body limit (M3e-2b).
- **`enrol.redeem`** refuses a password shorter than `SKIW_PW_MIN_LENGTH` (replacing "empty"), with the same `None` as any other refusal and before it writes anything, so a short password leaves the link working.
- **`POST /api/auth/enrol`** (`accounts.views.enrol_password`, `route(..., auth="cookie")`, CSRF like every mutating route), JSON or form-encoded (FE-32) `{token, password}`:
  - A body that is not a JSON object or a form, or lacks either string field, gets the failure answer with no query. The parsing is shared with the login route (`_fields`).
  - **Every refusal is one answer:** 401 `{"error": "enrol-failed"}`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, no cookie and an untouched session (AUTH-05). Bad signature, expired, used, user no longer `invited` and too short cannot be told apart; the page (M3i) states the minimum up front.
  - **Success:** `password_step.start_pending(request, verified, enrol=True)`, then 200 `{"next": "enrol_factor", "csrf": <rotated token>}`. The session is the same pending state as a password login (new key, a signed-in session retired with a marker first, `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN`) plus `_skiw_pending_enrol = True`. A login's pending state never carries that key, so it stays exactly three keys (M3e-2b test unchanged).
  - **No throttle** [A]: the link is a 256-bit HMAC, so nothing here can be guessed and a refusal names no account to count against. Only a valid link runs Argon2.
  - The route path `/api/auth/enrol` and the `next` value `enrol_factor` are builder values [A]; the plan names neither.
- **For M3f and M3g (carried):** only a pending session with `_skiw_pending_enrol` may add a first second factor, and only before `_skiw_pending_until`; that factor makes the user `active` and completes the login with `login()`. A pending login (no marker) may only use a factor it already has. M3i serves the `/enrol` page (the link's path; today only `/` serves the shell) and shows the minimum length.
- **Docs:** `host-rebuild.md` "Enrol a user": the password minimum and that a short one leaves the link working; two invites for the same new email at the same moment (PR 114 R1 note: the second is told "cannot be invited", run it again). The interim-values paragraph lists the new value and drops the stale "nothing reads them yet".
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/test_enrol_route_live.py` (new): a link sets the password and leaves exactly the three pending keys plus the enrolment marker, a masked token unlike the shell's, a session the wrapper does not treat as signed in, and the user still `invited`; the returned token passes CSRF and the earlier one gets `token-invalid`; works once; expired, tampered and no-longer-invited links get the byte-equal 401 (CSP removed) and no cookie; 11 and 1 characters are refused the same way and the same link then works with 12; a form post works; 9 malformed bodies get the same answer with no query; a good link with a password that is not a string (list, number, null) gets the same answer and writes nothing; a signed-in session is retired with a marker; a refusal leaves a signed-in session alone. `test_enrol_live.py`: the empty-password test becomes "shorter than the minimum" (empty, 1, 11 ASCII, 11 non-ASCII; the link then works with 12 non-ASCII characters); the other-shape test uses a long password so it still tests the shape. Route registry and CSRF gate: the new `auth-enrol` entries.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline of 357 first), all 18 killed:** no length check; `<=`; 11 allowed; empty only (the M3e-3a rule); no enrolment marker; the marker on every pending state; the marker set before the flush; a 400; the login's failure body; no `no-store`; a refusal that flushes the session; `next` = `second_factor`; the CSRF token taken before the rotation; signed in at enrolment; no type check on the password; no form branch; the route as token-auth; the bound's weak end at 11.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 AUTH-02, AUTH-05, AUTH-08, Flow 5.1 (4), D7, FE-32, UNI-02, UNI-03; plan M3 §3 M3e, §4 M3e row; owner 2026-11-05; PR 114 R1 notes.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a distinct "too short" answer (a second answer, which the reviewer asked to avoid; the page can check the length before posting); signing the user in at enrolment (D7: a second factor first); a separate pending-state shape for enrolment (one shape plus a marker keeps the M3d rotation and the M3f expiry checks in one place); throttling the route (nothing to guess, and it would add an account-counting path).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/views.py`, `app/accounts/auth/{enrol,password_step}.py`, `app/config/{urls,settings}.py`, the env wiring listed above, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_enrol_route_live.py` new; `test_enrol_live.py`; `app/tests/{csrf_fixtures,test_route_registry,settings_env,test_auth_settings}.py`; `infra/host/tests/test_compose_config.py`), `docs/runbooks/{host-rebuild,local-dev}.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (the minimum, 2026-11-05); builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3e for the rest; the `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry needs the reviewer's sign-off (AUTH-02).

## 2026-11-05 — M3f-1: TOTP codes, the factor-secret subkey, password normalisation (PR 115 R1 P3 #1) and JSON lone surrogates
- **Split:** plan M3f (≈ 350 lines) is three PRs, because this first part alone came to ≈ 390 added lines of code and tests (§1 split rule ≈ 450): **M3f-1** (this: the database-free primitives and the carried P3), **M3f-2** (recovery codes, `accounts.auth.recovery`), **M3f-3** (TOTP enrolment and the second-factor endpoint: the pending-state checks carried from M3e-3b, `login()`, the stored step, the AUTH-07 revocation on a factor change, the `otpauth://` link and its issuer label). Nothing here has a caller yet; M3f-3 is the first, as M3b-2's blob and hash waited for M3e.
- **Password normalisation (PR 115 R1 P3 #1, AUTH-04, T5):** `passwords.normalise` = NFKC, applied in one place: `hash_password` and `verify` (so also `dummy_verify`, which calls `verify`). No strip and no case folding: only the Unicode form changes. `enrol.redeem` counts `SKIW_PW_MIN_LENGTH` on the normalised password, the form that is hashed (six NFD "é" are 12 code points but 6 characters, and are refused). NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.1.2 says a verifier SHOULD apply NFKC or NFKD before hashing; NFKC matches `blob.normalise_email`. **It lands before any host has a hash** (the VPS is not bought), so no fallback verify of the raw form is needed; a hash of a password that is not already NFKC made before this change would no longer verify (tested, to show the boundary).
- **Lone surrogates (found while testing the above, UNI-03, AUTH-05):** `json.loads` accepts `"\ud800"`, which UTF-8 cannot encode, so Argon2 (and PostgreSQL) raised `UnicodeEncodeError`: a JSON login or enrolment with one in the password or email answered 500, not the uniform refusal. `accounts.views._fields` now refuses a field that does not encode as UTF-8, before any query. A form post cannot carry one (Django decodes it with replacement).
- **`accounts.auth.totp`** (plan M3f, Flow 5.1 (2)): RFC 4226 HOTP (HMAC-SHA-1 of the 8-byte big-endian counter, dynamic truncation, 31-bit mask) at 6 digits over RFC 6238 30-second steps from the Unix epoch, a 20-byte secret from `secrets` [A, plan]. `accept(secret, candidate, last, now)` returns the step a code matches within ±`SKIW_TOTP_SKEW_STEPS` of now if it is later than `last`, else None. Only exactly six ASCII digits are compared (`str.isdigit` also passes full-width and Arabic-Indic digits); every step in the window is computed and compared with `hmac.compare_digest`, with no early exit. The caller keeps the accepted step in `user_factor.sign_count` [A, plan] and must store it with a conditional update so two concurrent uses of one code cannot both pass (M3f-3). `pyotp` stays rejected (plan §2).
- **Factor secret seal** (plan M3f "an HKDF subkey of `login_blob_key`, label `skiw-factor-secret-v1`"): `blob.require_key(label)` now derives one cipher set per HKDF info, so `seal_factor_secret`/`open_factor_secret` use the subkey `skiw-factor-secret-v1` of every listed version and never the lookup blobs' `skiw-blob-key-v1` key. Same blob shape as M3e-1a: nonce ‖ AES-256-GCM ‖ tag, 12 + 20 + 16 = **48 bytes**, the newest version seals, every listed version is tried on open. AAD = version (2 bytes) ‖ `skiw-factor-secret-v1` ‖ factor id ‖ user id ‖ agency id: the plan's AAD with the version and purpose prefix every blob has, so a secret copied to another factor, user, agency or version fails its tag. A secret of any other length raises `ValueError`.
- **Tests:** `accounts/tests/test_auth_totp.py` (new): the RFC 4226 Appendix D HOTP values for counters 0–9; the RFC 6238 Appendix B SHA-1 rows (their last six digits; the step of each time; `accept` at that time); a leading zero kept; ±1 accepted, ±2 and ±3 refused; skew 0 accepts only the current step; the same step twice refused (also a step later, still inside the window); an earlier step after a later one refused; another secret refused; nine wrong shapes (lengths, spaces, a letter, full-width and Arabic-Indic digits) refused before any comparison; all three steps compared whichever matches. `test_auth_blob.py`: the factor secret round trip at 48 bytes, every flipped bit and a truncation refused, bound to each id and their order, only 20 bytes sealed, its own subkey (the lookup key fails with the exact AAD; the HKDF subkey opens it), and every listed version tried. `test_auth_passwords.py`: NFC and NFD each verify the other, the compatibility forms (ﬁ, full-width A), nothing else changed (spaces, case), `dummy_verify` normalises. Live: an NFC enrolment verifies NFD and the other way round, the minimum is counted after normalising, a lone surrogate in an enrolment password or a login email or password gets the uniform refusal with no query.
- **Mutants (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, a passing baseline of 160 first), all 29 killed:** normalise as identity; no normalising in hash, or in verify; NFC instead of NFKC; the minimum on the raw length; no surrogate check; offset nibble `0x07`; no 31-bit mask; SHA-256; a little-endian counter; no zero padding; `round` for the step; `>=` last; no replay check; the window missing +skew, or −skew; skew doubled; an early return on the first match; `isdigit` without `isascii`; no length check; `==` instead of `compare_digest`; a 10-byte secret; seal, or open, on the lookup key; the HKDF label ignored; ids reordered; agency not bound; no secret size check; open stopping at the first version.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 Flow 5.1 (2), AUTH-04, AUTH-05, T5, §4.1 `secret_ct` (C3), UNI-03; plan M3 §3 M3f, §4 M3f row; PR 115 R1 P3 #1.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a purpose AAD under the existing blob key for the TOTP secret (the plan asks for a subkey; a subkey keeps the secrets' AES key apart from the decoy-facing lookup key); NFKD (NFKC matches the email); normalising in the views (the review asked for one place, in `passwords`); accepting spaces or other digits in a code here (the endpoint may tidy input in M3f-3; the primitive compares only the canonical form).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/{totp (new),blob,passwords,enrol}.py`, `app/accounts/views.py`, tests (`app/accounts/tests/test_auth_totp.py` new; `test_auth_blob.py`, `test_auth_passwords.py`, `test_enrol_route_live.py`, `test_login_password_live.py`).
- **Versions:** none new (stdlib `hmac`, `secrets`, `unicodedata`; `cryptography` 50.0.2 as before).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier, Opus 5.5) within plan M3f and the PR 115 R1 P3 #1 correction; the split as recorded above.


## 2026-11-06 — Secret scan: allowlist for bare domain-separation labels (PR 117 R1 P1)
- **Problem:** CI `security` has failed on `main` since 2026-11-05 (run 42000260805) and on PR 117 (run 42138355018): gitleaks' default `generic-api-key` rule reads the HKDF label `skiw-factor-secret-v1` in review and log prose on `agent-logs` (`reviews/PR-116.md:59`, `log/2026-11-05_PR116_m3f-1-totp-primitives.md:90`) as a credential. The step exits 2, so "Secret rule test", the two dependency audits and the registry Semgrep steps never ran (UNI-13).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-11-06, asked at `fix` 117; option "Allowlist the label pattern", the reviewer's recommendation):** one `[[allowlists]]` entry in `.gitleaks.toml`: `targetRules = ["generic-api-key"]`, `regexTarget = "secret"`, regex `^skiw(?:-[a-z0-9]+)+-v[0-9]+$`. It is anchored on the whole captured secret, so only a bare `skiw-<words>-v<N>` label is skipped; a value next to a label, or a label with a value appended, is still a finding. It is not tied to a path or commit, so future review and log prose that quotes a label (the PR 117 review is already archived with one) does not trip it again. `skiw-secret-key-assignment` is untouched.
- **Tests** (`infra/host/tests/test_gitleaks_config.py`, run by `make secrets-test`): four labels in prose pass; a random value assigned next to a label is flagged (the finding is the value); a label with a random value appended is flagged. Mutants: allowlist removed → the prose test fails; anchors removed → the appended-value test fails. A full-history `--all` scan of a fresh clone (all remote branches, including `agent-logs` with the PR 117 archive) finds no leaks with this config; without it, 2 (3 with the PR 117 archive).
- **Alternatives rejected:** allowlisting commits d70f67b9 and f99952d0 (weaker; the next review that quotes a label repeats the failure); a path allowlist for `agent-logs` files (the repo rule: no path exemptions, PR 9 review R1).
- **Scope / affects:** `.gitleaks.toml`, `infra/host/tests/test_gitleaks_config.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`. **Versions:** none new (gitleaks v8.30.1 as pinned). **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (the choice), builder (the regex and tests).

## 2026-11-06 — M3f-2: recovery codes (`accounts.auth.recovery`)
- **Codes** (spec D7; plan M3f [A]): `COUNT` = 10 per user, each 16 Crockford base32 symbols (`0-9A-Z` without I, L, O, U) from `secrets.choice`, 32^16 = 2^80, shown once as `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`. `COUNT` is the spec's value, a constant like M3f-1's `DIGITS` (UNI-02).
- **Normalisation** [A] (UNI-03): longer than `INPUT_MAX` = 32 characters is refused before anything else; then ASCII only (before `upper()`, which maps `ı` to `I`), case ignored, hyphens and ASCII spaces dropped anywhere, I and L read as 1 and O as 0; exactly 16 alphabet symbols. Refused: U, other punctuation or whitespace (tab, newline), any non-ASCII character (full-width, Cyrillic, Unicode hyphens), 15 or 17 symbols. No NFKC: the page shows ASCII only.
- **Storage:** one `user_factor` row per code (kind `recovery`); `secret_ct` = HMAC-SHA-256 under `tenant_source_key` over `skiw-recovery-v1` ‖ field(user id) ‖ field(normalised code), the M2c length prefix (`source_key._field` becomes the public `field`, reused, not copied): 32 raw bytes with no version. The newest version signs; a use matches under every listed version in one `secret_ct = ANY(%s)`. No label is a prefix of the session or throttle labels (the throttle HMACs have no length prefix). Removing a key version voids the codes made under it (runbook `host-rebuild.md`, "Rotating `tenant_source_key`").
- **API, run in the caller's `with_agency()` transaction** [A] (as `revoke.mark`): so M3f-3 can write the AUTH-07 marker in the transaction that regenerates; outside an atomic block both raise `TransactionManagementError` rather than get RLS's empty answer. `generate(agency, user)` locks the `app_user` row (`FOR UPDATE`), deletes every recovery row of the user and inserts ten, so two regenerations take turns and leave exactly ten (without the lock they left twenty). `consume(user, candidate)` is one `DELETE … RETURNING id` (plan): a concurrent second use waits on the row lock and then finds no row. Scope = RLS + `user_id` (user ids are global keys), as `revoke.revoked`. No `remaining()` until a caller needs it (M3i). `consume` must commit before `login()`, which refuses to run inside an open atomic block: M3f-3 calls it in its own `with_agency()` block.
- **No constant-time compare:** the match is Postgres equality on a keyed MAC; timing could only say how far the MAC of the attacker's own guess agrees with a stored one, which without the key says nothing about any code.
- **Not here (M3f-3):** the second-factor endpoint, the pending-state checks, `login()`, the AUTH-07 marker on regeneration, throttling of failed uses, and any per-kind `secret_ct` length CHECK (it must be per kind: recovery rows are 32 bytes, TOTP 48; `isolation_cases.py` inserts a 32-byte `totp` row and changes with it).
- **Grants:** none new: `accounts.0007` grants `user_factor` SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ("recovery-code use"), and `test_queue_grants` lists it for DELETE. No Semgrep rule restricts `user_factor` writes, and `accounts.auth` is on the TEN-10 allow-list.
- **Tests:** `test_auth_recovery.py` (new, no DB): alphabet and 80 bits; 7 accepted forms; I/L/O; 13 refused forms; an over-long candidate refused before any method runs; MACs equal a hand-built construction, newest version first; bound to user and code; label prefix-free against the other source-key labels; both functions refuse outside a transaction. `test_auth_recovery_live.py` (new, skiw_app): ten codes shown, only their MACs stored; each code once, any accepted form, a wrong one deletes nothing; 10 parallel uses → one success; 10 parallel regenerations → exactly one set of ten; regenerating voids every old code; another user's code, agency B's code, a MAC copied to another user, and a generate for agency B's user (RLS, FK) all refused with nothing changed; a v1 code works with v2 newest, is refused once v1 is removed, new codes are signed by v2; no code in logs (DEBUG), captured SQL or the row's str/repr; a TOTP row is neither consumed nor deleted.
- **Mutants:** bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared, 19 mutants of `recovery.py` (length check missing or after `isascii`; no `isascii`; U read as V; `strip()` for separator removal; `>=` 16; wrong label; user id left out of the MAC; no length prefix; newest version only in `consume`; oldest version signs; no `kind` filter in the use or in the replace; use always True; select-then-delete; no user lock; no delete before insert; no transaction check; 9 codes). Baseline 36 passed; the first run left three alive (oldest signs, both `kind` filters), killed by `test_only_recovery_rows_are_used_or_replaced` and by generating the rotation test's fresh codes with both versions listed. Final run: 19 of 19 killed, baseline 37 passed.
- **Why:** spec v1.21 D7, Flow 5.1 (2), §4.1 `user_factor` (C3), UNI-03, UNI-09, TEN-08, TEN-09; plan M3 §3 M3f, §4 M3f row; the M3f-1 split.
- **Alternatives rejected:** functions that open their own `with_agency()` (M3f-3 could not commit the AUTH-07 marker with the regeneration); an agency filter beside RLS in `consume` (cannot be tested apart from RLS); a version prefix in `secret_ct` (the plan says the MAC; it would only let an operator count affected users at rotation); `compare_digest` over fetched rows (a second query and a read-then-delete race for nothing); a distinctness loop in `generate` (≈ 2^-74).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/recovery.py` (new), `app/accounts/source_key.py` (`_field` → `field`), `app/accounts/tests/test_auth_recovery.py` and `test_auth_recovery_live.py` (new), `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (stdlib `hmac`, `hashlib`, `secrets`).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier: design and code drafted by an Opus sub-agent, files written, run and mutation-tested by the Sonnet builder session) within plan M3f and the M3f-1 split.

## 2026-11-06 — M3f-3a: recovery-code key version in `secret_ct`, per-kind `secret_ct` lengths (PR 117 R1 P2)
- **Why this PR first:** M3f-3 is six PRs (3a, 3b-1, 3b-2, 3c-1, 3c-2, plus a CI fix "S"), ordered so that no half-built flow can make an active user without a second factor and no route generates recovery codes before this lands. The plan's ≈ 350 lines for M3f became ≈ 1,600 across the six; each stays under the §1 split rule.
- **Recovery rows carry their key version** (PR 117 R1 P2, an owner-visible operations gap): `secret_ct` = the `tenant_source_key` version that signed the code, 4 bytes big-endian (`RECOVERY_VERSION_BYTES`; versions run to 999999, so 1 or 2 bytes would not hold them), then the 32-byte MAC: 36 bytes. The version is in clear and outside the MAC: it is no secret (a session row names its version too), and a row whose version was changed matches no candidate. Before this, SQL could not say which users a version still signs, so removing a version from the key file voided codes with no way to count them first. `generate` stores the newest version's form, `consume` matches under every listed version in one `= ANY(%s)`; neither changed apart from `macs()`.
- **Per-kind CHECK constraints** (`accounts.0010`, `user_factor_totp_secret_ct_length`, `user_factor_recovery_secret_ct_length`): a TOTP row's `secret_ct` is exactly 48 bytes (`seal_factor_secret`) and a recovery row's exactly 36, neither NULL; other kinds are not checked (passkeys are M3g). Each is `NOT (kind = k) OR (length(secret_ct) = n AND secret_ct IS NOT NULL)`, since `length(NULL) = n` is NULL and a CHECK passes on NULL. No host holds a factor row yet (no route writes one before 3b-2), so the migration validates an empty table and takes a short lock; `lintmigrations --warnings-as-errors` passes.
- **Operator report:** `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` "Rotating `tenant_source_key`" now gives the SQL that counts users per signing version, a second SELECT that lists the emails of one version's users, the rule for a planned rotation (remove a version only when it no longer appears) and the emergency path (codes under it are void; tell the users). Both are a break-glass read as `postgres` (RLS hides the rows from `skiw_readonly`; one SELECT, no writes). `pg_hba` asks `postgres` for a password on the socket, so the block builds a 0600 `PGPASSFILE` from `/run/secrets/postgres_password`, removed on exit, as `restore.md` does (PR 119 R1 P2: the first draft ran `psql -U postgres` with no password and failed). Unit tests pin the runbook text to the exact SQL the live tests run, and the first block to the password-file wrapper, so the two cannot drift. The blocks were also run verbatim against a `postgres:17` container with the host `pg_hba.conf` rules and a password containing `:` and `\`: both printed their result, exit 0, no password file left behind. The `::bigint` cast is defensive: a version is at most 999999, so the top byte is 0 and `<< 24` cannot reach the sign bit; not mutated.
- **Fixtures:** `isolation_cases.py` inserted a 32-byte `totp` row (M3f-2) and now inserts `TOTP_SECRET_CT_LENGTH`; `test_user_tables_live.py` defaults `add_factor` to `passkey`, which has no length rule; `test_only_recovery_rows_are_used_or_replaced` now puts the recovery MAC in a `passkey` row (a `totp` row cannot hold one) and a real 48-byte `totp` row beside it.
- **Minimum password length:** the owner confirmed 12 (`SKIW_PW_MIN_LENGTH`) on 2026-11-06 ("Yes correct 12 min and build"), answering PR 115 R1's open question; recorded here so the reviewer can close it. The builder cannot relay it to the reviewer.
- **Mutants:** bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared. 13 on code and tests: prefix of 1 byte, of 2 bytes, little-endian, none; version paired with the wrong key; `consume` newest only; `generate` stores the last (oldest) form; TOTP length 47; recovery length 32; `CONSUME` and `DELETE_ALL` without the kind filter; the report's `get_byte` index changed; `isolation_cases.py` back to 32 bytes. 5 on the migration, each applied by migrating `accounts` back to 0009 and forward on the real database: no NULL guard on `totp`, none on `recovery`, the kind test not negated on each, lengths swapped. 18 of 18 killed (baselines 104 and 74 passed). PR 119 R1 fix: 6 more (runbook without `PGPASSFILE`, without the secret file, the user list outside the wrapper, the count SQL edited; the user list with a 3-byte prefix and without the kind filter): 6 of 6 killed. An edit of the model alone is not a mutant: the database keeps the migration's constraints.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a version column on `user_factor` (a schema change for every kind, for one kind's need); the version inside the MAC input only (SQL could not read it); a 1- or 2-byte version; one table-wide `secret_ct` length (passkey rows differ and are M3g's); a `CHECK` that only bounds the length (a swapped kind would pass); a re-sealing job for existing rows (none exist).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/factor_model.py`, `app/accounts/auth/recovery.py`, `app/accounts/migrations/0010_factor_secret_lengths.py` (new), the three accounts test files and `isolation_cases.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (deep tier: design and diffs drafted by an Opus sub-agent, written, run and mutation-tested by the Sonnet builder session) within plan M3f; the six-PR split and the D8 deferral (factor add, remove and regenerate on active sessions go to M3h) are flagged to the owner and the reviewer.

## 2026-11-06 — Secret scan: narrow the label allowlist (PR 118 R1 P2)
- **Decision:** the `generic-api-key` allowlist for domain-separation labels is now `^skiw(?:-[a-z]{2,12}){1,4}-v[0-9]{1,6}$` (was `^skiw(?:-[a-z0-9]+)+-v[0-9]+$`): one to four lowercase words of 2 to 12 letters and a version of at most six digits. All 15 labels in `app/`, `infra/`, `docs/` and `scripts/` still match (the longest word is `credential`, 10 letters; at most 3 words). The old pattern accepted a segment of any length and any digits, so `client_secret = "skiw-<32 random hex>-v1"` was skipped (the reviewer probed it: flagged under main's config, silent under PR 118's). The gate should be no wider than the labels.
- **Tests** (`infra/host/tests/test_gitleaks_config.py`, `make secrets-test`): 13 (was 5). New: a hex value in the label's outline is flagged under `client_secret` and under `api_key`; four shapes outside the bounds are flagged (one 40-letter word, twelve 3-letter words, a 30-digit version, a 12-digit hex word); a value in front of a label is flagged; a value on the same line as a label is flagged (the finding is the value); the prose test now covers all 15 labels. The first five fail under the old regex (confirmed before changing the config).
- **Values for the generic rule are derived from fixed hashes, not random.** `generic-api-key` skips a value that contains a common word, so a random value is not flagged on every run: in a 40-run loop of the PR 118 tests, 2 of ~26 runs failed (two different tests, `token_urlsafe(24)`), and 40 random letters were missed about 1 run in 6. The values come from `sha512` of a fixed label string: the same every run, no high-entropy literal in the source, and each is a finding under the pinned gitleaks v8.30.1. The custom rule's test keeps random values (that rule has no word filter). 40 of 40 runs clean after the change.
- **Mutants:** 11 of the regex and config: old pattern, word length `{2,64}`, word count `{1,40}`, version `[0-9]+`, digits in words, no `^`, no `$`, allowlist off: killed. Three survive and are equivalent: one-letter words `{1,12}`, zero words `{0,4}`, and a 9-letter cap (drops `credential`). Without the allowlist gitleaks flags only 3 of the 15 labels in prose (`skiw-enrol-v1`, `skiw-factor-secret-v1`, `skiw-throttle-ip-v1`), so a bound that only matters for the others cannot change a result; a too-narrow regex would instead turn the CI `security` job red, which is the safe direction.
- **Alternatives rejected:** an explicit list of the 15 labels (every new label then needs a config change and a PR; the shape bound is enough); `stopwords` or a path exemption (PR 9 R1: no path exemptions); keeping random test values and retrying (hides the flake instead of removing it).
- **Scope / affects:** `.gitleaks.toml`, `infra/host/tests/test_gitleaks_config.py`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none new (gitleaks stays v8.30.1).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session, with the regex the reviewer proposed in PR 118 R1 P2, widened from `{2,12}` words only by the version bound `{1,6}`, which the reviewer also gave); standalone PR "S" of the M3f-3 sequence.

## 2026-11-07 — App image: re-pin `python:3.12.15-alpine` to the rebuilt digest (UNI-13, PR 126 R2 P1)
- **Decision:** `Dockerfile` line 12 now pins `python:3.12.15-alpine@sha256:b658b05ce9c4eb97ec6fc19424818a1f31993659c2b505a532f470e83807815d` (Alpine 3.24.3, `musl` and `musl-utils` 1.2.6-r3, `zlib` 1.3.2-r1). It replaces `@sha256:1b668429…` (Alpine 3.24.2, `musl` 1.2.6-r2, `zlib` 1.3.2-r0). Same Python version, same tag; only the digest changed.
- **Why:** the CI `image` job (Trivy v0.75.0, `--severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 1`) began failing on 2026-11-07 on three fixed HIGH findings in the old digest: `musl` and `musl-utils` CVE-2026-107891 (fixed in 1.2.6-r3) and `zlib` CVE-2026-85091 (fixed in 1.3.2-r1). Main had been green at `d1b7abb` the day before; nothing in the repo changed, the vulnerability database did. PR 126's review (R2 P1) found the red gate.
- **Evidence:** the image built from this Dockerfile, scanned with `aquasec/trivy:0.75.0 image --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 1`: exit 0, `alpine 3.24.3` 0 findings, every `python-pkg` target 0. No allow-list, no `.trivyignore`, no `apk upgrade` layer.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `python:3.12.16-alpine` (a Python patch bump, a larger change than a security re-pin; Dependabot proposes version moves); an `apk upgrade` layer (leaves the pinned digest not reproducing the shipped image); a `.trivyignore` entry (the findings are fixed upstream).
- **Follow-up:** the dump image (`infra/host/backup/predeploy/Dockerfile`, its own postgres alpine pin) is scanned by the same job; its scan step was skipped in the failed PR 126 run (the app-image scan failed first), so it was built and scanned here too with the same Trivy command: exit 0, `alpine 3.24.3`, 0 findings. Not changed.
- **Scope / affects:** `Dockerfile`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** python base image digest only (3.12.15 unchanged).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), as the remediation the PR 126 reviewer required.

## 2026-11-07 — M3 plan: TOTP required, passkey optional; M3f-3 as six PRs; factor changes on active sessions to M3h
- **Decision:** the owner's answers of 2026-11-06 to the reviewer (`.agent/reviews/owner-decisions-m3f-3.md`, "1a 2ok 3yes 4yes") are now in the plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md`, amended 2026-11-07): (1) **TOTP is required for every user, owner and admin included; a passkey is optional for everyone** (spec v1.22, PR 124: D7, T5, Flow 5.1 (4), M3 row, cutover step; `PROJECT.md` reconciled by PR 125); (2) the TOTP issuer is the fixed string `SKIW Assistant`, the account name the user's email, label `SKIW Assistant:<email>`, not configurable, not the agency name; (3) M3f-3 is six PRs, S, 3a, 3b-1, 3b-2, 3c-1, 3c-2 (≈ 1 600 lines against the plan's ≈ 350 for all of M3f); (4) adding, removing or regenerating a factor on an *active* session is deferred to M3h behind step-up (D8).
- **Accepted risk (owner, 2026-11-06):** TOTP can be phished in real time, so an owner or admin account is protected by TOTP alone. The reviewer recommended keeping passkeys required for owner/admin; the owner declined. Recorded in spec T5.
- **Plan rows changed:** AUTH-03 row (the passkey half removed; AUTH-03 itself stays deferred to M4-rest), the M3f to M3i rows, the M3f bullets (six-PR sequence, issuer label), M3h (D8 actions carry AUTH-07 with a test, as the reviewer required; step-up by TOTP or an enrolled passkey), M3i (enrol TOTP, optional passkey), the §4 M3f and M3i tests (a user with no TOTP is not active and cannot reach data, replacing the deferred owner/admin passkey test), the §5 auth row, Q9 (AUTH-03 only), §9 item 11 and the Done-when gap note.
- **Consequence not stated by the owner:** step-up re-verifies TOTP when no passkey is enrolled (spec v1.22, reviewed in Round 26).
- **Until M3h:** a lost factor needs the owner-performed reset (Flow 5.1), and the runbook's emergency-rotation step "generate new ones at their next sign-in" cannot be done by users. Neither matters while the real-data gate is locked.
- **Not decided here:** whether M3g (optional passkeys) moves after M3i; the D20 wording in the spec (Round 26 P3, next spec touch).
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`. No code.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner (answers 1a, 2 ok, 3 yes, 4 yes, 2026-11-06, relayed by the reviewer); written by the Sonnet builder session.

## 2026-11-07 — M3f-3b-1: `accounts.auth.factors`, first-factor TOTP enrolment (no route)
- **Decision:** `accounts.auth.factors` enrols a user's first second factor, TOTP, in two calls. `begin_totp` makes a 20-byte secret and keeps it in the session **sealed** (`blob.seal_factor_secret`, bound to a new factor id, the user and the agency), so the session row holds no readable secret (C3); it returns the base32 text and the `otpauth://` URI. `confirm_totp` checks the first code and, in one `with_agency()` transaction, locks the user (`invited` only), inserts the `user_factor` row (the same sealed bytes, 48 long; `sign_count` = the accepted step, so the enrolling code cannot be replayed at sign-in), sets the user `active`, replaces the recovery codes and writes the user-wide `session_revocation` marker (AUTH-07). Any failure rolls back all of it, so no user is `active` without a factor and ten codes. The pending keys are cleared after the commit (login() keeps them, DECISIONS M3e-2). No route: 3b-2 adds the two enrolment endpoints and calls `login()`.
- **Owner decisions applied (2026-11-06):** TOTP is required and is the first factor; the key URI is `otpauth://totp/SKIW%20Assistant:<email>` with `issuer=SKIW Assistant`, SHA1, 6 digits, 30 s; the issuer is fixed, not the agency name. The email is percent-encoded with `+` kept out (`%20` for spaces).
- **Design calls [A]:** (1) the unconfirmed secret lives in the session, not in a table row: there is no "pending" column and a row without a confirmed code would be a factor that is not one; (2) wrong first codes are counted per **user** (`throttle.factor_blocked` / `record_factor_failure`, key = HMAC of `skiw-throttle-factor-v1` over the user id, the existing `SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_ACCOUNT` and `SKIW_AUTH_WINDOW_MIN`, no new setting), because the user is known after the password and a password holder guessing from many addresses is still counted; windowed, never reset by a success, as the account counter; 3c-1 uses the same helpers for the sign-in code; (3) a malformed or expired pending state is cleared when read; (4) no `security_event` kind for a wrong factor code yet: events carry an email and an IP HMAC, which a factor attempt does not hold; 3c-1 decides with the route in view.
- **Not here:** adding, removing or regenerating a factor on an active session (D8, M3h behind step-up, with its AUTH-07 test); the sign-in second factor (3c-1, 3c-2); the enrolment routes (3b-2).
- **Alternatives rejected:** a `pending` factor row (needs a column and a sweep, and an unconfirmed secret then sits in a tenant table); keeping the raw secret in the session (the MAC protects the row against change, not against reading); a new setting for the factor limit (one more value to bound for no use yet).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/factors.py` (new), `app/accounts/auth/totp.py` (`text`, `uri`, `ISSUER`), `app/accounts/auth/throttle.py` (`factor_*`), tests, this log. No migration, no dependency, no setting, no runbook change.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on the owner's answers of 2026-11-06 and the plan amended by PR 126.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3b-2: the two TOTP enrolment routes
- **Decision:** `POST /api/auth/enrol/totp` (name `auth-enrol-totp`) calls `factors.begin_totp` and answers 200 `{secret, uri}`. `POST /api/auth/enrol/totp/confirm` (name `auth-enrol-totp-confirm`) takes `{code}`, calls `factors.confirm_totp` and, on success, calls `login(request, user, factor="totp")` and answers 200 `{next: "signed_in", recovery_codes, csrf}`; `csrf` is the rotated token (`get_token` after `login`). Both are public (`auth="cookie"`, CSRF enforced): the caller holds only the pending enrolment session. Every answer is `no-store`.
- **Statuses:** no enrolment session, a login-style pending session, a signed-in session or a pending state past `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN` → 401 `enrol-expired` and no cookie; wrong code → 401 `code-wrong`; too many wrong codes for the user → 429 `limited` (checked before the code is verified, so a right code during the block is also refused); a body with no text `code` → 400 `invalid`, counted nowhere.
- **Design calls [A]:** (1) **no extension on expiry.** The 5-minute window ends the enrolment; the password stays set and the user stays `invited`. Recovery is the operator's invite again (new link, new password); the runbook says so (UNI-16). Extending the window on each call would let a stolen enrolment cookie stay alive. (2) The recovery codes appear **only** in the confirm answer. `login()` runs after the commit, outside the atomic block; if it failed after the commit the user would be `active` with a factor and normal sign-in would work, but the codes would be lost and the owner's reset would be needed. This is accepted: `login()` has no step that can fail on a user that was just read, and holding the codes anywhere to resend them would be worse. (3) `_fields` now reads the body through a new `_body` (JSON object or form post, else `None`), shared with the confirm route; behaviour of the existing routes is unchanged.
- **Tests:** a full flow over HTTP to a signed-in `totp` session; both routes refused without an enrolment session and with a login-style one; wrong codes to `limited`; malformed bodies counted nowhere; form post; second begin replaces the first secret; expiry with the `clock` fixture and a new invite end to end. Carried from the PR 128 review (P3): two sessions confirming at once enrol one factor and ten codes (killed the mutant without `select_for_update` in 5 of 5 runs).
- **Not here:** the sign-in second factor (3c-1, 3c-2); factor changes on an active session (D8, M3h).
- **Alternatives rejected:** extending `PENDING_UNTIL` on begin (above); returning the codes from a separate `GET` (they would have to be kept somewhere); one route doing begin and confirm (the secret must be shown before a code can exist).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/views.py`, `app/config/urls.py`, `app/tests/test_route_registry.py` (`PUBLIC_ROUTES`), `app/tests/csrf_fixtures.py`, tests, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md` ("Enrol a user": TOTP is required, the 5-minute window, re-invite on expiry, codes shown once). No migration, no dependency, no setting.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on the plan M3 §3 M3f and the owner's answers of 2026-11-06.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3c-1a: the second-factor attempt count is atomic (PR 128 R1 P2)
- **Split [A]:** plan M3f-3c-1 (≈ 350) came to +531 lines with `second_factor.attempt` and its tests, past the plan's ≈ 450 split rule. So it is two PRs in order: **3c-1a** (this: the atomic count, `confirm_totp` on it, the parallel tests, the two runbook lines from PR 129 R1) and **3c-1b** (`accounts.auth.second_factor.attempt`, no route; its own entry). 3c-2 (the route) follows.
- **Decision:** `throttle.factor_reserve(user)` counts a second-factor attempt BEFORE the code is checked, in one statement: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET count = count + 1 WHERE count < SKIW_AUTH_FAILS_PER_ACCOUNT RETURNING count`. The conflicting upsert takes the row lock and re-reads the count, so of K concurrent attempts exactly the limit are let through and the rest get no row back (`limited`), with nothing changed. It runs in autocommit and raises inside an atomic block (a rolled-back transaction would take the count with it and a guess would go uncounted). `confirm_totp` (enrolment) uses it; `factor_blocked` and `record_factor_failure` are gone, because the check-then-record shape they made is what PR 128's review refused.
- **A success gives its attempt back** (`factor_release` decrements the window the attempt was counted in, never below zero), as does a fault and an "unavailable" answer. So only wrong codes stay counted, as in the account counter, and a user who signs in many times in 15 minutes is never limited by their own sign-ins. Cost accepted: a legitimate sign-in gives an attacker who holds the password one extra guess in flight (3 valid codes in 10⁶ per guess). TOTP and recovery codes will share the one per-user budget (3c-1b).
- **Lost recovery codes (PR 129 R1 P3, restated):** they can be lost because a database failure in `login()` (`cycle_key` writes a session row) or in the session save after the view returns happens after the enrolment has committed. Accepted: the user stays active with TOTP and can regenerate later (M3h).
- **Still owed to 3c-1b (PR 128 R1 P3 #1), the reason for the factor event:** the PR 128 entry said events "carry an email and an IP HMAC, which a factor attempt does not hold". That was wrong, both columns are nullable. The real reason, to be decided and written with the sign-in step, is that `security_event` is for events before a tenant is known (spec §4.1) and a second-factor attempt has the user and agency, so it belongs in `audit_event` (M5).
- **Tests:** 7 in `test_factor_count_live.py`, including K = 3 × limit parallel wrong codes giving exactly the limit `wrong_code` answers and the rest `limited`. Runbook "Enrol a user": lost codes point to regenerate (M3h) and the owner's reset only if the authenticator is lost too; the throttle is not lifted by a new invite (PR 129 R1 P3).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/throttle.py`, `app/accounts/auth/factors.py` (`confirm_totp`), tests, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, this log. No migration, no dependency, no setting.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a per-user advisory or row lock across check and record (holds a lock across a TOTP check and an open transaction); counting successes too (locks out users who sign in often).
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on PR 128 R1 P2 and PR 129 R1 P3.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3c-1b: the second-factor soft-lock and owner alert (PR 130 R1 P2)
- **Split [A]:** with the sign-in attempt, the lock and the fault rules, the PR measured +650 lines, past the plan's ≈ 450. So the sign-in step is one PR further on: **3c-1b** (this: the lock and its alert, as primitives with their own tests, no caller yet), then **3c-1c** (`accounts.auth.second_factor.attempt`, no route, which calls them and carries PR 130 R1 P3 #1 and PR 128 R1 P3 #1), then 3c-2 (the route). PR 130 R1 named the sign-in step for the P2; the condition is that the lock exists and is tested before any route can sign in, which this order keeps.
- **Decision (PR 130 R1 P2; Flow 5.1 "Failures: 10 consecutive fails → account soft-lock 30 min + owner alert") [A]:** the spec sentence is read as covering the second factor too. The budget alone (5 wrong codes per 15 minutes, 480 a day) lets a holder of the password guess all day unseen, and the password counter is cleared at every verified password, so it never sees them. `throttle.factor_failed(user, email, ip)` counts the user's consecutive wrong codes in one row (HMAC of the user id under its own label, `window_start` = RUN, never windowed). Each `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_AFTER`-th (10) writes a marker row; `factor_reserve` refuses (returns None, changes nothing) while a marker is newer than `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` (30 minutes). It also writes `factor.locked` to `security_event` (HMACs of the email and IP, as `account.locked` does, and the count) and a `second factor soft-locked after N consecutive wrong codes` warning: that is the owner alert, the same mechanism as `account.locked`. `factor_reset` (a verified code) ends the run.
- **Properties:** the lock is the user's own, so it survives a new password step and `reset_on_success` does not touch it. It is not the password step's lock: the password step stays open (the user must reach the second step; nothing is guessed there). The lock is read before the count, not with it, so attempts already in flight when it is set are bounded by the count (the hard cap). Enrolment's wrong codes do not feed it (they guess a secret the user just made); a locked user is `limited` at enrolment too, because both use `factor_reserve`. Concurrent failures get distinct consecutive numbers from the upsert, so each multiple of the threshold locks once.
- **Effect, stated plainly:** a known-password attacker still gets 5 guesses per window, about 10 per 45 minutes with the lock, so the lock is mainly the alert (sustained guessing becomes visible), not a large cut in guesses.
- **Correction to the 3c-1a entry (PR 130 R1 P3 #2):** its "one extra guess in flight" cost for a success giving its attempt back does not exist. The count is wrong codes plus attempts in flight, capped at the limit, and only non-wrong outcomes give back, so wrong codes per user per window never exceed the limit with or without the user's own sign-ins. The decision stands. Fixed windows still let up to 2 × the limit through around a window boundary, as for the account counter.
- **Runbook:** "Enrol a user" gains the lock and its alert (what it is, where it shows, what to do), and the factor-throttle line says sign-in checks it from M3f-3c (PR 130 R1 P3 #2).
- **Tests:** `test_factor_lock_live.py` (6): the lock at the 10th wrong code with the event and the warning (no raw id, email or IP), refusing and changing nothing until `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` after the marker (to the second), a new budget window not lifting it, the run consecutive across windows, a verified code ending the run, one user's lock not another's and not the password lock's (and surviving `reset_on_success`), HMAC-only event columns, and 8 parallel failures counting 8 and locking at 3 and 6 once each. 11 mutants killed.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/throttle.py` (`factor_failed`, `factor_reset`, the lock check in `factor_reserve`), `app/accounts/auth/events.py` (kind `factor.locked`), the new test, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, this log. No migration (`security_event.kind` has no constraint), no dependency, no setting.
- **Alternatives rejected:** feeding the existing password lock (`reset_on_success` clears it at every verified password); locking the password step as well (the user could not reach the second step, and nothing is guessed there); a lock without an alert (the point is that someone is told); one atomic statement for lock check and count (a second counter and a lock-aware upsert for a case the count already bounds).
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on PR 130 R1 P2 and P3 #2; the reading of Flow 5.1 "Failures" is a builder call [A] for the reviewer and Ying to overrule.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3c-1c: the second-factor attempt (PR 128 R1 P3 #1, PR 130 R1 P3 #1)
- **Split [A]:** the attempt with the lock wiring, the transaction guards and the lock tests measured +590, past the plan's ≈ 450. So **3c-1c** (this: `accounts.auth.second_factor.attempt`, no route, count and fault rules) and **3c-1d** (feeds wrong codes to the factor soft-lock from `attempt`, the guards on `factor_failed`/`factor_reset`, the wiring tests and the alert wording of PR 131 R1). The lock exists and is tested (3c-1b); nothing can sign in before 3c-2, which comes after 3c-1d.
- **Decision:** `attempt(request, candidate)` checks one code for the user a password step left pending (not an enrolment's). A TOTP code is exactly six ASCII digits, anything else is a recovery code. Order: the pending state; `factor_reserve` (the count, before anything is checked; `limited` over the limit or while the factor lock holds); then in one `with_agency()` transaction the user must still be `active` and the code is compared; a verified code clears the pending state and gives its attempt back, and the caller then calls `login()` with the factor. TOTP: a conditional `UPDATE … SET sign_count = step WHERE sign_count < step` whose row count decides, so of two parallel submissions of one code one passes (a read-then-write check passes both). Recovery: `recovery.consume` (`DELETE … RETURNING`). A user with no TOTP row runs `totp.accept` on a throwaway secret and cannot pass. Using a recovery code writes no revocation marker (a use of the factor, not a change to the factor set; AUTH-07, plan M3f lists adding, removing and regenerating only).
- **Fault rule (PR 130 R1 P3 #1):** only a fault BEFORE the comparison gives the attempt back. `_check` records, inside the transaction, that the code was compared and was wrong; if leaving the transaction then raises, the count stays, so a failure an attacker can provoke does not hand every guess back. A fault after a right code rolls the use back, so the attempt is given back and the code still works.
- **The real reason for no per-attempt factor `security_event` (PR 128 R1 P3 #1):** PR 128's reason (the HMAC columns are required) was wrong; both are nullable. `security_event` is for events before a tenant is known (spec §4.1). A second-factor attempt happens after the password with the user and agency known, so per-attempt evidence belongs in `audit_event` (M5, not built): M5 adds `login.success` and the failed-factor evidence there. The one event written for the factor is `factor.locked` (3c-1b), because the lock's alert goes where `account.locked` goes.
- **Tests:** `test_second_factor_live.py` (27): replay and older steps, ±1 and ±2 skew, parallel same code (one passes), a recovery code once in any spelling and in parallel, non-codes, the no-TOTP-row path, pending/expired/inactive unavailable and uncounted, the limit including the shared budget, success not using the budget, parallel wrong codes (exactly the limit), faults before and after the comparison. Mutants: see the PR (one equivalent: dropping `last` from `totp.accept` changes nothing, because the conditional update also refuses a replay).
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/second_factor.py` (new), its test, this log. No migration, no dependency, no setting.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a read-then-write TOTP replay check (passes two parallel submissions); releasing on every fault (a provoked failure would hide guesses); a per-attempt `security_event` (pre-tenant table).
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on PR 128 R1 P3 #1 and PR 130 R1 P3 #1.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3c-1d: the attempt feeds the factor lock (PR 131 R1 P2, P3 #1, P3 #2; PR 132 R1 P2)
- **Decision:** `second_factor.attempt` calls `throttle.factor_failed(user, email, ip)` for every wrong code, TOTP or recovery, including a wrong code whose transaction then failed on leaving (the guess stays counted, so it also stays in the run), and `throttle.factor_reset(user)` for a verified one. So ten wrong codes in a row, across any number of windows, lock both kinds and the right code for `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN` and write one `factor.locked` event; a verified code ends the run. A fault before the comparison and an `unavailable` result touch neither the run nor the lock. Enrolment's wrong codes still do not feed the run, but a locked user is refused there too (3c-1b).
- **The email comes from the `app_user` row (PR 132 R1 P2):** `factor_failed` needs it for the event's HMACs. `_check` reads `user.email` from the row it already loads under `with_agency(state.agency_id)` (the agency from the MAC-verified pending session) and keeps it in `_Seen`; no request input reaches the event. The IP is `throttle.client_ip(request)`, the same as the password step.
- **Guards (PR 131 R1 P3 #1):** `factor_failed` and `factor_reset` refuse an open transaction, as `factor_reserve` does (shared `_outside_transactions`). Inside a caller's transaction a rollback would take the run, the marker and the event with it, and wrong codes would never reach the lock.
- **Wording (PR 131 R1 P3 #2):** the lock's alert is the M3 stand-in for the owner alert (owner Q4 (a)): an event and a warning nobody is sent until that path exists. The runbook now gives the checks (`compose logs --since 24h web | grep 'soft-locked'`, or the `security_event` count) and says there is no password change yet, so repeated locks for one user mean telling Ying.
- **A double-click costs one count (PR 132 R1 design call 4):** of two parallel submissions of one right code the loser comes back `wrong_code` and is counted and fed to the lock; the winner gives its attempt back. Ten double-clicks in a row is a lock; that is accepted.
- **Tests:** wiring through `attempt` (10 consecutive wrong codes alternating TOTP and recovery across windows: both kinds and the right code `limited`, exactly one `factor.locked` event whose HMACs match the user's email and the request IP, one warning without the user or email; the right code works after `SKIW_AUTH_LOCK_MIN`; a success resets the run; the lock is the user's own and survives a new password step; parallel wrong codes lock once and lose no count; enrolment's wrong codes do not feed the run), faults after a wrong TOTP and a wrong recovery code keep the count and feed the run, the double-click, the guards. Mutants: see the PR.
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts.auth.second_factor`, `accounts.auth.throttle` (guards, docstrings), two live test files, the runbook ("Enrol a user" lock bullet), this log. No migration, no dependency, no setting.
- **Alternatives rejected:** feeding the run from `throttle.factor_reserve` (counts attempts, not wrong codes); reading the email from request input or the session (not trusted); a lock event per wrong code (the lock event is the alert).
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on PR 131 R1 and PR 132 R1.

## 2026-11-08 — M3f-3c-2: the second-factor route (PR 129 R1 P2, PR 132 R1 P3, PR 133 R1)
- **Decision:** `POST /api/auth/second-factor` (`accounts.views.login_second_factor`, name `auth-second-factor`, `auth="cookie"`, JSON or form `{"code": "…"}`) calls `second_factor.attempt` and, on `Passed`, `login(request, user, factor=outcome.factor)`, the one place a session becomes a login. Answers (AUTH-05, all `Cache-Control: no-store`): 200 `{"next": "signed_in", "csrf"}` (no recovery codes: they are shown once, at enrolment); 400 `invalid` for a body without a text `code` (counts nothing); 401 `login-expired` for every pending-state failure (none, an enrolment's, expired, user no longer `active`: the caller holds a pending session, so it tells nothing about any account); 401 `code-wrong`; 429 `limited` for the attempt budget and the factor lock alike.
- **`PUBLIC_ROUTES["auth-second-factor"]` (AUTH-01 pass by pattern, as `auth-login` and `auth-enrol-totp-confirm`):** a public pre-session route whose ordered check (pending state, count, the compare, the lock) runs inside `second_factor` before anything is signed in. CSRF gate fixture: no `code` → 400.
- **A failure after `Passed` spends the code (PR 132 R1 P3), accepted:** between `Passed` and `login()` the route reads the `AppUser` row (the only step in between; `login()` takes a model). If that or `login()` raises, the code is already used up (a recovery code deleted, a TOTP step stored) and stays so: restoring it would reopen replay. The 500 saves no session, so the stored pending login is as it was and the user can try another code. Tested (`test_a_failure_in_login_after_the_attempt_spends_the_code`), so nobody "fixes" it by undoing the use.
- **PR 129 R1 P2:** `test_enrol_factor_route_live` now calls the test-only `action-probe` (an `authorized_action`) after the enrolment (200) and shows a session logged in before the enrolment marker is refused; `test_second_factor_route_live` does the same after a sign-in (200) and shows a recovery-code sign-in writes no marker (a second session stays signed in, AUTH-07).
- **PR 133 review:** the lock is tested through the route: ten wrong codes (authenticator and recovery alternately) across two windows, then the right code in a fresh window gets the same 429 `limited` with the budget untouched.
- **Runbook:** the lock bullet is checked at sign-in now and groups the `factor.locked` events by user (PR 133 R1 P3 #1), saying the hash cannot be reversed.
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts.views`, `config.urls`, the registry entry and CSRF fixture, two live test files, the runbook, this log. No migration, no dependency, no setting. Web screens are M3i.
- **Alternatives rejected:** returning `unavailable` as 401 `code-wrong` (hides a lost session from the page, which must send the user back to the password step); a distinct 423 for the lock (tells the holder of the password a lock exists, and the budget answer is already 429 `limited`); restoring a spent code when `login()` fails.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session), on PR 129 R1 P2, PR 132 R1 P3 and PR 133 R1 P3 #1.

## 2026-11-09 — M3 order: passkeys last (M3f → M3h → M3i → M3g)
- **Decision:** the owner chose option B on 2026-11-09 when asked where the optional passkeys (M3g) belong. Option A was to keep the plan's order (M3f → M3g → M3h → M3i); B: build step-up, logout and factor changes (M3h), then the sign-in screens (M3i), then passkeys (M3g). The plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md`, amended 2026-11-09) now reads M3f → M3h → M3i → M3g. This settles the "not decided here" item of "2026-11-07 — M3 plan".
- **Dependency check (builder):** the plan's "M3h depends on M3g" is soft. A passkey is only an additional step-up factor, and M3g's assertion bullet already covers step-up, so M3g can add that branch later. M3h now depends on M3f; step-up accepts TOTP only until M3g. Factor changes in M3h cover TOTP and recovery codes; adding or removing a passkey arrives in M3g, behind step-up and revoking other sessions (AUTH-07), with a test.
- **Cost:** M3g grows. The passkey button on the second-factor screen, the optional passkey step after enrolment and the virtual-authenticator e2e move from M3i into it, next to the server work, so it will probably split into M3g-1 (server) and M3g-2 (screens and e2e). M3i shrinks to the TOTP and recovery-code screens.
- **Until M3g:** no passkey exists anywhere. Nothing is lost because a passkey is optional for everyone (spec v1.22); the app is complete with TOTP and recovery codes.
- **Plan rows changed:** §1 table rows and Order line, §3 M3g, M3h and M3i, §4 test rows, §5 universal and frontend rows and the `make e2e` sentence, §7 item 9, A2, carry-over (f), "Done when" (the Playwright item).
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`. No code.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** owner ("B", 2026-11-09); written by the Sonnet builder session.

## 2026-11-10 — M3h split into six PRs (M3h-1 … M3h-6)
