- **Decision:** the owner said yes (2026-11-10, `build`) to building M3h as six reviewed PRs, each near 400 lines: **M3h-1** `auth_stepup` table; **M3h-2** evidence MAC, `record`, `recent`; **M3h-3** step-up route (shared factor-attempt core, wrapper `own_transaction`); **M3h-4** logout and password change; **M3h-5** add a TOTP; **M3h-6** remove a TOTP and regenerate recovery codes. The design is the deep-tier (Opus) M3h design of 2026-11-10.
- **Why:** the plan estimated ≈ 330 lines; the design measures ≈ 2 270 (M3f was planned at ≈ 350 and took ≈ 1 600). The plan's split rule (split at ≈ 450) applies; the order keeps every half-built state safe: no route writes evidence before `recent()` verifies it, and no factor change exists without step-up and AUTH-07.
- **Naming:** the "(D8)" in the plan's M3h row is the owner decision of 2026-11-06 item 4 (factor changes on an active session, behind step-up; `.agent/reviews/owner-decisions-m3f-3.md`), not spec D8. Entries cite it that way.
- **Owner answer, lost authenticator:** with recovery codes excluded from step-up, a user who lost the authenticator can sign in with a recovery code but cannot add a TOTP or regenerate codes; only an owner-performed reset (Flow 5.1) helps. The owner chose to build that reset in M4-rest "Manage staff", not in M3. There is no reset before M4-rest: the runbook says the owner's reset "is not built", `accounts_invite` refuses a user who has already enrolled, and the documented step is "tell Ying". If a real person needs one earlier, Ying decides then; nothing is built now. **The reset is a must-have item of the M4-rest plan, needed before real data (the M7 import or cutover).** Nothing depends on it while the real-data gate is locked.
- **Scope / affects:** `docs/plans/2026-10-31-m3-auth.md` (amendment note, M3h row and section), this file. No code in this entry.
- **Versions:** none new.
- **Exception to a rule?** no (a split, not an exception).
- **Decided by:** owner (yes to the split and the M4-rest answer, 2026-11-10); design by the Opus deep tier; written by the Sonnet builder session.

## 2026-11-10 — M3h-1: `auth_stepup` table (append-only evidence, factor FK NO ACTION, factors retired not deleted)
- **Decision:** `auth_stepup` (spec §4.1) is a tenant-owned, append-only table (`accounts.0011` model, `0012` RLS through `tenant_table()`): columns `id`, `agency_id`, `user_id`, `factor_id` (null only for a step-up no factor proved; a named factor is never deleted), `purpose`, `verified_at`, `session_key_hash`, `evidence_mac`. `skiw_app` has SELECT and INSERT only (TEN-03). CHECKs: `session_key_hash` is 64 lower-case hex (`stored_key` format); `purpose` has a shape only (`a`, `a_b`, `a.b`), the closed list is in Python (M3h-2), so a later purpose needs no migration; `evidence_mac` is exactly 36 bytes.
- **`evidence_mac` layout (fixed here, built in M3h-2):** a 4-byte big-endian `tenant_source_key` version, then HMAC-SHA-256 under that version's key over `skiw-stepup-v1` followed by `id`, `agency_id`, `user_id`, `factor_id` (empty when null), `purpose`, `verified_at` (UTC, microseconds) and `session_key_hash`, each field length-prefixed as in M2c (`source_key.field`). The version is a prefix and not a column because spec §4.1 lists no `key_version` for this table [A]; it follows the recovery-code format (M3f-3a).
- **Factor FK (PR 136 R1 P2 replaced `ON DELETE SET NULL (factor_id)`):** `FOREIGN KEY (agency_id, factor_id) REFERENCES user_factor(agency_id, id)`, the default NO ACTION. A factor that a step-up names cannot be deleted. Reason: `factor_id` is inside `evidence_mac`, so clearing it would make a genuine row stop verifying, and the spec's consumers verify later and outside the session: the M9 executor and approval (§4.3, T10: a forged row is refused "by approval and executor", up to `approval_expires_at` after the approval) and the M16 `drive_writer` (D25: the step-up id is stored on the `drive_operation` and checked before any Drive call, including a waiting operation and a person's Retry). A genuine row would then look forged, and the table would lose which factor proved it. The first version of this entry called the loss harmless because a factor change re-issues the session; that holds only for use inside the session and the ≤ 5 minute window, and is withdrawn. The Django model says `PROTECT`, so the ORM raises `ProtectedError` before the database does. Proven live as `skiw_app`: the delete is refused, nothing moves, and the catalog pins the definition with no ON DELETE action.
- **Retire, not delete (plan for M3h-6 and M3g):** replacing a TOTP marks the old `user_factor` row retired instead of deleting it. M3h-6 adds the column (for example `retired_at`) in its own migration, clears or destroys `secret_ct` on retirement, and makes every factor lookup refuse a retired row (login second factor, step-up, add/remove/last-factor count). M3g does the same for a passkey. Recovery codes are never step-up factors, so their `DELETE … RETURNING` is unaffected. The `user_factor` change is M3h-6's, not this PR's. This stays inside spec §4.1 (it says FK to `user_factor`, never that a factor row is deleted); no owner decision is needed unless Ying wants to override.
- **Alternatives rejected:** `ON DELETE SET NULL (factor_id)` (the first version, rejected by the reviewer, above); no FK on `factor_id` or leaving `factor_id` out of the MAC (each deviates from spec §4.1 "FK→user_factor" and "over all other columns", needs an owner decision and a spec change, not recommended).
- **Retention:** rows stay (no DELETE grant), a few per user per day; purging is designed with `outbox_approval` (M9), which references `stepup_id` [A].
- **Also:** `jobs.tests.test_queue_grants` now asserts `auth_stepup` exists, so the TEN-03 evidence-table check no longer passes vacuously for it; TEN-08 case added (parents `user_id`, `factor_id`). Runbook: `host-rebuild.md` says step-up evidence is a MAC under `tenant_source_key`; until M9 and M16 store step-up ids, removing a version voids at most 5 minutes of step-ups, and from then a version is removed only after no pending approval or Drive operation references a step-up under it; the "passkey added later" line now says M3g.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/stepup_model.py`, `models.py`, migrations 0011-0012, `tests/isolation_cases.py`, `tests/test_stepup_table_live.py`, `jobs/tests/test_queue_grants.py`, `docs/runbooks/host-rebuild.md`, plan, this file. No route, no setting, no dependency.
- **Versions:** none new (Django 5.2.17, PostgreSQL 17 as before).
- **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session) on the Opus deep-tier design; the factor FK action was changed after PR 136 R1 (reviewer's recommended option 1).

## 2026-11-10 — M3h-2a: step-up evidence MAC, `factor_id` required; M3h-2 split into 2a and 2b
- **Decision:** (1) M3h-2 is two PRs. The Opus deep-tier design measured it at ≈ 790 lines (`stepup.py` 169, tests 466, Semgrep 94, migration and edits ≈ 60) against the 400 target and the ≈ 450 split point; the live forgery tests are the point of the PR and cannot shrink. **M3h-2a** (this PR): `accounts/auth/stepup.py` with `LABEL`, `PURPOSES`, `_message`, `evidence`, `_genuine`; migration `accounts.0013`; Semgrep rule `auth-stepup-insert-stepup-only` with its fixture; unit tests. **M3h-2b**: `record`, `recent`, `StepUp`, `_signed`, the live tests. The builder applied the standing size rule; the plan and the M3h row are amended.
- **`evidence_mac` as fixed in M3h-1:** 4-byte big-endian key version, then HMAC-SHA-256 under that version over `skiw-stepup-v1`, then `id`, `agency_id`, `user_id`, `factor_id`, `purpose`, `verified_at` (UTC, `isoformat(timespec="microseconds")`) and `session_key_hash`, each behind its 8-byte length (`source_key.field`). The newest version signs; any listed version verifies; a removed or changed version verifies nothing. The unit tests build the layout by hand and move each column on its own.
- **`factor_id` is required (PR 136 R2 P3 #2):** `accounts.0013` adds CHECK `auth_stepup_factor_named` (`factor_id IS NOT NULL`). Not `AlterField(null=False)`: that is a contract operation (`skiw_contract`, no rollback across it); an added CHECK is an expand step (UNI-15), as `accounts.0010` was for `secret_ct`. The table holds no rows (nothing wrote one), the model keeps `null=True` plus the constraint, and `_message` has no empty case: a NULL factor raises. Amends M3h-1's "`factor_id` (null only for a step-up no factor proved)".
- **Designed here, built in M3h-2b:** `record(request, factor, purpose)` re-reads the factor and requires one of the session user's own rows of kind `totp` (PR 136 R2 P3 #1: a recovery row is deleted by raw DELETE at sign-in and on regenerate, which a step-up naming it would block through the NO ACTION FK; another user's factor would pass the agency-only FK). Passkey joins in M3g; M3h-6 adds "not retired". `recent(request, purpose)` takes user, agency and session only from the signed session (TEN-11) and returns the newest row with `now − SKIW_STEPUP_MAX_AGE_MIN ≤ verified_at ≤ now` whose MAC verifies; a failing row is skipped with one fixed warning. Both run in the caller's `with_agency()`. The earlier design's `record(request, user, factor_id, purpose)` becomes `record(request, factor, purpose)`.
- **Lint:** Semgrep `auth-stepup-insert-stepup-only` keeps the `AuthStepup` model and SQL writes to `auth_stepup` inside `accounts/auth/stepup.py` (plus migrations and the accounts tests), so the spec's "inserted only by accounts.auth (lint)" holds inside `accounts.auth` too (UNI-04); `auth-tables-accounts-only` excludes all of `accounts/auth/` and does not know the model.
- **[A]:** the age check uses the app clock on both sides; `factor.change` is not in the spec's purpose list and goes on plan §9 for the next spec revision.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/stepup.py`, `stepup_model.py`, migration 0013, `semgrep/auth-stepup-insert-stepup-only.{yml,py}`, `tests/test_auth_stepup.py`, `tests/test_stepup_table_live.py`, plan, this file. No route, setting, dependency or runbook change (UNI-16: no new env var).
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session) on the Opus deep-tier M3h-2 design.

## 2026-11-11 — M3h-2b: `record`, `recent` built; PR 137 P3s taken
- **Decision:** `accounts/auth/stepup.py` gains `StepUp`, `record(request, factor, purpose)` and `recent(request, purpose)` as designed in 'M3h-2a'. `FACTOR_KINDS = {totp}` (passkey joins in M3g). User, agency and session come only from the signed session (`_signed`, TEN-11). `recent` skips a row whose MAC fails with one fixed warning (logger `django.security.SuspiciousStepUp`, no row, key or session material).
- **PR 137 P3s:** (1) `_message` raises `ValueError` on a timezone-naive `verified_at`, as `source_key._mac` does; (2) the Semgrep rule message now says it governs the ORM and model, and flags raw SQL only when it writes (a migration or trigger may read the table); (3) live stored-then-read test keeps microseconds and instant. PR 136 R2 P3 #1 (`record` refuses a recovery or another user's factor) is done.
- **Scope / affects:** `app/accounts/auth/stepup.py`, `semgrep/auth-stepup-insert-stepup-only.yml`, `tests/test_auth_stepup.py`, `tests/test_auth_stepup_live.py`.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Sonnet session) on the Opus deep-tier design.

## 2026-11-11 — Base images: a pinned-minimum `apk add` for krb5-libs (and libxslt in the dump image) until upstream rebuilds
- **Decision:** both images get one `RUN apk add --no-cache` line: the app `Dockerfile` (runtime stage) `"krb5-libs>=1.22.3-r0"`; `infra/host/backup/predeploy/Dockerfile` `"krb5-libs>=1.22.3-r0" "libxslt>=1.1.43-r4"`. Both carry the same marker `REMOVE-WHEN-UPSTREAM-REBUILDS (DECISIONS 2026-11-11, "base images krb5-libs")`. **Remove each line when its base tag is re-pinned to a digest that ships those versions** (the app image: `python:3.12.15-alpine`; the dump image: `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24`), and delete `APK_FIX` and its planted cases from `infra/host/tests/test_predeploy.py` at the same time. Check on every base-image digest bump.
- **Why:** from 2026-11-11 the CI `image` job fails on `main` (fb49783) and on every PR (found by PR 139 R1, P2): Trivy v0.75.0 reports 16 HIGH CVEs, all `krb5-libs` 1.22.2-r1 (pulled in by `libtirpc`), fixed in 1.22.3-r0, in the app image. Nothing in the repo changed; the vulnerability database did (as 2026-11-07). The dump image is scanned right after, so its findings were hidden behind the first failure: `krb5-libs` (16) and `libxslt` 1.1.43-r3 (2, fixed 1.1.43-r4), 18 in all.
- **Evidence (local, `aquasec/trivy:0.75.0 image --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 1`):** before: app image exit 1 (16 HIGH), dump image exit 1 (18 HIGH). After this change: both exit 0, 0 findings; `apk info` shows krb5-libs 1.22.3-r0 and libxslt 1.1.43-r4; the dump image still reports `pg_dump`/`pg_restore` 17.11, `age` v1.3.2 and a working `python3 -I` import check; Django 5.2.17 imports in the app image. No `.trivyignore`.
- **Alternatives rejected:** re-pinning the digest (the rule of 2026-11-07): `python:3.12.15-alpine` still resolves to the pinned `b658b05c…`; `python:3.12.16-alpine` (`e064671c…`) still has krb5-libs 1.22.2-r1; `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24` now resolves to a different digest (`3540c036…`) that still has krb5-libs 1.22.2-r1 and libxslt 1.1.43-r3. Waiting for upstream (CI stays red on every PR for an unknown time: the gate stops being trusted). A time-boxed `.trivyignore` (ships known HIGH findings and reverses 2026-11-07). An exact `=1.22.3-r0` pin: it breaks the build as soon as Alpine's index drops that version.
- **Exception to a rule: yes, two, both deliberate and owner-approved.** (1) `>=` is not an exact pin (UNI-14, "pinned inputs only"): a floor from Alpine's signed repository, chosen because an exact pin breaks when the index moves on. (2) The dump image's Dockerfile test (`check_dockerfile`) forbade `apk` altogether (DECISIONS 2026-10-23, "Dump image passes the Trivy gate"); it now allows exactly one `RUN apk add --no-cache` of `krb5-libs` and `libxslt`, each `>=` a version, and still rejects any other apk line, package, or a second apk layer (planted cases in `test_the_pin_check_fails_on_a_weakened_dockerfile`).
- **Review:** the dump image is part of the backup path (H3 step 4, H6); this PR is reviewed on the deep tier and `image` must be green on its head.
- **Scope / affects:** `Dockerfile`, `infra/host/backup/predeploy/Dockerfile`, `infra/host/tests/test_predeploy.py`, this file. No code, setting, dependency or runbook change (build inputs only; UNI-16 n/a).
- **Versions:** krb5-libs 1.22.3-r0 and libxslt 1.1.43-r4 (Alpine 3.24 main) as minimums; base image digests unchanged.
- **Decided by:** owner (2026-11-11, `approved 139` chat: option "allow pinned apk layers", with four conditions: exactly these packages, `>=` recorded as a deliberate exception with a removal note and the same marker in both Dockerfiles, its own PR on the deep tier with `image` green, and PR 139 given a new head afterwards); built by the Sonnet builder.

## 2026-11-11 — M3h-3: step-up route, shared factor-attempt core, wrapper `own_transaction`
- **Decision:** `second_factor.attempt` is split into `counted(request, user_id, agency_id, check, *, feeds_lock)` (the one copy of the reserve, release, `factor_failed`/`factor_reset` and fault rules, UNI-05) and `match_totp(user, candidate) -> UserFactor | None` (the conditional `sign_count < step` update, now returning the row); `attempt` keeps its behaviour (its live suites pass unchanged). `stepup.verify(request, signed, purpose, candidate) -> StepUp | "invalid" | "unavailable" | "limited" | "wrong_code"` refuses anything but six ASCII digits as `invalid` with nothing counted (a recovery code is never step-up), then runs `counted(..., feeds_lock=True)` whose check calls `match_totp` and, on a match, `record(...)` in the same transaction: the step is used only if the evidence row commits, and a fault there rolls the use back and gives the attempt back.
- **Throttle (design §2.1) [A]:** step-up shares the user's one factor budget, feeds the same consecutive run and lock (`factor.locked`), and a right step-up ends the run, as at sign-in: it is the same TOTP secret, so a separate budget would double the guesses per window for a session holder. Sign-in and step-up share `sign_count`, so a code used at one is refused at the other in the same step (the user waits for the next code). The password counters are not touched.
- **Wrapper:** `authorized_action(..., own_transaction=False)`. With `True`, steps 1-4 (session, tenant with idle/active/revocation, factor, schema) run and commit in the wrapper's `with_agency()` exactly as before, then the handler runs outside any transaction and opens its own `with_agency(signed.agency_id)` for tenant queries (TEN-09). Every wrapper refusal now carries `Cache-Control: no-store` (AUTH-05).
- **Route:** `POST /api/auth/stepup` (`auth-stepup`, `accounts.views.stepup`, own_transaction, permission `auth.stepup`), body exactly `{"purpose": <listed>, "code": <text>}`. 200 `{"next": "verified", "until": verified_at + SKIW_STEPUP_MAX_AGE_MIN}` (session key unchanged, so no new CSRF token); 400 `invalid` (bad body, unlisted purpose, not six ASCII digits; nothing counted); 401 `code-wrong`; 429 `limited` (budget or lock); 401 `signed-out` if the user stopped being `active` after the wrapper; all no-store. Wrapped, so no `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry; CSRF fixture `{}` → 400.
- **Test-only consumer:** `stepup-probe` in `tests/csrf_urls.py` (an `authorized_action` that answers 403 `stepup-required` unless `recent(request, FACTOR_CHANGE)`), mounted only by `pytest.mark.urls`; `test_route_registry` asserts `config.urls` serves no probe.
- **PR 138 R1 P3:** one `accounts.auth.transactions.require_open(what)` replaces the two `_in_transaction` copies in `recovery` and `stepup`; its docstring says it checks that a transaction is open, not the tenant binding.
- **Deviations from the Opus design:** (1) the probe lives in `tests/csrf_urls.py` (with the real routes and the CSRF inventory, which calls it signed in) and checks `FACTOR_CHANGE`, not a monkeypatched `test.probe` purpose: an unlisted purpose would make `recent` raise in the gate tests. (2) No `stepup.required()` yet: it has no production caller until M3h-5 (UNI-05). (3) `record(request, factor, purpose)` as merged in M3h-2b, not the design's `record(request, user, factor_id, purpose)`. (4) "Only `verify` calls `record`" is not a Semgrep rule: the M3h-2a rule already keeps every `auth_stepup` write in `stepup.py`, and `record` refuses anything but the session user's own TOTP. (5) An unlisted purpose is refused by the route's schema (the wrapper's 400 `invalid`), and `verify` raises ValueError for it, as `record` and `recent` do.
- **Tests:** `test_stepup_route_live.py` (23, incl. `verify` refusing an unknown purpose before judging the code, and `counted(feeds_lock=False)` counting but neither feeding nor ending the run, which M3h-5's confirm will use): step-up then the probe passes, another session of the user does not, a wrong code writes no evidence; replay both ways between sign-in and step-up; two parallel step-ups with one code pass once; the shared budget; ten wrong codes mixed between sign-in and step-up lock both with one `factor.locked`; a right step-up ends the run; nine bodies that cannot be a step-up are 400 with nothing counted; a fault writing the evidence rolls the code back; a fault after a wrong code keeps the count and feeds the run; a user disabled after the wrapper is `unavailable`; the own-transaction handler sees no atomic block (the default one does) and never runs after a refusal; the anonymous 401 is no-store.
- **Runbook:** "Enrol a user" budget and lock bullets say step-up shares them (UNI-16: a new auth route; no env var).
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts.auth.second_factor`, `stepup`, `wrapper`, `recovery`, new `transactions`, `accounts.views`, `config.urls`, `tests/csrf_urls.py`, `tests/csrf_fixtures.py`, `tests/test_route_registry.py`, the new live test, the runbook, this log. No migration, no setting, no dependency.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Opus deep-tier sub-agent wrote the code on the Opus M3h design; the Sonnet builder ran the gates, mutants and review prep).

## 2026-11-11 — M3h-4: logout, password change, `revoke.hold`, `login.reissue`
- **Decision:** two signed-in routes. `POST /api/auth/logout` (`auth-logout`, permission `auth.logout`, schema `wrapper.nothing` = exactly `{}`) runs in the wrapper's tenant transaction: a marker for this session, `flush()` (the row delete, same transaction), `rotate_token`; 200 `{"next": "signed_out", "csrf"}`, no-store; the empty session makes the middleware delete the cookie (AUTH-13, server half; the client half is M3i). `POST /api/auth/password` (`auth-password`, `auth.password`, own_transaction), body exactly `{"current", "new"}` (`views._text`, no NUL in `new`), through `accounts.auth.password_change.change`: a new password shorter than `SKIW_PW_MIN_LENGTH` is 400 `password-short` before any count or Argon2; the user's email and blob are read in `with_agency()`; `throttle.blocked` gives 429 `limited`; exactly one Argon2 verify (the blob's hash, or `dummy_verify` when the blob does not open or names another user or agency); every failure is `record_failure` and 401 `password-wrong`; success is `reset_on_success`, the new hash made outside any transaction, then one `with_agency()` transaction: `hold`, the blob swapped only where the row still holds the blob that was verified (409 `changed` otherwise), a user-wide marker (AUTH-07), `reissue`; 200 `{"next": "changed", "csrf"}`.
- **Order in every credential change** (this PR and M3h-5/6): `hold` → (`stepup.recent`, factor changes only) → the change → `revoke.mark(agency, user, None)` → `login.reissue`.
- **`revoke.hold(signed, session_key) -> bool` (resurrection race):** inside the caller's `with_agency()`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` of the user's `active` row, then `revoked()`. Two sessions A and B of one user change credentials at once; B passed the wrapper's revocation check before A committed its user-wide marker. Without the lock B's re-issue would commit after A with a login time newer than A's marker and survive it. The lock makes B wait; under READ COMMITTED B's `revoked()` then sees A's marker, B answers 401 `signed-out`, flushes, and changes nothing. Live test with a lock-waiter barrier.
- **`login.reissue(request, signed)` keeps the absolute deadline [A]:** a marker for this key, `cycle_key()` (the store keeps the row's deadline), `AUTH_AT = SEEN_AT = now`, `FACTOR` unchanged, `rotate_token`. It never calls `start_new_lifetime` (Semgrep `start-new-lifetime-login-only` unchanged): a credential change is not a new login and must not stretch a session past `SKIW_SESSION_ABSOLUTE_H`. The new `AUTH_AT` is the app clock, read after the marker's statement time, so the re-issued session outlives the marker; AUTH-07 tests therefore run on the real clock, not the `clock` fixture. The new key also ends every step-up of the old one.
- **Password change reuses the login throttle [A]:** the same account, IP and lock keys as the login form (5 per 15 minutes per account, 10 in a row write `account.locked`). A session thief guessing here can lock the login for 30 minutes, which anyone who knows the email can already do. No `security_event` per attempt (pre-tenant table, M3f-3c-1c); the lock's `account.locked` still fires. Neither password is logged.
- **Current password only, no step-up [A]:** plan §3 M3h says "current password required" and spec D7's step-up list does not include password change.
- **Pending-login residue: fixed in M3h-5, no longer accepted (PR 141 R1 P2).** As merged, a password change did not cancel a *pending* login (password step done, factor not yet) by someone who knew the old password; it could still finish within `SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN` (5). M3h-5 makes `factors.pending` refuse it (entry 'M3h-5'). Unchanged: a sign-in that completes during the change transaction is not revoked (marker window, M3d). `revoked_at` is `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, the **transaction start time** (not the statement time, PR 141 R1 P3 #2), so the window is the whole change transaction, the wait on `hold`'s row lock included; Argon2 runs outside it, so it stays short.
- **Tests:** `test_logout_password_live.py` (21): logout deletes the row and the cookie, writes the marker, and a row put back by SQL stays refused; logout takes `{}` only; logout rotates the CSRF token and the old one gets 403; a change ends the other sessions, re-issues this one with a new cookie and the same deadline, keeps the factor, and the new password signs in and the old does not; a step-up made before the change stops counting; wrong current passwords count on the login budget (then 429 for the right one and at the login form) and change nothing; ten in a row write one `account.locked`; one Argon2 verify per attempt, the dummy for a blob naming another user; a short new password is 400 with nothing counted and no Argon2; eight malformed bodies are 400; a password set meanwhile gives 409 and revokes nothing; the resurrection race.
- **Runbook:** "Enrol a user" gets "Change the password" (all other sessions end) and "Sign out" (UNI-16: new auth routes, no env var).
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts.auth.revoke`, `login`, `wrapper` (`nothing`), new `password_change`, `accounts.views`, `config.urls`, `tests/csrf_fixtures.py`, the new live test, the runbook, this log. No migration, setting or dependency. Permission names `auth.logout`, `auth.password`: M4-rest allows both for every role.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Opus deep-tier sub-agent on the Opus M3h design).

## 2026-11-11 — M3h-5: list factors, add a TOTP behind step-up; a credential change ends a pending login
- **Decision:** three signed-in routes, logic in the new `accounts.auth.factor_change` (as `password_change`), views in `accounts/views.py` (the one module `config.urls` may import). `GET /api/auth/factors` (`auth-factors`, permission `auth.factors`, schema `nothing`, no step-up [A]) answers `{"totp": [{"id"}], "recovery_left": n}` for the session user only, no secret. `POST /api/auth/factors/totp` (`auth-factor-totp`, `auth.factor-change`, body `{}`) needs `stepup.recent(FACTOR_CHANGE)` of this session (403 `stepup-required`), then `factors.seal_new(request, ADD_TOTP, user)` keeps a new sealed secret and answers `{secret, uri}`. `POST /api/auth/factors/totp/confirm` (`auth-factor-totp-confirm`, `auth.factor-change`, own_transaction, body exactly `{"code"}`): 409 `no-pending` when nothing was begun and 400 `invalid` for anything but six ASCII digits, both before any count; then `second_factor.counted(..., feeds_lock=False)` whose check, in one transaction, runs `revoke.hold` (401 `signed-out`, the session flushed), `recent` (403), opens the secret, `totp.accept(secret, code, 0)`, creates the `user_factor` row with `sign_count` = the accepted step (the confirming code cannot sign in), pops the pending secret, writes the user-wide marker (AUTH-07) and `login.reissue`; 200 `{"next": "added", "factor_id", "csrf"}`; 401 `code-wrong`, 429 `limited`. A refusal inside the check is not a guess: the attempt is given back. All answers no-store.
- **Own session key `_skiw_add_totp`**, never enrolment's `_skiw_pending_totp`, so an enrolment's pending secret cannot be confirmed as an added factor or the reverse. `factors.seal_new` is the one copy of the seal-and-keep step (enrolment's `begin_totp` calls it); `factors.stored_secret(request, key)` reads either.
- **`feeds_lock=False` (the first production caller):** the user typed the code of a secret they just made, which is not a guess at anyone's factor (as at enrolment, M3f-3c-1b), so a wrong code is counted on the one budget but neither feeds nor ends the lock run; the budget and the lock check still apply. PR 139 R2 P3: the fault-after-wrong test for this path now exists.
- **A credential change ends a pending login (PR 141 R1 P2; replaces M3h-4's accepted residue):** `password_step.start_pending` stores `_skiw_pending_at` (the app clock), and `factors.pending` refuses, clears and answers "unavailable" for a pending state with a user-wide marker of that user at or after it (`revoke.changed_since`; a tie counts as revoked), before anything is counted. It is in `pending`, so it covers the sign-in's second factor and an enrolment alike, and every credential change (password, add TOTP, M3h-6's remove and regenerate) through the one marker. A stored start, not `PENDING_UNTIL − SKIW_LOGIN_PENDING_MIN`: it does not move if the setting changes, and a pending state from before this deploy (no `_skiw_pending_at`) is refused as malformed (that user types the password again, at most 5 minutes of logins). A change committing between this check and `login()` is the existing marker window (M3d), not new. The pending start is the app clock and the marker the database's transaction start; both read the host's one clock, and skew towards the past refuses (fails closed).
- **No TOTP count cap [A]** (design §3.4); replacing a TOTP is add, then remove (M3h-6).
- **Docstrings (PR 141 R1 P3 #2):** `revoke.py` and `login.reissue` say the marker time is the transaction start (`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`); so does M3h-4's window note.
- **Tests:** `test_factor_change_live.py` (11): without a step-up begin is 403 and a begun secret is not confirmed (nothing counted, nothing revoked); the right code adds the factor with its step, ends the other sessions, re-issues this one (same deadline, factor kept, secret gone), the confirming code is refused at sign-in and the next code signs in, and a second confirm is 409; wrong codes count without feeding the run, then 429; a fault after a wrong code keeps the count and leaves the run; four codes that cannot be one are 400; nothing begun is 409; the list holds only the user's own TOTP and recovery count (a neighbour in the same agency); the resurrection race: a confirm waiting on `hold` while another change commits its marker answers 401 and adds nothing. `test_logout_password_live.py` (+2): a password change rotates the CSRF token and the old one gets 403 `token-invalid` (PR 141 R1 P3 #1); a login past the password step before a change answers `unavailable` with nothing counted, and a login with the new password then passes.
- **Runbook:** "Change the password" no longer states the residue; new "Add an authenticator" bullet (UNI-16: new auth routes, no env var).
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts.auth.factor_change` (new), `factors`, `password_step`, `revoke`, `login` (docstring), `accounts.views`, `config.urls`, `tests/csrf_fixtures.py`, two live test files and two session-shape assertions, the runbook, this log. No migration, setting or dependency. Permission names `auth.factors`, `auth.factor-change`: M4-rest allows both for every role.
- **Deviations from the Opus design:** (1) `seal_new` and `stored_secret` are public and take the request (the design's `_seal_new(session, key, user)`): `factor_change` is another module and the request carries the store check. (2) A non-six-digit code is 400 `invalid` with nothing counted, as at step-up. (3) The pending-login fix sits in `factors.pending`, not only in `second_factor.attempt`, so enrolment's pending state gets it too.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Opus deep-tier sub-agent on the Opus M3h design and the PR 139 / 141 review conditions).

## 2026-11-11 — M3h-6: remove a TOTP (retired, never deleted), regenerate recovery codes
- **Decision:** two signed-in routes, logic in `accounts.auth.factor_change`, both in the wrapper's tenant transaction, permission `auth.factor-change` (reused), every answer no-store. `POST /api/auth/factors/totp/remove` (`auth-factor-totp-remove`, body exactly `{"id": <uuid text>}`, else 400 `invalid`): `revoke.hold` (401 `signed-out`, session flushed), `stepup.recent(FACTOR_CHANGE)` (403 `stepup-required`), then the ids of the user's TOTPs that are not retired, read after the lock; an id not among them (another user's, a recovery row's, a retired one, unknown) is 404 `not-found`; one id left is **409 `last-factor`** and the row stays in use (PR 135 R1 P3 #2: TOTP is required, spec D7); else the row is retired, a user-wide marker (AUTH-07) and `login.reissue`; 200 `{"next": "removed", "csrf"}`. `POST /api/auth/factors/recovery` (`auth-recovery-regenerate`, body `{}`): `hold`, `recent`, `recovery.generate(agency, user)` (it locks the user row again, harmless, and deletes every old code), marker, `reissue`; 200 `{"next": "regenerated", "recovery_codes", "csrf"}`, the only time the codes are shown. Add-confirm (M3h-5) now shares the same two helpers (`_changing` = hold then recent, `_changed` = marker then reissue), so the order of DECISIONS M3h-4 is written once.
- **Retire, not delete (as planned in M3h-1 after PR 136 R1 P2):** `auth_stepup.factor_id` is NO ACTION and inside `evidence_mac`, so a factor a step-up names cannot be deleted without making genuine evidence look forged to the later consumers (M9 approval/executor, M16 Drive). `accounts.0014` adds `user_factor.retired_at` (nullable `timestamptz`, kind-agnostic, so M3g retires a passkey the same way). Retiring sets it to the database's `now()` and `secret_ct` to NULL. New CHECK `user_factor_retired_no_secret`: a retired row has no `secret_ct`, `public_key` or `lookup_blob_ct`, so it cannot verify anything even for code that ignores the column (the previous digest included: it would open an empty blob and match nothing). `user_factor_totp_secret_ct_length` now exempts a retired row (otherwise a retired TOTP would need 48 bytes). A recovery row cannot be retired (its unchanged length rule forbids NULL; codes are still deleted by `DELETE ... RETURNING`). `credential_id` is not cleared: M3g decides whether a retired passkey's credential id may come back.
- **Every lookup refuses a retired row:** `second_factor.match_totp` (sign-in and step-up) reads only rows in use, and its conditional `sign_count` update also requires `retired_at IS NULL`, so a factor retired between the read and the use is not used (the step-up does not take the user lock); `stepup.record` refuses a retired factor; the list, the remove id set and the last-factor count are one query (`factor_change._totps`). Step-up evidence that names a retired factor **stays and still verifies**: it records that the factor proved the user then. `recent` cannot return such a row to anyone: the remove re-issues this session's key (the evidence is bound to the old key hash) and its user-wide marker ends every other session.
- **Replacement flow:** step up with the old TOTP; add the new one (begin, confirm; this re-issues the session, ending that step-up); step up again with either; remove the old one. Nothing leaves zero TOTPs. No count cap [A] (M3h-5).
- **Lost authenticator (unchanged, entry 'M3h split'):** a recovery code is never a step-up, so a user without the authenticator can neither add, remove nor regenerate; only the owner-performed reset (Flow 5.1) helps, which is not built: **a must-have item of M4-rest "Manage staff", needed before real data** (M7 import or cutover). The runbook says "tell Ying".
- **Migration (UNI-15, expand only):** the column and the two CHECKs; the totp rule is swapped in one `ALTER TABLE` (DROP + ADD) in the migration's transaction. The database side is SQL (`SeparateDatabaseAndState`) for the reverse: skiw_migrator sees no tenant rows under FORCE RLS, so it cannot repair a retired row; dropping the column would turn each retired TOTP into a secret-less active one that a re-apply cannot validate. So the reverse **keeps the column and its values** (only Django's state drops it), puts the 0010 rule back `NOT VALID`, and the re-apply (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) validates both rules. Deploys stay forward-only (D16); the reverse matters for the tenancy reversal test and local work. `lintmigrations --warnings-as-errors`, `makemigrations --check` and `check_tenancy_schema` pass; grants are table-level, so skiw_app's UPDATE covers the column (no grant change).
- **PR 142 R1 P3s taken:** (1) a begun secret sealed for another factor id gives 409 `no-pending`, nothing counted, no row, no marker (that mutant now dies); (2) add, remove and regenerate each end a login waiting at its second factor, and each answer's `csrf` passes the gate while the old token is 403. The near-equivalent `__gt` mutant (P3 #2.3) was not re-run.
- **Tests:** `test_factor_remove_live.py` (new, 23 cases): last TOTP 409 and still signs in; removing the stepped-up TOTP retires it (secret NULL), ends the other sessions, re-issues this one (same deadline), the evidence row stays genuine, the new key has no step-up, the list shows only the other; a retired TOTP fails sign-in, step-up and `record`, removing it again is 404, and the remaining one is the last (409); a factor retired after the read is not used; 404 for a neighbour's TOTP, a recovery row and an unknown id; 403 without step-up for both routes (old codes still work); five bad bodies 400; two parallel removes of the two TOTPs from two stepped-up sessions leave one (barrier after the id read, so without `hold` both would pass); regenerate: ten new codes, every old code fails, a new one works once, other sessions end, this one re-issued; the six P3 cases; 0014 reverses and re-applies with a retired factor kept. `test_user_tables_live.py` (+7): a retired passkey or TOTP without secret is accepted; retired with a secret, public key or lookup blob, or a retired recovery code, is refused by the named rule. `test_factor_change_live.py` (+1, P3 #1). Real clock for AUTH-07.
- **Mutants** (bytecode off, `__pycache__` cleared; live suites of accounts factor/sign-in/step-up/recovery files and the CSRF gate, baseline 314 passed): no `len(ids) <= 1`; no `hold`; no kind filter; no `user_id` filter; remove without `recent`; regenerate without `recent`; no marker; no reissue; regenerate keeps the old codes; the conditional update keeps retired rows; `record` keeps retired rows; list/remove ids keep retired rows; retire keeps the secret; the unopenable secret counted as a guess (P3 #1). 14 of 14 killed. On the migration, by hand: the reverse without `NOT VALID`, the reverse dropping the column (both killed by the reversal test), the forward without the retired exemption (6 remove tests fail), no `retired_no_secret` rule (7 table tests fail). **Equivalent:** `match_totp`'s read without the retired filter: a retired row has no secret by the DB rule, so it opens to nothing; the filter stays as the stated rule (every lookup refuses a retired row), and the use-time filter is the one that matters.
- **Runbook:** "Enrol a user": recovery codes regenerated signed in after a step-up; "Replace or remove an authenticator"; the lost-authenticator line (not built, M4-rest must-have, tell Ying); "Rotating `tenant_source_key`" emergency step now says how users make new codes (UNI-16: new auth routes, no env var).
- **Deviations from the Opus design:** retire instead of `DELETE` (the design predates PR 136 R1); 404 also for a retired id; the shared `_changing`/`_changed` helpers; the migration reverse keeps the column.
- **Scope / affects:** `accounts/factor_model.py`, migration `0014_factor_retired`, `accounts.auth.factor_change`, `second_factor`, `stepup`, `accounts.views`, `config.urls`, `tests/csrf_fixtures.py`, three live test files, the runbook, this log. No setting or dependency.
- **Versions:** none new. **Exception to a rule?** no.
- **Decided by:** builder (Opus deep-tier sub-agent on the Opus M3h design, the PR 136 R1 retire decision and the PR 142 review P3s).

## 2026-11-11 — Repo moved to the organization skiw-dev1; PR size rule text
- **Decision:** (1) The repo is now `https://github.com/skiw-dev1/skiw-platform` (private). The organization `skiw-platform` hit its GitHub Actions spending limit: every CI job failed in about 2 seconds with "recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased" (PR 143, first run), a billing failure and not a code failure. The owner moved the repo; PR numbers and history are kept (PR 143 merged there). `agents/PROJECT.md` §4 Remote is updated; the 2026-10-21 entry stays as history. (2) **Not changed here and still open:** the image paths `ghcr.io/skiw-platform/skiw-platform` and `ghcr.io/skiw-platform/skiw-predeploy` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`publish`), `infra/host/env/{ci,production}.env`, `docs/runbooks/deploy.md`, `docs/runbooks/ci.md` and the M1 plan still name the old organization, and the old organization's `gh` and `ghcr` paths in `deploy.md`. The `publish` job pushes with the repository's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which cannot write another organization's packages, so the first push to `main` that passes `checks` is expected to fail at the push until those paths follow the repo. It has not run yet on the new organization (the `checks` job on `cecddf3` failed twice on a runner network error, unrelated). Nothing is deployed from these images yet (no VPS). The path change touches the deploy path, so it is its own PR on the deep tier. (3) **PR size rule** (PR 143 review suggestion): `agents/PROJECT.md` §4 now states the rule both roles apply: target 400 LOC, split past about 450 or say why in the PR body, stop and ask the owner past 600. PRs 141 to 143 went over 450 (+488, +521, +665) with a stated reason each, and the reviewer accepted them.
- **Why:** CI is the review gate (UNI-13) and cannot run without Actions minutes; the owner chose to move rather than raise the limit. The size rule was written down because the reviewer and the builder each cited a different line.
- **Alternatives rejected:** paying for Actions minutes in the old organization (the owner's choice); changing the image paths in this PR (deploy-path change, needs its own deep-tier review and a real `publish` run to learn what the new organization's token may push).
- **Scope / affects:** `agents/PROJECT.md` §4 (Remote, PR size rule), `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Versions:** none
- **Exception to a rule?** no
- **Decided by:** owner (the move); builder (the wording of the size rule, from the PR 143 review suggestion; the owner approves it by merging)
